### Q094 · Greatest Divisor & Remainders (HCF)

Let x be the greatest number which when divides 955, 1027, and 1075 leaves the same remainder in each case. Which of the following is NOT a factor of x?

A. 6
B. 8
C. 12
D. 16

**Answer:** D. 16
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 16 (Correct):** When three numbers leave the same unspecified remainder upon division by x, x is the HCF of their pairwise differences: 1027 − 955 = 72, 1075 − 1027 = 48, and 1075 − 955 = 120. Thus x = HCF(72, 48, 120) = 24. The factors of 24 are 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, and 24. Among the options, 16 is NOT a factor of 24.
- **A — 6:** Selecting 6, which is a valid factor of 24 rather than a non-factor.
- **B — 8:** Selecting 8, which is a valid factor of 24 rather than a non-factor.
- **C — 12:** Selecting 12, which is a valid factor of 24 rather than a non-factor.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** HCF of differences (72, 48, 120) = 24; check which option does not divide 24 (16 ∤ 24).

### Q095 · Greatest Divisor & Remainders (HCF)

When 7897, 8110, and 8536 are divided by the greatest number x, the remainder in each case is the same. What is the sum of the digits of x?

A. 6
B. 7
C. 9
D. 12

**Answer:** A. 6
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 6 (Correct):** Compute the pairwise differences: 8110 − 7897 = 213, 8536 − 8110 = 426, and 8536 − 7897 = 639. Since 426 = 2 × 213 and 639 = 3 × 213, we have x = HCF(213, 426, 639) = 213. Therefore, the sum of the digits of x is 2 + 1 + 3 = 6.
- **B — 7:** Result of summing the digits of the common remainder 16 (1 + 6 = 7) instead of the divisor 213.
- **C — 9:** Result of miscomputing the HCF as 234 (2 + 3 + 4 = 9).
- **D — 12:** Result of an arithmetic error when computing the pairwise differences.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Find just the two smallest differences (213 and 426); their HCF is 213, and digit sum = 2 + 1 + 3 = 6.

### Q096 · Greatest Divisor & Remainders (HCF)

Find the greatest number that will divide 398, 436, and 542 leaving remainders 7, 11, and 15 respectively.

A. 13
B. 17
C. 19
D. 23

**Answer:** B. 17
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 17 (Correct):** Subtract the respective remainders: 398 − 7 = 391, 436 − 11 = 425, and 542 − 15 = 527. Factorizing: 391 = 17 × 23, 425 = 17 × 25, and 527 = 17 × 31. Since gcd(23, 25, 31) = 1, the HCF is 17 (which is greater than 15).
- **A — 13:** Result of guessing a small prime divisor without verifying divisibility of all three adjusted numbers.
- **C — 19:** Result of an arithmetic slip when subtracting remainders or testing prime divisors.
- **D — 23:** Result of taking the cofactor 23 from 391 = 17 × 23 without checking 425 and 527.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Subtract remainders to get 391, 425, 527; take difference 425 − 391 = 34 = 2 × 17, and verify 17 divides all three.

### Q097 · Greatest Divisor & Remainders (HCF)

A milkman has 3 containers containing 403 litres, 434 litres, and 465 litres of milk respectively. What is the maximum capacity of a measuring vessel that can measure the milk of all three containers an exact number of times?

A. 13 litres
B. 21 litres
C. 31 litres
D. 42 litres

**Answer:** C. 31 litres
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 31 litres (Correct):** The maximum capacity of the measuring vessel is HCF(403, 434, 465). Notice 434 − 403 = 31 and 465 − 434 = 31. Also 403 = 31 × 13, 434 = 31 × 14, and 465 = 31 × 15. Thus the HCF is 31 litres.
- **A — 13 litres:** Result of reporting the quotient for 403 (403 ÷ 31 = 13) instead of the HCF.
- **B — 21 litres:** Result of an arithmetic error when subtracting consecutive volumes.
- **D — 42 litres:** Result of summing the quotients (13 + 14 + 15 = 42) instead of finding the vessel capacity.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Take the difference between consecutive volumes (434 − 403 = 31); since 31 is prime and divides 403, it is the HCF.

### Q098 · Greatest Divisor & Remainders (HCF)

When 3738, 5659, and 9501 are divided by the greatest possible number d, the remainder in each case is r. What is the value of (d + r)?

A. 1817
B. 1921
C. 3634
D. 3738

**Answer:** D. 3738
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 3738 (Correct):** Compute the pairwise differences: 5659 − 3738 = 1921, 9501 − 5659 = 3842 = 2 × 1921, and 9501 − 3738 = 5763 = 3 × 1921. Thus d = HCF(1921, 3842, 5763) = 1921. Dividing 3738 by 1921 gives 3738 = 1921 × 1 + 1817, so r = 1817. Therefore, d + r = 1921 + 1817 = 3738.
- **A — 1817:** Result of reporting only the common remainder r (1817) instead of (d + r).
- **B — 1921:** Result of reporting only the greatest divisor d (1921) instead of (d + r).
- **C — 3634:** Result of doubling the remainder (2 × 1817) instead of adding d and r.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Since 3738 = 1 × d + r (as 1921 < 3738 < 2 × 1921), d + r is automatically equal to the smallest dividend 3738!

### Q099 · Greatest Divisor & Remainders (HCF)

Three rods of lengths 44 m, 66 m, and 110 m are to be cut into pieces of equal length. What is the minimum total number of pieces that can be obtained?

A. 10
B. 11
C. 20
D. 22

**Answer:** A. 10
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 10 (Correct):** For the minimum number of equal pieces, each piece must have the maximum possible length, which is HCF(44, 66, 110) = 22 m. The number of pieces obtained is (44 / 22) + (66 / 22) + (110 / 22) = 2 + 3 + 5 = 10.
- **B — 11:** Result of taking half the HCF (11 m) or miscounting the pieces.
- **C — 20:** Result of using 11 m pieces instead of the maximum 22 m length.
- **D — 22:** Result of reporting the maximum length of each piece (22 m) instead of the total number of pieces.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Find HCF(44, 66, 110) = 22, then add the quotients: 2 + 3 + 5 = 10.

### Q100 · Greatest Divisor & Remainders (HCF)

In a seminar, the number of participants in Hindi, English, and Mathematics are 60, 84, and 108 respectively. Find the minimum number of rooms required if in each room the same number of participants are to be seated and all of them belong to the same subject.

A. 12
B. 18
C. 21
D. 42

**Answer:** C. 21
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 21 (Correct):** To minimize the number of rooms, maximize the number of participants per room, which is HCF(60, 84, 108) = 12. The minimum number of rooms required is (60 / 12) + (84 / 12) + (108 / 12) = 5 + 7 + 9 = 21.
- **A — 12:** Result of reporting the number of participants per room (HCF = 12) instead of the number of rooms.
- **B — 18:** Result of an arithmetic slip when adding 5 + 7 + 9.
- **D — 42:** Result of seating 6 participants per room instead of the maximum 12 per room.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Minimum rooms = (Sum of participants) ÷ HCF = (60 + 84 + 108) ÷ 12 = 252 ÷ 12 = 21.

### Q101 · Bells, Lights & Runners (LCM)

Four bells ring at intervals of 18, 24, 32, and 40 seconds respectively. If they all ring together at 9:00:00 AM, at what time will they next ring together?

A. 9:18:00 AM
B. 9:24:00 AM
C. 9:20:00 AM
D. 9:36:00 AM

**Answer:** B. 9:24:00 AM
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 9:24:00 AM (Correct):** The bells will next ring together after LCM(18, 24, 32, 40) seconds. Factorizing: 18 = 2 × 3², 24 = 2³ × 3, 32 = 2⁵, 40 = 2³ × 5. Thus LCM = 2⁵ × 3² × 5 = 32 × 9 × 5 = 1440 seconds. Converting to minutes: 1440 / 60 = 24 minutes. Hence they next ring together at 9:24:00 AM.
- **A — 9:18:00 AM:** Result of miscalculating the LCM as 1080 seconds (18 minutes) instead of 1440 seconds.
- **C — 9:20:00 AM:** Result of miscalculating the LCM as 1200 seconds (20 minutes) instead of 1440 seconds.
- **D — 9:36:00 AM:** Result of miscalculating the LCM as 2160 seconds (36 minutes) by including an extra factor.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** LCM(18, 24, 32, 40) = 1440 s = 24 min; add 24 min to 9:00:00 AM → 9:24:00 AM.

### Q102 · Bells, Lights & Runners (LCM)

Six bells commence tolling together and toll at intervals of 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, and 12 seconds respectively. In 30 minutes, how many times do they toll together (including the initial toll)?

A. 14
B. 15
C. 18
D. 16

**Answer:** D. 16
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 16 (Correct):** First find LCM(2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12) = 120 seconds = 2 minutes. In 30 minutes, the bells toll together every 2 minutes, which is 30 / 2 = 15 times after the start. Including the initial toll at t = 0, total simultaneous tolls = 15 + 1 = 16 times.
- **A — 14:** Result of subtracting 1 instead of adding 1 for the initial simultaneous toll.
- **B — 15:** Result of dividing 30 by 2 and forgetting to add 1 for the initial toll at t = 0.
- **C — 18:** Result of miscalculating the LCM as 100 seconds instead of 120 seconds.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Always use (Total time ÷ LCM) + 1 when counting from the initial toll: (30 ÷ 2) + 1 = 16.

### Q103 · Bells, Lights & Runners (LCM)

The traffic lights at three different road crossings change after every 48 seconds, 72 seconds, and 108 seconds respectively. If they all change simultaneously at 8:20:00 AM, when will they next change simultaneously?

A. 8:27:12 AM
B. 8:27:24 AM
C. 8:28:12 AM
D. 8:34:24 AM

**Answer:** A. 8:27:12 AM
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 8:27:12 AM (Correct):** Compute LCM(48, 72, 108): 48 = 2⁴ × 3, 72 = 2³ × 3², 108 = 2² × 3³. Thus LCM = 2⁴ × 3³ = 16 × 27 = 432 seconds. Converting 432 seconds to minutes and seconds: 432 = 7 × 60 + 12, which is 7 minutes 12 seconds. Adding this to 8:20:00 AM gives 8:27:12 AM.
- **B — 8:27:24 AM:** Result of converting 432 seconds as 7 min 24 sec instead of 7 min 12 sec.
- **C — 8:28:12 AM:** Result of converting 432 seconds as 8 min 12 sec instead of 7 min 12 sec.
- **D — 8:34:24 AM:** Result of doubling the LCM to 864 seconds (14 min 24 sec).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** LCM(48, 72, 108) = 432 s = 7 min 12 s; add directly to 8:20:00 AM → 8:27:12 AM.

### Q104 · Bells, Lights & Runners (LCM)

A, B, and C start at the same time in the same direction to run around a circular stadium. A completes a round in 252 seconds, B in 308 seconds, and C in 198 seconds, all starting at the same point. After what time will they meet again at the starting point?

A. 26 min 18 sec
B. 42 min 36 sec
C. 45 min 12 sec
D. 46 min 12 sec

**Answer:** D. 46 min 12 sec
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 46 min 12 sec (Correct):** They will meet again at the starting point after LCM(252, 308, 198) seconds. Factorizing: 252 = 2² × 3² × 7, 308 = 2² × 7 × 11, 198 = 2 × 3² × 11. Thus LCM = 2² × 3² × 7 × 11 = 4 × 9 × 77 = 2772 seconds. Dividing by 60: 2772 = 46 × 60 + 12, which is 46 minutes 12 seconds.
- **A — 26 min 18 sec:** Result of miscalculating the prime powers when finding the LCM.
- **B — 42 min 36 sec:** Result of omitting a factor of 11 or 7 while computing the LCM.
- **C — 45 min 12 sec:** Result of dividing 2772 by 60 as 45 minutes instead of 46 minutes.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Notice 252 = 36×7, 308 = 28×11, 198 = 18×11; LCM = 36 × 77 = 2772 s = 46 min 12 sec.

### Q105 · Bells, Lights & Runners (LCM)

Four runners start running simultaneously from a point on a circular track of length 1200 m at speeds of 4 m/s, 5 m/s, 6 m/s, and 8 m/s in the same direction. After how much time will they all meet together at the starting point for the first time?

A. 10 minutes
B. 20 minutes
C. 15 minutes
D. 24 minutes

**Answer:** B. 20 minutes
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 20 minutes (Correct):** First find the time taken by each runner to complete one round: A takes 1200 / 4 = 300 s, B takes 1200 / 5 = 240 s, C takes 1200 / 6 = 200 s, and D takes 1200 / 8 = 150 s. They meet at the starting point after LCM(300, 240, 200, 150) = 1200 seconds = 1200 / 60 = 20 minutes.
- **A — 10 minutes:** Result of computing the LCM as 600 seconds (10 minutes), which is not divisible by 240.
- **C — 15 minutes:** Result of computing the LCM as 900 seconds (15 minutes), which is not divisible by 240 or 200.
- **D — 24 minutes:** Result of dividing 1200 seconds by 50 instead of 60 when converting to minutes.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Meeting time at start = Track length ÷ HCF(speeds) = 1200 ÷ HCF(4, 5, 6, 8) = 1200 ÷ 1 = 1200 s = 20 min!

### Q106 · Bells, Lights & Runners (LCM)

Three electronic devices beep after every 45 minutes, 1 hour 15 minutes, and 1 hour 30 minutes respectively. All the devices beeped together at 12:00 noon. At what time will they next beep together?

A. 4:30 PM
B. 6:00 PM
C. 7:30 PM
D. 9:00 PM

**Answer:** C. 7:30 PM
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 7:30 PM (Correct):** Convert all intervals to minutes: 45 min, 75 min (1 hr 15 min), and 90 min (1 hr 30 min). Factorizing: 45 = 3² × 5, 75 = 3 × 5², 90 = 2 × 3² × 5. Thus LCM = 2 × 3² × 5² = 450 minutes = 7 hours 30 minutes. Adding 7 hours 30 minutes to 12:00 noon gives 7:30 PM.
- **A — 4:30 PM:** Result of computing the LCM as 270 minutes (4.5 hours) by missing a factor of 5 in 75.
- **B — 6:00 PM:** Result of computing the LCM as 360 minutes (6 hours), which is not divisible by 75.
- **D — 9:00 PM:** Result of computing the LCM as 540 minutes (9 hours), which is not divisible by 75.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** LCM(45, 75, 90) = 450 min = 7.5 hours; 12:00 noon + 7.5 hours = 7:30 PM.

### Q107 · Tiling Square Slabs & Cubes (HCF)

A rectangular courtyard 18 m 72 cm long and 13 m 20 cm broad is to be paved with square tiles of the same size. Find the least possible number of such tiles.

A. 3960
B. 4290
C. 4560
D. 5148

**Answer:** B. 4290
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 4290 (Correct):** Convert dimensions to centimetres: Length = 1872 cm, Breadth = 1320 cm. The side of the largest square tile is HCF(1872, 1320) = 24 cm (since 1872 = 24 × 78 and 1320 = 24 × 55, with gcd(78, 55) = 1). The least number of tiles = (1872 / 24) × (1320 / 24) = 78 × 55 = 4290.
- **A — 3960:** Result of an arithmetic slip when multiplying 78 × 55.
- **C — 4560:** Result of using a tile side of 22 cm instead of 24 cm.
- **D — 5148:** Result of using a tile side of 20 cm or multiplying 78 × 66.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Find HCF(1872, 1320) = 24 cm, then multiply the reduced dimensions: 78 × 55 = 4290.

### Q108 · Tiling Square Slabs & Cubes (HCF)

The floor of a room is 15 m 17 cm long and 9 m 2 cm broad. What is the minimum number of square tiles required to pave the floor?

A. 814
B. 740
C. 841
D. 902

**Answer:** A. 814
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 814 (Correct):** Convert dimensions to centimetres: Length = 1517 cm, Breadth = 902 cm. Using Euclidean algorithm: 1517 − 902 = 615; 902 − 615 = 287; 615 − 2 × 287 = 41; 287 = 7 × 41. So HCF(1517, 902) = 41 cm. Minimum number of tiles = (1517 / 41) × (902 / 41) = 37 × 22 = 814.
- **B — 740:** Result of multiplying 37 × 20 instead of 37 × 22.
- **C — 841:** Result of confusing the tile count with 29² or transposing digits of 814.
- **D — 902:** Result of reporting the breadth in centimetres instead of the number of tiles.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use difference method for HCF: 1517 − 902 = 615, 902 − 615 = 287 = 7 × 41; 37 × 22 = 814.

### Q109 · Tiling Square Slabs & Cubes (HCF)

A cuboid of dimensions 36 cm × 48 cm × 60 cm is cut into the minimum possible number of identical cubes with no wastage. What is the total number of cubes formed?

A. 12
B. 36
C. 60
D. 120

**Answer:** C. 60
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 60 (Correct):** For the minimum number of identical cubes, the side length of each cube must be HCF(36, 48, 60) = 12 cm. The total number of cubes formed is (36 / 12) × (48 / 12) × (60 / 12) = 3 × 4 × 5 = 60.
- **A — 12:** Result of reporting the side length of each cube (12 cm) instead of the total number of cubes.
- **B — 36:** Result of computing 3 × 4 × 3 instead of 3 × 4 × 5.
- **D — 120:** Result of doubling the answer or summing dimensions incorrectly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Divide each dimension by HCF(36, 48, 60) = 12 to get 3, 4, 5, then multiply: 3 × 4 × 5 = 60.

### Q110 · Tiling Square Slabs & Cubes (HCF)

A rectangular field is 112 m long and 84 m wide. It is to be divided into identical square plots of maximum possible area. What is the area of each square plot?

A. 196 m²
B. 392 m²
C. 1176 m²
D. 784 m²

**Answer:** D. 784 m²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 784 m² (Correct):** The side of the largest possible square plot is HCF(112, 84) = 28 m (since 112 = 28 × 4 and 84 = 28 × 3). Therefore, the area of each square plot is 28² = 784 m².
- **A — 196 m²:** Result of using half the HCF (14 m) as the side of the square (14² = 196).
- **B — 392 m²:** Result of computing 28 × 14 instead of 28².
- **C — 1176 m²:** Result of computing 28 × 42 instead of 28².
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Side = HCF(112, 84) = 112 − 84 = 28 m; Area = 28² = 784 m².

### Q111 · Tiling Square Slabs & Cubes (HCF)

A hall is 39 m 10 cm long and 35 m 70 cm wide. What is the side length of the largest square marble slab that can be used to pave the floor without cutting any slab?

A. 1.3 m
B. 1.7 m
C. 1.9 m
D. 3.4 m

**Answer:** B. 1.7 m
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 1.7 m (Correct):** Convert dimensions to centimetres: 3910 cm and 3570 cm. Find HCF(3910, 3570): 3910 − 3570 = 340, and 3570 = 10 × 340 + 170, with 340 = 2 × 170. Thus HCF = 170 cm = 1.7 m.
- **A — 1.3 m:** Result of an incorrect prime factorization of 3910 or 3570.
- **C — 1.9 m:** Result of mistaking 391 = 17 × 23 as divisible by 19.
- **D — 3.4 m:** Result of taking the difference (39.1 − 35.7 = 3.4 m) directly as the HCF without checking divisibility.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Difference = 39.1 − 35.7 = 3.4 m; since 3.4 does not divide 35.7, try half of it: 1.7 m divides both (23 and 21 times).

### Q112 · Tiling Square Slabs & Cubes (HCF)

A rectangular sheet of paper is 68 cm long and 51 cm wide. If it is cut into the minimum number of identical square pieces with no paper left over, what is the perimeter of each square piece?

A. 68 cm
B. 17 cm
C. 34 cm
D. 51 cm

**Answer:** A. 68 cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 68 cm (Correct):** The side of each identical square piece is HCF(68, 51) = 17 cm (since 68 = 17 × 4 and 51 = 17 × 3). Therefore, the perimeter of each square piece is 4 × 17 = 68 cm.
- **B — 17 cm:** Result of reporting the side length of each square (17 cm) instead of its perimeter.
- **C — 34 cm:** Result of computing 2 × side (34 cm) instead of 4 × side.
- **D — 51 cm:** Result of computing 3 × side (51 cm) instead of 4 × side.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Side = HCF(68, 51) = 68 − 51 = 17 cm, so Perimeter = 4 × 17 = 68 cm.

### Q113 · LCM & HCF of Fractions & Decimals

What is the LCM of 2/3, 4/9, 5/6, and 7/12?

A. 70/3
B. 140/3
C. 35/9
D. 1/36

**Answer:** B. 140/3
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 140/3 (Correct):** All four fractions are already in lowest terms. Using the formula LCM of fractions = LCM(numerators) / HCF(denominators): LCM(2, 4, 5, 7) = 140 and HCF(3, 9, 6, 12) = 3. Thus the required LCM is 140/3.
- **A — 70/3:** Result of missing a factor of 2 when computing LCM(2, 4, 5, 7) as 70 instead of 140.
- **C — 35/9:** Result of taking the LCM of the denominators (36) instead of their HCF (3), giving 140/36 = 35/9.
- **D — 1/36:** Result of computing the HCF of the fractions instead of their LCM.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** LCM(fractions) = LCM(numerators) ÷ HCF(denominators) = 140 ÷ 3 = 140/3.

### Q114 · LCM & HCF of Fractions & Decimals

What is the HCF of 3/4, 9/10, and 15/16?

A. 3/40
B. 3/160
C. 3/80
D. 45/2

**Answer:** C. 3/80
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 3/80 (Correct):** All three fractions are already in lowest terms. Using HCF of fractions = HCF(numerators) / LCM(denominators): HCF(3, 9, 15) = 3 and LCM(4, 10, 16) = 80. Therefore, the required HCF is 3/80.
- **A — 3/40:** Result of miscalculating LCM(4, 10, 16) as 40 instead of 80.
- **B — 3/160:** Result of multiplying 4 × 10 × 4 = 160 instead of finding LCM(4, 10, 16) = 80.
- **D — 45/2:** Result of computing the LCM of the fractions (45/2) instead of their HCF.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** HCF(fractions) = HCF(numerators) ÷ LCM(denominators) = 3 ÷ 80 = 3/80.

### Q115 · LCM & HCF of Fractions & Decimals

Find the LCM of 1.08, 0.36, and 0.9.

A. 0.18
B. 1.08
C. 10.8
D. 5.4

**Answer:** D. 5.4
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 5.4 (Correct):** Equalize the decimal places to two decimal places: 1.08, 0.36, and 0.90. Multiplying each by 100 gives integers 108, 36, and 90. Their LCM is 540 (since 108 = 2² × 3³, 36 = 2² × 3², 90 = 2 × 3² × 5, so LCM = 4 × 27 × 5 = 540). Dividing by 100 gives 5.4.
- **A — 0.18:** Result of finding the HCF (0.18) of the decimals instead of their LCM.
- **B — 1.08:** Result of assuming the largest given decimal (1.08) is the LCM without checking 0.9.
- **C — 10.8:** Result of scaling 0.9 as 9 instead of 90 (inconsistent decimal places), or doubling 5.4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Pad to equal decimal places (108, 36, 90), find LCM = 540, then shift decimal back 2 places → 5.4.

## Chapter 3: Percentage

### Q116 · Percentage Comparison

If A's income is 25% more than B's income, by what percent is B's income less than A's income?

A. 20%
B. 25%
C. 16.67%
D. 15%

**Answer:** A. 20%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 20% (Correct):** Let B's income = 100. Then A's income = 100 + 25 = 125. Required percentage = [(125 - 100) / 125] × 100 = (25 / 125) × 100 = 20%.
- **B — 25%:** Result of assuming percentage difference is symmetric and keeping 25%.
- **C — 16.67%:** Result of using 1/6 instead of 1/5 when converting the fraction.
- **D — 15%:** Result of taking an arbitrary subtraction of 10% from 25%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If A is x% more than B, B is [x / (100 + x)] × 100% less than A. Since 25% = 1/4, the reverse is 1/(4+1) = 1/5 = 20%.

### Q117 · Percentage Comparison

If a number A is 37.5% less than B, then B is what percent more than A?

A. 37.5%
B. 60%
C. 62.5%
D. 40%

**Answer:** B. 60%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 60% (Correct):** 37.5% = 3/8. Let B = 8. Then A = 8 - 3 = 5. Percentage by which B is more than A = [(8 - 5) / 5] × 100 = (3 / 5) × 100 = 60%.
- **A — 37.5%:** Result of assuming the percentage remains unchanged in reverse.
- **C — 62.5%:** Result of taking the complement (100% - 37.5%) instead of changing the base.
- **D — 40%:** Result of computing 3/8 of 100 as 40% approximately or misreading 2/5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Convert 37.5% to 3/8. When A is 3/8 less than B, B is 3/(8 - 3) = 3/5 = 60% more than A.

### Q118 · Percentage Comparison

Two numbers are respectively 20% and 50% more than a third number. What percent of the second number is the first number?

A. 75%
B. 85%
C. 80%
D. 70%

**Answer:** C. 80%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 80% (Correct):** Let the third number be 100. First number = 120, Second number = 150. Required percentage = (120 / 150) × 100 = (4 / 5) × 100 = 80%.
- **A — 75%:** Result of computing (150 - 120) / 120 or miscalculating 4/5 as 75%.
- **B — 85%:** Result of subtracting 15% from 100% instead of dividing 120 by 150.
- **D — 70%:** Result of taking 100% - (50% - 20%) = 70% directly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Assume the third number is 100; the ratio of first to second is 120 : 150 = 4 : 5, and 4/5 = 80% immediately.

### Q119 · Percentage Comparison

If 60% of (x - y) = 45% of (x + y), and y = k% of x, then what is 21% of k?

A. 7
B. 6
C. 4
D. 3

**Answer:** D. 3
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 3 (Correct):** 60(x - y) = 45(x + y) gives 4(x - y) = 3(x + y), so 4x - 4y = 3x + 3y, which means x = 7y. Thus y = (1/7)x, so k = 100/7. Therefore, 21% of k = (21/100) × (100/7) = 3.
- **A — 7:** Result of taking x/y = 7 directly as the value of 21% of k.
- **B — 6:** Result of making a sign error in 4x - 3x = 4y + 3y and getting 6.
- **C — 4:** Result of using ratio 4:3 instead of solving for x and y.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use componendo-dividendo: (x + y)/(x - y) = 60/45 = 4/3, so x/y = (4+3)/(4-3) = 7/1. Then 21% of (100/7) = 21/7 = 3.

### Q120 · Percentage Comparison

A is 40% less than B, and C is 40% of the sum of A and B. By what percentage is B greater than C?

A. 56.25%
B. 36%
C. 62.5%
D. 43.75%

**Answer:** A. 56.25%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 56.25% (Correct):** Let B = 100. Then A = 100 - 40 = 60. Sum (A + B) = 160, so C = 40% of 160 = 64. B is greater than C by 100 - 64 = 36. Required percentage = (36 / 64) × 100 = (9 / 16) × 100 = 56.25%.
- **B — 36%:** Result of taking B - C = 36 directly as the percentage without dividing by C.
- **C — 62.5%:** Result of dividing 36 by A (60) instead of C (64), giving 60% or miscalculating.
- **D — 43.75%:** Result of computing (100 - 64) / 100 or taking the complement of 56.25%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Set B = 100, so A = 60 and C = 0.4 × 160 = 64. Difference is 36 on base 64; 36/64 = 9/16 = 56.25% (since 1/16 = 6.25%).

### Q121 · Percentage Comparison

If 20% of a = b, then b% of 20 is the same as which of the following?

A. 20% of a
B. 4% of a
C. 5% of a
D. 10% of a

**Answer:** B. 4% of a
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 4% of a (Correct):** Given b = 20% of a = 0.2a. Now b% of 20 = (b / 100) × 20 = b / 5 = 0.2a / 5 = 0.04a = 4% of a.
- **A — 20% of a:** Result of confusing b% of 20 with 20% of a directly.
- **C — 5% of a:** Result of dividing 20% by 4 instead of 5.
- **D — 10% of a:** Result of halving 20% instead of taking 20% of 20%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Remember x% of y = y% of x. So b% of 20 = 20% of b = 20% of (20% of a) = 4% of a.

### Q122 · Percentage Comparison

A student multiplied a number by 3/5 instead of 5/3. What is the percentage error in the calculation?

A. 36%
B. 44%
C. 64%
D. 54%

**Answer:** C. 64%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 64% (Correct):** Let the number be LCM(5, 3) = 15. Correct result = 15 × (5/3) = 25. Incorrect result = 15 × (3/5) = 9. Error = 25 - 9 = 16. Percentage error = (16 / 25) × 100 = 64%.
- **A — 36%:** Result of computing 9/25 × 100 instead of the error (25 - 9)/25.
- **B — 44%:** Result of subtracting 5/3 and 3/5 without dividing by the correct value.
- **D — 54%:** Result of miscalculating 16/25 × 100 or taking an incorrect denominator.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Cross-multiply 5/3 and 3/5 to get 25 (correct) and 9 (wrong). Error% = (25 - 9)/25 × 100 = 16 × 4 = 64%.

### Q123 · Percentage Comparison

If 85% of a number is added to 75, then the result is the number itself. Find the number.

A. 450
B. 600
C. 550
D. 500

**Answer:** D. 500
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 500 (Correct):** Let the number be x. According to the question, 85% of x + 75 = 100% of x. Thus, (100% - 85%) of x = 75, so 15% of x = 75. Hence x = (75 × 100) / 15 = 500.
- **A — 450:** Result of dividing 75 by 16.67% or taking 9 × 50.
- **B — 600:** Result of taking 12.5% instead of 15% for the remaining part.
- **C — 550:** Result of adding 50 to 500 due to an arithmetic slip.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 100% - 85% = 15% corresponds to 75. Since 15% = 75, 1% = 5, so 100% = 500.

### Q124 · Percentage Comparison

When 60 is subtracted from 60% of a number, the result is 60. What is the number?

A. 200
B. 100
C. 150
D. 240

**Answer:** A. 200
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 200 (Correct):** Let the number be x. Then 60% of x - 60 = 60, which gives 60% of x = 120. Therefore, x = (120 × 100) / 60 = 200.
- **B — 100:** Result of equating 60% of x to 60 instead of 120.
- **C — 150:** Result of dividing 90 by 0.60 instead of 120 by 0.60.
- **D — 240:** Result of multiplying 60 by 4 instead of solving 0.6x = 120.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 60% of N = 60 + 60 = 120. Since 60% = 120, 10% = 20, so 100% = 200.

### Q125 · Successive Percentage Change

The price of an article is first increased by 20% and then decreased by 25%. What is the net percentage change in its price?

A. 5% decrease
B. 10% decrease
C. 5% increase
D. 10% increase

**Answer:** B. 10% decrease
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 10% decrease (Correct):** Using the successive percentage formula: Net change = a + b + (ab / 100) = 20 - 25 + (20 × (-25)) / 100 = -5 - 5 = -10%. The negative sign indicates a 10% decrease.
- **A — 5% decrease:** Result of simply taking 20% - 25% = -5% and ignoring the ab/100 term.
- **C — 5% increase:** Result of subtracting 20 from 25 with the wrong sign.
- **D — 10% increase:** Result of getting the magnitude 10% right but misinterpreting the sign as an increase.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use 100 -> 120 -> (120 × 3/4) = 90. Net change from 100 to 90 is a 10% decrease.

### Q126 · Successive Percentage Change

If the radius of a right circular cylinder is increased by 20% and its height is decreased by 25%, what is the percentage change in its volume?

A. 5% increase
B. 8% decrease
C. 8% increase
D. 12% increase

**Answer:** C. 8% increase
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 8% increase (Correct):** Volume of a cylinder is proportional to r²h. Initial r = 5, h = 4 gives V = 25 × 4 = 100. New r = 6 (20% up), new h = 3 (25% down) gives V_new = 36 × 3 = 108. Percentage change = +8% (8% increase).
- **A — 5% increase:** Result of treating r as linear (r × h) instead of quadratic (r² × h): 20 - 25 - 5 = -10% or +5%.
- **B — 8% decrease:** Result of misreading 108 vs 100 as a decrease instead of an increase.
- **D — 12% increase:** Result of applying +20% twice without the -25% interaction term properly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use ratio method: r: 5 -> 6 (twice for r²: 25 -> 36) and h: 4 -> 3. Multiply: 25×4=100 to 36×3=108, so +8%.

### Q127 · Successive Percentage Change

The length of a rectangle is increased by 25%. By what percent should its breadth be decreased so that the area remains unchanged?

A. 25%
B. 16.67%
C. 15%
D. 20%

**Answer:** D. 20%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 20% (Correct):** Area = Length × Breadth. Since 25% = 1/4, length increases from 4 to 5. To keep area constant, breadth must decrease from 5 to 4. Percentage decrease = (1 / 5) × 100 = 20%.
- **A — 25%:** Result of assuming breadth must decrease by the same 25% as length increased.
- **B — 16.67%:** Result of using 1/6 instead of 1/5 for the reverse fraction.
- **C — 15%:** Result of taking an arbitrary 15% decrease.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For constant area, L ↑ by 1/4 means B ↓ by 1/(4+1) = 1/5 = 20%.

### Q128 · Successive Percentage Change

A number is first increased by 10%, then increased by 20%, and finally decreased by 20%. What is the net percentage change in the number?

A. 5.6% increase
B. 10% increase
C. 4.4% decrease
D. 5.6% decrease

**Answer:** A. 5.6% increase
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 5.6% increase (Correct):** Let the initial number be 100. After +10%: 110. After +20%: 110 × 1.2 = 132. After -20%: 132 × 0.8 = 105.6. Net change = 105.6 - 100 = +5.6%, i.e., a 5.6% increase.
- **B — 10% increase:** Result of cancelling +20% and -20% directly and leaving +10%.
- **C — 4.4% decrease:** Result of subtracting 105.6 from 110 instead of 100.
- **D — 5.6% decrease:** Result of getting 5.6% but mistaking the sign for a decrease.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** +20% and -20% give -(20²/100)% = -4%. Now combine +10% and -4%: 10 - 4 - (40/100) = +5.6%.

### Q129 · Successive Percentage Change

If each side of a square is increased by 15%, what is the percentage increase in its area?

A. 30%
B. 32.25%
C. 22.5%
D. 31.25%

**Answer:** B. 32.25%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 32.25% (Correct):** Area of a square = side². Using successive change: 15 + 15 + (15 × 15)/100 = 30 + 2.25 = 32.25%.
- **A — 30%:** Result of doubling 15% to 30% and forgetting the 15²/100 term.
- **C — 22.5%:** Result of misplacing the decimal or multiplying 15 by 1.5.
- **D — 31.25%:** Result of computing 15² = 125 instead of 225, giving 31.25%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For a square, area % change = 2x + x²/100. Here 2(15) + 225/100 = 32.25%.

### Q130 · Successive Percentage Change

If the radius of a sphere is increased by 10%, what is the percentage increase in its surface area?

A. 20%
B. 33.1%
C. 21%
D. 10%

**Answer:** C. 21%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 21% (Correct):** Surface area of a sphere is 4πr², which is proportional to r². Using successive percentage change for r × r: 10 + 10 + (10 × 10)/100 = 21%.
- **A — 20%:** Result of doubling 10% without adding the 10²/100 = 1% term.
- **B — 33.1%:** Result of computing volume increase (r³ -> 33.1%) instead of surface area (r²).
- **D — 10%:** Result of assuming surface area scales linearly with radius.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Surface area depends on r² (not r³!). For +10%, r² increases by 10 + 10 + 1 = 21%.

### Q131 · Successive Percentage Change

The salary of a worker was first reduced by 10% and then raised by 10%. What is the net percentage change in his salary?

A. No change
B. 1% increase
C. 2% decrease
D. 1% decrease

**Answer:** D. 1% decrease
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 1% decrease (Correct):** Net change = -10 + 10 + (-10 × 10)/100 = -1%. Thus, there is a net 1% decrease in his salary.
- **A — No change:** Result of assuming -10% and +10% cancel each other out completely.
- **B — 1% increase:** Result of getting the 1% magnitude right but taking it as an increase.
- **C — 2% decrease:** Result of subtracting 1% twice.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** An equal x% increase and x% decrease always results in a net loss of (x²/100)%. Here 10²/100 = 1% decrease.

### Q132 · Successive Percentage Change

The value of a laptop depreciates by 10% in the first year, 20% in the second year, and 25% in the third year. What is the overall percentage depreciation after 3 years?

A. 46%
B. 55%
C. 54%
D. 45%

**Answer:** A. 46%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 46% (Correct):** Let initial value = 100. After 1st year: 100 × 0.9 = 90. After 2nd year: 90 × 0.8 = 72. After 3rd year: 72 × 0.75 = 54. Overall depreciation = 100 - 54 = 46%.
- **B — 55%:** Result of simply adding 10% + 20% + 25% = 55%.
- **C — 54%:** Result of reporting the remaining value (54%) instead of the depreciation (46%).
- **D — 45%:** Result of making a multiplication error in 72 × 0.75.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Multiply remaining fractions: (9/10) × (4/5) × (3/4) = 27/50 = 54%. Depreciation = 100% - 54% = 46%.

### Q133 · Successive Percentage Change

If the numerator of a fraction is increased by 20% and its denominator is decreased by 25%, the resulting fraction becomes 16/15. What was the original fraction?

A. 3/4
B. 2/3
C. 4/5
D. 5/6

**Answer:** B. 2/3
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 2/3 (Correct):** Let the original fraction be x/y. Then (120% of x) / (75% of y) = 16/15, which simplifies to (8/5)(x/y) = 16/15. Therefore, x/y = (16/15) × (5/8) = 2/3.
- **A — 3/4:** Result of inverting the multiplier or miscalculating 120/75.
- **C — 4/5:** Result of multiplying 16/15 by 3/4 directly.
- **D — 5/6:** Result of adding 20% and 25% wrongly to the numerator and denominator.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Write (120/75) × (x/y) = 16/15. Simplify 120/75 = 8/5; then x/y = (16/15) × (5/8) = 2/3.

### Q134 · Successive Percentage Change

A's salary increases by 20% every year. If his present salary is ₹43,200, what was his salary 2 years ago?

A. ₹32,000
B. ₹28,800
C. ₹30,000
D. ₹36,000

**Answer:** C. ₹30,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹30,000 (Correct):** Let the salary 2 years ago be P. Then P × (1.2)² = 43,200, so 1.44P = 43,200. Thus P = 43,200 / 1.44 = ₹30,000.
- **A — ₹32,000:** Result of subtracting 26% or estimating roughly instead of dividing by 1.44.
- **B — ₹28,800:** Result of decreasing ₹43,200 by 20% once and then taking 80% again (43200 × 0.8 × 0.8 = 27648, or similar).
- **D — ₹36,000:** Result of dividing ₹43,200 by 1.2 once (only 1 year ago) instead of 1.44.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 20% = 1/5, so 2 years ratio is 25 : 36. Here 36 units = ₹43,200 -> 1 unit = ₹1,200 -> 25 units = ₹30,000.

### Q135 · Price-Consumption & Expenditure Invariance

The price of sugar increases by 25%. By what percentage should a family reduce its consumption so that the expenditure on sugar remains unchanged?

A. 25%
B. 16.67%
C. 15%
D. 20%

**Answer:** D. 20%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 20% (Correct):** 25% = 1/4, so price ratio (Old : New) = 4 : 5. For constant expenditure, consumption ratio must be 5 : 4. Percentage reduction = [(5 - 4) / 5] × 100 = 20%.
- **A — 25%:** Result of assuming consumption reduction equals the price increase (25%).
- **B — 16.67%:** Result of confusing 1/5 (20%) with 1/6 (16.67%).
- **C — 15%:** Result of subtracting 10% from 25% arbitrarily.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Price ↑ by 1/4 => Consumption ↓ by 1/(4+1) = 1/5 = 20%.

### Q136 · Price-Consumption & Expenditure Invariance

The price of petrol increases by 20%, but a motorist wants his expenditure on petrol to increase by only 8%. By what percentage must he reduce his consumption?

A. 10%
B. 12%
C. 8%
D. 15%

**Answer:** A. 10%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 10% (Correct):** Let initial price = 100 and initial consumption = 100 (initial expenditure = 100). New price = 120 and target expenditure = 108. New consumption = 108 / 120 = 0.90 (i.e., 90% of original). Reduction = (120 - 108) / 120 × 100 = 10%.
- **B — 12%:** Result of taking the difference 20% - 8% = 12% directly without dividing by 120.
- **C — 8%:** Result of using the expenditure increase (8%) as the consumption change.
- **D — 15%:** Result of miscalculating 12/120 × 100.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Draw the 100 -> 120 (price) and 100 -> 108 (exp) bridge. Reduction = (120 - 108) / 120 × 100 = 12/120 × 100 = 10%.

### Q137 · Price-Consumption & Expenditure Invariance

A reduction of 20% in the price of rice enables a buyer to purchase 5 kg more for ₹600. What is the reduced price of rice per kg?

A. ₹30
B. ₹24
C. ₹25
D. ₹20

**Answer:** B. ₹24
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹24 (Correct):** Due to a 20% reduction in price, the buyer saves 20% of ₹600 = ₹120. With this ₹120 saved, he buys 5 kg more rice. Therefore, the reduced price per kg = 120 / 5 = ₹24.
- **A — ₹30:** Result of finding the original price (24 / 0.8 = ₹30) instead of the reduced price.
- **C — ₹25:** Result of dividing ₹600 by 24 instead of using the 20% saving.
- **D — ₹20:** Result of dividing 100 by 5 instead of 120 by 5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Reduced price per kg = (Reduction% × Total Money) / Extra Quantity = (20% × 600) / 5 = 120 / 5 = ₹24.

### Q138 · Price-Consumption & Expenditure Invariance

When the price of wheat increases by 25%, a person is able to buy 4 kg less for ₹400. What was the original price of wheat per kg?

A. ₹25
B. ₹18
C. ₹20
D. ₹24

**Answer:** C. ₹20
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹20 (Correct):** New price of 4 kg = 25% of ₹400 = ₹100, so the new price = 100 / 4 = ₹25/kg. Since this is 125% (5/4) of the original price, original price = 25 × (4/5) = ₹20/kg.
- **A — ₹25:** Result of reporting the new increased price (₹25/kg) instead of the original price.
- **B — ₹18:** Result of taking 75% of ₹25 instead of dividing by 1.25.
- **D — ₹24:** Result of miscalculating 25 × 4/5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** New price = (25% of 400)/4 = ₹25. Since 25% = 1/4 increase (4 -> 5), 5 units = ₹25 => Original (4 units) = ₹20.

### Q139 · Price-Consumption & Expenditure Invariance

The price of cooking oil decreases by 15%, and a family increases its consumption by 20%. What is the percentage change in the family's expenditure on oil?

A. 5% increase
B. 2% decrease
C. 5% decrease
D. 2% increase

**Answer:** D. 2% increase
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 2% increase (Correct):** Expenditure = Price × Consumption. Using successive percentage change: -15 + 20 + (-15 × 20)/100 = 5 - 3 = +2%. Thus, expenditure increases by 2%.
- **A — 5% increase:** Result of taking 20% - 15% = 5% and forgetting the interaction term (-15 × 20)/100.
- **B — 2% decrease:** Result of getting the magnitude 2% correct but choosing decrease instead of increase.
- **C — 5% decrease:** Result of subtracting 20 from 15 with the wrong sign and ignoring the product.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 0.85 × 1.20 = 1.02, which is directly a 2% increase.

### Q140 · Price-Consumption & Expenditure Invariance

Due to a 10% increase in the price of tea, a family reduces its consumption by 10%. What is the percentage change in its expenditure on tea?

A. 1% decrease
B. No change
C. 1% increase
D. 2% decrease

**Answer:** A. 1% decrease
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 1% decrease (Correct):** Expenditure = Price × Consumption. Net change = +10 - 10 + (10 × (-10))/100 = -1%, meaning a 1% decrease.
- **B — No change:** Result of assuming +10% and -10% cancel out completely.
- **C — 1% increase:** Result of mistaking -1% for a 1% increase.
- **D — 2% decrease:** Result of doubling 1%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equal +x% and -x% changes always yield -(x²/100)% = -(10²/100)% = 1% decrease.

### Q141 · Price-Consumption & Expenditure Invariance

When the price of a ticket is reduced by 20%, its sales increase by x%. If the total revenue increases by 20%, what is the value of x?

A. 40
B. 50
C. 45
D. 60

**Answer:** B. 50
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 50 (Correct):** Revenue = Price × Sales. Let initial price = 100 and sales = 100. New price = 80 and new revenue = 120. Thus new sales = (120 / 80) × 100 = 150. Hence sales increased by x = 50%.
- **A — 40:** Result of simply adding 20% + 20% = 40%.
- **C — 45:** Result of taking the average of 40 and 50.
- **D — 60:** Result of dividing 120 by 75 instead of 80.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** From 80 (new price) to 120 (new revenue), the jump is +40 on base 80: (40/80) × 100 = 50%.

### Q142 · Price-Consumption & Expenditure Invariance

If the price of milk rises by 12.5%, by what percentage must a household reduce its milk consumption to keep its monthly milk budget unchanged?

A. 12.5%
B. 10%
C. 11.11%
D. 14.28%

**Answer:** C. 11.11%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 11.11% (Correct):** 12.5% = 1/8, so the price ratio (Old : New) = 8 : 9. For the budget to remain unchanged, consumption ratio must be 9 : 8. Required reduction = (1 / 9) × 100 = 11.11%.
- **A — 12.5%:** Result of assuming consumption must drop by the same 12.5%.
- **B — 10%:** Result of using 1/10 (10%) instead of 1/9.
- **D — 14.28%:** Result of using 1/7 (14.28%) by subtracting 1 from 8 instead of adding 1 to 8.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 12.5% = 1/8 increase => reduction = 1/(8+1) = 1/9 = 11.11%.

### Q143 · Price-Consumption & Expenditure Invariance

The price of rice rises by 30%, and a family's expenditure on rice increases by 17%. By what percentage did the family's consumption of rice decrease?

A. 13%
B. 15%
C. 12%
D. 10%

**Answer:** D. 10%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 10% (Correct):** Let initial price = 100 and consumption = 100 (expenditure = 100). New price = 130, new expenditure = 117. New consumption = (117 / 130) × 100 = 90. Thus consumption decreased by 100 - 90 = 10%.
- **A — 13%:** Result of subtracting 17% from 30% directly without dividing by 130.
- **B — 15%:** Result of miscalculating 13/130 × 100.
- **C — 12%:** Result of taking 17/130 approximately.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Consumption change = (117 - 130) / 130 × 100 = -13 / 130 × 100 = -10% (10% decrease).

### Q144 · Election Votes

In an election between two candidates, the winning candidate got 65% of the total votes and won by a majority of 600 votes. Assuming all votes were valid, find the total number of votes polled.

A. 2,000
B. 1,800
C. 2,400
D. 1,500

**Answer:** A. 2,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 2,000 (Correct):** Winner got 65% of total votes, so the loser got 100% - 65% = 35%. Margin = 65% - 35% = 30% of total votes. Given 30% = 600, total votes = (600 × 100) / 30 = 2,000.
- **B — 1,800:** Result of taking 33.3% instead of 30% as the margin.
- **C — 2,400:** Result of dividing 600 by 25% instead of 30%.
- **D — 1,500:** Result of dividing 600 by 40% instead of 30%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Margin% = 2 × (Winner% - 50%) = 2 × 15% = 30%. 30% = 600 => 100% = 2,000.

### Q145 · Election Votes

In an election between two candidates, 10% of the voters did not cast their votes, and 10% of the votes polled were declared invalid. The winner secured 54% of the valid votes and won by 1,620 votes. Find the total number of voters on the voting list.

A. 20,000
B. 25,000
C. 22,500
D. 30,000

**Answer:** B. 25,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 25,000 (Correct):** Let total voters = V. Votes polled = 0.9V. Valid votes = 0.9 × 0.9V = 0.81V. Winner got 54% of valid votes and loser got 46%, so margin = 8% of valid votes. Thus 0.08 × 0.81V = 1,620, giving 0.81V = 20,250, so V = 20,250 / 0.81 = 25,000.
- **A — 20,000:** Result of assuming 20% total reduction (0.80V) instead of successive 10% and 10% (0.81V).
- **C — 22,500:** Result of forgetting one of the 10% deductions (using 0.9V instead of 0.81V).
- **D — 30,000:** Result of miscalculating the margin as 6% instead of 8%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Chain equation: V × (9/10) × (9/10) × (8/100) = 1,620. Since 81 × 8 = 648 and 1620/648 = 2.5, V = 2.5 × 10,000 = 25,000.

### Q146 · Election Votes

In an election between two candidates, 20% of the voters on the list did not cast their votes, and 120 votes were declared invalid. The winner received 48% of the total votes on the list and won by 200 votes. Find the total number of voters on the list.

A. 1,000
B. 800
C. 500
D. 600

**Answer:** C. 500
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 500 (Correct):** Let total voters on the list = V. Votes polled = 0.8V, and valid votes = 0.8V - 120. Winner got 0.48V, so loser got (0.8V - 120) - 0.48V = 0.32V - 120. Margin = 0.48V - (0.32V - 120) = 0.16V + 120 = 200. Thus 0.16V = 80, giving V = 500.
- **A — 1,000:** Result of using 0.16V = 200 without subtracting the 120 invalid votes.
- **B — 800:** Result of adding 120 to 200 (0.16V = 320 -> V = 2,000) or using wrong denominator.
- **D — 600:** Result of miscalculating 80 / 0.16.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Shortcut: Give the 120 invalid votes to the loser's tally; the margin drops by 120 to 80! So (48% - 32%) = 16% of V = 80 => V = 500.

### Q147 · Election Votes

In an election between two candidates, 15% of the voters did not cast their votes and 20% of the votes cast were found invalid. The winning candidate got 75% of the valid votes and won by 8,500 votes. Find the total number of voters.

A. 20,000
B. 30,000
C. 24,000
D. 25,000

**Answer:** D. 25,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 25,000 (Correct):** Let total voters = V. Votes cast = 0.85V. Valid votes = 0.80 × 0.85V = 0.68V. Winner got 75% of valid votes and loser got 25%, so margin = 50% of valid votes = 0.50 × 0.68V = 0.34V. Given 0.34V = 8,500, we get V = 8,500 / 0.34 = 25,000.
- **A — 20,000:** Result of taking 1 - (0.15 + 0.20) = 0.65V instead of 0.85 × 0.80 = 0.68V.
- **B — 30,000:** Result of dividing 8,500 by 0.283 instead of 0.34.
- **C — 24,000:** Result of miscalculating 8,500 / 0.34.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Chain: V × (17/20) × (4/5) × (1/2) = 8,500 => V × (17/50) = 8,500 => V = 500 × 50 = 25,000.

### Q148 · Election Votes

In an election between two candidates, A got 55% of the total valid votes. 20% of the total votes were declared invalid. If the total number of votes polled was 7,500, how many valid votes did the other candidate B receive?

A. 2,700
B. 3,300
C. 3,375
D. 2,500

**Answer:** A. 2,700
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 2,700 (Correct):** Total votes = 7,500. Valid votes = 80% of 7,500 = 6,000. Since A got 55% of valid votes, B got 45% of valid votes. Valid votes for B = 45% of 6,000 = 2,700.
- **B — 3,300:** Result of finding A's valid votes (55% of 6,000 = 3,300) instead of B's.
- **C — 3,375:** Result of taking 45% of total votes (7,500) without removing the 20% invalid votes.
- **D — 2,500:** Result of subtracting 20% from 55% and taking 35% of 7,500.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Valid votes = 0.8 × 7,500 = 6,000. B's share = 45% of 6,000 = (9/20) × 6,000 = 2,700.

### Q149 · Election Votes

Three candidates contested an election. The first candidate got 40% of the votes and the second got 36% of the votes. If the total number of votes polled was 36,000, how many votes did the third candidate get?

A. 7,200
B. 8,640
C. 9,000
D. 12,960

**Answer:** B. 8,640
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 8,640 (Correct):** Percentage of votes for the third candidate = 100% - (40% + 36%) = 24%. Number of votes = 24% of 36,000 = 0.24 × 36,000 = 8,640.
- **A — 7,200:** Result of miscalculating 100 - 76 as 20% instead of 24%.
- **C — 9,000:** Result of using 25% instead of 24%.
- **D — 12,960:** Result of computing votes for the second candidate (36% of 36,000) instead of the third.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Remaining % = 24%. 24% of 36,000 = 24 × 360 = 8,640 (or 25% - 1% = 9,000 - 360 = 8,640).

### Q150 · Election Votes

In a college election between two candidates, 10% of the students on the list did not vote, and 60 votes were declared invalid. The winner secured 47% of the total students on the list and won by 308 votes. Find the total number of students on the list.

A. 7,700
B. 5,400
C. 6,200
D. 6,800

**Answer:** C. 6,200
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 6,200 (Correct):** Let total students = V. Votes polled = 0.9V; valid votes = 0.9V - 60. Winner = 0.47V, so loser = (0.9V - 60) - 0.47V = 0.43V - 60. Margin = 0.47V - (0.43V - 60) = 0.04V + 60 = 308. Thus 0.04V = 248, so V = 6,200.
- **A — 7,700:** Result of setting 0.04V = 308 without subtracting the 60 invalid votes.
- **B — 5,400:** Result of adding 60 to 308 or taking 0.06V.
- **D — 6,800:** Result of miscalculating 248 / 0.04.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Give the 60 invalid votes to the loser: margin becomes 308 - 60 = 248. Since (47% - 43%) = 4% = 248, 100% = 248 × 25 = 6,200.

### Q151 · Election Votes

In an election between two candidates, 8% of the voters on the voting list did not cast their votes. The winning candidate secured 48% of the total votes on the list and defeated his rival by 1,100 votes. Find the total number of voters on the list.

A. 25,000
B. 22,000
C. 30,000
D. 27,500

**Answer:** D. 27,500
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 27,500 (Correct):** Let total voters = V. Votes cast = 100% - 8% = 92% of V. Winner got 48% of V, so the rival got 92% - 48% = 44% of V. Margin = 48% - 44% = 4% of V = 1,100. Therefore, V = 1,100 × 25 = 27,500.
- **A — 25,000:** Result of assuming rival got 52% - 8% or taking 4.4% as margin.
- **B — 22,000:** Result of taking 5% instead of 4% as the winning margin.
- **C — 30,000:** Result of miscalculating 1,100 × 25.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Rival's % = (100 - 8) - 48 = 44%. Margin = 48% - 44% = 4% = 1,100 => 100% = 1,100 × 25 = 27,500.

### Q152 · Election Votes

In an election between two candidates, 10% of the voters did not cast their votes and 11.11% (1/9) of the votes polled were declared invalid. The winner got 75% of the valid votes and won by 2,000 votes. Find the total number of voters on the list.

A. 5,000
B. 4,500
C. 6,000
D. 4,000

**Answer:** A. 5,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 5,000 (Correct):** Let total voters = V. Polled = (9/10)V. Since 1/9 of polled votes were invalid, valid votes = (8/9) × (9/10)V = (4/5)V. Winner got 75% and loser got 25% of valid votes, so margin = 50% of valid votes = (1/2) × (4/5)V = (2/5)V. Given (2/5)V = 2,000, V = 5,000.
- **B — 4,500:** Result of subtracting 1/9 from 1 and forgetting the 1/2 margin factor.
- **C — 6,000:** Result of taking 1/3 instead of 2/5 of V.
- **D — 4,000:** Result of taking 50% of V = 2,000.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Multiply fractions: V × (9/10) × (8/9) × (1/2) = 2,000 => (2/5)V = 2,000 => V = 5,000.

### Q153 · Examination Pass-Fail

A student needs to score 35% of the total marks to pass an examination. He scored 125 marks and failed by 50 marks. What are the maximum marks for the examination?

A. 450
B. 500
C. 550
D. 400

**Answer:** B. 500
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 500 (Correct):** Passing marks = 125 + 50 = 175. Since 35% of maximum marks = 175, maximum marks = (175 × 100) / 35 = 500.
- **A — 450:** Result of subtracting 50 from 125 or miscalculating 175/35.
- **C — 550:** Result of adding 50 extra to the final answer.
- **D — 400:** Result of using 40% instead of 35%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Passing marks = 125 + 50 = 175. Since 35% = 175, 1% = 5, so 100% = 500.

### Q154 · Examination Pass-Fail

In an examination, a candidate who scores 25% marks fails by 30 marks, while another candidate who scores 50% marks gets 20 marks more than the minimum passing marks. Find the maximum marks.

A. 250
B. 150
C. 200
D. 300

**Answer:** C. 200
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 200 (Correct):** Difference in percentage = 50% - 25% = 25%. Difference in marks = 30 + 20 = 50. Thus 25% of maximum marks = 50, so maximum marks = 50 × 4 = 200.
- **A — 250:** Result of subtracting 20 from 30 (getting 10) instead of adding them.
- **B — 150:** Result of miscalculating 50 / 0.25.
- **D — 300:** Result of dividing 75 by 0.25.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Percentage difference = Sum of deviations: (50% - 25%) = 25% = 30 + 20 = 50 => 100% = 200.

### Q155 · Examination Pass-Fail

A student scores 30% marks and fails by 15 marks, while another student scores 45% marks and gets 30 marks more than the minimum passing marks. What is the minimum passing percentage?

A. 32%
B. 38%
C. 40%
D. 35%

**Answer:** D. 35%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 35% (Correct):** Difference in percentage = 45% - 30% = 15%. Difference in marks = 15 + 30 = 45. Hence 15% = 45 marks, so 1% = 3 marks (maximum marks = 300). The first student failed by 15 marks = 15 / 3 = 5%. Thus passing percentage = 30% + 5% = 35%.
- **A — 32%:** Result of taking 15% = 30 - 15 = 15 instead of adding the deviations.
- **B — 38%:** Result of subtracting 5% from 43% wrongly.
- **C — 40%:** Result of adding 10% instead of 5% to 30%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 15% = 45 marks => 1% = 3 marks. Failing by 15 marks means failing by 15/3 = 5%, so Pass% = 30% + 5% = 35%.

### Q156 · Examination Pass-Fail

In an examination, the maximum marks for Mathematics, Physics, and Chemistry are 100, 150, and 250 respectively. A student scores 70% in Mathematics, 60% in Physics, and 50% in Chemistry. What is his overall percentage?

A. 57%
B. 60%
C. 58%
D. 55%

**Answer:** A. 57%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 57% (Correct):** Marks scored: Maths = 70% of 100 = 70; Physics = 60% of 150 = 90; Chemistry = 50% of 250 = 125. Total marks scored = 70 + 90 + 125 = 285 out of 100 + 150 + 250 = 500. Overall percentage = (285 / 500) × 100 = 57%.
- **B — 60%:** Result of taking the unweighted average (70 + 60 + 50)/3 = 60%.
- **C — 58%:** Result of miscalculating 60% of 150 or 50% of 250.
- **D — 55%:** Result of dividing 275 by 500.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Take ratio of max marks 2 : 3 : 5 (sum = 10). Weighted % = (2×70 + 3×60 + 5×50) / 10 = (140 + 180 + 250)/10 = 57%.

### Q157 · Examination Pass-Fail

A test has 150 questions of 1 mark each. Meera answered 40% of the first 90 questions correctly. What percentage of the remaining 60 questions must she answer correctly to score 60% in the entire test?

A. 80%
B. 90%
C. 85%
D. 75%

**Answer:** B. 90%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 90% (Correct):** Target correct answers = 60% of 150 = 90. Correct answers in first 90 questions = 40% of 90 = 36. Remaining correct answers needed = 90 - 36 = 54 out of 60. Required percentage = (54 / 60) × 100 = 90%.
- **A — 80%:** Result of taking 60% + (60% - 40%) = 80% without weighting by 90 and 60.
- **C — 85%:** Result of miscalculating 54 / 60 as 85%.
- **D — 75%:** Result of taking the average of 60% and 90%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use alligation on 90 : 60 = 3 : 2. Overall 60% is 20% above 40%, so remaining must be 20% × (3/2) = 30% above 60% => 90%.

### Q158 · Examination Pass-Fail

In an examination, 60% of the students who appeared are boys and the rest are girls. If 75% of the boys and 80% of the girls passed, and 138 students failed, find the total number of students who appeared.

A. 500
B. 550
C. 600
D. 650

**Answer:** C. 600
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 600 (Correct):** Let total students = 100. Boys = 60, Girls = 40. Failed boys = 25% of 60 = 15; failed girls = 20% of 40 = 8. Total failed = 15 + 8 = 23%. Given 23% = 138, 1% = 6, so total students = 600.
- **A — 500:** Result of taking average fail rate (25% + 20%)/2 = 22.5%.
- **B — 550:** Result of miscalculating 138 / 23 as 5.5.
- **D — 650:** Result of using 21% instead of 23%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Failed% = (60% × 25%) + (40% × 20%) = 15% + 8% = 23%. Since 23 × 6 = 138, total = 600.

### Q159 · Examination Pass-Fail

A candidate scores 20% marks and fails by 35 marks, while another candidate scores 42% marks and gets 12% of the maximum marks more than the minimum passing marks. Find the maximum marks.

A. 300
B. 400
C. 250
D. 350

**Answer:** D. 350
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 350 (Correct):** The second candidate scored 42%, which is 12% above the passing percentage. So passing percentage = 42% - 12% = 30%. The first candidate scored 20% and failed by 30% - 20% = 10% of maximum marks. Given 10% = 35 marks, maximum marks = 350.
- **A — 300:** Result of taking 42% - 20% = 22% as 35 marks.
- **B — 400:** Result of adding 12% to 42% instead of subtracting.
- **C — 250:** Result of miscalculating 35 / 0.14.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Pass% = 42% - 12% = 30%. Gap for first student = 30% - 20% = 10% = 35 marks => Max = 350.

### Q160 · Examination Pass-Fail

In an examination, A scored 25% more marks than B, B scored 10% less than C, and C scored 25% more than D. If D scored 320 marks out of 500, how many marks did A score?

A. 450
B. 420
C. 480
D. 405

**Answer:** A. 450
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 450 (Correct):** D's marks = 320. C's marks = 320 × 1.25 = 400. B's marks = 400 × 0.90 = 360. A's marks = 360 × 1.25 = 450.
- **B — 420:** Result of forgetting one of the 25% steps or miscalculating 360 × 1.25.
- **C — 480:** Result of adding 40% net directly to 320 (320 × 1.5 = 480).
- **D — 405:** Result of computing 450 × 0.9 = 405 by applying -10% at the end.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Chain multiply: 320 × (5/4) × (9/10) × (5/4) = 20 × 9 × 5 / 2 = 450.

### Q161 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

A man spends 75% of his income. If his income increases by 20% and his expenditure increases by 10%, what is the percentage increase in his savings?

A. 30%
B. 50%
C. 40%
D. 25%

**Answer:** B. 50%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 50% (Correct):** Let initial income = 100. Expenditure = 75, Savings = 25. New income = 120; New expenditure = 75 × 1.10 = 82.5. New savings = 120 - 82.5 = 37.5. Increase in savings = 37.5 - 25 = 12.5. Percentage increase = (12.5 / 25) × 100 = 50%.
- **A — 30%:** Result of subtracting 10% from 20% and adding 20% arbitrarily.
- **C — 40%:** Result of dividing 12.5 by 37.5 instead of 25.
- **D — 25%:** Result of confusing savings increase with initial savings (25%).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use weighted average: E:S = 3:1. 3(10%) + 1(x%) = 4(20%) => 30 + x = 80 => x = 50%.

### Q162 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

Radha saves 25% of her income. If her expenditure increases by 20% and her income increases by 29%, by what percentage do her savings increase?

A. 52%
B. 48%
C. 56%
D. 64%

**Answer:** C. 56%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 56% (Correct):** Let income = 100, so savings = 25 and expenditure = 75. New income = 129; New expenditure = 75 × 1.20 = 90. New savings = 129 - 90 = 39. Increase = 39 - 25 = 14. Percentage increase = (14 / 25) × 100 = 56%.
- **A — 52%:** Result of miscalculating 14/25 × 100 as 52%.
- **B — 48%:** Result of taking 12/25 × 100 = 48%.
- **D — 64%:** Result of using wrong expenditure growth.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Ratio E:S = 3:1 (sum 4). 3×20% + 1×x% = 4×29% => x = 116 - 60 = 56%.

### Q163 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

A person spends 60% of his income. If his income increases by 15% and his expenditure increases by 5%, what is the percentage increase in his savings?

A. 20%
B. 25%
C. 35%
D. 30%

**Answer:** D. 30%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 30% (Correct):** Let initial income = 100. Expenditure = 60, Savings = 40. New income = 115; New expenditure = 60 × 1.05 = 63. New savings = 115 - 63 = 52. Increase = 52 - 40 = 12. Percentage increase = (12 / 40) × 100 = 30%.
- **A — 20%:** Result of taking 15% + 5% = 20%.
- **B — 25%:** Result of dividing 12 by 52 or taking 10/40 = 25%.
- **C — 35%:** Result of miscalculating 12 / 40 × 100.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** E:S = 3:2. 3(5%) + 2(x%) = 5(15%) => 2x = 75 - 15 = 60 => x = 30%.

### Q164 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

The ratio of a person's income to expenditure is 7 : 5. If his income increases by 20% and his expenditure decreases by 10%, what is the percentage increase in his savings?

A. 95%
B. 85%
C. 90%
D. 75%

**Answer:** A. 95%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 95% (Correct):** Let income = 700 and expenditure = 500, so savings = 200. New income = 700 × 1.20 = 840. New expenditure = 500 × 0.90 = 450. New savings = 840 - 450 = 390. Percentage increase = [(390 - 200) / 200] × 100 = (190 / 200) × 100 = 95%.
- **B — 85%:** Result of an arithmetic slip in 840 - 450.
- **C — 90%:** Result of dividing 190 by 210 or approximating.
- **D — 75%:** Result of adding 10% instead of subtracting 10% from expenditure (which gives 840 - 550 = 290, +45%).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** E:S = 5:2 (sum 7). 5(-10%) + 2(x%) = 7(20%) => 2x = 140 + 50 = 190 => x = 95%.

### Q165 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

A man spends 20% of his monthly income on food, 25% of the remaining on rent, and 10% of the remaining on transport. If he is left with ₹5,400, what is his monthly income?

A. ₹12,000
B. ₹10,000
C. ₹9,000
D. ₹10,800

**Answer:** B. ₹10,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹10,000 (Correct):** Let monthly income = I. Remaining after food = 0.80I; after rent = 0.80I × 0.75 = 0.60I; after transport = 0.60I × 0.90 = 0.54I. Given 0.54I = ₹5,400, I = 5,400 / 0.54 = ₹10,000.
- **A — ₹12,000:** Result of adding 20% + 25% + 10% = 55% directly (0.45I = 5,400 -> ₹12,000).
- **C — ₹9,000:** Result of forgetting the 10% transport deduction (0.60I = 5,400 -> ₹9,000).
- **D — ₹10,800:** Result of multiplying ₹5,400 by 2 directly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Chain remaining fractions: I × (4/5) × (3/4) × (9/10) = 5,400 => (27/50)I = 5,400 => I = 200 × 50 = ₹10,000.

### Q166 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

Anu spends 68% of her monthly income. If her monthly income increases by 20% and her monthly savings increase by 9.375% (9 3/8%), then by what percentage does her monthly expenditure increase?

A. 20%
B. 22%
C. 25%
D. 28%

**Answer:** C. 25%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 25% (Correct):** Let income = 100, expenditure = 68, savings = 32. New income = 120. 9.375% = 3/32, so increase in savings = 32 × (3/32) = 3, making new savings = 35. New expenditure = 120 - 35 = 85. Increase in expenditure = 85 - 68 = 17. Percentage increase = (17 / 68) × 100 = 25%.
- **A — 20%:** Result of assuming expenditure grows at the same rate as income (20%).
- **B — 22%:** Result of miscalculating 17/68 as 22%.
- **D — 28%:** Result of adding 3 to 25.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** E:S = 68:32 = 17:8 (sum 25). 17x + 8(75/8) = 25(20) => 17x = 500 - 75 = 425 => x = 25%.

### Q167 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

A man spends 35% of his income on household expenses and 20% of his total income on education. He then spends 25% of the remaining amount on travel. If he saves ₹5,400, what is his total income?

A. ₹15,000
B. ₹18,000
C. ₹14,400
D. ₹16,000

**Answer:** D. ₹16,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹16,000 (Correct):** Expenses on household + education = 35% + 20% = 55% of total income, leaving 45%. After spending 25% of this remaining amount on travel, he saves 75% of 45% = (3/4) × (9/20) = 27/80 of his income. Given (27/80)I = ₹5,400, I = 5,400 × (80 / 27) = ₹16,000.
- **A — ₹15,000:** Result of treating 20% as '20% of remaining' instead of '20% of total'.
- **B — ₹18,000:** Result of adding 35% + 20% + 25% = 80% directly (0.20I = 5,400 -> ₹27,000 or ₹18,000).
- **C — ₹14,400:** Result of miscalculating 5,400 × 80 / 27.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Read carefully: 35% + 20% = 55% of total -> 45% left. Savings = 75% of 45% = 27/80. 27 units = 5,400 => 80 units = ₹16,000.

### Q168 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

The monthly income of A is 20% more than that of B, and B's monthly income is 25% less than that of C. If their combined monthly income is ₹53,000, what is A's monthly income?

A. ₹18,000
B. ₹15,000
C. ₹20,000
D. ₹16,000

**Answer:** A. ₹18,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹18,000 (Correct):** Let C's income = 100. Then B's income = 75, and A's income = 75 × 1.20 = 90. Total = 90 + 75 + 100 = 265 units. Given 265 units = ₹53,000, 1 unit = ₹200. Thus A's income = 90 × 200 = ₹18,000.
- **B — ₹15,000:** Result of finding B's income (75 × 200 = ₹15,000) instead of A's.
- **C — ₹20,000:** Result of finding C's income (100 × 200 = ₹20,000) instead of A's.
- **D — ₹16,000:** Result of taking A = 95 instead of 90.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Set C = 100 -> B = 75 -> A = 90. Sum = 265. Since 53,000 / 265 = 200, A = 90 × 200 = ₹18,000.

### Q169 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

A man saves 20% of his income. If his income increases by 28% and he still saves the exact same amount as before, what is the percentage increase in his expenditure?

A. 28%
B. 35%
C. 32%
D. 40%

**Answer:** B. 35%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 35% (Correct):** Let initial income = 100. Savings = 20, Expenditure = 80. New income = 128. Since savings remain 20, new expenditure = 128 - 20 = 108. Increase in expenditure = 108 - 80 = 28. Percentage increase = (28 / 80) × 100 = 35%.
- **A — 28%:** Result of reporting the income increase (28%) as the expenditure increase.
- **C — 32%:** Result of dividing 28 by 87.5 instead of 80.
- **D — 40%:** Result of dividing 28 by 70 instead of 80.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** All extra income (28) goes into expenditure (80). So Exp % increase = (28 / 80) × 100 = (7 / 20) × 100 = 35%.

### Q170 · Venn Diagram & Sets

In an examination, 65% of students passed in Mathematics, 55% passed in English, and 15% failed in both subjects. If 420 students passed in both subjects, find the total number of students who appeared.

A. 1,000
B. 1,400
C. 1,200
D. 1,500

**Answer:** C. 1,200
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 1,200 (Correct):** Students who passed in at least one subject = 100% - 15% = 85%. Using n(M ∪ E) = n(M) + n(E) - n(M ∩ E), we get 85% = 65% + 55% - Both%, so Both% = 120% - 85% = 35%. Given 35% = 420, total students = (420 × 100) / 35 = 1,200.
- **A — 1,000:** Result of taking Both% = 65 + 55 - 100 = 20% and ignoring the 15% who failed both.
- **B — 1,400:** Result of taking 30% instead of 35% for Both%.
- **D — 1,500:** Result of miscalculating 420 / 0.28.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Both Pass% = (Pass1% + Pass2% + Both Fail%) - 100% = (65 + 55 + 15) - 100 = 35%. 35% = 420 => 100% = 1,200.

### Q171 · Venn Diagram & Sets

In an office survey, 70% of employees drink tea, 55% drink coffee, and every employee drinks at least one of the two beverages. If 250 employees drink both tea and coffee, how many employees were surveyed?

A. 1,250
B. 800
C. 900
D. 1,000

**Answer:** D. 1,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 1,000 (Correct):** Since everyone drinks at least one beverage, n(T ∪ C) = 100%. Thus Both% = 70% + 55% - 100% = 25%. Given 25% = 250 employees, total employees = 250 × 4 = 1,000.
- **A — 1,250:** Result of using 20% instead of 25% for the overlap.
- **B — 800:** Result of dividing 250 by 0.3125.
- **C — 900:** Result of miscalculating 250 × 4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Overlap% = 70% + 55% - 100% = 25%. Since 25% = 250, 100% = 1,000.

### Q172 · Venn Diagram & Sets

In a school, 60% of the students play cricket, 50% play football, and 20% play neither sport. What percentage of the students play ONLY cricket?

A. 30%
B. 40%
C. 10%
D. 20%

**Answer:** A. 30%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 30% (Correct):** Students playing at least one sport = 100% - 20% = 80%. Students playing both = 60% + 50% - 80% = 30%. Therefore, students playing only cricket = 60% - 30% = 30%.
- **B — 40%:** Result of subtracting 20% directly from 60%.
- **C — 10%:** Result of taking Only Cricket = 60% - 50% = 10%.
- **D — 20%:** Result of reporting the 'neither' percentage (20%).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Only Cricket = Total (at least one) - Football = (100% - 20%) - 50% = 80% - 50% = 30%!

### Q173 · Venn Diagram & Sets

In an examination, 42% of candidates failed in Hindi and 52% failed in English. If 17% failed in both subjects, what percentage of candidates passed in both subjects?

A. 17%
B. 23%
C. 27%
D. 33%

**Answer:** B. 23%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 23% (Correct):** Percentage of candidates who failed in at least one subject = 42% + 52% - 17% = 77%. Therefore, percentage who passed in both subjects = 100% - 77% = 23%.
- **A — 17%:** Result of reporting the 'failed in both' percentage (17%).
- **C — 27%:** Result of adding 17% to 10% instead of subtracting correctly.
- **D — 33%:** Result of forgetting to subtract 17% (42 + 52 = 94 -> 6%, or similar arithmetic slip).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Both Pass% = 100% - (Fail1% + Fail2% - Both Fail%) = 100 - (42 + 52 - 17) = 23%.

### Q174 · Venn Diagram & Sets

In a group of 800 people, 550 can speak Hindi and 450 can speak English, and every person speaks at least one of the two languages. What percentage of the group can speak both Hindi and English?

A. 20%
B. 30%
C. 25%
D. 15%

**Answer:** C. 25%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 25% (Correct):** Number of people speaking both = 550 + 450 - 800 = 200. Percentage of people speaking both = (200 / 800) × 100 = 25%.
- **A — 20%:** Result of dividing 200 by 1,000 instead of 800.
- **B — 30%:** Result of miscalculating 200 / 800.
- **D — 15%:** Result of dividing 120 by 800.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Both = (550 + 450) - 800 = 200. As a % of 800: 200/800 = 1/4 = 25%.

### Q175 · Venn Diagram & Sets

In a class, 72% of the students opted for Biology and 44% opted for Mathematics. If each student opted for at least one of these two subjects and 40 students opted for both, what is the total number of students in the class?

A. 200
B. 300
C. 320
D. 250

**Answer:** D. 250
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 250 (Correct):** Students opting for both = 72% + 44% - 100% = 16%. Given 16% = 40, total students = (40 × 100) / 16 = 250.
- **A — 200:** Result of taking 20% instead of 16% for the overlap.
- **B — 300:** Result of miscalculating 40 / 0.16.
- **C — 320:** Result of dividing 40 by 0.125.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Overlap = 72% + 44% - 100% = 16%. Since 16% = 40, divide by 4: 4% = 10 => 100% = 250.

### Q176 · Venn Diagram & Sets

In an examination, 52% of students failed in English, 42% failed in Mathematics, and 17% failed in both. If 115 students passed in both subjects, find the total number of students.

A. 500
B. 450
C. 600
D. 550

**Answer:** A. 500
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 500 (Correct):** Failed in at least one subject = 52% + 42% - 17% = 77%. Passed in both = 100% - 77% = 23%. Given 23% = 115, 1% = 5, so total students = 500.
- **B — 450:** Result of taking 25% instead of 23%.
- **C — 600:** Result of miscalculating 115 / 0.23.
- **D — 550:** Result of adding 50 to 500.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Both Pass% = 100 - (52 + 42 - 17) = 23%. Since 23 × 5 = 115, total = 500.

### Q177 · Population Growth & Depreciation

The population of a town increases by 5% annually. If its present population is 17,640, what was its population 2 years ago?

A. 15,876
B. 16,000
C. 16,800
D. 15,000

**Answer:** B. 16,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 16,000 (Correct):** 5% = 1/20, so each year the population multiplies by 21/20. Let population 2 years ago be P. Then P × (21/20)² = 17,640, so P × (441/400) = 17,640. Since 17,640 / 441 = 40, P = 40 × 400 = 16,000.
- **A — 15,876:** Result of multiplying 17,640 by 0.90 instead of dividing by 1.1025.
- **C — 16,800:** Result of dividing 17,640 by 1.05 once (only 1 year ago).
- **D — 15,000:** Result of subtracting 10% directly from 17,640 and rounding.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 5% = 1/20 => 2-year ratio is 20² : 21² = 400 : 441. 441 units = 17,640 => 1 unit = 40 => 400 units = 16,000.

### Q178 · Population Growth & Depreciation

The value of a machine depreciates at the rate of 10% per annum. If its present value is ₹81,000, what will be its value after 2 years?

A. ₹64,800
B. ₹65,000
C. ₹65,610
D. ₹72,900

**Answer:** C. ₹65,610
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹65,610 (Correct):** Value after 2 years = 81,000 × (1 - 0.10)² = 81,000 × 0.81 = ₹65,610.
- **A — ₹64,800:** Result of subtracting 20% directly (81,000 × 0.80 = ₹64,800) instead of compounding.
- **B — ₹65,000:** Result of rounding 81 × 810.
- **D — ₹72,900:** Result of computing value after 1 year (81,000 × 0.9 = ₹72,900) instead of 2 years.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 10% depreciation twice = × 0.81. 81,000 × 0.81 = 81² × 10 = 6,561 × 10 = ₹65,610.

### Q179 · Population Growth & Depreciation

The present population of a village is 8,000. If the male population increases by 10% and the female population increases by 8% in a year, the total population becomes 8,720. Find the initial male population.

A. 4,500
B. 3,500
C. 4,200
D. 4,000

**Answer:** D. 4,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 4,000 (Correct):** Total population increase = 8,720 - 8,000 = 720. Overall percentage increase = (720 / 8,000) × 100 = 9%. By alligation between 10% (males) and 8% (females) around mean 9%, the ratio M : F = (9 - 8) : (10 - 9) = 1 : 1. Thus initial males = 8,000 / 2 = 4,000.
- **A — 4,500:** Result of assuming a 9:7 ratio instead of 1:1.
- **B — 3,500:** Result of making an arithmetic slip in 720 / 8000.
- **C — 4,200:** Result of miscalculating the alligation difference.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Overall % increase = 720/80 = 9%. Since 9% is the exact midpoint of 8% and 10%, Males = Females = 8,000/2 = 4,000.

### Q180 · Tax & Commission

A salesman is given a commission of 12% on total sales and an additional bonus of 1% on sales exceeding ₹15,000. If his total earnings are ₹7,650, find his total sales.

A. ₹60,000
B. ₹55,000
C. ₹65,000
D. ₹50,000

**Answer:** A. ₹60,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹60,000 (Correct):** Let total sales be S. Commission = 12% of S + 1% of (S - 15,000) = 7,650. Thus 0.13S - 150 = 7,650, so 0.13S = 7,800. Hence S = 7,800 / 0.13 = ₹60,000.
- **B — ₹55,000:** Result of forgetting to add ₹150 to ₹7,650 before dividing by 0.13.
- **C — ₹65,000:** Result of subtracting ₹150 instead of adding ₹150.
- **D — ₹50,000:** Result of dividing ₹7,650 by 0.15.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Assume he gets 1% bonus on the first ₹15,000 too (+₹150). Now flat 13% = 7,650 + 150 = ₹7,800 => 100% = 600 × 100 = ₹60,000.
