# Section II: Commercial Mathematics

## Chapter 4: Profit, Loss & Discount

### Q181 · Profit & Loss Basics

By selling an article for ₹1,536, a trader incurs a loss of 20%. At what price should he sell it to earn a profit of 25%?

A. ₹2,304
B. ₹2,400
C. ₹2,250
D. ₹2,460

**Answer:** B. ₹2,400
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹2,400 (Correct):** 80% of CP = ₹1,536 ⇒ CP = 1,536 × (100/80) = ₹1,920. To earn a 25% profit, SP = 1,920 × (125/100) = ₹2,400.
- **A — ₹2,304:** Result of increasing ₹1,536 directly by 50% instead of finding the true cost price first.
- **C — ₹2,250:** Value obtained by adding 20% and 25% as a 45% markup on ₹1,536 and rounding.
- **D — ₹2,460:** Result of computing 20% of ₹1,536 as the loss and adding 25% profit on that wrong CP.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use direct scaling: New SP = Old SP × (100 + P₂)/(100 - L₁) = 1536 × 125/80 = 1536 × 25/16 = ₹2,400.

### Q182 · Profit & Loss Basics

A shopkeeper calculates his profit percentage on the selling price and finds it to be 20%. What is his actual profit percentage on the cost price?

A. 25%
B. 16.67%
C. 22.5%
D. 30%

**Answer:** A. 25%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 25% (Correct):** Let SP = ₹100. Profit = 20% of ₹100 = ₹20. Therefore, CP = SP − Profit = 100 − 20 = ₹80. Actual profit % = (20 / 80) × 100 = 25%.
- **B — 16.67%:** Result of taking CP = ₹120 instead of ₹80, which gives 20/120 = 16.67%.
- **C — 22.5%:** Value obtained by averaging 20% and 25% instead of using the exact CP base.
- **D — 30%:** Result of adding 10% arbitrarily to the selling-price profit percentage.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If profit on SP is 1/n, then profit on CP is 1/(n − 1). Here 20% = 1/5, so actual profit = 1/(5 − 1) = 1/4 = 25%.

### Q183 · Profit & Loss Basics

If a merchant's loss is 25% of the selling price, what is his actual loss percentage on the cost price?

A. 33.33%
B. 25%
C. 20%
D. 18.75%

**Answer:** C. 20%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 20% (Correct):** Let SP = ₹100. Loss = 25% of ₹100 = ₹25. Thus CP = SP + Loss = 100 + 25 = ₹125. Actual loss % = (25 / 125) × 100 = 20%.
- **A — 33.33%:** Result of subtracting 25 from 100 to get CP = 75, giving 25/75 = 33.33%.
- **B — 25%:** Value obtained by assuming loss % on SP equals loss % on CP.
- **D — 18.75%:** Result of computing 25% of 75% instead of dividing loss by cost price.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If loss on SP is 1/n, then loss on CP is 1/(n + 1). Here 25% = 1/4, so actual loss = 1/(4 + 1) = 1/5 = 20%.

### Q184 · Profit & Loss Basics

An article is sold for ₹1,470 at a profit of 16⅔%. What is the cost price of the article?

A. ₹1,225
B. ₹1,240
C. ₹1,280
D. ₹1,260

**Answer:** D. ₹1,260
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹1,260 (Correct):** 16⅔% = 1/6. If CP = 6 units, Profit = 1 unit, so SP = 7 units. Given 7 units = ₹1,470 ⇒ 1 unit = ₹210. Thus, CP = 6 × 210 = ₹1,260.
- **A — ₹1,225:** Result of subtracting 16.67% of ₹1,470 directly from ₹1,470 (1470 × 5/6 = ₹1,225).
- **B — ₹1,240:** Value obtained by using 16% instead of the exact fraction 1/6.
- **C — ₹1,280:** Result of miscalculating 1,470 / 7 as 213.33 and rounding.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Convert 16⅔% to 1/6. Since SP = (7/6)CP, CP = SP × 6/7 = 1,470 × 6/7 = ₹1,260.

### Q185 · Profit & Loss Basics

A sold a watch to B at a profit of 20%, and B sold it to C at a loss of 10%. If C paid ₹2,160 for the watch, what did A pay for it?

A. ₹2,000
B. ₹1,944
C. ₹2,050
D. ₹1,800

**Answer:** A. ₹2,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹2,000 (Correct):** Let A's CP = x. B's CP = 1.20x. C's CP = 0.90 × (1.20x) = 1.08x. Given 1.08x = 2,160 ⇒ x = 2,160 / 1.08 = ₹2,000.
- **B — ₹1,944:** Result of taking net change as +10% (20 − 10) and computing 2,160 × 0.9.
- **C — ₹2,050:** Value obtained by dividing 2,160 by 1.05 instead of 1.08.
- **D — ₹1,800:** Result of dividing 2,160 by 1.20 and ignoring B's 10% loss.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Net multiplier = (6/5) × (9/10) = 27/25. So A's CP = 2,160 × 25/27 = 80 × 25 = ₹2,000.

### Q186 · Profit & Loss Basics

The ratio of the cost price to the selling price of an article is 16 : 19. What is the profit percentage?

A. 15.79%
B. 18.75%
C. 19.25%
D. 16.67%

**Answer:** B. 18.75%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 18.75% (Correct):** Let CP = 16 units and SP = 19 units. Profit = 19 − 16 = 3 units. Profit % = (3 / 16) × 100 = 18.75%.
- **A — 15.79%:** Result of calculating profit on SP instead of CP (3/19 × 100 = 15.79%).
- **C — 19.25%:** Value obtained by miscomputing 300/16 as 19.25%.
- **D — 16.67%:** Result of using 3/18 instead of 3/16.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Memorize 1/16 = 6.25%. Since profit is 3/16, multiply 3 × 6.25% = 18.75%.

### Q187 · Profit & Loss Basics

Rohan bought an old scooter for ₹18,400 and spent ₹1,600 on its repairs. He then sold it for ₹22,400. What is his profit percentage?

A. 14.5%
B. 10.7%
C. 12%
D. 21.7%

**Answer:** C. 12%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 12% (Correct):** Effective CP = ₹18,400 + ₹1,600 = ₹20,000. SP = ₹22,400. Profit = 22,400 − 20,000 = ₹2,400. Profit % = (2,400 / 20,000) × 100 = 12%.
- **A — 14.5%:** Result of subtracting ₹1,600 from ₹22,400 and dividing by ₹18,400 with a decimal slip.
- **B — 10.7%:** Value obtained by computing profit % on the selling price ₹22,400 (2,400 / 22,400 ≈ 10.7%).
- **D — 21.7%:** Result of ignoring the ₹1,600 repair cost and computing (4,000 / 18,400) × 100.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Always add overhead/repair expenses to CP before computing profit %: 2,400 / 20,000 = 12 / 100 = 12%.

### Q188 · Profit & Loss Basics

The profit earned by selling an article for ₹1,680 is equal to the loss incurred when the same article is sold for ₹1,320. At what price should it be sold to make a 20% profit?

A. ₹1,750
B. ₹1,860
C. ₹1,720
D. ₹1,800

**Answer:** D. ₹1,800
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹1,800 (Correct):** Since Profit = Loss, CP = (1,680 + 1,320) / 2 = 3,000 / 2 = ₹1,500. For 20% profit, SP = 1,500 × 1.20 = ₹1,800.
- **A — ₹1,750:** Result of taking CP as ₹1,458 or adding 20% to ₹1,460.
- **B — ₹1,860:** Value obtained by adding 20% of ₹1,800 instead of ₹1,500.
- **C — ₹1,720:** Result of computing 15% profit on ₹1,500 instead of 20%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When Profit at SP₁ = Loss at SP₂, CP = (SP₁ + SP₂)/2 = 1,500. Then SP = 1,500 × 1.2 = ₹1,800.

### Q189 · CP of x = SP of y

If the cost price of 24 articles is equal to the selling price of 18 articles, what is the profit percentage?

A. 33.33%
B. 25%
C. 30%
D. 20%

**Answer:** A. 33.33%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 33.33% (Correct):** 24 × CP = 18 × SP ⇒ CP / SP = 18 / 24 = 3 / 4. Profit = 4 − 3 = 1 unit on CP of 3 units. Profit % = (1 / 3) × 100 = 33.33%.
- **B — 25%:** Result of dividing the difference (6) by 24 instead of 18 (6/24 = 25%).
- **C — 30%:** Value obtained by taking 6/20 = 30%.
- **D — 20%:** Result of dividing 6 by 30 or misreading the ratio.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When CP of x = SP of y, Profit/Loss % = ((x − y) / y) × 100 = (6 / 18) × 100 = 33.33%.

### Q190 · CP of x = SP of y

The cost price of 15 pens is equal to the selling price of 20 pens. Find the loss percentage.

A. 33.33%
B. 25%
C. 20%
D. 15%

**Answer:** B. 25%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 25% (Correct):** 15 × CP = 20 × SP ⇒ CP / SP = 20 / 15 = 4 / 3. Loss = 4 − 3 = 1 unit on CP of 4 units. Loss % = (1 / 4) × 100 = 25%.
- **A — 33.33%:** Result of dividing the difference (5) by 15 instead of 20 (5/15 = 33.33%).
- **C — 20%:** Value obtained by dividing 5 by 25 instead of 20.
- **D — 15%:** Result of taking the number of pens (15) directly as the loss percentage.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use ((x − y) / y) × 100 = ((15 − 20) / 20) × 100 = −25%, meaning 25% loss.

### Q191 · CP of x = SP of y

By selling 36 oranges, a fruit vendor suffers a loss equal to the selling price of 4 oranges. What is his loss percentage?

A. 11.11%
B. 12.5%
C. 10%
D. 9.09%

**Answer:** C. 10%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 10% (Correct):** Let SP of 1 orange = ₹1. Total SP = ₹36, Loss = ₹4. Therefore, CP of 36 oranges = SP + Loss = 36 + 4 = ₹40. Loss % = (4 / 40) × 100 = 10%.
- **A — 11.11%:** Result of dividing loss (4) by 36 directly (4/36 = 11.11%).
- **B — 12.5%:** Value obtained by subtracting 4 from 36 to get 32 in the denominator (4/32 = 12.5%).
- **D — 9.09%:** Result of using 4/44 = 9.09% instead of 4/40.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When selling N items gives loss = SP of L items, Loss % = L / (N + L) × 100 = 4 / (36 + 4) × 100 = 10%.

### Q192 · CP of x = SP of y

By selling 45 metres of cloth, a merchant gains an amount equal to the selling price of 9 metres of cloth. Find his gain percentage.

A. 20%
B. 16.67%
C. 22.5%
D. 25%

**Answer:** D. 25%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 25% (Correct):** Let SP of 1 m = ₹1. Total SP = ₹45, Gain = ₹9. Therefore, CP of 45 m = SP − Gain = 45 − 9 = ₹36. Gain % = (9 / 36) × 100 = 25%.
- **A — 20%:** Result of dividing gain (9) directly by 45 (9/45 = 20%).
- **B — 16.67%:** Value obtained by adding 9 to 45 to get 54 in the denominator (9/54 = 16.67%).
- **C — 22.5%:** Result of averaging 20% and 25%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When selling N items gives profit = SP of P items, Gain % = P / (N − P) × 100 = 9 / (45 − 9) × 100 = 25%.

### Q193 · CP of x = SP of y

By selling 32 pens, a shopkeeper earns a profit equal to the cost price of 8 pens. What is his profit percentage?

A. 25%
B. 33.33%
C. 20%
D. 30%

**Answer:** A. 25%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 25% (Correct):** Let CP of 1 pen = ₹1. CP of 32 pens = ₹32, Profit = ₹8. Profit % = (8 / 32) × 100 = 25%.
- **B — 33.33%:** Result of subtracting 8 from 32 and computing 8/24 = 33.33% (mistaking CP-based gain for SP-based gain).
- **C — 20%:** Value obtained by adding 8 to 32 and computing 8/40 = 20%.
- **D — 30%:** Result of miscalculating 8/32 as 30%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When profit is given in terms of CP of P items on N items sold, Gain % = (P / N) × 100 = (8 / 32) × 100 = 25%.

### Q194 · CP of x = SP of y

On selling 18 balls for ₹900, there is a loss equal to the cost price of 6 balls. What is the cost price of one ball?

A. ₹50
B. ₹75
C. ₹60
D. ₹37.50

**Answer:** B. ₹75
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹75 (Correct):** 18 × CP − 18 × SP = 6 × CP ⇒ 12 × CP = 18 × SP = ₹900. Therefore, CP of 1 ball = 900 / 12 = ₹75.
- **A — ₹50:** Result of dividing ₹900 directly by 18 (the SP of 1 ball).
- **C — ₹60:** Value obtained by computing 900 / 15 instead of 900 / 12.
- **D — ₹37.50:** Result of adding 6 to 18 and dividing ₹900 by 24.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Loss of 6 CP on 18 balls means SP of 18 balls = CP of (18 − 6) = 12 balls. So CP of 1 ball = 900 / 12 = ₹75.

### Q195 · CP of x = SP of y

On selling 17 notebooks for ₹1,020, a stationer earns a profit equal to the cost price of 3 notebooks. Find the cost price of one notebook.

A. ₹60
B. ₹72.85
C. ₹51
D. ₹54

**Answer:** C. ₹51
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹51 (Correct):** 17 × SP − 17 × CP = 3 × CP ⇒ 20 × CP = 17 × SP = ₹1,020. Therefore, CP of 1 notebook = 1,020 / 20 = ₹51.
- **A — ₹60:** Result of dividing ₹1,020 by 17 (which gives the SP of 1 notebook, ₹60).
- **B — ₹72.85:** Value obtained by subtracting 3 from 17 and dividing 1,020 by 14.
- **D — ₹54:** Result of miscalculating 1,020 / 20.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Profit of 3 CP on 17 items means ₹1,020 = CP of (17 + 3) = 20 notebooks. Thus CP = 1,020 / 20 = ₹51.

### Q196 · CP of x = SP of y

The cost price of 40 articles is equal to the selling price of x articles. If the profit is 25%, find the value of x.

A. 30
B. 50
C. 35
D. 32

**Answer:** D. 32
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 32 (Correct):** 40 × CP = x × SP ⇒ SP / CP = 40 / x. For 25% profit, SP / CP = 125 / 100 = 5 / 4. Thus 40 / x = 5 / 4 ⇒ x = 32.
- **A — 30:** Result of computing 40 − 25% of 40 = 30 (taking 25% off 40 directly).
- **B — 50:** Value obtained by computing 40 × 1.25 = 50 (confusing profit with loss).
- **C — 35:** Result of subtracting 5 instead of dividing by 1.25.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** SP/CP = 5/4 = 40/x ⇒ x = 40 × 4/5 = 32.

### Q197 · CP of x = SP of y

A vendor buys 12 lemons for ₹10 and sells 10 lemons for ₹12. What is his overall profit percentage?

A. 44%
B. 20%
C. 40%
D. 36%

**Answer:** A. 44%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 44% (Correct):** CP of 1 lemon = 10/12 = ₹5/6. SP of 1 lemon = 12/10 = ₹6/5. Equalizing to 60 lemons: CP = 5 × 10 = ₹50, SP = 6 × 12 = ₹72. Profit % = (22 / 50) × 100 = 44%.
- **B — 20%:** Result of taking simple percentage difference (12 − 10)/10 = 20%.
- **C — 40%:** Value obtained by adding 20% + 20% instead of compounding.
- **D — 36%:** Result of dividing profit (22) by 60 instead of 50.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Cross-multiply (Items × Price): CP = 10 × 10 = 100, SP = 12 × 12 = 144. Profit % = 144 − 100 = 44%.

### Q198 · Two Items at Same SP

Two wristwatches are sold for ₹1,980 each. On one, the seller gains 10%, and on the other, he loses 10%. What is his overall profit or loss percentage?

A. No profit, no loss
B. 1% loss
C. 1% profit
D. 2% loss

**Answer:** B. 1% loss
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 1% loss (Correct):** When two articles are sold at the same SP, one at x% gain and the other at x% loss, there is always a net loss of (x² / 100)%. Here, loss % = 10² / 100 = 1% loss.
- **A — No profit, no loss:** Result of assuming +10% and −10% cancel out to zero.
- **C — 1% profit:** Value obtained by misidentifying the net result as a profit instead of a loss.
- **D — 2% loss:** Result of computing 2x / 10 instead of x² / 100.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Same SP with +x% and −x% ALWAYS yields a loss of (x/10)² % = (10/10)² = 1%.

### Q199 · Two Items at Same SP

A dealer sold two bikes for ₹9,600 each, gaining 20% on one and losing 20% on the other. Find his overall profit or loss in rupees.

A. ₹400 loss
B. ₹800 profit
C. ₹800 loss
D. No profit, no loss

**Answer:** C. ₹800 loss
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹800 loss (Correct):** Net loss % = 20² / 100 = 4%. Total SP = 9,600 × 2 = ₹19,200, which equals 96% of total CP. Thus Total CP = 19,200 / 0.96 = ₹20,000. Net loss = 20,000 − 19,200 = ₹800.
- **A — ₹400 loss:** Result of computing 4% of ₹9,600 (one bike) instead of total CP, or halving the loss.
- **B — ₹800 profit:** Value obtained by treating the 4% change as a net profit.
- **D — No profit, no loss:** Result of thinking +20% and −20% cancel each other out.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Net loss % = 4%. Total SP = 96% of CP ⇒ Loss = Total SP × 4/96 = 19,200 / 24 = ₹800.

### Q200 · Two Items at Same SP

A shopkeeper sells two articles at ₹1,500 each. On the first, he makes a profit of 50%, and on the second, he suffers a loss of 25%. What is his overall profit or loss percentage?

A. 12.5% profit
B. 6.25% loss
C. 12.5% loss
D. No profit, no loss

**Answer:** D. No profit, no loss
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — No profit, no loss (Correct):** For the first article, CP₁ = 1,500 / 1.50 = ₹1,000. For the second, CP₂ = 1,500 / 0.75 = ₹2,000. Total CP = 1,000 + 2,000 = ₹3,000, and Total SP = 1,500 + 1,500 = ₹3,000. Hence, no profit, no loss.
- **A — 12.5% profit:** Result of taking the simple average of +50% and −25% (+12.5%).
- **B — 6.25% loss:** Value obtained by applying the symmetric loss formula incorrectly.
- **C — 12.5% loss:** Result of subtracting 25% from 50% with the wrong sign.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Check CP = SP/(1±r): 1/1.5 + 1/0.75 = 2/3 + 4/3 = 2. Since Total CP = 2 × SP = Total SP, there is no profit, no loss.

### Q201 · Two Items at Same SP

A trader buys two fans for ₹2,400 each. He sells one at a profit of 15% and the other at a loss of 10%. Find his overall profit or loss percentage.

A. 2.5% profit
B. 2.5% loss
C. 5% profit
D. 1.5% loss

**Answer:** A. 2.5% profit
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 2.5% profit (Correct):** Total CP = 2,400 + 2,400 = ₹4,800. Net profit = 15% of 2,400 − 10% of 2,400 = 360 − 240 = ₹120. Overall profit % = (120 / 4,800) × 100 = 2.5% profit.
- **B — 2.5% loss:** Result of confusing profit with loss when averaging.
- **C — 5% profit:** Value obtained by forgetting to divide (+15% − 10%) by 2.
- **D — 1.5% loss:** Result of applying the same-SP formula (−15×10/100 = −1.5%) to a same-CP problem.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When CP is the SAME, overall % = (P₁ + P₂) / 2 = (+15 − 10) / 2 = +2.5% (profit).

### Q202 · Two Items at Same SP

A man bought two chairs for a total of ₹1,950. He sold one at a profit of 20% and the other at a loss of 25%. If the selling prices of both chairs are equal, find the cost price of the chair sold at a profit.

A. ₹1,200
B. ₹750
C. ₹900
D. ₹1,050

**Answer:** B. ₹750
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹750 (Correct):** Let CPs be x and y. Given 1.20x = 0.75y ⇒ x / y = 75 / 120 = 5 / 8. Since x + y = 1,950, x = 1,950 × (5 / 13) = ₹750.
- **A — ₹1,200:** Result of finding the cost price of the second chair (₹1,200) instead of the first.
- **C — ₹900:** Value obtained by dividing ₹1,950 in a 6:7 ratio.
- **D — ₹1,050:** Result of swapping the numerator and denominator in the percentage ratio.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equal SP ⇒ CP₁ : CP₂ = (100 − L) : (100 + P) = 75 : 120 = 5 : 8. So CP₁ = 1,950 × 5/13 = ₹750.

### Q203 · Two Items at Same SP

Two tables are sold for ₹6,000 each. One is sold at a profit of 25% and the other at a loss of 14²⁄₇%. What is the overall profit or loss in rupees?

A. ₹200 loss
B. ₹400 profit
C. ₹200 profit
D. ₹300 loss

**Answer:** C. ₹200 profit
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹200 profit (Correct):** For the first table (25% = 1/4 profit), CP₁ = 6,000 × (4/5) = ₹4,800. For the second (14²⁄₇% = 1/7 loss), CP₂ = 6,000 × (7/6) = ₹7,000. Total CP = 4,800 + 7,000 = ₹11,800; Total SP = ₹12,000. Net profit = 12,000 − 11,800 = ₹200.
- **A — ₹200 loss:** Result of subtracting Total SP from Total CP with a sign error.
- **B — ₹400 profit:** Value obtained by miscomputing 6,000 × 4/5 as ₹4,600.
- **D — ₹300 loss:** Result of using 14% instead of the exact fraction 1/7.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use fractions: 25% = 1/4 ⇒ CP₁ = 6000×4/5 = 4800; 14²⁄₇% = 1/7 ⇒ CP₂ = 6000×7/6 = 7000. Profit = 12000 − 11800 = ₹200.

### Q204 · Two Items at Same SP

A man buys two cycles for ₹3,600 in total. He sells the first at a profit of 15% and the second at a loss of 9%, resulting in neither profit nor loss overall. Find the cost price of the first cycle.

A. ₹2,250
B. ₹1,500
C. ₹1,200
D. ₹1,350

**Answer:** D. ₹1,350
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹1,350 (Correct):** For no profit, no loss: 15% of CP₁ = 9% of CP₂ ⇒ CP₁ / CP₂ = 9 / 15 = 3 / 5. Thus CP₁ = 3,600 × (3 / 8) = ₹1,350.
- **A — ₹2,250:** Result of finding the cost price of the second cycle (₹2,250) instead of the first.
- **B — ₹1,500:** Value obtained by dividing ₹3,600 in a 5:7 ratio.
- **C — ₹1,200:** Result of dividing ₹3,600 by 3 directly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** By Alligation (0% overall): CP₁ : CP₂ = 9 : 15 = 3 : 5. So CP₁ = 3,600 × 3/8 = ₹1,350.

### Q205 · Two Items at Same SP

A merchant sells two articles at the same selling price. He makes a 20% profit on the first and a 10% loss on the second. If his overall profit is ₹120, what is the selling price of each article?

A. ₹2,160
B. ₹1,800
C. ₹2,400
D. ₹1,920

**Answer:** A. ₹2,160
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹2,160 (Correct):** Let SP of each = 18x (LCM of 6 and 9). Then CP₁ = 18x × (5/6) = 15x and CP₂ = 18x × (10/9) = 20x. Total CP = 35x, Total SP = 36x. Profit = x = ₹120. Therefore, SP of each = 18 × 120 = ₹2,160.
- **B — ₹1,800:** Result of finding CP₁ (15 × 120 = ₹1,800) instead of SP.
- **C — ₹2,400:** Value obtained by finding CP₂ (20 × 120 = ₹2,400) instead of SP.
- **D — ₹1,920:** Result of taking 16 × 120 instead of 18 × 120.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Ratio CP₁:SP = 5:6 = 15:18; CP₂:SP = 10:9 = 20:18. Total CP = 35, Total SP = 36. 1 unit = ₹120 ⇒ SP = 18 × 120 = ₹2,160.

### Q206 · Two Items at Same SP

A dealer buys two televisions for ₹15,000 each. He sells the first at a profit of 12%. At what profit percentage must he sell the second television to earn an overall profit of 18%?

A. 30%
B. 24%
C. 21%
D. 26%

**Answer:** B. 24%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 24% (Correct):** Since the cost prices are equal, overall profit % = (P₁ + P₂) / 2. Thus (12 + P₂) / 2 = 18 ⇒ 12 + P₂ = 36 ⇒ P₂ = 24%.
- **A — 30%:** Result of adding 12% + 18% = 30%.
- **C — 21%:** Value obtained by taking 18% + 3% instead of doubling 18%.
- **D — 26%:** Result of miscomputing 36 − 12 as 26%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equal CPs ⇒ P₂ = 2 × Overall% − P₁ = 2(18) − 12 = 24%.

### Q207 · Dishonest Shopkeeper

A dishonest grocer professes to sell rice at cost price, but he uses a weight of 800 grams instead of 1 kilogram. What is his actual profit percentage?

A. 20%
B. 22.5%
C. 25%
D. 18%

**Answer:** C. 25%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 25% (Correct):** He gives 800 g while charging for 1,000 g. Profit = 1,000 − 800 = 200 g on an actual outlay of 800 g. Profit % = (200 / 800) × 100 = 25%.
- **A — 20%:** Result of dividing 200 g by 1,000 g instead of the actual weight 800 g (20%).
- **B — 22.5%:** Value obtained by averaging 20% and 25%.
- **D — 18%:** Result of computing 180 / 1,000.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** False weight profit % = (Error / True_used_weight) × 100 = (200 / 800) × 100 = 25%.

### Q208 · Dishonest Shopkeeper

A shopkeeper marks his goods 20% above the cost price and also uses a false weight of 900 grams for a kilogram. What is his overall profit percentage?

A. 30%
B. 32%
C. 28.5%
D. 33.33%

**Answer:** D. 33.33%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 33.33% (Correct):** Let CP of 1,000 g = ₹1,000. He gives 900 g (actual CP = ₹900) and charges 20% above ₹1,000 = ₹1,200. Profit = 1,200 − 900 = ₹300. Profit % = (300 / 900) × 100 = 33.33%.
- **A — 30%:** Result of simply adding 20% markup and 10% weight error (30%).
- **B — 32%:** Value obtained by compounding 20% and 10% (1.2 × 1.1 − 1 = 32%).
- **C — 28.5%:** Result of dividing profit by 1,050 instead of 900.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Multiply factors: (120/100) × (1000/900) = 4/3 ⇒ Profit = 1/3 = 33.33%.

### Q209 · Dishonest Shopkeeper

A trader claims to sell sugar at a loss of 10%, but he uses a weight of 750 grams instead of 1 kilogram. Find his actual profit or loss percentage.

A. 20% profit
B. 15% profit
C. 15% loss
D. 25% profit

**Answer:** A. 20% profit
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 20% profit (Correct):** Let CP of 1,000 g = ₹1,000. Actual CP of 750 g = ₹750. SP = 90% of ₹1,000 = ₹900. Profit = 900 − 750 = ₹150. Profit % = (150 / 750) × 100 = 20% profit.
- **B — 15% profit:** Result of subtracting 10% from 25% weight gain directly (15% profit).
- **C — 15% loss:** Value obtained by treating the claimed 10% loss as dominant.
- **D — 25% profit:** Result of dividing 250 by 1,000 instead of 750.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Net factor = (90/100) × (1000/750) = 0.9 × 4/3 = 1.2 ⇒ 20% profit.

### Q210 · Dishonest Shopkeeper

A dishonest dealer uses a faulty balance that measures 1,100 grams for 1 kg while buying from the wholesaler, and 880 grams for 1 kg while selling to customers at cost price. What is his profit percentage?

A. 22%
B. 25%
C. 20%
D. 21%

**Answer:** B. 25%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 25% (Correct):** For the price of 1,000 g, he buys 1,100 g. While selling, he gives 880 g for the price of 1,000 g. Effective ratio SP/CP = 1,100 / 880 = 5 / 4. Profit % = (1 / 4) × 100 = 25%.
- **A — 22%:** Result of adding 10% and 12% directly.
- **C — 20%:** Value obtained by taking (1,100 − 880) / 1,100 = 20%.
- **D — 21%:** Result of using the successive formula 10 + 10 + 1 = 21%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Ratio of quantities = Bought / Sold = 1,100 / 880 = 5 / 4 ⇒ Profit = 1/4 = 25%.

### Q211 · Dishonest Shopkeeper

A shopkeeper sells goods at a profit of 12% and also uses a weight that is 16% less than the standard weight. What is his total profit percentage?

A. 28%
B. 29.92%
C. 33.33%
D. 30%

**Answer:** C. 33.33%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 33.33% (Correct):** Let CP of 100 g = ₹100. He gives 84 g (actual CP = ₹84) and charges 12% profit on 100 g (SP = ₹112). Profit = 112 − 84 = ₹28. Profit % = (28 / 84) × 100 = 33.33%.
- **A — 28%:** Result of simply adding 12% and 16% (28%).
- **B — 29.92%:** Value obtained by compounding 12% and 16% (1.12 × 1.16 − 1 = 29.92%).
- **D — 30%:** Result of dividing 28 by 93.33 instead of 84.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Total factor = 112 / 84 = 4 / 3 ⇒ Overall profit = 1/3 = 33.33%.

### Q212 · Dishonest Shopkeeper

A trader marks his goods 40% above cost price, allows a 15% discount, and uses an 850-gram weight instead of 1 kg. Find his net profit percentage.

A. 25%
B. 19%
C. 35%
D. 40%

**Answer:** D. 40%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 40% (Correct):** Let CP of 1,000 g = ₹1,000. MP = ₹1,400. After 15% discount, SP = 1,400 × 0.85 = ₹1,190. Actual CP of 850 g given = ₹850. Profit = 1,190 − 850 = ₹340. Profit % = (340 / 850) × 100 = 40%.
- **A — 25%:** Result of computing 40 − 15 = 25% and ignoring the false weight.
- **B — 19%:** Value obtained by finding 1.40 × 0.85 − 1 = 19% without accounting for the 850 g weight.
- **C — 35%:** Result of subtracting 15% discount and adding 15% weight gain inconsistently.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Net multiplier = 1.40 × 0.85 × (1000 / 850) = 1.40 ⇒ 40% profit (the 15% discount and 850 g weight cancel out!).

### Q213 · Dishonest Shopkeeper

A milkman mixes water with pure milk and sells the mixture at the cost price of pure milk, thereby earning a 25% profit. What is the percentage of water in the mixture?

A. 20%
B. 25%
C. 16.67%
D. 15%

**Answer:** A. 20%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 20% (Correct):** 25% profit = 1/4 means 1 part water is added to 4 parts milk. Total mixture = 4 + 1 = 5 parts. Percentage of water in mixture = (1 / 5) × 100 = 20%.
- **B — 25%:** Result of confusing the ratio of water to milk (25%) with water in the total mixture.
- **C — 16.67%:** Value obtained by using 1/6 instead of 1/5.
- **D — 15%:** Result of subtracting 10% from 25%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If profit = 1/n, then Water : Milk = 1 : n, so Water % in mixture = 1 / (n + 1) × 100 = 1/5 = 20%.

### Q214 · Dishonest Shopkeeper

A grocer claims to sell wheat at cost price, yet he earns a profit of 11¹⁄₉% by using a false weight. How many grams does he actually give for 1 kilogram?

A. 888.9 g
B. 900 g
C. 850 g
D. 910 g

**Answer:** B. 900 g
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 900 g (Correct):** 11¹⁄₉% = 1/9. Thus CP : SP = 9 : 10, which means actual weight given : billed weight = 9 : 10. For 1,000 g billed, actual weight = 1,000 × (9 / 10) = 900 g.
- **A — 888.9 g:** Result of subtracting 11.11% of 1,000 g directly (1,000 − 111.1 = 888.9 g).
- **C — 850 g:** Value obtained by approximating 11¹⁄₉% as 15%.
- **D — 910 g:** Result of subtracting 90 g instead of 100 g.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Profit = 1/9 ⇒ Weight used = 1,000 × 9/(9 + 1) = 900 g.

### Q215 · Dishonest Shopkeeper

A cloth merchant uses a 90 cm metre-rod instead of a 100 cm rod while selling, and also marks his selling price 8% above cost price. What is his net profit percentage?

A. 18%
B. 18.8%
C. 20%
D. 22%

**Answer:** C. 20%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 20% (Correct):** Actual cloth given = 90 cm (CP = ₹90 if 1 cm = ₹1). Price charged = 108% of ₹100 = ₹108. Profit = 108 − 90 = ₹18. Profit % = (18 / 90) × 100 = 20%.
- **A — 18%:** Result of adding 8% and 10% directly (18%).
- **B — 18.8%:** Value obtained by compounding 8% and 10% (1.08 × 1.10 − 1 = 18.8%).
- **D — 22%:** Result of dividing 20 by 90.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Net factor = (108 / 100) × (100 / 90) = 108 / 90 = 6 / 5 ⇒ 20% profit.

### Q216 · Successive Discounts

Find the single equivalent discount for three successive discounts of 20%, 15%, and 10%.

A. 45%
B. 40.2%
C. 36.5%
D. 38.8%

**Answer:** D. 38.8%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 38.8% (Correct):** Remaining price factor = 0.80 × 0.85 × 0.90 = 0.612, or 61.2%. Single equivalent discount = 100% − 61.2% = 38.8%.
- **A — 45%:** Result of simply adding the three discounts (20 + 15 + 10 = 45%).
- **B — 40.2%:** Value obtained by an arithmetic slip when multiplying 0.68 × 0.9.
- **C — 36.5%:** Result of subtracting 3.5% twice instead of compounding.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Multiply remaining percentages: 0.8 × 0.85 = 0.68; 0.68 × 0.9 = 0.612 ⇒ Discount = 100 − 61.2 = 38.8%.

### Q217 · Successive Discounts

What is the difference between a single discount of 35% and two successive discounts of 20% and 15% on a bill of ₹4,000?

A. ₹120
B. ₹0
C. ₹80
D. ₹150

**Answer:** A. ₹120
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹120 (Correct):** Equivalent of 20% and 15% = 20 + 15 − (20 × 15)/100 = 32%. Difference in discount = 35% − 32% = 3%. Thus, difference = 3% of ₹4,000 = ₹120.
- **B — ₹0:** Result of assuming 20% + 15% equals 35%, giving ₹0 difference.
- **C — ₹80:** Value obtained by taking 2% of ₹4,000 instead of 3%.
- **D — ₹150:** Result of computing 3.75% of ₹4,000.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Difference between (a+b)% and successive a%, b% is ALWAYS (ab/100)% of MP = (20×15/100)% of 4,000 = 3% of 4,000 = ₹120.

### Q218 · Successive Discounts

The marked price of a jacket is ₹2,500. After two successive discounts of 20% and x%, it is sold for ₹1,700. Find the value of x.

A. 12%
B. 15%
C. 18%
D. 10%

**Answer:** B. 15%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 15% (Correct):** Price after first 20% discount = 2,500 × 0.80 = ₹2,000. Second discount = 2,000 − 1,700 = ₹300. Thus x = (300 / 2,000) × 100 = 15%.
- **A — 12%:** Result of subtracting 20% from total discount 32% incorrectly as 12%.
- **C — 18%:** Value obtained by dividing ₹300 by ₹1,700 instead of ₹2,000.
- **D — 10%:** Result of taking ₹250 as the second discount.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Apply first discount: 2,500 × 0.8 = 2,000. Then x% = (2,000 − 1,700)/2,000 = 300/2,000 = 15%.

### Q219 · Successive Discounts

Two successive discounts of 12.5% and 20% are equivalent to a single discount of:

A. 32.5%
B. 27.5%
C. 30%
D. 31.25%

**Answer:** C. 30%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 30% (Correct):** 12.5% = 1/8 (remaining = 7/8) and 20% = 1/5 (remaining = 4/5). Net remaining = (7/8) × (4/5) = 7/10 = 70%. Equivalent discount = 100% − 70% = 30%.
- **A — 32.5%:** Result of adding 12.5% and 20% without subtracting their product.
- **B — 27.5%:** Value obtained by subtracting 5% instead of 2.5% from 32.5%.
- **D — 31.25%:** Result of computing 12.5 × 20 / 100 = 2.5% wrongly subtracted from 33.75%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use fractions: (7/8) × (4/5) = 7/10 remaining ⇒ 3/10 = 30% discount.

### Q220 · Successive Discounts

An article marked at ₹1,600 is sold for ₹1,156 after two equal successive discounts of x%. What is the value of x?

A. 12.5%
B. 14%
C. 16%
D. 15%

**Answer:** D. 15%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 15% (Correct):** 1,600 × (1 − x/100)² = 1,156 ⇒ (1 − x/100)² = 1,156 / 1,600 = 289 / 400. Taking square root: 1 − x/100 = 17 / 20 = 0.85 ⇒ x = 15%.
- **A — 12.5%:** Result of halving the total discount 27.75% approximately.
- **B — 14%:** Value obtained by dividing 27.75% by 2 without accounting for compounding.
- **C — 16%:** Result of miscalculating √289 as 16.8.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Take √(SP/MP) = √(1156/1600) = 34/40 = 85/100 ⇒ Discount = 100 − 85 = 15%.

### Q221 · Successive Discounts

A store offers three discount schemes: (i) two successive discounts of 20% and 20%, (ii) 25% and 15%, and (iii) 30% and 10%. What is the maximum effective discount percentage a customer can get?

A. 37%
B. 36.25%
C. 36%
D. 40%

**Answer:** A. 37%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 37% (Correct):** Scheme (i): 20+20 − 4 = 36%. Scheme (ii): 25+15 − 3.75 = 36.25%. Scheme (iii): 30+10 − 3 = 37%. The maximum effective discount is 37%.
- **B — 36.25%:** Result of choosing the 25% + 15% scheme (36.25%).
- **C — 36%:** Value obtained by choosing the 20% + 20% scheme (36%).
- **D — 40%:** Result of adding the two discounts in any scheme without subtracting ab/100.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When a + b is constant, the LARGEST successive discount comes from the pair with the LARGEST difference (30% and 10% ⇒ 40 − 3 = 37%).

### Q222 · Successive Discounts

After allowing two successive discounts of 10% and 12% on the marked price, a mixer is sold for ₹1,980. What is its marked price?

A. ₹2,400
B. ₹2,500
C. ₹2,538
D. ₹2,475

**Answer:** B. ₹2,500
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹2,500 (Correct):** Net SP factor = 0.90 × 0.88 = 0.792. Given 0.792 × MP = 1,980 ⇒ MP = 1,980 / 0.792 = ₹2,500.
- **A — ₹2,400:** Result of adding 22% directly to ₹1,980 (1,980 × 1.22 ≈ ₹2,415) or rounding.
- **C — ₹2,538:** Value obtained by dividing 1,980 by 0.78 (using 22% single discount).
- **D — ₹2,475:** Result of adding 25% to ₹1,980 directly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Reverse sequentially: 1,980 / 0.9 = 2,200; then 2,200 / 0.88 = 2,200 × 25/22 = ₹2,500.

### Q223 · Successive Discounts

A television marked at ₹4,000 is sold after two successive discounts of 15% and 20%. If a 5% GST is charged on the discounted price, what is the final amount paid by the customer?

A. ₹2,720
B. ₹2,800
C. ₹2,856
D. ₹2,920

**Answer:** C. ₹2,856
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹2,856 (Correct):** Price after discounts = 4,000 × 0.85 × 0.80 = ₹2,720. Adding 5% GST = 2,720 × 1.05 = 2,720 + 136 = ₹2,856.
- **A — ₹2,720:** Result of forgetting to add the 5% GST (₹2,720).
- **B — ₹2,800:** Value obtained by applying net change as 15 + 20 − 5 = 30% discount (4,000 × 0.70).
- **D — ₹2,920:** Result of computing 5% GST on ₹4,000 instead of the discounted price ₹2,720.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Chain factors: 4,000 × 0.85 × 0.80 = 2,720; add 5% (half of 10% = 136) ⇒ ₹2,856.

### Q224 · Successive Discounts

Two successive discounts of 14²⁄₇% and 12½% are equivalent to a single discount of:

A. 26.78%
B. 24.5%
C. 22.5%
D. 25%

**Answer:** D. 25%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 25% (Correct):** 14²⁄₇% = 1/7 (remaining = 6/7) and 12½% = 1/8 (remaining = 7/8). Net remaining factor = (6/7) × (7/8) = 6/8 = 3/4 = 75%. Thus equivalent discount = 25%.
- **A — 26.78%:** Result of simply adding 14.28% + 12.5% = 26.78%.
- **B — 24.5%:** Value obtained by rounding fractions before multiplying.
- **C — 22.5%:** Result of subtracting 4.28% from 26.78%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Convert to fractions: (6/7) × (7/8) = 3/4 remaining ⇒ 1/4 = 25% discount.

### Q225 · MP/CP Rule & Markup

A shopkeeper allows a 20% discount on the marked price of an article and still earns a profit of 20%. If the marked price is ₹1,800, what is its cost price?

A. ₹1,200
B. ₹1,440
C. ₹1,152
D. ₹1,260

**Answer:** A. ₹1,200
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹1,200 (Correct):** Using MP / CP = (100 + P) / (100 − D) = (100 + 20) / (100 − 20) = 120 / 80 = 3 / 2. Thus CP = 1,800 × (2 / 3) = ₹1,200.
- **B — ₹1,440:** Result of finding the selling price (1,800 × 0.80 = ₹1,440) instead of the cost price.
- **C — ₹1,152:** Value obtained by reducing SP by 20% instead of dividing by 1.20 (1,440 × 0.80 = ₹1,152).
- **D — ₹1,260:** Result of subtracting 30% from ₹1,800.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Golden SSC Rule: CP : MP = (100 − D) : (100 + P) = 80 : 120 = 2 : 3. So CP = 1,800 × 2/3 = ₹1,200.

### Q226 · MP/CP Rule & Markup

A trader allows a discount of 15% on the marked price and still makes a profit of 19%. By what percentage above the cost price is the article marked?

A. 34%
B. 40%
C. 36%
D. 42%

**Answer:** B. 40%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 40% (Correct):** MP / CP = (100 + P) / (100 − D) = (100 + 19) / (100 − 15) = 119 / 85 = 7 / 5 = 1.40. Hence, the article is marked 40% above CP.
- **A — 34%:** Result of simply adding 15% and 19% (34%).
- **C — 36%:** Value obtained by miscomputing 119 / 85.
- **D — 42%:** Result of adding 15 + 19 + 8.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** MP/CP = 119/85. Divide both by 17 ⇒ 7/5 = 140/100 ⇒ 40% markup.

### Q227 · MP/CP Rule & Markup

A merchant marks his goods 35% above the cost price and allows a discount of 20% on the marked price. What is his profit percentage?

A. 15%
B. 10%
C. 8%
D. 12%

**Answer:** C. 8%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 8% (Correct):** Let CP = 100. MP = 135. SP = 135 × 0.80 = 108. Profit = 108 − 100 = 8%.
- **A — 15%:** Result of subtracting 20% from 35% directly (15%).
- **B — 10%:** Value obtained by an arithmetic slip in 135 × 0.8.
- **D — 12%:** Result of taking 20% of 100 instead of 20% of 135.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use successive formula: M − D − (MD/100) = 35 − 20 − (35×20)/100 = 15 − 7 = 8%.

### Q228 · MP/CP Rule & Markup

A shopkeeper marks his goods 25% above the cost price and allows a discount of x% to earn a profit of 10%. Find the value of x.

A. 15%
B. 12.5%
C. 10%
D. 12%

**Answer:** D. 12%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 12% (Correct):** Let CP = 100, then MP = 125 and SP = 110. Discount = 125 − 110 = 15 on MP of 125. Discount % = (15 / 125) × 100 = 12%.
- **A — 15%:** Result of subtracting 10% from 25% directly (15%).
- **B — 12.5%:** Value obtained by dividing 15 by 120 instead of 125.
- **C — 10%:** Result of confusing profit % with discount %.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Discount = MP − SP = 125 − 110 = 15. Discount % = 15/125 = 3/25 = 12%.

### Q229 · MP/CP Rule & Markup

If an article is sold at its marked price without any discount, a profit of 25% is earned. If a discount of 12% is allowed on the marked price, what will be the profit percentage?

A. 10%
B. 13%
C. 12%
D. 11.5%

**Answer:** A. 10%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 10% (Correct):** No discount means MP = 125 when CP = 100. With a 12% discount, SP = 125 × 0.88 = 110. Thus, profit % = 110 − 100 = 10%.
- **B — 13%:** Result of subtracting 12% from 25% directly (13%).
- **C — 12%:** Value obtained by taking 12% of 100 instead of 125.
- **D — 11.5%:** Result of miscalculating 12% of 125.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Markup = 25%, Discount = 12%. Net profit = 25 − 12 − (25×12)/100 = 13 − 3 = 10%.

### Q230 · MP/CP Rule & Markup

When a shopkeeper gives a 10% discount on the marked price, he earns a 20% profit. What will be his profit or loss percentage if he gives a 25% discount?

A. 5% profit
B. No profit, no loss
C. 5% loss
D. 2.5% profit

**Answer:** B. No profit, no loss
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — No profit, no loss (Correct):** MP / CP = (100 + 20) / (100 − 10) = 120 / 90 = 4 / 3. At 25% discount, SP = 0.75 × MP = (3/4) × (4/3) × CP = CP. Thus, no profit, no loss.
- **A — 5% profit:** Result of subtracting the 15% extra discount directly from 20% profit (5% profit).
- **C — 5% loss:** Value obtained by miscalculating (3/4) × (4/3).
- **D — 2.5% profit:** Result of averaging 20% and −15%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** SP₁ : SP₂ = 90 : 75 = 6 : 5. Since SP₁ = 120% of CP, SP₂ = 120 × (5/6) = 100% of CP ⇒ 0% (no profit, no loss).

### Q231 · MP/CP Rule & Markup

The cost price of a pair of shoes is ₹1,400. What should be its marked price so that after allowing a discount of 12.5%, the seller still earns a 25% profit?

A. ₹1,925
B. ₹2,100
C. ₹2,000
D. ₹1,960

**Answer:** C. ₹2,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹2,000 (Correct):** MP / CP = (100 + 25) / (100 − 12.5) = 125 / 87.5 = 10 / 7. Therefore, MP = 1,400 × (10 / 7) = ₹2,000.
- **A — ₹1,925:** Result of adding 25% + 12.5% = 37.5% to ₹1,400 (1,400 × 1.375 = ₹1,925).
- **B — ₹2,100:** Value obtained by increasing ₹1,400 by 50%.
- **D — ₹1,960:** Result of multiplying ₹1,750 by 1.12 instead of dividing by 0.875.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 12.5% = 1/8 ⇒ SP = (7/8)MP = (5/4)CP ⇒ MP = CP × (5/4) × (8/7) = 1,400 × 10/7 = ₹2,000.

### Q232 · MP/CP Rule & Markup

The ratio of the cost price to the marked price of an article is 4 : 7. If a discount of 20% is offered on the marked price, what is the profit percentage?

A. 35%
B. 45%
C. 32%
D. 40%

**Answer:** D. 40%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 40% (Correct):** Let CP = 400 and MP = 700. After a 20% discount, SP = 700 × 0.80 = 560. Profit = 560 − 400 = 160. Profit % = (160 / 400) × 100 = 40%.
- **A — 35%:** Result of computing 75% markup minus 20% discount with an arithmetic error.
- **B — 45%:** Value obtained by taking 700 − 20% of 400.
- **C — 32%:** Result of dividing 160 by 500 instead of 400.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Take CP = 4, MP = 7. SP = 7 × 0.8 = 5.6. Profit = 1.6/4 = 0.40 = 40%.

### Q233 · MP/CP Rule & Markup

A dealer earns a profit of 26% after allowing a discount of 10% on the marked price. What will be his profit percentage if he allows a discount of 15%?

A. 19%
B. 21%
C. 18.5%
D. 20%

**Answer:** A. 19%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 19% (Correct):** MP / CP = (100 + 26) / (100 − 10) = 126 / 90 = 1.4. At 15% discount, SP = 1.4 × 0.85 × CP = 1.19 × CP. Hence, profit % = 19%.
- **B — 21%:** Result of subtracting 5% extra discount directly from 26% profit (21%).
- **C — 18.5%:** Value obtained by miscomputing 1.4 × 0.85.
- **D — 20%:** Result of taking 15% of 135 instead of 140.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** SP₁ = 126% of CP at 90% of MP ⇒ 1% of MP = 1.4% of CP. At 85% of MP, SP₂ = 85 × 1.4 = 119% of CP ⇒ 19% profit.

### Q234 · MP/CP Rule & Markup

The difference between the selling prices of an article at discounts of 12% and 18% is ₹192. If the cost price of the article is ₹2,500, by what percentage is it marked above the cost price?

A. 25%
B. 28%
C. 32%
D. 24%

**Answer:** B. 28%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 28% (Correct):** (18% − 12%) of MP = ₹192 ⇒ 6% of MP = 192 ⇒ MP = 192 × (100 / 6) = ₹3,200. Markup over CP = 3,200 − 2,500 = ₹700. Markup % = (700 / 2,500) × 100 = 28%.
- **A — 25%:** Result of computing 700 / 2,800 = 25%.
- **C — 32%:** Value obtained by dividing ₹192 by 6 and misplacing a factor, getting MP = ₹3,300.
- **D — 24%:** Result of computing 600 / 2,500 = 24%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 6% of MP = 192 ⇒ MP = 3,200. Since 2,500 = 100 × 25, divide 3,200 by 25 ⇒ 128 ⇒ 28% markup.

### Q235 · Free Articles & Schemes

A clothing store advertises a 'Buy 3, Get 1 Free' offer on shirts. What is the effective discount percentage?

A. 33.33%
B. 20%
C. 25%
D. 30%

**Answer:** C. 25%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 25% (Correct):** The customer receives 3 + 1 = 4 shirts while paying for only 3. Free articles = 1 out of 4 total articles. Effective discount % = (1 / 4) × 100 = 25%.
- **A — 33.33%:** Result of dividing 1 free shirt by 3 paid shirts (1/3 = 33.33%).
- **B — 20%:** Value obtained by using 1/5 = 20%.
- **D — 30%:** Result of subtracting 10% from 40%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For 'Buy x, Get y Free', Discount % = y / (x + y) × 100 = 1 / (3 + 1) × 100 = 25%.

### Q236 · Free Articles & Schemes

Under a festive scheme, a supermarket offers 'Buy 5, Get 3 Free' on soap bars. What is the net percentage discount offered?

A. 60%
B. 40%
C. 35%
D. 37.5%

**Answer:** D. 37.5%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 37.5% (Correct):** Total items taken = 5 + 3 = 8, of which 3 are free. Discount % = (3 / 8) × 100 = 37.5%.
- **A — 60%:** Result of dividing 3 free items by 5 paid items (3/5 = 60%).
- **B — 40%:** Value obtained by dividing 3 by 7.5 instead of 8.
- **C — 35%:** Result of rounding 37.5% down to 35%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Discount % = Free / Total = 3 / (5 + 3) = 3/8. Memorize 3/8 = 37.5%.

### Q237 · Free Articles & Schemes

A bookstore offers 'Buy 4, Get 1 Free' along with an additional 10% discount on the total bill. What is the net effective discount percentage?

A. 28%
B. 30%
C. 35%
D. 26%

**Answer:** A. 28%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 28% (Correct):** The 'Buy 4, Get 1 Free' offer gives a 1/(4+1) = 20% discount. Combining 20% and 10% successively: Net discount = 20 + 10 − (20 × 10)/100 = 28%.
- **B — 30%:** Result of adding 20% and 10% without compounding (30%).
- **C — 35%:** Value obtained by taking 25% + 10% = 35%.
- **D — 26%:** Result of miscomputing 20 × 10 / 100 as 4 instead of 2.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Remaining factor = (Paid / Total) × (1 − d) = (4/5) × 0.9 = 0.72 ⇒ Net discount = 28%.

### Q238 · Free Articles & Schemes

A wholesaler gives 1 article free on the purchase of every 15 articles and also allows a 4% cash discount, yet he earns a 35% profit. By what percentage is the marked price above the cost price?

A. 45%
B. 50%
C. 40%
D. 55%

**Answer:** B. 50%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 50% (Correct):** Customer gets 16 articles and pays for 15 at 96% of MP. So effective SP per article = (15/16) × 0.96 × MP = 0.90 × MP (a net 10% discount). Using MP/CP = (100 + 35)/(100 − 10) = 135/90 = 1.50, MP is 50% above CP.
- **A — 45%:** Result of ignoring the 1 free article and using only the 4% discount.
- **C — 40%:** Value obtained by computing 35% + 4% + 1% ≈ 40%.
- **D — 55%:** Result of miscalculating 135 / 90.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Effective SP factor = (15/16) × (24/25) = 9/10 ⇒ 10% net discount. Thus MP/CP = 135/90 = 3/2 ⇒ 50% markup.

### Q239 · Free Articles & Schemes

A retailer offers a 'Buy 7, Get 2 Free' scheme and still earns a profit of 20%. If the cost price of each article is ₹350, what is the marked price of each article?

A. ₹480
B. ₹520
C. ₹540
D. ₹560

**Answer:** C. ₹540
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹540 (Correct):** For 9 articles given, Total CP = 9 × 350 = ₹3,150. To earn 20% profit, Total SP = 3,150 × 1.20 = ₹3,780. Since the customer pays for only 7 articles, MP of 1 article = 3,780 / 7 = ₹540.
- **A — ₹480:** Result of computing 350 × 1.20 × (9/8) instead of (9/7).
- **B — ₹520:** Value obtained by adding 20% to ₹433.
- **D — ₹560:** Result of dividing 3,780 by 6.75 instead of 7.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** MP × Paid = CP × Total × (1 + P) ⇒ MP = 350 × (9/7) × 1.2 = 50 × 9 × 1.2 = ₹540.

### Q240 · Free Articles & Schemes

A showroom offers 'Buy 6, Get 2 Free' on jackets and an additional 20% discount for members. What is the total percentage discount a member receives?

A. 45%
B. 36%
C. 35%
D. 40%

**Answer:** D. 40%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 40% (Correct):** With 'Buy 6, Get 2 Free', the member pays for 6 out of 8 jackets (75% of MP). After an additional 20% discount, amount paid = 75% × 80% = 60% of MP. Net discount = 100% − 60% = 40%.
- **A — 45%:** Result of adding 25% (from 2/8) and 20% directly (45%).
- **B — 36%:** Value obtained by using 2/6 = 33.33% for the free jackets.
- **C — 35%:** Result of miscalculating 75% × 80% as 65%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Remaining fraction = (6/8) × (4/5) = 3/5 = 60% ⇒ Total discount = 40%.

### Q241 · Free Articles & Schemes

A customer buys 6 bottles of juice (each marked at ₹300) under a 'Buy 4, Get 2 Free' offer. How much money does the customer save compared to paying the full marked price for all 6 bottles?

A. ₹600
B. ₹900
C. ₹450
D. ₹300

**Answer:** A. ₹600
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹600 (Correct):** Full MP for 6 bottles = 6 × 300 = ₹1,800. Under 'Buy 4, Get 2 Free', the customer pays for only 4 bottles = 4 × 300 = ₹1,200. Savings = 1,800 − 1,200 = ₹600.
- **B — ₹900:** Result of computing 50% of ₹1,800 (using 2/4 instead of 2/6).
- **C — ₹450:** Value obtained by taking 25% of ₹1,800.
- **D — ₹300:** Result of counting only 1 free bottle.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Savings = Cost of free items = 2 × ₹300 = ₹600 (which is 2/6 = 33.33% off).

### Q242 · Selling Price Shifts

An article is sold at a profit of 12%. Had it been sold for ₹96 more, the profit would have been 20%. Find the cost price of the article.

A. ₹1,000
B. ₹1,200
C. ₹1,120
D. ₹1,440

**Answer:** B. ₹1,200
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹1,200 (Correct):** Increase in profit % = 20% − 12% = 8%. Given 8% of CP = ₹96 ⇒ CP = (96 / 8) × 100 = ₹1,200.
- **A — ₹1,000:** Result of dividing 96 by 12% instead of 8% (₹800) or misplacing a zero.
- **C — ₹1,120:** Value obtained by finding the first selling price (1,200 × 1.12) instead of CP.
- **D — ₹1,440:** Result of finding the second selling price (1,200 × 1.20) instead of CP.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Difference in % = 20 − 12 = 8%. Since 8% = ₹96, 1% = ₹12 ⇒ CP = ₹1,200.

### Q243 · Selling Price Shifts

A book is sold at a loss of 15%. Had it been sold for ₹210 more, there would have been a profit of 20%. What is the cost price of the book?

A. ₹4,200
B. ₹1,050
C. ₹600
D. ₹700

**Answer:** C. ₹600
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹600 (Correct):** Going from −15% to +20% is a total shift of 15 + 20 = 35% of CP. Given 35% of CP = ₹210 ⇒ CP = 210 × (100 / 35) = ₹600.
- **A — ₹4,200:** Result of subtracting 15% from 20% (5% = ₹210 ⇒ ₹4,200).
- **B — ₹1,050:** Value obtained by dividing 210 by 20% instead of 35%.
- **D — ₹700:** Result of dividing 210 by 30% instead of 35%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Loss to Profit ⇒ ADD percentages: 15% + 20% = 35%. 35% = 210 ⇒ 1% = 6 ⇒ CP = ₹600.

### Q244 · Selling Price Shifts

A man sells an article at a profit of 20%. If he had bought it at 10% less and sold it for ₹90 less, he would have gained 25%. What was the original cost price?

A. ₹1,500
B. ₹1,080
C. ₹1,350
D. ₹1,200

**Answer:** D. ₹1,200
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹1,200 (Correct):** Let original CP = 100x. Original SP = 120x. New CP = 90x. New SP = 90x × 1.25 = 112.5x. Difference in SP = 120x − 112.5x = 7.5x = ₹90 ⇒ x = 12. Original CP = 100 × 12 = ₹1,200.
- **A — ₹1,500:** Result of taking 10% less and 25% profit as 115x − 109x = 6x = 90.
- **B — ₹1,080:** Value obtained by finding the new CP (90 × 12 = ₹1,080) instead of the original CP.
- **C — ₹1,350:** Result of computing 90 / 6.67%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Compare SPs: Old SP = 120%, New SP = 90% × 1.25 = 112.5%. Diff = 7.5% = ₹90 ⇒ 100% = ₹1,200.

### Q245 · Selling Price Shifts

An article is sold at a 10% profit. If it had been bought at 20% less and sold for ₹140 more, the profit would have been 40%. Find the original cost price.

A. ₹7,000
B. ₹5,600
C. ₹3,500
D. ₹6,000

**Answer:** A. ₹7,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹7,000 (Correct):** Let original CP = 100x. Old SP = 110x. New CP = 80x. New SP = 80x × 1.40 = 112x. Difference = 112x − 110x = 2x = ₹140 ⇒ x = 70. Original CP = ₹7,000.
- **B — ₹5,600:** Result of finding the new CP (80 × 70 = ₹5,600) instead of the original CP.
- **C — ₹3,500:** Value obtained by taking the difference as 4x instead of 2x.
- **D — ₹6,000:** Result of computing 40% on 100x instead of 80x.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Old SP = 110%, New SP = 80% × 1.4 = 112%. Difference = 2% = ₹140 ⇒ 100% = ₹7,000.

### Q246 · Selling Price Shifts

A trader sells an article at a profit of 15%. If both the cost price and the selling price are reduced by ₹150, his profit increases to 20%. Find the original cost price.

A. ₹750
B. ₹600
C. ₹690
D. ₹500

**Answer:** B. ₹600
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹600 (Correct):** Let original CP = 20x and SP = 23x. New ratio: (23x − 150) / (20x − 150) = 6 / 5. Cross-multiplying: 115x − 750 = 120x − 900 ⇒ 5x = 150 ⇒ x = 30. Original CP = 20 × 30 = ₹600.
- **A — ₹750:** Result of taking CP = 25x instead of 20x.
- **C — ₹690:** Value obtained by finding the original SP (23 × 30 = ₹690) instead of CP.
- **D — ₹500:** Result of setting 5% of CP = ₹150 directly (getting ₹3,000) or mis-scaling.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When both CP and SP change by the same amount Δ, Profit (SP−CP) stays constant! Old Profit = 15% = 3/20, New Profit = 20% = 3/15. CP drops from 20 to 15 (5 units = ₹150) ⇒ 20 units = ₹600.

### Q247 · Part Sales & Mixtures

A merchant sells two-thirds of his stock at a profit of 15% and the remaining one-third at a loss of 9%. What is his overall profit or loss percentage?

A. 6% profit
B. 3% profit
C. 7% profit
D. 5% loss

**Answer:** C. 7% profit
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 7% profit (Correct):** Overall % = (2/3) × (+15%) + (1/3) × (−9%) = 10% − 3% = +7%, i.e., 7% profit.
- **A — 6% profit:** Result of taking the simple difference 15% − 9% = 6%.
- **B — 3% profit:** Value obtained by averaging +15% and −9% without weighting (3%).
- **D — 5% loss:** Result of miscomputing (2/3)×15 − (1/3)×9.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Weighted average: (2 × 15 − 1 × 9) / 3 = (30 − 9) / 3 = 21 / 3 = 7% profit.

### Q248 · Part Sales & Mixtures

A trader buys goods worth ₹6,000. He sells half of them at a loss of 10%. At what profit percentage must he sell the remaining half to earn an overall profit of 25%?

A. 40%
B. 50%
C. 35%
D. 60%

**Answer:** D. 60%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 60% (Correct):** Required total profit = 25% of 6,000 = ₹1,500. Loss on first half = 10% of 3,000 = ₹300. Profit needed on second half = 1,500 + 300 = ₹1,800. Profit % = (1,800 / 3,000) × 100 = 60%.
- **A — 40%:** Result of computing 25 + 15 = 40%.
- **B — 50%:** Value obtained by forgetting to compensate for the ₹300 loss (1,500 / 3,000 = 50%).
- **C — 35%:** Result of adding 25% and 10% directly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equal halves ⇒ (−10 + x)/2 = 25 ⇒ x = 50 + 10 = 60%.

### Q249 · Chain Transactions

A sells a bicycle to B at a 25% profit, B sells it to C at a 20% profit, and C sells it to D at a 10% loss. If D pays ₹2,700, how much did A pay for the bicycle?

A. ₹2,000
B. ₹1,800
C. ₹2,160
D. ₹2,250

**Answer:** A. ₹2,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹2,000 (Correct):** A × (5/4) × (6/5) × (9/10) = 2,700 ⇒ A × (27/20) = 2,700 ⇒ A = 2,700 × (20 / 27) = ₹2,000.
- **B — ₹1,800:** Result of dividing ₹2,700 by 1.50 (ignoring C's 10% loss).
- **C — ₹2,160:** Value obtained by taking net change as +25% instead of +35%.
- **D — ₹2,250:** Result of computing 2,700 × 0.833.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Chain fractions: (5/4) × (6/5) × (9/10) = 27/20. So A = 2,700 × 20/27 = ₹2,000.

### Q250 · Selling Price Shifts

By selling 60 mangoes at ₹35 each, a fruit seller suffers a loss equal to the cost price of 10 mangoes. What is the cost price of one mango?

A. ₹40
B. ₹42
C. ₹45
D. ₹38

**Answer:** B. ₹42
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹42 (Correct):** Total SP = 60 × 35 = ₹2,100. Since 60 CP − 60 SP = 10 CP, we get 50 CP = 60 SP = ₹2,100. Thus, CP of 1 mango = 2,100 / 50 = ₹42.
- **A — ₹40:** Result of adding 10/60 of ₹35 instead of dividing by 50.
- **C — ₹45:** Value obtained by miscalculating 2,100 / 50 as ₹45.
- **D — ₹38:** Result of dividing 2,100 by 55.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Loss of 10 CP on 60 mangoes ⇒ Total SP = CP of (60 − 10) = 50 mangoes ⇒ CP = 2,100 / 50 = ₹42.
