## Chapter 5: Ratio, Proportion, Mixture & Average

### Q251 · Compounding Ratios

If A : B = 3 : 4, B : C = 5 : 7 and C : D = 8 : 9, then what is A : D?

A. 10 : 21
B. 15 : 28
C. 5 : 14
D. 20 : 27

**Answer:** A. 10 : 21
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 10 : 21 (Correct):** Multiply the given ratios to eliminate intermediate terms: (A/B) × (B/C) × (C/D) = (3/4) × (5/7) × (8/9). Simplifying: (3 × 5 × 8) / (4 × 7 × 9) = 120 / 252 = 10 / 21. Thus, A : D = 10 : 21.
- **B — 15 : 28:** Obtained by multiplying only the first two ratios (3/4 × 5/7) and ignoring C : D.
- **C — 5 : 14:** Obtained by cancelling 8 with 4 to get 2, but forgetting to cancel 3 with 9 properly or dropping a factor of 2.
- **D — 20 : 27:** Obtained by inverting one of the intermediate ratios or miscalculating 4 × 7 × 9.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** To find A : D directly, multiply the fractions vertically and cross-cancel common factors before multiplying: (3/4)×(5/7)×(8/9) = 10/21.

### Q252 · Compounding Ratios

A sum of ₹12,400 is divided among A, B and C such that 2 times A's share = 3 times B's share = 5 times C's share. What is B's share?

A. ₹6,000
B. ₹4,000
C. ₹2,400
D. ₹3,600

**Answer:** B. ₹4,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹4,000 (Correct):** Given 2A = 3B = 5C = k. Then A = k/2, B = k/3, C = k/5. Multiplying by LCM(2, 3, 5) = 30 gives A : B : C = 15 : 10 : 6. Total parts = 15 + 10 + 6 = 31. Value of 1 part = 12400 / 31 = ₹400. Therefore, B's share = 10 × 400 = ₹4,000.
- **A — ₹6,000:** Represents A's share (15 × 400) instead of B's share.
- **C — ₹2,400:** Represents C's share (6 × 400) instead of B's share.
- **D — ₹3,600:** Results from taking A : B : C directly as 2 : 3 : 5 and misdividing 12400.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For pA = qB = rC, use the cover-up rule: A = q×r, B = p×r, C = p×q. Here A:B:C = (3×5):(2×5):(2×3) = 15:10:6.

### Q253 · Compounding Ratios

₹8,400 is divided among A, B, C and D such that A : B = 2 : 3, B : C = 4 : 5 and C : D = 6 : 7. What is the difference between the shares of D and A?

A. ₹1,440
B. ₹1,680
C. ₹1,520
D. ₹1,280

**Answer:** C. ₹1,520
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹1,520 (Correct):** First combine A : B = 2 : 3 and B : C = 4 : 5 to get A : B : C = 8 : 12 : 15. Next combine with C : D = 6 : 7 (LCM of 15 and 6 is 30): A : B : C : D = 16 : 24 : 30 : 35. Total parts = 16 + 24 + 30 + 35 = 105. 1 part = 8400 / 105 = ₹80. Difference between D and A = (35 - 16) × 80 = 19 × 80 = ₹1,520.
- **A — ₹1,440:** Obtained by taking D - A as 18 parts instead of 19 parts (18 × 80).
- **B — ₹1,680:** Obtained by taking D - A as 21 parts (21 × 80) due to an error in scaling C : D.
- **D — ₹1,280:** Results from using 16 parts (A's share) instead of the difference D - A.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Scale ratios using LCM at each junction: 2:3 & 4:5 → 8:12:15; then ×2 and ×5 with 6:7 → 16:24:30:35.

### Q254 · Compounding Ratios

A sum of ₹5,640 was to be divided among A, B and C in the ratio 1/3 : 1/4 : 1/5, but by mistake it was divided in the ratio 3 : 4 : 5. How much did C gain due to this mistake?

A. ₹890
B. ₹950
C. ₹810
D. ₹910

**Answer:** D. ₹910
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹910 (Correct):** Correct ratio = 1/3 : 1/4 : 1/5 = 20 : 15 : 12 (sum = 47). C's intended share = (12 / 47) × 5640 = 12 × 120 = ₹1,440. Mistaken ratio = 3 : 4 : 5 (sum = 12). C's actual share = (5 / 12) × 5640 = 5 × 470 = ₹2,350. Thus, C gained 2350 - 1440 = ₹910.
- **A — ₹890:** Results from an arithmetic error when subtracting 1440 from 2330.
- **B — ₹950:** Results from using 470 × 5 = 2390 instead of 2350.
- **C — ₹810:** Obtained by taking C's intended share as ₹1,540 instead of ₹1,440.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Convert fractional ratios by multiplying by LCM of denominators (60) first: 20:15:12, then compare fractions directly.

### Q255 · Compounding Ratios

Two numbers are in the ratio 5 : 8. If 12 is added to each number, the ratio becomes 3 : 4. What is the sum of the original two numbers?

A. 39
B. 52
C. 26
D. 65

**Answer:** A. 39
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 39 (Correct):** Let the numbers be 5x and 8x. Given (5x + 12) / (8x + 12) = 3 / 4. Cross-multiplying: 4(5x + 12) = 3(8x + 12) → 20x + 48 = 24x + 36 → 4x = 12 → x = 3. Sum of the original numbers = 5x + 8x = 13x = 13 × 3 = 39.
- **B — 52:** Obtained by taking x = 4 instead of 3 (13 × 4).
- **C — 26:** Obtained by finding only the larger number (8 × 3 = 24) or miscomputing 13 × 2.
- **D — 65:** Obtained by finding the sum after adding 12 to each number (39 + 24 = 63, or 13 × 5).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equalize the difference between terms: 8-5 = 3 and 4-3 = 1. Multiply 3:4 by 3 → 9:12. Increase = 9-5 = 4 parts = 12 → 1 part = 3. Sum = 13×3 = 39.

### Q256 · Compounding Ratios

If (3x + 2y) : (3x - 2y) = 5 : 2, then what is the value of (2x + 3y) : (3x + 2y)?

A. 12 : 11
B. 11 : 12
C. 7 : 9
D. 14 : 9

**Answer:** B. 11 : 12
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 11 : 12 (Correct):** By componendo and dividendo: (3x) / (2y) = (5 + 2) / (5 - 2) = 7 / 3, which gives x / y = 14 / 9. Substituting x = 14 and y = 9 into (2x + 3y) : (3x + 2y): (2×14 + 3×9) : (3×14 + 2×9) = (28 + 27) : (42 + 18) = 55 : 60 = 11 : 12.
- **A — 12 : 11:** Inverted ratio (3x + 2y) : (2x + 3y) instead of (2x + 3y) : (3x + 2y).
- **C — 7 : 9:** Value of (3x) : (2y) obtained right after applying componendo-dividendo, stopping too early.
- **D — 14 : 9:** Value of x : y itself, rather than the required expression (2x + 3y) : (3x + 2y).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Apply Componendo-Dividendo immediately: (a+b)/(a-b) = p/q ⟹ a/b = (p+q)/(p-q), then substitute x and y directly.

### Q257 · Compounding Ratios

If the duplicate ratio of (x + 3) : (x + 7) is 4 : 9, then what is the value of x (given x > 0)?

A. 3
B. 7
C. 5
D. 9

**Answer:** C. 5
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 5 (Correct):** The duplicate ratio of a : b is a² : b². Thus, (x + 3)² / (x + 7)² = 4 / 9. Taking the positive square root on both sides (since x > 0): (x + 3) / (x + 7) = 2 / 3. Cross-multiplying: 3x + 9 = 2x + 14 → x = 5.
- **A — 3:** Obtained by taking the sub-duplicate ratio or miscomputing 14 - 9 as 3.
- **B — 7:** Result of solving (x + 3)/(x + 7) = 4/9 without taking the square root.
- **D — 9:** Results from setting 3x - 2x = 14 - 5 instead of 14 - 9.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Duplicate ratio of a:b is a²:b². Take square root of 4:9 → 2:3; difference (3-2=1) equals (7-3=4), so x+3 = 2×4 = 8 ⟹ x = 5.

### Q258 · Compounding Ratios

Three positive numbers are in the ratio 2 : 3 : 5. If the sum of their squares is 342, what is the largest number?

A. 10
B. 25
C. 18
D. 15

**Answer:** D. 15
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 15 (Correct):** Let the numbers be 2k, 3k and 5k. Sum of squares: (2k)² + (3k)² + (5k)² = 4k² + 9k² + 25k² = 38k². Given 38k² = 342 → k² = 9 → k = 3 (positive). Therefore, the largest number = 5k = 5 × 3 = 15.
- **A — 10:** Obtained by dividing 342 by (2+3+5) = 10 or finding the middle/smaller number incorrectly.
- **B — 25:** Results from forgetting to take the square root of k² = 9 (taking k = 5 or 5×5).
- **C — 18:** Represents 6k instead of 5k, or an error in computing 342 / 38.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Sum of squares of ratio parts = 2² + 3² + 5² = 38. Divide 342/38 = 9, take √9 = 3, then multiply by 5.

### Q259 · Compounding Ratios

If a : (b + c) = 1 : 3 and c : (a + b) = 5 : 7, then what is b : (a + c)?

A. 1 : 2
B. 2 : 3
C. 1 : 3
D. 3 : 5

**Answer:** A. 1 : 2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 1 : 2 (Correct):** From a : (b + c) = 1 : 3, the total (a + b + c) = 1 + 3 = 4 parts. From c : (a + b) = 5 : 7, the total (a + b + c) = 5 + 7 = 12 parts. Equalizing the total to 12: multiply the first ratio by 3 → a = 3, b + c = 9. Since c = 5, b = 9 - 5 = 4. Therefore, b : (a + c) = 4 : (3 + 5) = 4 : 8 = 1 : 2.
- **B — 2 : 3:** Obtained by taking b = 4 and a + c = 6 instead of 8.
- **C — 1 : 3:** Obtained by using b = 3 instead of 4 (confusing a with b).
- **D — 3 : 5:** Obtained by failing to equalize (a + b + c) across both given ratios.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Always equalize the total sum (a+b+c) in both ratios using LCM(4, 12) = 12: a=3, c=5, so b = 12 - 8 = 4.

### Q260 · Compounding Ratios

In a school of 720 students, the ratio of boys to girls is 7 : 5. How many more girls should be admitted to make the ratio of boys to girls 1 : 1?

A. 100
B. 120
C. 140
D. 150

**Answer:** B. 120
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 120 (Correct):** Total parts = 7 + 5 = 12. 1 part = 720 / 12 = 60. Number of boys = 7 × 60 = 420, and number of girls = 5 × 60 = 300. To make the ratio 1 : 1, the number of girls must equal 420. Additional girls needed = 420 - 300 = 120.
- **A — 100:** Obtained by taking 1 part = 50 instead of 60 (2 × 50).
- **C — 140:** Results from miscalculating 720 / 12 as 70 (2 × 70).
- **D — 150:** Obtained by taking half of 300 instead of the difference 420 - 300.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Difference in ratio parts = 7 - 5 = 2. Since 12 parts = 720, 2 parts = 720 / 6 = 120 directly.

### Q261 · Mean, Third & Fourth Proportional

What is the mean proportional between 14.4 and 3.6?

A. 6.4
B. 7.8
C. 7.2
D. 9.0

**Answer:** C. 7.2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 7.2 (Correct):** The mean proportional between a and b is √(ab). Here, √(14.4 × 3.6) = √(144 × 36 / 100) = (12 × 6) / 10 = 72 / 10 = 7.2.
- **A — 6.4:** Results from taking √144 = 12 and √36 = 6 but miscalculating 12 × 6 or 8 × 0.8.
- **B — 7.8:** Results from an arithmetic error in √51.84.
- **D — 9.0:** Represents the arithmetic mean (14.4 + 3.6)/2 = 9.0 instead of the geometric (mean) proportional.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Factor out decimals: √(14.4 × 3.6) = √(144 × 36) / 10 = (12 × 6) / 10 = 7.2.

### Q262 · Mean, Third & Fourth Proportional

What is the ratio of the third proportional to 12 and 30, to the mean proportional between 9 and 25?

A. 3 : 1
B. 4 : 1
C. 2 : 1
D. 5 : 1

**Answer:** D. 5 : 1
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 5 : 1 (Correct):** Third proportional to a and b is b² / a = 30² / 12 = 900 / 12 = 75. Mean proportional between c and d is √(cd) = √(9 × 25) = 3 × 5 = 15. Required ratio = 75 : 15 = 5 : 1.
- **A — 3 : 1:** Obtained by taking the third proportional as 45 instead of 75.
- **B — 4 : 1:** Obtained by taking the third proportional as 60 instead of 75.
- **C — 2 : 1:** Obtained by using 30/12 × 12 instead of 30²/12.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Third proportional = b²/a = 900/12 = 75; Mean proportional = √(9×25) = 15. Ratio = 75:15 = 5:1.

### Q263 · Mean, Third & Fourth Proportional

Find the fourth proportional to 0.12, 0.21 and 8.

A. 14
B. 1.4
C. 12
D. 16

**Answer:** A. 14
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 14 (Correct):** If x is the fourth proportional to a, b, c, then a : b = c : x, so x = (b × c) / a. Here, x = (0.21 × 8) / 0.12 = (21 × 8) / 12 = 168 / 12 = 14.
- **B — 1.4:** Decimal placement error when dividing 0.21 × 8 by 0.12.
- **C — 12:** Obtained by misdividing 168 / 12 as 12 or using 0.14.
- **D — 16:** Obtained by computing (0.24 × 8) / 0.12 instead of (0.21 × 8) / 0.12.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Cancel decimals between a and b first: 0.21 / 0.12 = 21 / 12 = 7 / 4. Then (7 / 4) × 8 = 14.

### Q264 · Mean, Third & Fourth Proportional

When x is subtracted from each of 23, 39, 32 and 56, the numbers so obtained in this order are in proportion. What is the mean proportional between (x + 4) and (3x + 1)?

A. 10
B. 12
C. 14
D. 15

**Answer:** B. 12
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 12 (Correct):** Given (23 - x)/(39 - x) = (32 - x)/(56 - x). Using difference of terms: (39 - 23) = 16 and (56 - 32) = 24, whose ratio is 2 : 3. Thus, (23 - x) : (32 - x) = 2 : 3 → 3(23 - x) = 2(32 - x) → 69 - 3x = 64 - 2x → x = 5. Now, x + 4 = 9 and 3x + 1 = 16. Mean proportional = √(9 × 16) = 3 × 4 = 12.
- **A — 10:** Obtained if x is miscalculated as 4, giving √(8 × 13) ≈ 10.
- **C — 14:** Obtained by taking the arithmetic mean (9 + 16)/2 instead of the geometric mean, or miscalculating √144.
- **D — 15:** Obtained by taking x = 6, or using 3 × 5 = 15.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Shortcut for x: x = (ad - bc) / ((a + d) - (b + c)) = (23×56 - 39×32) / (79 - 71) = 40 / 8 = 5. Then √(9×16) = 12.

### Q265 · Mean, Third & Fourth Proportional

If p is the third proportional to 8 and 20, and q is the fourth proportional to 3, 5 and 24, then what is the value of (2p + q)?

A. 120
B. 130
C. 140
D. 150

**Answer:** C. 140
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 140 (Correct):** Third proportional p = 20² / 8 = 400 / 8 = 50. Fourth proportional q = (5 × 24) / 3 = 5 × 8 = 40. Therefore, 2p + q = 2(50) + 40 = 100 + 40 = 140.
- **A — 120:** Results from computing p + 2q = 50 + 80 = 130 or missing a factor in 2p.
- **B — 130:** Results from computing p + 2q instead of 2p + q (50 + 80 = 130).
- **D — 150:** Results from miscomputing p = 55 instead of 50.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Compute p = 400/8 = 50 and q = 5×8 = 40 mentally in 5 seconds, then 2(50) + 40 = 140.

### Q266 · Mean, Third & Fourth Proportional

What is the mean proportional between (15 + √200) and (27 - √648)?

A. 5√3
B. 9√5
C. 4√5
D. 3√5

**Answer:** D. 3√5
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 3√5 (Correct):** Simplify the surds: √200 = 10√2 and √648 = √(324 × 2) = 18√2. Factoring both terms: 15 + 10√2 = 5(3 + 2√2) and 27 - 18√2 = 9(3 - 2√2). Their product = 45(3² - (2√2)²) = 45(9 - 8) = 45. Mean proportional = √45 = 3√5.
- **A — 5√3:** Obtained by writing √45 = √(9 × 5) and swapping the coefficients to 5√3.
- **B — 9√5:** Obtained by forgetting to take the square root of 9 in √45.
- **C — 4√5:** Results from miscalculating (2√2)² as 4 instead of 8, or mis-simplifying √45.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Factor out 5 and 9 to spot the conjugate pair (3 + 2√2)(3 - 2√2) = 9 - 8 = 1, leaving √(5×9) = 3√5.

### Q267 · Mean, Third & Fourth Proportional

What is the third proportional to (x² - y²) and (x - y), where x ≠ ±y?

A. (x - y) / (x + y)
B. (x + y) / (x - y)
C. x + y
D. (x - y)² / (x + y)

**Answer:** A. (x - y) / (x + y)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — (x - y) / (x + y) (Correct):** The third proportional to a and b is b² / a. Here, a = x² - y² = (x - y)(x + y) and b = x - y. Thus, third proportional = (x - y)² / [(x - y)(x + y)] = (x - y) / (x + y).
- **B — (x + y) / (x - y):** Result of inverting the formula to a / b² or swapping numerator and denominator.
- **C — x + y:** Obtained by finding the third proportional to (x - y) and (x² - y²) instead.
- **D — (x - y)² / (x + y):** Result of cancelling (x - y) incorrectly and leaving (x - y)² in the numerator.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Third proportional = b²/a = (x-y)² / [(x-y)(x+y)] = (x-y)/(x+y).

### Q268 · Mean, Third & Fourth Proportional

If (2x + 1), (x + 2), 7 and 5 are in proportion, what is the mean proportional between 4(x - 1) and 2(x + 6)?

A. 16
B. 12
C. 10
D. 14

**Answer:** B. 12
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 12 (Correct):** Since (2x + 1), (x + 2), 7 and 5 are in proportion: (2x + 1) / (x + 2) = 7 / 5. Cross-multiplying: 5(2x + 1) = 7(x + 2) → 10x + 5 = 7x + 14 → 3x = 9 → x = 3. Then 4(x - 1) = 4(2) = 8 and 2(x + 6) = 2(9) = 18. Mean proportional = √(8 × 18) = √144 = 12.
- **A — 16:** Obtained by using x = 4 instead of 3, or miscalculating √144.
- **C — 10:** Results from taking x = 2, giving 4(1) = 4 and 2(8) = 16, or arithmetic error.
- **D — 14:** Represents the arithmetic mean (8 + 18)/2 = 13 rounded or miscomputed.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Solve (2x+1)/(x+2) = 7/5 → x = 3, then √(8 × 18) = √144 = 12.

### Q269 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

The ratio of incomes of A and B is 5 : 7 and the ratio of their expenditures is 3 : 5. If each saves ₹4,000 per month, what is A's monthly income?

A. ₹14,000
B. ₹12,000
C. ₹10,000
D. ₹8,000

**Answer:** C. ₹10,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹10,000 (Correct):** Notice that the difference between income and expenditure parts is equal for both: 5 - 3 = 2 parts for A, and 7 - 5 = 2 parts for B. Since each saves ₹4,000, 2 parts = ₹4,000 → 1 part = ₹2,000. Therefore, A's monthly income = 5 × 2,000 = ₹10,000.
- **A — ₹14,000:** Represents B's monthly income (7 × 2,000) instead of A's.
- **B — ₹12,000:** Represents B's expenditure (5 × 2,000 + 2,000) or taking 1 part = 2,400.
- **D — ₹8,000:** Obtained by taking 4 parts instead of 5 parts (4 × 2,000).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When (I₁ - E₁) = (I₂ - E₂) in ratio units, directly equate that difference (2 units) to savings (₹4,000).

### Q270 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

The incomes of A and B are in the ratio 5 : 3 and their expenditures are in the ratio 9 : 5. If A saves ₹2,600 and B saves ₹1,800, what is the sum of their incomes?

A. ₹11,200
B. ₹14,400
C. ₹9,600
D. ₹12,800

**Answer:** D. ₹12,800
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹12,800 (Correct):** Let incomes be 5x and 3x. Then (5x - 2600) / (3x - 1800) = 9 / 5. Cross-multiplying: 25x - 13000 = 27x - 16200 → 2x = 3200 → x = 1600. Sum of their incomes = 5x + 3x = 8x = 8 × 1600 = ₹12,800.
- **A — ₹11,200:** Obtained by taking x = 1,400 instead of 1,600 (8 × 1,400).
- **B — ₹14,400:** Obtained by taking x = 1,800 instead of 1,600 (8 × 1,800).
- **C — ₹9,600:** Obtained by taking 6x instead of 8x (6 × 1,600).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Cross-multiplication method: |5×5 - 3×9| = 2 units; |5×2600 - 9×1800| = 3200 → 1 unit = 1600. Sum = 8×1600 = ₹12,800.

### Q271 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

One year ago, the ratio of incomes of A and B was 3 : 4. The ratios of their individual incomes last year to this year are 4 : 5 and 2 : 3 respectively. If the sum of their present incomes is ₹41,600, what is A's present income?

A. ₹16,000
B. ₹25,600
C. ₹15,000
D. ₹18,000

**Answer:** A. ₹16,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹16,000 (Correct):** Let last year's incomes of A and B be 3x and 4x. A's present income = 3x × (5/4) = 15x/4. B's present income = 4x × (3/2) = 6x. Ratio of present incomes A : B = (15/4) : 6 = 15 : 24 = 5 : 8. Total parts = 5 + 8 = 13. 1 part = 41,600 / 13 = ₹3,200. A's present income = 5 × 3,200 = ₹16,000.
- **B — ₹25,600:** Represents B's present income (8 × 3,200) instead of A's.
- **C — ₹15,000:** Obtained by using 15 parts out of 39 without simplifying properly or misdividing 41,600.
- **D — ₹18,000:** Results from inverting the individual ratio for A or taking 5 × 3,600.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Multiply last year's ratio (3:4) by (this/last) fractions: A_now : B_now = 3×(5/4) : 4×(3/2) = 5 : 8.

### Q272 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

A spends 75% of his income and B spends 80% of his income. The ratio of their savings is 5 : 6. If the sum of their incomes is ₹75,000, what is B's income?

A. ₹30,000
B. ₹45,000
C. ₹40,000
D. ₹50,000

**Answer:** B. ₹45,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹45,000 (Correct):** A saves 100% - 75% = 25% of his income, and B saves 100% - 80% = 20% of his income. Given (25% of A) / (20% of B) = 5 / 6 → (5/4) × (A/B) = 5/6 → A/B = 2/3. Thus A : B = 2 : 3 (total 5 parts = ₹75,000 → 1 part = ₹15,000). B's income = 3 × 15,000 = ₹45,000.
- **A — ₹30,000:** Represents A's income (2 × 15,000) instead of B's income.
- **C — ₹40,000:** Obtained by taking A : B = 75 : 80 or miscomputing the savings ratio.
- **D — ₹50,000:** Results from using 2/5 instead of 3/5 and miscalculating.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equate savings directly: A×25% : B×20% = 5 : 6 ⟹ A/B = (5/25) : (6/20) = 1/5 : 3/10 = 2 : 3.

### Q273 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

The incomes of A, B and C are in the ratio 3 : 7 : 4 and their expenditures are in the ratio 4 : 3 : 5. If A saves ₹300 out of an income of ₹2,400, what are the savings of C?

A. ₹625
B. ₹525
C. ₹575
D. ₹475

**Answer:** C. ₹575
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹575 (Correct):** A's income = 3x = ₹2,400 → x = 800. Hence C's income = 4x = 4 × 800 = ₹3,200. A's expenditure = 2,400 - 300 = ₹2,100 = 4y → y = 525. Hence C's expenditure = 5y = 5 × 525 = ₹2,625. C's savings = 3,200 - 2,625 = ₹575.
- **A — ₹625:** Obtained by miscalculating 3,200 - 2,625 as 625.
- **B — ₹525:** Represents the value of 1 expenditure part (y = 525) instead of C's savings.
- **D — ₹475:** Obtained by taking C's expenditure as 5 × 545 = 2,725.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Find x = 2400/3 = 800 (C's income = 3200) and y = 2100/4 = 525 (C's exp = 2625); subtract to get ₹575.

### Q274 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

The ratio of incomes of A and B is 3 : 2 and the ratio of their expenditures is 4 : 3. If A saves ₹2,000 and B saves ₹900, what is A's income?

A. ₹4,800
B. ₹6,000
C. ₹8,400
D. ₹7,200

**Answer:** D. ₹7,200
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹7,200 (Correct):** Let incomes be 3x and 2x. Then (3x - 2000) / (2x - 900) = 4 / 3. Cross-multiplying: 9x - 6000 = 8x - 3600 → x = 2400. Therefore, A's income = 3x = 3 × 2400 = ₹7,200.
- **A — ₹4,800:** Represents B's income (2 × 2,400) instead of A's income.
- **B — ₹6,000:** Obtained by taking x = 2,000 instead of 2,400.
- **C — ₹8,400:** Obtained by subtracting 3,600 from 6,000 as 2,800 (3 × 2,800).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Cross-method: |3×3 - 2×4| = 1 unit; |3×2000 - 4×900| = 2400. So 1 unit = 2400, A's income = 3×2400 = ₹7,200.

### Q275 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

The combined income of A and B is ₹1,65,000. A spends 70% of his income and B spends 80% of his income. If A's savings are 25% more than B's savings, what is A's income?

A. ₹75,000
B. ₹90,000
C. ₹80,000
D. ₹65,000

**Answer:** A. ₹75,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹75,000 (Correct):** A saves 30% of A, and B saves 20% of B. Given A's savings = 125% of B's savings = (5/4) of B's savings: 0.30A = (5/4) × 0.20B = 0.25B → 6A = 5B → A : B = 5 : 6. Total parts = 11 = ₹1,65,000 → 1 part = ₹15,000. Thus, A's income = 5 × 15,000 = ₹75,000.
- **B — ₹90,000:** Represents B's income (6 × 15,000) instead of A's income.
- **C — ₹80,000:** Obtained by miscalculating 6A = 5B as A : B = 6 : 5 and rounding.
- **D — ₹65,000:** Results from taking 25% more as +25% on expenditure instead of savings.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 30% of A = (5/4)×20% of B = 25% of B ⟹ 6A = 5B ⟹ A:B = 5:6. 11 parts = 1.65L ⟹ A = ₹75,000.

### Q276 · Income, Expenditure & Savings

The ratio of incomes of A and B is 7 : 5. A and B save ₹4,000 and ₹3,500 respectively. If the expenditure of B is half the expenditure of A, then what is the total income of A and B?

A. ₹10,000
B. ₹12,000
C. ₹14,000
D. ₹15,000

**Answer:** B. ₹12,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹12,000 (Correct):** Let incomes of A and B be 7x and 5x. A's expenditure = 7x - 4000, B's expenditure = 5x - 3500. Given B's expenditure = (1/2) × A's expenditure → 7x - 4000 = 2(5x - 3500) → 7x - 4000 = 10x - 7000 → 3x = 3000 → x = 1000. Total income = 7x + 5x = 12x = ₹12,000.
- **A — ₹10,000:** Obtained by using 5x - 3500 = 2(7x - 4000) (inverting whose expenditure is half).
- **C — ₹14,000:** Obtained by taking x = 1,000 and adding 2,000 or taking 14 parts.
- **D — ₹15,000:** Results from miscomputing 7000 - 4000 as 3,750.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Expenditure ratio A:B = 2:1. Cross-method: |7×1 - 5×2| = 3 units; |1×4000 - 2×3500| = 3000 ⟹ 1 unit = 1000 ⟹ Total = 12×1000 = ₹12,000.

### Q277 · Coin Bag Problems

A bag contains ₹1, 50 paise and 25 paise coins in the ratio 5 : 7 : 12. If the total amount in the bag is ₹230, how many 50 paise coins are there?

A. 100
B. 240
C. 140
D. 160

**Answer:** C. 140
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 140 (Correct):** Let the number of ₹1, 50p and 25p coins be 5x, 7x and 12x. Total value in rupees = 5x(1) + 7x(0.5) + 12x(0.25) = 5x + 3.5x + 3x = 11.5x. Given 11.5x = 230 → x = 230 / 11.5 = 20. Number of 50 paise coins = 7x = 7 × 20 = 140.
- **A — 100:** Represents the number of ₹1 coins (5 × 20) instead of 50 paise coins.
- **B — 240:** Represents the number of 25 paise coins (12 × 20) instead of 50 paise coins.
- **D — 160:** Obtained by miscalculating 230 / 11.5 as 22.8 or taking 8x.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Value of 1 set of (5, 7, 12) coins = 5 + 3.5 + 3 = ₹11.5. Number of sets = 230 / 11.5 = 20 → 50p coins = 7 × 20 = 140.

### Q278 · Coin Bag Problems

A box contains ₹5, ₹2 and ₹1 coins whose values are in the ratio 15 : 4 : 3. If the total number of coins is 160, what is the total amount in the box?

A. ₹400
B. ₹480
C. ₹360
D. ₹440

**Answer:** D. ₹440
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹440 (Correct):** Ratio of number of coins = (15/5) : (4/2) : (3/1) = 3 : 2 : 3. Total coin parts = 3 + 2 + 3 = 8. Since total coins = 160, 1 part = 160 / 8 = 20. Total value of 1 set = 15 + 4 + 3 = ₹22. Therefore, total amount = 22 × 20 = ₹440.
- **A — ₹400:** Obtained by taking 1 part = 18 or forgetting to include ₹2 coins properly.
- **B — ₹480:** Obtained by misdividing 160 by 7 or taking 24 × 20.
- **C — ₹360:** Results from taking total value of 1 set as 18 instead of 22.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Divide value ratio by denomination to get coin-count ratio: (15/5):(4/2):(3/1) = 3:2:3 (8 parts = 160 ⟹ 20 sets × ₹22 = ₹440).

### Q279 · Coin Bag Problems

A bag contains 378 coins consisting of ₹1, 50 paise and 25 paise coins. The ratio of their values is 13 : 11 : 7. How many 50 paise coins are in the bag?

A. 132
B. 168
C. 78
D. 126

**Answer:** A. 132
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 132 (Correct):** Number of coins is proportional to (Value / Denomination): ₹1 : 50p : 25p = (13/1) : (11/0.5) : (7/0.25) = 13 : 22 : 28. Total parts = 13 + 22 + 28 = 63. 1 part = 378 / 63 = 6. Number of 50 paise coins = 22 × 6 = 132.
- **B — 168:** Represents the number of 25 paise coins (28 × 6) instead of 50 paise coins.
- **C — 78:** Represents the number of ₹1 coins (13 × 6) instead of 50 paise coins.
- **D — 126:** Obtained by multiplying 11 × 11.45 or taking 21 × 6.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Convert value ratio to coin ratio by multiplying 50p by 2 and 25p by 4: 13 : 22 : 28 (sum 63 = 378 ⟹ ×6).

### Q280 · Coin Bag Problems

A purse contains ₹1, 50 paise and 10 paise coins in the ratio 3 : 2 : 3. If the total money in the purse is ₹25.80, how many 50 paise coins are there?

A. 18
B. 12
C. 15
D. 24

**Answer:** B. 12
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 12 (Correct):** Value of one set of (3, 2, 3) coins = 3(₹1) + 2(₹0.50) + 3(₹0.10) = 3 + 1 + 0.30 = ₹4.30. Number of sets = 25.80 / 4.30 = 6. Number of 50 paise coins = 2 × 6 = 12.
- **A — 18:** Represents the number of ₹1 or 10 paise coins (3 × 6) instead of 50 paise coins.
- **C — 15:** Obtained by misdividing 25.80 / 4.30 as 7.5.
- **D — 24:** Obtained by doubling 12 (confusing value ₹12 with 24 coins, or taking 4 × 6).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Value per set = 3 + 1 + 0.3 = ₹4.3. 25.8 / 4.3 = 6 sets ⟹ 50p coins = 2 × 6 = 12.

### Q281 · Coin Bag Problems

A bag contains ₹5, ₹2 and ₹1 coins in the ratio 2 : 3 : 5. If the total value of all coins is ₹210, what is the difference between the number of ₹1 coins and ₹5 coins?

A. 20
B. 50
C. 30
D. 40

**Answer:** C. 30
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 30 (Correct):** Value of one set of coins = 2(₹5) + 3(₹2) + 5(₹1) = 10 + 6 + 5 = ₹21. Number of sets = 210 / 21 = 10. Number of ₹1 coins = 5 × 10 = 50, and ₹5 coins = 2 × 10 = 20. Difference = 50 - 20 = 30.
- **A — 20:** Represents the number of ₹5 coins (20) instead of the difference.
- **B — 50:** Represents the number of ₹1 coins (50) instead of the difference.
- **D — 40:** Obtained by taking (5 - 1) × 10 = 40.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 1 set = 10 + 6 + 5 = ₹21 → 210/21 = 10 sets. Difference in parts = 5 - 2 = 3 → 3 × 10 = 30.

### Q282 · Coin Bag Problems

A box contains an equal number of ₹1, 50 paise and 25 paise coins. If the total value of the coins in the box is ₹70, how many coins of each type are there?

A. 35
B. 50
C. 45
D. 40

**Answer:** D. 40
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 40 (Correct):** Let there be x coins of each type. Total value = x(1 + 0.50 + 0.25) = 1.75x = (7/4)x. Given (7/4)x = 70 → x = 70 × (4/7) = 40 coins of each type.
- **A — 35:** Obtained by dividing 70 by 2 instead of 1.75.
- **B — 50:** Obtained by using 1 + 0.4 + 0.0 = 1.4 instead of 1.75.
- **C — 45:** Obtained by miscomputing 70 × 4 / 7.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 1 + 0.5 + 0.25 = ₹7/4 per triplet. Number of triplets = 70 ÷ (7/4) = 40.

### Q283 · Partnership

A starts a business with ₹45,000. After 4 months, B joins him with ₹60,000, and after 2 more months, C joins with ₹90,000. At the end of the year, they earn a profit of ₹13,000. What is C's share in the profit?

A. ₹4,500
B. ₹4,000
C. ₹5,000
D. ₹3,500

**Answer:** A. ₹4,500
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹4,500 (Correct):** Profit is shared in the ratio of (Investment × Time). A invests for 12 months, B for (12 - 4) = 8 months, and C for (12 - 4 - 2) = 6 months. Ratio = (45×12) : (60×8) : (90×6) = 540 : 480 : 540 = 9 : 8 : 9. Total parts = 9 + 8 + 9 = 26. C's share = (9 / 26) × 13,000 = 9 × 500 = ₹4,500.
- **B — ₹4,000:** Represents B's share (8 × 500) instead of C's share.
- **C — ₹5,000:** Obtained by giving C 8 months instead of 6 months.
- **D — ₹3,500:** Obtained by miscalculating 12 - 4 - 2 as 5 months.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Drop trailing 000s: (45×12) : (60×8) : (90×6) = 9 : 8 : 9. 26 parts = 13,000 ⟹ 1 part = 500 ⟹ C = ₹4,500.

### Q284 · Partnership

A and B start a business by investing in the ratio 3 : 5. After 6 months, C joins the business with an amount equal to B's investment. In what ratio should the profit at the end of 1 year be distributed among A, B and C?

A. 3 : 5 : 5
B. 6 : 10 : 5
C. 6 : 5 : 5
D. 3 : 10 : 5

**Answer:** B. 6 : 10 : 5
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 6 : 10 : 5 (Correct):** Let A and B invest 3 and 5 units for 12 months each, and C invests 5 units for 6 months. Ratio of (Investment × Time) = (3 × 12) : (5 × 12) : (5 × 6) = 36 : 60 : 30. Dividing by 6 gives 6 : 10 : 5.
- **A — 3 : 5 : 5:** Ignores the time factor completely and takes only the investment ratio.
- **C — 6 : 5 : 5:** Divides B's share by 2 instead of keeping B's 12-month investment intact.
- **D — 3 : 10 : 5:** Forgets to multiply A's investment by 2 (or 12 months) while multiplying B's.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Take time in 6-month units (A=2, B=2, C=1): (3×2) : (5×2) : (5×1) = 6 : 10 : 5.

### Q285 · Partnership

A, B and C invest ₹20,000, ₹30,000 and ₹40,000 respectively in a business. A is an active partner and receives 10% of the total profit as salary, while the remaining profit is shared in the ratio of their investments. If the total profit is ₹90,000, how much does A receive in total?

A. ₹18,000
B. ₹29,000
C. ₹27,000
D. ₹20,000

**Answer:** C. ₹27,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹27,000 (Correct):** A's salary = 10% of 90,000 = ₹9,000. Remaining profit = 90,000 - 9,000 = ₹81,000. Investment ratio A : B : C = 2 : 3 : 4 (sum = 9). A's share from remaining profit = (2 / 9) × 81,000 = ₹18,000. Total received by A = 9,000 + 18,000 = ₹27,000.
- **A — ₹18,000:** Represents only A's investment share (₹18,000), forgetting to add the ₹9,000 salary.
- **B — ₹29,000:** Obtained by taking (2/9) × 90,000 = 20,000 and adding 9,000 (not deducting salary before sharing).
- **D — ₹20,000:** Represents A's share if no salary was given at all: (2/9) × 90,000 = ₹20,000.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Deduct salary first (90,000 - 9,000 = 81,000), then split 81,000 in 2:3:4 → A gets 9,000 + 18,000 = ₹27,000.

### Q286 · Partnership

In a partnership, A invests 1/4 of the capital for 1/4 of the time, B invests 1/5 of the capital for 1/2 of the time, and C invests the remaining capital for the whole time. Out of a total profit of ₹11,400, what is B's share?

A. ₹1,000
B. ₹8,800
C. ₹2,400
D. ₹1,600

**Answer:** D. ₹1,600
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹1,600 (Correct):** C's capital share = 1 - (1/4 + 1/5) = 1 - 9/20 = 11/20. Profit ratio = (1/4 × 1/4) : (1/5 × 1/2) : (11/20 × 1) = 1/16 : 1/10 : 11/20. Multiplying by LCM(16, 10, 20) = 80 gives 5 : 8 : 44. Total parts = 57. 1 part = 11,400 / 57 = ₹200. B's share = 8 × 200 = ₹1,600.
- **A — ₹1,000:** Represents A's share (5 × 200) instead of B's share.
- **B — ₹8,800:** Represents C's share (44 × 200) instead of B's share.
- **C — ₹2,400:** Obtained by miscalculating C's capital or taking B's share as 12 parts.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Multiply fractions directly: 1/16 : 1/10 : 11/20 → ×80 gives 5 : 8 : 44 (sum 57 = 11,400 ⟹ ×200).

### Q287 · Partnership

A, B and C invested capitals in the ratio 5 : 6 : 8. At the end of the business term, they received profits in the ratio 5 : 3 : 12. What is the ratio of the time periods for which they contributed their capitals?

A. 2 : 1 : 3
B. 1 : 2 : 3
C. 3 : 2 : 4
D. 2 : 3 : 4

**Answer:** A. 2 : 1 : 3
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 2 : 1 : 3 (Correct):** Since Profit = Investment × Time, Time = Profit / Investment. Thus, ratio of time = (5/5) : (3/6) : (12/8) = 1 : 1/2 : 3/2. Multiplying by 2 gives 2 : 1 : 3.
- **B — 1 : 2 : 3:** Obtained by inverting 1/2 to 2 instead of clearing the denominator by multiplying all terms by 2.
- **C — 3 : 2 : 4:** Obtained by taking Investment / Profit instead of Profit / Investment.
- **D — 2 : 3 : 4:** Results from simplifying 12/8 as 2 instead of 3/2.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Time ratio = Profit ratio ÷ Investment ratio = (5/5) : (3/6) : (12/8) = 1 : 0.5 : 1.5 = 2 : 1 : 3.

### Q288 · Partnership

A starts a business with ₹60,000 and withdraws ₹20,000 after 3 months. B invests ₹50,000 from the start and adds ₹10,000 more after 6 months. In what ratio will they share the profit at the end of 1 year?

A. 11 : 9
B. 9 : 11
C. 8 : 11
D. 9 : 10

**Answer:** B. 9 : 11
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 9 : 11 (Correct):** Working in thousands of rupees: A's investment-months = (60 × 3) + (40 × 9) = 180 + 360 = 540. B's investment-months = (50 × 6) + (60 × 6) = 300 + 360 = 660. Profit ratio A : B = 540 : 660 = 9 : 11.
- **A — 11 : 9:** Reversed ratio B : A instead of A : B.
- **C — 8 : 11:** Obtained by treating A's withdrawal as 40 × 8 instead of 40 × 9.
- **D — 9 : 10:** Obtained by taking B's investment as 50 × 12 = 600, forgetting the added ₹10,000.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Compute in thousands: A = 60×3 + 40×9 = 540; B = 50×6 + 60×6 = 660 → 54 : 66 = 9 : 11.

### Q289 · Partnership

A and B invest in a business in the ratio 3 : 2. If 5% of the total profit goes to charity and A's share of the remaining profit is ₹855, what is the total profit?

A. ₹1,425
B. ₹1,600
C. ₹1,500
D. ₹1,400

**Answer:** C. ₹1,500
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹1,500 (Correct):** Remaining profit after 5% charity = 95% of total profit. A's share = (3 / 5) of 95% = 57% of total profit. Given 57% = ₹855 → 1% = 855 / 57 = ₹15. Therefore, total profit = 100 × 15 = ₹1,500.
- **A — ₹1,425:** Represents the remaining profit after charity (₹1,425), not the total profit.
- **B — ₹1,600:** Obtained by miscalculating 1425 / 0.95 or adding 5% of 855.
- **D — ₹1,400:** Obtained by taking (5/3) × 855 and rounding instead of dividing by 0.95.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** A gets (3/5) × 95% = 57% of total profit. 855 / 57 = 15 ⟹ Total profit = 15 × 100 = ₹1,500.

### Q290 · Partnership

A invests ₹16,000 more than B in a business. A invests for 8 months while B invests for 10 months. Out of a total profit of ₹22,000, A receives ₹2,000 more than B. What is B's investment?

A. ₹48,000
B. ₹24,000
C. ₹40,000
D. ₹32,000

**Answer:** D. ₹32,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹32,000 (Correct):** A's profit = (22,000 + 2,000) / 2 = ₹12,000, and B's profit = ₹10,000. Thus, profit ratio A : B = 6 : 5. Let B's investment be ₹x, so A's is ₹(x + 16,000). Then [8(x + 16,000)] / (10x) = 6 / 5 → 4(x + 16,000) / (5x) = 6 / 5 → 4x + 64,000 = 6x → 2x = 64,000 → x = ₹32,000.
- **A — ₹48,000:** Represents A's investment (32,000 + 16,000 = ₹48,000) instead of B's.
- **B — ₹24,000:** Obtained by using profit ratio 5 : 4 instead of 6 : 5.
- **C — ₹40,000:** Obtained by forgetting the time ratio 8 : 10 and equating (x+16000)/x = 6/5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Split ₹22,000 with diff ₹2,000 → ₹12,000 : ₹10,000 = 6 : 5. Time-adjusted: (x+16k)×8 / (x×10) = 6/5 ⟹ x = ₹32,000.

### Q291 · Partnership

A, B and C start a business with ₹40,000, ₹60,000 and ₹80,000 respectively. After 4 months, A withdraws ₹10,000, B adds ₹20,000 and C withdraws ₹20,000. What is the ratio of their profits at the end of 1 year?

A. 5 : 11 : 10
B. 5 : 10 : 11
C. 4 : 11 : 10
D. 5 : 9 : 10

**Answer:** A. 5 : 11 : 10
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 5 : 11 : 10 (Correct):** Working in thousands of rupees: A = (40×4) + (30×8) = 160 + 240 = 400. B = (60×4) + (80×8) = 240 + 640 = 880. C = (80×4) + (60×8) = 320 + 480 = 800. Dividing 400 : 880 : 800 by 80 gives 5 : 11 : 10.
- **B — 5 : 10 : 11:** Swaps B's and C's profit shares.
- **C — 4 : 11 : 10:** Obtained by miscalculating A's share as 320 instead of 400.
- **D — 5 : 9 : 10:** Obtained by using 6 months instead of 8 months for the second period.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use 4-month blocks (1st block = 1 unit, remaining 8 months = 2 units): A = 40+60=100, B = 60+160=220, C = 80+120=200 → 5 : 11 : 10.

### Q292 · Mixture & Alligation

A vessel contains 60 litres of pure milk. 6 litres of milk is drawn out and replaced with water. This process is repeated two more times. How much milk is left in the vessel?

A. 48.60 litres
B. 43.74 litres
C. 42.00 litres
D. 44.20 litres

**Answer:** B. 43.74 litres
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 43.74 litres (Correct):** Using the replacement formula: Remaining milk = C × (1 - x/C)ⁿ, where C = 60, x = 6, and n = 1 + 2 = 3. Remaining milk = 60 × (1 - 6/60)³ = 60 × (9/10)³ = 60 × (729 / 1000) = 43.74 litres.
- **A — 48.60 litres:** Result of repeating the process only 2 times (60 × 0.9²) instead of 3 times.
- **C — 42.00 litres:** Result of simply subtracting 6 × 3 = 18 litres without accounting for dilution.
- **D — 44.20 litres:** Result of miscalculating 60 × 0.729.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Formula: Final = Initial × (1 - x/C)ⁿ. With n=3 and 10% removed each time, multiply 60 by 0.729 = 43.74 L.

### Q293 · Mixture & Alligation

A 80-litre mixture contains milk and water in the ratio 3 : 1. How much water must be added to this mixture to make the ratio of milk to water 1 : 2?

A. 80 litres
B. 120 litres
C. 100 litres
D. 60 litres

**Answer:** C. 100 litres
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 100 litres (Correct):** In 80 litres, milk = (3/4) × 80 = 60 L and water = 20 L. Only water is added, so milk remains 60 L. For the new ratio of milk to water to be 1 : 2, water must be 2 × 60 = 120 L. Water to be added = 120 - 20 = 100 litres.
- **A — 80 litres:** Obtained by equating 20 + x = 100 incorrectly as x = 80.
- **B — 120 litres:** Represents the final quantity of water (120 L) instead of the added quantity.
- **D — 60 litres:** Obtained by making the ratio 2 : 3 instead of 1 : 2, or taking 60 L.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Milk is constant: 3:1 → 1:2 becomes 3:6 (multiply by 3). Water increases from 1 to 6 parts (+5 parts). 4 parts = 80 L ⟹ 5 parts = 100 L.

### Q294 · Mixture & Alligation

Two vessels A and B contain mixtures of milk and water in the ratios 4 : 3 and 2 : 3 respectively. In what ratio should the mixtures from A and B be mixed to obtain a new mixture containing half milk and half water?

A. 5 : 7
B. 4 : 5
C. 3 : 5
D. 7 : 5

**Answer:** D. 7 : 5
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 7 : 5 (Correct):** Fraction of milk in A = 4/7, in B = 2/5, and in the target mixture = 1/2. By alligation: (1/2 - 2/5) : (4/7 - 1/2) = (5 - 4)/10 : (8 - 7)/14 = 1/10 : 1/14 = 14 : 10 = 7 : 5.
- **A — 5 : 7:** Reversed ratio B : A instead of A : B.
- **B — 4 : 5:** Obtained by comparing 4:3 and 2:3 without converting to fractions of total mixture.
- **C — 3 : 5:** Obtained by miscalculating 4/7 - 1/2 as 1/6.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use milk fractions (4/7, 2/5, 1/2) in alligation: (1/2 - 2/5) : (4/7 - 1/2) = 1/10 : 1/14 = 7 : 5.

### Q295 · Mixture & Alligation

In what ratio must a grocer mix two varieties of rice costing ₹42 per kg and ₹54 per kg so that the resulting mixture is worth ₹46 per kg?

A. 2 : 1
B. 1 : 2
C. 3 : 2
D. 4 : 3

**Answer:** A. 2 : 1
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 2 : 1 (Correct):** By the rule of alligation: Quantity of cheaper (₹42) : Quantity of dearer (₹54) = (54 - 46) : (46 - 42) = 8 : 4 = 2 : 1.
- **B — 1 : 2:** Reversed ratio (46 - 42) : (54 - 46) = 1 : 2.
- **C — 3 : 2:** Obtained by using 48 as the mean price instead of 46.
- **D — 4 : 3:** Obtained by taking the ratio of prices 42 : 54 directly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Alligation cross: (Dearer - Mean) : (Mean - Cheaper) = (54 - 46) : (46 - 42) = 8 : 4 = 2 : 1.

### Q296 · Mixture & Alligation

Two vessels contain milk and water in the ratios 3 : 2 and 7 : 3 respectively. If the mixtures from the two vessels are mixed in the ratio 2 : 3, what is the ratio of milk to water in the new mixture?

A. 31 : 19
B. 33 : 17
C. 17 : 33
D. 27 : 23

**Answer:** B. 33 : 17
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 33 : 17 (Correct):** Total parts in Vessel 1 = 3 + 2 = 5, and in Vessel 2 = 7 + 3 = 10. Equalize both to 10 parts: Vessel 1 becomes 6 : 4, Vessel 2 is 7 : 3. Now mix in the ratio 2 : 3: Milk = 2(6) + 3(7) = 12 + 21 = 33; Water = 2(4) + 3(3) = 8 + 9 = 17. New ratio = 33 : 17.
- **A — 31 : 19:** Obtained by mixing in ratio 1 : 1 or miscalculating 2(6) + 3(7).
- **C — 17 : 33:** Reversed ratio of water to milk.
- **D — 27 : 23:** Obtained by multiplying directly by 2 and 3 without equalizing the base totals (5 and 10) first.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equalize vessel totals to 10 first (6:4 and 7:3), then weight by 2 and 3: Milk = 12+21 = 33, Water = 8+9 = 17.

### Q297 · Mixture & Alligation

A container contains 50 litres of pure milk. 5 litres of milk is taken out and replaced with water. Then 10 litres of the mixture is taken out and replaced with water. How much milk is left in the container?

A. 35 litres
B. 38 litres
C. 36 litres
D. 34 litres

**Answer:** C. 36 litres
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 36 litres (Correct):** Remaining milk = 50 × (1 - 5/50) × (1 - 10/50) = 50 × (9/10) × (4/5) = 50 × 0.9 × 0.8 = 36 litres.
- **A — 35 litres:** Result of subtracting 5 + 10 = 15 litres directly from 50 litres.
- **B — 38 litres:** Obtained by miscalculating 50 × 0.9 × 0.85.
- **D — 34 litres:** Obtained by applying 10% removal twice instead of 10% then 20%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Multiply by remaining fractions sequentially: 50 × (45/50) × (40/50) = 50 × 0.9 × 0.8 = 36 L.

### Q298 · Mixture & Alligation

A milkman claims to sell milk at its cost price, but he mixes water with it and thereby gains 25%. What is the ratio of water to milk in the mixture?

A. 1 : 5
B. 4 : 1
C. 1 : 3
D. 1 : 4

**Answer:** D. 1 : 4
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 1 : 4 (Correct):** Since water is free and milk is sold at cost price, the 25% profit comes entirely from the added water. Let milk = 100 units (CP = ₹100). To gain 25%, total mixture sold = 125 units, so water = 25 units. Ratio of water to milk = 25 : 100 = 1 : 4.
- **A — 1 : 5:** Represents the ratio of water to total mixture (25 : 125) instead of water to milk.
- **B — 4 : 1:** Represents the ratio of milk to water instead of water to milk.
- **C — 1 : 3:** Obtained by taking 25% as 1/3 instead of 1/4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Profit % = (Water / Milk) × 100. So Water : Milk = 25 : 100 = 1 : 4.

### Q299 · Mixture & Alligation

In 60 kg of an alloy, zinc and copper are in the ratio 2 : 3. How much copper must be melted with it so that the ratio of zinc to copper becomes 1 : 2?

A. 12 kg
B. 15 kg
C. 18 kg
D. 10 kg

**Answer:** A. 12 kg
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 12 kg (Correct):** In 60 kg alloy, zinc = (2/5) × 60 = 24 kg and copper = 36 kg. Since only copper is added, zinc remains 24 kg. For the new ratio to be 1 : 2, copper must be 2 × 24 = 48 kg. Copper to be added = 48 - 36 = 12 kg.
- **B — 15 kg:** Obtained by miscalculating 60/5 as 15 and multiplying by 1.
- **C — 18 kg:** Obtained by taking 48 - 30 = 18 kg.
- **D — 10 kg:** Obtained by taking 1 part = 10 kg instead of 12 kg.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Zinc is unchanged: 2:3 → 1:2 becomes 2:4. Copper increases by 1 part. Since 5 parts = 60 kg, 1 part = 12 kg.

### Q300 · Mixture & Alligation

A vessel is full of a mixture of spirit and water in which 20% is water. 10 litres of the mixture is drawn off and replaced with pure water. If the percentage of water now becomes 36%, what is the capacity of the vessel?

A. 40 litres
B. 50 litres
C. 60 litres
D. 45 litres

**Answer:** B. 50 litres
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 50 litres (Correct):** Track the unchanged component (spirit): initially spirit = 80%, finally spirit = 100% - 36% = 64%. Using replacement on spirit: 64 = 80 × (1 - 10/C) → 1 - 10/C = 64/80 = 4/5 → 10/C = 1/5 → C = 50 litres.
- **A — 40 litres:** Obtained by using 20/36 instead of tracking the spirit percentage (80 → 64).
- **C — 60 litres:** Obtained by miscalculating 10 / (1/6) = 60 litres.
- **D — 45 litres:** Obtained by taking 10 × 4.5 = 45 litres.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Track the pure component (spirit): 80% → 64% (a 1/5th drop). Since 10 L caused a 1/5th drop, capacity = 10 × 5 = 50 L.

### Q301 · Mixture & Alligation

A trader has 100 kg of sugar, part of which he sells at 8% profit and the rest at 18% profit. He gains 14% on the whole. What quantity (in kg) was sold at 18% profit?

A. 40 kg
B. 50 kg
C. 60 kg
D. 70 kg

**Answer:** C. 60 kg
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 60 kg (Correct):** By alligation: Quantity at 8% : Quantity at 18% = (18 - 14) : (14 - 8) = 4 : 6 = 2 : 3. Quantity sold at 18% profit = (3 / 5) × 100 = 60 kg.
- **A — 40 kg:** Represents the quantity sold at 8% profit (40 kg) instead of 18% profit.
- **B — 50 kg:** Obtained by taking the simple average (50 kg) instead of weighted alligation.
- **D — 70 kg:** Obtained by miscalculating 18 - 14 as 3 instead of 4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Alligation: (18-14) : (14-8) = 4 : 6 = 2 : 3. Quantity at 18% = 3/5 of 100 = 60 kg.

### Q302 · Mixture & Alligation

In a 45-litre mixture, the ratio of milk to water is 4 : 1. How much water must be added to make the ratio of milk to water 3 : 2?

A. 12 litres
B. 18 litres
C. 24 litres
D. 15 litres

**Answer:** D. 15 litres
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 15 litres (Correct):** Milk = (4/5) × 45 = 36 L, Water = 9 L. Since milk is unchanged, 3 parts of the new ratio = 36 L → 1 part = 12 L. New water quantity = 2 × 12 = 24 L. Water added = 24 - 9 = 15 litres.
- **A — 12 litres:** Represents the value of 1 part (12 L) instead of the added water.
- **B — 18 litres:** Obtained by taking 36/2 = 18 L.
- **C — 24 litres:** Represents the final quantity of water (24 L) instead of the added water.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equalize milk: 4:1 (×3 → 12:3) and 3:2 (×4 → 12:8). Total 15 parts = 45 L (×3); water added = 8-3 = 5 parts = 15 L.

### Q303 · Mixture & Alligation

Three vessels of equal capacity contain mixtures of alcohol and water in the ratios 2 : 1, 3 : 2 and 5 : 3 respectively. If all three are emptied into a single large vessel, what is the ratio of alcohol to water in the resulting mixture?

A. 227 : 133
B. 133 : 227
C. 217 : 143
D. 113 : 67

**Answer:** A. 227 : 133
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 227 : 133 (Correct):** Totals of the ratios are 3, 5 and 8; LCM(3, 5, 8) = 120. Scale each to 120: Vessel 1 = 80 : 40, Vessel 2 = 72 : 48, Vessel 3 = 75 : 45. Total alcohol = 80 + 72 + 75 = 227; total water = 40 + 48 + 45 = 133. Ratio = 227 : 133.
- **B — 133 : 227:** Reversed ratio of water to alcohol.
- **C — 217 : 143:** Obtained by scaling Vessel 3 as 65 : 55 instead of 75 : 45.
- **D — 113 : 67:** Obtained by taking 10 : 6 instead of scaling to LCM 120.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Scale all 3 vessels to LCM(3,5,8) = 120: (80:40) + (72:48) + (75:45) = 227 : 133.

### Q304 · Ages Problems

The present ages of A and B are in the ratio 5 : 4. Three years hence, the ratio of their ages will become 11 : 9. What is the present age of B?

A. 30 years
B. 24 years
C. 27 years
D. 21 years

**Answer:** B. 24 years
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 24 years (Correct):** Equalize the age difference: 5 - 4 = 1 and 11 - 9 = 2. Multiply 5 : 4 by 2 to get 10 : 8. Now 10 : 8 becomes 11 : 9 in 3 years, so 1 part = 3 years. B's present age = 8 × 3 = 24 years.
- **A — 30 years:** Represents A's present age (10 × 3 = 30 years) instead of B's.
- **C — 27 years:** Represents B's age 3 years hence (9 × 3 = 27 years) instead of present age.
- **D — 21 years:** Obtained by subtracting 3 from 24 instead of stopping at 24.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equalize difference: 5:4 → 10:8 vs 11:9. 1 part = 3 years ⟹ B = 8 × 3 = 24 years.

### Q305 · Ages Problems

Six years ago, the ratio of the ages of Kunal and Sagar was 6 : 5. Four years hence, the ratio of their ages will be 11 : 10. What is Sagar's present age?

A. 18 years
B. 12 years
C. 16 years
D. 20 years

**Answer:** C. 16 years
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 16 years (Correct):** The difference between ratio parts is 1 in both (6 - 5 = 1 and 11 - 10 = 1). Over 6 + 4 = 10 years, each ratio increases by 5 parts (11 - 6 = 5). Thus, 5 parts = 10 years → 1 part = 2 years. Six years ago, Sagar was 5 × 2 = 10 years old. Sagar's present age = 10 + 6 = 16 years.
- **A — 18 years:** Represents Kunal's present age (6 × 2 + 6 = 18 years) instead of Sagar's.
- **B — 12 years:** Obtained by adding only 2 years or miscomputing 10 + 6.
- **D — 20 years:** Represents Sagar's age 4 years hence (10 × 2 = 20 years) instead of present age.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Diff is 1 in both. Time gap = 6 + 4 = 10 yrs = 5 parts ⟹ 1 part = 2 yrs. Sagar = 5×2 + 6 = 16 yrs.

### Q306 · Ages Problems

A father's present age is 3 times that of his son. Five years ago, the father was 4 times as old as his son. What is the sum of their present ages?

A. 45 years
B. 50 years
C. 55 years
D. 60 years

**Answer:** D. 60 years
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 60 years (Correct):** Let son's present age be x, so father's is 3x. Five years ago: 3x - 5 = 4(x - 5) → 3x - 5 = 4x - 20 → x = 15. Father's present age = 45. Sum of present ages = 45 + 15 = 60 years.
- **A — 45 years:** Represents only the father's present age (45 years) instead of the sum.
- **B — 50 years:** Obtained by adding 5 years to 45 instead of 15.
- **C — 55 years:** Obtained by taking x = 14 or miscomputing 45 + 15.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equalize diff: 3:1 (×3 → 9:3) and 4:1 (×2 → 8:2). 1 part = 5 yrs ⟹ Sum = 12 × 5 = 60 yrs.

### Q307 · Ages Problems

The ratio of the present ages of a mother and her daughter is 7 : 1. Four years hence, the ratio of their ages will be 4 : 1. What will be the daughter's age 4 years hence?

A. 8 years
B. 4 years
C. 12 years
D. 10 years

**Answer:** A. 8 years
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 8 years (Correct):** Present ratio = 7 : 1 (difference = 6). Four years hence ratio = 4 : 1 (difference = 3). Multiply 4 : 1 by 2 to equalize the difference → 8 : 2. Increase = 1 part = 4 years. Thus, daughter's age 4 years hence = 2 × 4 = 8 years.
- **B — 4 years:** Represents the daughter's present age (4 years) instead of her age 4 years hence.
- **C — 12 years:** Obtained by taking 3 × 4 = 12 years.
- **D — 10 years:** Obtained by miscalculating 7x + 4 = 4(x + 4).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equalize difference: 7:1 vs 8:2. 1 part = 4 yrs ⟹ Daughter 4 yrs hence = 2 × 4 = 8 yrs.

### Q308 · Ages Problems

Three years ago, the average age of a husband, wife and their child was 27 years. Five years ago, the average age of the wife and the child was 20 years. What is the present age of the husband?

A. 35 years
B. 40 years
C. 45 years
D. 38 years

**Answer:** B. 40 years
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 40 years (Correct):** Sum of present ages of husband, wife and child = (27 × 3) + (3 × 3) = 81 + 9 = 90 years. Sum of present ages of wife and child = (20 × 2) + (5 × 2) = 40 + 10 = 50 years. Husband's present age = 90 - 50 = 40 years.
- **A — 35 years:** Obtained by adding only 3 and 5 once instead of multiplying by the number of people.
- **C — 45 years:** Obtained by forgetting to add 5 × 2 = 10 to the wife and child's sum.
- **D — 38 years:** Obtained by miscalculating 81 + 9 as 88.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Present avg(H,W,C) = 27+3 = 30 (sum 90); Present avg(W,C) = 20+5 = 25 (sum 50). Husband = 90 - 50 = 40 yrs.

### Q309 · Ages Problems

The ratio of the present ages of A and B is 3 : 5, and the sum of their present ages is 80 years. What will be the ratio of their ages after 10 years?

A. 3 : 4
B. 4 : 5
C. 2 : 3
D. 5 : 7

**Answer:** C. 2 : 3
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 2 : 3 (Correct):** Total parts = 3 + 5 = 8 = 80 years → 1 part = 10 years. Thus, present ages are A = 30 years and B = 50 years. After 10 years, their ages will be 40 and 60 years, whose ratio is 40 : 60 = 2 : 3.
- **A — 3 : 4:** Obtained by adding 10 to 3 and 5 directly or mis-simplifying 40 : 60.
- **B — 4 : 5:** Obtained by adding 1 part to both (4 : 6) and then mis-simplifying.
- **D — 5 : 7:** Obtained by taking ages after 20 years instead of 10 years.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 1 part = 80/8 = 10 yrs. Adding 10 yrs adds 1 part to each: (3+1) : (5+1) = 4 : 6 = 2 : 3.

### Q310 · Ages Problems

The product of the present ages of A and B is 240. If twice B's age exceeds A's age by 4 years, what is B's present age?

A. 20 years
B. 10 years
C. 15 years
D. 12 years

**Answer:** D. 12 years
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 12 years (Correct):** Given A × B = 240 and 2B - A = 4 → A = 2B - 4. Substituting: B(2B - 4) = 240 → B(B - 2) = 120. Since 12 × 10 = 120, B = 12 years (and A = 20 years).
- **A — 20 years:** Represents A's present age (20 years) instead of B's.
- **B — 10 years:** Represents (B - 2) = 10 instead of B = 12.
- **C — 15 years:** Obtained by taking 240 / 16 = 15.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** From B(B - 2) = 120, factor 120 into two numbers differing by 2: 12 × 10 ⟹ B = 12 years.

### Q311 · Ages Problems

The average age of a family of 5 members is 24 years. If the present age of the youngest member is 8 years, what was the average age of the family (excluding the newborn) just before the birth of the youngest member?

A. 20 years
B. 16 years
C. 18 years
D. 22 years

**Answer:** A. 20 years
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 20 years (Correct):** Present sum of ages of 5 members = 5 × 24 = 120 years. Eight years ago (at the birth of the youngest member), the sum of ages of the other 4 members = 120 - (5 × 8) = 120 - 40 = 80 years. Average age of those 4 members = 80 / 4 = 20 years.
- **B — 16 years:** Result of dividing 80 by 5 instead of 4 members.
- **C — 18 years:** Obtained by subtracting 8 from 24 directly (24 - 8 = 16) and adding 2.
- **D — 22 years:** Obtained by subtracting 8 from 120 once (112 / 5 ≈ 22).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 8 years ago, sum = 5×(24 - 8) = 80. Divide by 4 existing members: 80 / 4 = 20 years.

### Q312 · Averages

A batsman had a certain average of runs for 11 innings. In the 12th innings, he scored 90 runs and thereby decreased his average by 5 runs. What is his average after the 12th innings?

A. 150
B. 145
C. 140
D. 155

**Answer:** B. 145
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 145 (Correct):** Let his new average after 12 innings be x. Then his previous average for 11 innings was (x + 5). Total runs: 11(x + 5) + 90 = 12x → 11x + 55 + 90 = 12x → x = 145.
- **A — 150:** Represents his old average before the 12th innings (145 + 5 = 150).
- **C — 140:** Obtained by subtracting 5 from 145 instead of recognizing x is already the new average.
- **D — 155:** Obtained by adding 5 to 150 instead of subtracting.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** New average = Score + (Old innings × Decrease) = 90 + (11 × 5) = 145.

### Q313 · Averages

A cricketer had a bowling average of 12.4 runs per wicket. In his next match, he took 5 wickets for 26 runs, thereby improving (decreasing) his average by 0.4. How many wickets had he taken before the last match?

A. 90
B. 80
C. 85
D. 75

**Answer:** C. 85
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 85 (Correct):** Let wickets taken before the last match be w. New average = 12.4 - 0.4 = 12.0. Total runs: 12.4w + 26 = 12(w + 5) → 12.4w + 26 = 12w + 60 → 0.4w = 34 → w = 85.
- **A — 90:** Represents total wickets after the last match (85 + 5 = 90) instead of before.
- **B — 80:** Obtained by misdividing 32 / 0.4 instead of 34 / 0.4.
- **D — 75:** Obtained by subtracting 5 twice or miscomputing 12×5 - 26.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Alligation: Last match avg = 26/5 = 5.2. (12.0 - 5.2) : (12.4 - 12.0) = 6.8 : 0.4 = 17 : 1. 1 part = 5 wickets ⟹ 17 parts = 85 wickets.

### Q314 · Averages

The average weight of 8 persons increases by 2.5 kg when a new person comes in place of one of them weighing 65 kg. What is the weight of the new person?

A. 75 kg
B. 80 kg
C. 90 kg
D. 85 kg

**Answer:** D. 85 kg
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 85 kg (Correct):** Total increase in weight for 8 persons = 8 × 2.5 = 20 kg. Therefore, the weight of the new person = 65 + 20 = 85 kg.
- **A — 75 kg:** Obtained by adding 10 kg instead of 20 kg (miscomputing 8 × 2.5).
- **B — 80 kg:** Obtained by subtracting 2.5 or using 6 persons instead of 8.
- **C — 90 kg:** Obtained by taking 9 × 2.5 = 22.5 and rounding.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** New weight = Replaced weight + (Number of persons × Increase in avg) = 65 + (8 × 2.5) = 85 kg.

### Q315 · Averages

The average marks of 40 students in an exam were calculated as 68. Later, it was found that the marks of two students were wrongly entered as 48 and 64 instead of 84 and 46 respectively. What is the correct average?

A. 68.45
B. 67.55
C. 68.90
D. 68.15

**Answer:** A. 68.45
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 68.45 (Correct):** Sum of correct marks = 84 + 46 = 130; sum of wrong marks = 48 + 64 = 112. Net difference = 130 - 112 = +18. Increase in average = 18 / 40 = +0.45. Correct average = 68 + 0.45 = 68.45.
- **B — 67.55:** Obtained by subtracting 0.45 instead of adding it.
- **C — 68.90:** Obtained by forgetting to divide 18 by 40 properly (taking 18/20 = 0.90).
- **D — 68.15:** Obtained by taking the difference as 6 instead of 18.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Net change = (Correct - Wrong) / N = (130 - 112) / 40 = +0.45 ⟹ 68 + 0.45 = 68.45.

### Q316 · Averages

The average of 11 numbers is 50. If the average of the first 6 numbers is 49 and that of the last 6 numbers is 52, what is the 6th number?

A. 50
B. 56
C. 54
D. 52

**Answer:** B. 56
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 56 (Correct):** Sum of all 11 numbers = 11 × 50 = 550. Sum of first 6 = 6 × 49 = 294, and sum of last 6 = 6 × 52 = 312. Since the 6th number is counted twice: 6th number = (294 + 312) - 550 = 606 - 550 = 56.
- **A — 50:** Obtained by taking the overall average as the middle number.
- **C — 54:** Obtained by miscalculating 6 × 49 + 6 × 52.
- **D — 52:** Obtained by taking the average of the last 6 numbers directly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Deviation method: First 6 deviate by -1 each (-6); Last 6 deviate by +2 each (+12). Net = +6 ⟹ 6th number = 50 + 6 = 56.

### Q317 · Averages

The average of 7 consecutive odd numbers is 43. What is the product of the smallest and the largest of these numbers?

A. 1,763
B. 1,849
C. 1,813
D. 1,797

**Answer:** C. 1,813
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 1,813 (Correct):** For an odd count of consecutive odd numbers, the average is the middle (4th) number, which is 43. The 7 numbers are 37, 39, 41, 43, 45, 47, 49. Product of smallest and largest = 37 × 49 = (43 - 6)(43 + 6) = 43² - 36 = 1849 - 36 = 1,813.
- **A — 1,763:** Obtained by taking 37 × 47 or miscalculating 43² - 86.
- **B — 1,849:** Represents 43² = 1,849 without subtracting 6² = 36.
- **D — 1,797:** Obtained by miscalculating 1849 - 36.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Middle number = 43. Smallest & largest are 43 ± 6. Product = 43² - 6² = 1849 - 36 = 1,813.

### Q318 · Averages

The average weight of 24 students in a class is 45 kg. When the weight of the teacher is included, the average weight increases by 1.5 kg. What is the weight of the teacher?

A. 81.0 kg
B. 84.0 kg
C. 79.5 kg
D. 82.5 kg

**Answer:** D. 82.5 kg
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 82.5 kg (Correct):** New average = 45 + 1.5 = 46.5 kg. Total number of people = 24 + 1 = 25. Teacher's weight = Old average + (New count × Increase) = 45 + (25 × 1.5) = 45 + 37.5 = 82.5 kg.
- **A — 81.0 kg:** Result of multiplying 24 × 1.5 = 36 instead of 25 × 1.5 = 37.5.
- **B — 84.0 kg:** Obtained by miscalculating 25 × 1.5 as 39.
- **C — 79.5 kg:** Obtained by taking 23 × 1.5 instead of 25 × 1.5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Included value = Old avg + (New count × Increase) = 45 + (25 × 1.5) = 82.5 kg.

### Q319 · Averages

The average temperature from Monday to Wednesday was 37°C, and from Tuesday to Thursday was 34°C. If the temperature on Thursday was 4/5 of that on Monday, what was the temperature on Thursday?

A. 36°C
B. 45°C
C. 32°C
D. 40°C

**Answer:** A. 36°C
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 36°C (Correct):** M + Tu + W = 3 × 37 = 111°C, and Tu + W + Th = 3 × 34 = 102°C. Subtracting: M - Th = 111 - 102 = 9°C. Given Th = (4/5)M, so M - (4/5)M = (1/5)M = 9 → M = 45°C. Therefore, Thursday's temperature = (4/5) × 45 = 36°C.
- **B — 45°C:** Represents Monday's temperature (45°C) instead of Thursday's.
- **C — 32°C:** Obtained by miscalculating M - Th as 8°C.
- **D — 40°C:** Obtained by taking 4/5 of 50°C.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** M - Th = 3×(37 - 34) = 9°C. Since Th : M = 4 : 5 (diff 1 part = 9°C), Th = 4 × 9 = 36°C.

### Q320 · Averages

In a class of 60 students, 40% are girls. The average weight of the boys is 62 kg and that of the girls is 55 kg. What is the average weight of the whole class?

A. 58.5 kg
B. 59.2 kg
C. 58.8 kg
D. 59.6 kg

**Answer:** B. 59.2 kg
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 59.2 kg (Correct):** Boys = 60%, Girls = 40%, so Boys : Girls = 3 : 2. Weighted average = (3 × 62 + 2 × 55) / (3 + 2) = (186 + 110) / 5 = 296 / 5 = 59.2 kg.
- **A — 58.5 kg:** Represents the simple average (62 + 55)/2 = 58.5 kg without weighting by 3 : 2.
- **C — 58.8 kg:** Obtained by reversing the weights to 2 : 3 (girls : boys) → 57.8 or miscomputing.
- **D — 59.6 kg:** Obtained by miscalculating 296 / 5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use ratio 3:2 with base 55: Avg = 55 + (3×7)/5 = 55 + 4.2 = 59.2 kg.
