## Chapter 6: Simple & Compound Interest

### Q321 · Simple Interest Basics & Rate Changes

Find the simple interest on ₹18,000 at 7.5% per annum for 2 years 4 months.

A. ₹2,700
B. ₹3,150
C. ₹3,240
D. ₹3,600

**Answer:** B. ₹3,150
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹3,150 (Correct):** Time = 2 years 4 months = 2 + 4/12 = 2 + 1/3 = 7/3 years. SI = (P × R × T) / 100 = (18,000 × 7.5 × 7) / (100 × 3) = 60 × 52.5 = ₹3,150.
- **A — ₹2,700:** Value obtained if time is taken as 2 years instead of 2 years 4 months.
- **C — ₹3,240:** Result of mistakenly converting 2 years 4 months to 2.4 years instead of 2.33 years.
- **D — ₹3,600:** Value obtained if rate is taken as 8.5% or time is rounded up to 2 years 8 months.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Convert months to years by dividing by 12 (4 months = 1/3 yr), then cancel 3 with 18,000/100 = 180 to get 60 × 7.5 × 7 mentally.

### Q322 · Simple Interest Basics & Rate Changes

The simple interest on a certain sum for 4 years at 6% per annum exceeds the simple interest on the same sum for 3 years at 5% per annum by ₹2,250. Find the sum.

A. ₹25,000
B. ₹22,500
C. ₹27,500
D. ₹30,000

**Answer:** A. ₹25,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹25,000 (Correct):** Effective SI% for 4 years at 6% = 24%. Effective SI% for 3 years at 5% = 15%. Difference = 24% - 15% = 9% of Principal. Given 9% of P = ₹2,250, so P = (2,250 × 100) / 9 = ₹25,000.
- **B — ₹22,500:** Result of dividing 2,250 by 10% instead of the actual 9% difference.
- **C — ₹27,500:** Value obtained by using 8% as the effective percentage difference.
- **D — ₹30,000:** Result of using 7.5% as the difference instead of 9%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Treat SI as R×T%. Difference = (6×4 - 5×3)% = 9% = ₹2,250 → 1% = ₹250 → 100% = ₹25,000.

### Q323 · Simple Interest Basics & Rate Changes

A certain sum of money amounts to ₹10,160 in 3 years and ₹11,600 in 5 years at simple interest. Find the rate of interest per annum.

A. 8%
B. 10%
C. 9%
D. 7.5%

**Answer:** C. 9%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 9% (Correct):** SI for (5 - 3) = 2 years = ₹11,600 - ₹10,160 = ₹1,440. Therefore, SI for 1 year = ₹720. SI for 3 years = 3 × 720 = ₹2,160. Principal P = ₹10,160 - ₹2,160 = ₹8,000. Rate R = (720 / 8,000) × 100 = 9% p.a.
- **A — 8%:** Result of dividing 1-year SI by ₹9,000 instead of the true principal ₹8,000.
- **B — 10%:** Value obtained if 1-year SI is divided by ₹7,200 (subtracting 4 years' SI).
- **D — 7.5%:** Result of dividing 2-year SI directly by ₹10,160 and halving roughly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 1-year SI = (A₂ - A₁)/(T₂ - T₁) = 720. Principal = A₁ - 3×720 = 8,000. Rate = 720/80 = 9%.

### Q324 · Simple Interest Basics & Rate Changes

₹14,500 amounts to ₹19,140 in 4 years at a certain rate of simple interest. If the rate of interest is increased by 3.5%, what will ₹14,500 amount to in the same time?

A. ₹20,590
B. ₹21,170
C. ₹21,460
D. ₹20,155

**Answer:** B. ₹21,170
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹21,170 (Correct):** Increase in rate = 3.5% p.a. for 4 years = 3.5 × 4 = 14% extra interest. Extra SI = 14% of ₹14,500 = 0.14 × 14,500 = ₹2,030. New Amount = ₹19,140 + ₹2,030 = ₹21,170.
- **A — ₹20,590:** Result of adding 10% extra interest (₹1,450) instead of 14%.
- **C — ₹21,460:** Value obtained by taking 16% extra interest instead of 14%.
- **D — ₹20,155:** Result of adding only 2 years' extra interest (7% = ₹1,015) instead of 4 years'.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Don't find the old rate! Just add Extra SI = P × ΔR × T / 100 = 145 × 14 = ₹2,030 to ₹19,140.

### Q325 · Simple Interest Basics & Rate Changes

The simple interest on a sum of money is 9/25 of the principal, and the number of years is equal to the rate percent per annum. Find the rate percent.

A. 5%
B. 7.5%
C. 8%
D. 6%

**Answer:** D. 6%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 6% (Correct):** Let Rate = R% and Time = R years. Given SI = (9/25)P. Using SI = (P × R × T)/100: (9/25)P = (P × R × R)/100 ⇒ R² = (9/25) × 100 = 36 ⇒ R = 6%.
- **A — 5%:** Result of taking the square root of 25 instead of 36.
- **B — 7.5%:** Value obtained if 9/16 is mistakenly used or arithmetic error in 900/25.
- **C — 8%:** Result of multiplying 6% by a wrong factor or misreading 9/25.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When R = T and SI = (a/b)P, always use R = 10 × √(a/b) = 10 × (3/5) = 6%.

### Q326 · Simple Interest Basics & Rate Changes

Find the simple interest on ₹24,000 at 12.5% per annum for 219 days (take 1 year = 365 days).

A. ₹1,800
B. ₹1,500
C. ₹2,400
D. ₹1,200

**Answer:** A. ₹1,800
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹1,800 (Correct):** 219 days = 219/365 = 3/5 year (since 73 × 3 = 219 and 73 × 5 = 365). 12.5% = 1/8. Thus, SI = ₹24,000 × (1/8) × (3/5) = ₹3,000 × (3/5) = ₹1,800.
- **B — ₹1,500:** Value obtained by taking 219 days as 1/2 year (182.5 days) instead of 3/5 year.
- **C — ₹2,400:** Result of taking 219 days as 4/5 year (292 days).
- **D — ₹1,200:** Value obtained if 219 days is taken as 2/5 year (146 days).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Memorize 73-day multiples: 73 = 1/5 yr, 146 = 2/5 yr, 219 = 3/5 yr, 292 = 4/5 yr. And 12.5% = 1/8!

### Q327 · Simple Interest Basics & Rate Changes

A sum of ₹20,000 is invested in two schemes: part A at 8% p.a. and part B at 11% p.a. simple interest. If the total annual interest earned is ₹1,960, find the amount invested in scheme A.

A. ₹12,000
B. ₹10,000
C. ₹8,000
D. ₹9,000

**Answer:** C. ₹8,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹8,000 (Correct):** Overall effective rate = (1,960 / 20,000) × 100 = 9.8%. By alligation: (11 - 9.8) : (9.8 - 8) = 1.2 : 1.8 = 2 : 3. Amount in scheme A = (2 / 5) × ₹20,000 = ₹8,000.
- **A — ₹12,000:** This is the amount invested in scheme B (11%), not scheme A (8%).
- **B — ₹10,000:** Result of assuming an equal 50-50 split of the ₹20,000 principal.
- **D — ₹9,000:** Value obtained by miscalculating the alligation ratio as 9:11.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use Alligation: Overall rate = 1960/200 = 9.8%. Ratio = (11-9.8):(9.8-8) = 2:3 → Scheme A = 2/5 of 20,000 = ₹8,000.

### Q328 · Simple Interest Basics & Rate Changes

The simple interest on a sum for 4 years at 7% p.a. is ₹2,720 less than the simple interest on the same sum for 5 years at 9% p.a. What will be the simple interest on the same sum for 2 years at 10% p.a.?

A. ₹3,400
B. ₹3,200
C. ₹3,600
D. ₹2,800

**Answer:** B. ₹3,200
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹3,200 (Correct):** Difference in effective SI% = (5 × 9)% - (4 × 7)% = 45% - 28% = 17%. Given 17% of P = ₹2,720 ⇒ 1% of P = ₹160 ⇒ P = ₹16,000. Required SI for 2 years at 10% = 20% of ₹16,000 = ₹3,200.
- **A — ₹3,400:** Result of taking 1% of P as ₹170 instead of ₹160.
- **C — ₹3,600:** Value obtained if 20% of ₹18,000 is calculated due to an arithmetic slip.
- **D — ₹2,800:** Result of subtracting 28% from 45% incorrectly as 19%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 17% → ₹2,720, so 1% = 160. Directly find 20% = 20 × 160 = ₹3,200 without even computing P = 16,000!

### Q329 · Sum Doubling, Tripling & Multiples

A sum of money becomes 3 times itself in 8 years at simple interest. In how many years will it become 7 times itself at the same rate?

A. 24 years
B. 28 years
C. 21 years
D. 18 years

**Answer:** A. 24 years
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 24 years (Correct):** At SI, becoming 3 times means earning interest = 2P in 8 years. So, interest of 1P is earned in 4 years. To become 7 times, interest needed = 6P. Time = 6 × 4 = 24 years.
- **B — 28 years:** Result of using direct proportion 7/3 × 8 instead of (n-1) proportion.
- **C — 21 years:** Value obtained if 7 × 3 = 21 is used instead of (7-1) × 4.
- **D — 18 years:** Result of miscalculating 6 × 3 instead of 6 × 4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use T₂ = [(n₂ - 1)/(n₁ - 1)] × T₁ = (6/2) × 8 = 24 years.

### Q330 · Sum Doubling, Tripling & Multiples

At what rate percent per annum simple interest will a sum of money become 5 times itself in 16 years?

A. 20%
B. 31.25%
C. 25%
D. 18.75%

**Answer:** C. 25%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 25% (Correct):** If sum becomes 5 times, SI = 5P - P = 4P. Using SI = (P × R × T)/100: 4P = (P × R × 16)/100 ⇒ R = 400 / 16 = 25% p.a.
- **A — 20%:** Result of taking SI = 3.2P or dividing 320 by 16.
- **B — 31.25%:** Value obtained by mistakenly using Amount (5P) instead of Interest (4P): 500/16 = 31.25%.
- **D — 18.75%:** Result of using 3P as the interest instead of 4P: 300/16 = 18.75%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Formula: R = 100(n - 1) / T = 100(5 - 1) / 16 = 400 / 16 = 25%.

### Q331 · Sum Doubling, Tripling & Multiples

A sum of money placed at compound interest doubles itself in 6 years. In how many years will it amount to 16 times itself at the same rate?

A. 48 years
B. 24 years
C. 18 years
D. 30 years

**Answer:** B. 24 years
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 24 years (Correct):** At CI, the sum multiplies by 2 every 6 years. Since 16 = 2⁴, it needs 4 cycles of 6 years. Total time = 4 × 6 = 24 years.
- **A — 48 years:** Result of applying simple interest logic: (16 - 1) × 6 = 90 or 8 × 6 = 48.
- **C — 18 years:** Value obtained by writing 16 = 2³ instead of 2⁴.
- **D — 30 years:** Result of adding 6 + 24 or using 5 cycles instead of 4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Write target multiple as power of base multiple: 16 = 2⁴ → Time = exponent × base time = 4 × 6 = 24 years.

### Q332 · Sum Doubling, Tripling & Multiples

A sum of money becomes 27 times itself in 15 years at compound interest. In how many years will it become 9 times itself?

A. 5 years
B. 10 years
C. 12 years
D. 7.5 years

**Answer:** B. 10 years
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 10 years (Correct):** 27 = 3³ takes 15 years, so each factor of 3 takes 15 / 3 = 5 years. To become 9 = 3² times, it takes 2 × 5 = 10 years.
- **A — 5 years:** This is the time to become 3 times itself, not 9 times.
- **C — 12 years:** Result of taking 4/5 of 15 years instead of 2/3.
- **D — 7.5 years:** Value obtained by dividing 15 by 2 instead of multiplying 5 by 2.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Match exponents: 3³ → 15 yrs, so 3¹ → 5 yrs, hence 3² (9) → 10 yrs.

### Q333 · Sum Doubling, Tripling & Multiples

A sum of money becomes 1.728 times of itself in 3 years at compound interest compounded annually. Find the rate of interest per annum.

A. 20%
B. 15%
C. 12%
D. 24%

**Answer:** A. 20%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 20% (Correct):** A/P = (1 + R/100)³ = 1.728 = 1728/1000 = (12/10)³ = (1.2)³. Taking cube root: 1 + R/100 = 1.2 ⇒ R/100 = 0.2 ⇒ R = 20%.
- **B — 15%:** Result of dividing 72.8% roughly by 5 instead of taking cube root.
- **C — 12%:** Value obtained by confusing 1.728 with 12% due to the digits '12' in 1728.
- **D — 24%:** Result of dividing 72.8% by 3 (simple interest approximation: 24.27%).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Recognize 1728 = 12³. So 1.728 = 1.2³ → Rate = (1.2 - 1) × 100 = 20%.

### Q334 · Sum Doubling, Tripling & Multiples

A sum of money becomes 7/5 of itself in 4 years at a certain rate of simple interest. What is the rate of interest per annum?

A. 12%
B. 8%
C. 15%
D. 10%

**Answer:** D. 10%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 10% (Correct):** Amount = (7/5)P ⇒ SI = (7/5)P - P = (2/5)P = 40% of P. Since 40% interest is earned in 4 years, annual rate = 40% / 4 = 10% p.a.
- **A — 12%:** Value obtained by taking SI as (3/5)P or miscalculating 40/4.
- **B — 8%:** Result of dividing 32% by 4 instead of 40%.
- **C — 15%:** Value obtained by using 7/5 directly or taking 60%/4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 7/5 = 140% → 40% is interest. Rate = 40% / 4 yrs = 10%.

### Q335 · Sum Doubling, Tripling & Multiples

If a sum of money triples itself in 5 years at compound interest, how many times of itself will it become in 20 years?

A. 12 times
B. 27 times
C. 81 times
D. 64 times

**Answer:** C. 81 times
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 81 times (Correct):** Number of 5-year cycles in 20 years = 20 / 5 = 4. In each cycle, the sum triples. Thus, after 20 years it becomes 3⁴ = 81 times itself.
- **A — 12 times:** Result of multiplying 3 × 4 instead of raising 3 to the power 4.
- **B — 27 times:** Value obtained by using 3³ (for 15 years) instead of 3⁴.
- **D — 64 times:** Result of computing 4³ instead of 3⁴.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Cycles = 20/5 = 4 → Multiple = (base multiple)^cycles = 3⁴ = 81 times.

### Q336 · Sum Doubling, Tripling & Multiples

A sum of money becomes 4 times itself in 15 years at simple interest. If the rate of interest is doubled, in how many years will the same sum become 6 times itself?

A. 10 years
B. 12.5 years
C. 25 years
D. 15 years

**Answer:** B. 12.5 years
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 12.5 years (Correct):** Originally, becoming 4 times means SI = 3P in 15 years, so R = (3 × 100)/15 = 20% p.a. When rate is doubled, new R = 40% p.a. To become 6 times, SI needed = 5P. Time = (5 × 100)/40 = 12.5 years.
- **A — 10 years:** Result of taking 4P as the required interest instead of 5P (400/40 = 10).
- **C — 25 years:** Value obtained if you forget to double the rate of interest (500/20 = 25).
- **D — 15 years:** Result of using 6P instead of 5P as the interest (600/40 = 15).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** At same rate, 6 times takes (5/3)×15 = 25 yrs. Doubling the rate halves the time → 25/2 = 12.5 yrs.

### Q337 · Different Rate Slabs (SI)

The rate of simple interest on a sum is 6% p.a. for the first 2 years, 9% p.a. for the next 3 years, and 14% p.a. for the period beyond 5 years. If a man pays ₹11,400 as total interest at the end of 9 years, find the sum borrowed.

A. ₹12,000
B. ₹10,000
C. ₹15,000
D. ₹11,400

**Answer:** A. ₹12,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹12,000 (Correct):** Remaining period beyond 5 years = 9 - 5 = 4 years. Total SI% = (2 × 6)% + (3 × 9)% + (4 × 14)% = 12% + 27% + 56% = 95%. Given 95% of P = ₹11,400 ⇒ P = (11,400 × 100) / 95 = ₹12,000.
- **B — ₹10,000:** Result of taking total SI% as 114% instead of 95%.
- **C — ₹15,000:** Value obtained by miscalculating the last slab as 2 years instead of 4 years.
- **D — ₹11,400:** Result of assuming total interest equals 100% of the principal.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Sum up R×T for each slab: (2×6) + (3×9) + (4×14) = 95%. Since 11,400 / 95 = 120, P = ₹12,000.

### Q338 · Different Rate Slabs (SI)

A sum of ₹15,000 is lent at simple interest at 8% p.a. for the first 3 years, 8.5% p.a. for the next 4 years, and 10% p.a. for the period beyond 7 years. Find the total simple interest at the end of 10 years.

A. ₹12,600
B. ₹13,200
C. ₹14,700
D. ₹11,700

**Answer:** B. ₹13,200
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹13,200 (Correct):** Period beyond 7 years = 10 - 7 = 3 years. Total SI% = (3 × 8)% + (4 × 8.5)% + (3 × 10)% = 24% + 34% + 30% = 88%. Total SI = 88% of ₹15,000 = 0.88 × 15,000 = ₹13,200.
- **A — ₹12,600:** Result of calculating 4 × 8.5 incorrectly as 30% instead of 34% (total 84%).
- **C — ₹14,700:** Value obtained if the last slab is taken as 4 years instead of 3 years (total 98%).
- **D — ₹11,700:** Result of taking the last slab as 2 years instead of 3 years (total 78%).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Total % = 24 + 34 + 30 = 88%. Compute 88% of 15,000 as 150 × 88 = 300 × 44 = ₹13,200.

### Q339 · Different Rate Slabs (SI)

The rate of simple interest is 4% p.a. for the first 2 years, 6% p.a. for the next 4 years, and 8% p.a. beyond 6 years. If the total simple interest for 9 years is ₹1,120, find the principal.

A. ₹2,500
B. ₹1,750
C. ₹2,000
D. ₹2,240

**Answer:** C. ₹2,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹2,000 (Correct):** Period beyond 6 years = 9 - 6 = 3 years. Total SI% = (2 × 4)% + (4 × 6)% + (3 × 8)% = 8% + 24% + 24% = 56%. Given 56% of P = ₹1,120 ⇒ P = (1,120 × 100) / 56 = ₹2,000.
- **A — ₹2,500:** Result of using 44.8% as total interest instead of 56%.
- **B — ₹1,750:** Value obtained by taking the last slab as 4 years (64% total) instead of 3 years.
- **D — ₹2,240:** Result of taking total SI% as 50% instead of 56%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 8 + 24 + 24 = 56%. Notice 1,120 is 20 × 56, so P = 20 × 100 = ₹2,000.

### Q340 · Different Rate Slabs (SI)

₹12,000 is invested at simple interest at 5% p.a. for the first 3 years, 7% p.a. for the next 5 years, and 9% p.a. for the period beyond 8 years. Find the total amount received at the end of 11 years.

A. ₹9,240
B. ₹20,160
C. ₹22,320
D. ₹21,240

**Answer:** D. ₹21,240
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹21,240 (Correct):** Period beyond 8 years = 11 - 8 = 3 years. Total SI% = (3 × 5)% + (5 × 7)% + (3 × 9)% = 15% + 35% + 27% = 77%. SI = 77% of ₹12,000 = ₹9,240. Total Amount = ₹12,000 + ₹9,240 = ₹21,240.
- **A — ₹9,240:** This is only the simple interest earned, not the total amount (Principal + SI).
- **B — ₹20,160:** Result of taking the last slab as 2 years instead of 3 years (total 68% SI).
- **C — ₹22,320:** Value obtained by taking the last slab as 4 years instead of 3 years (total 86% SI).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Total SI% = 15 + 35 + 27 = 77%. Amount = 177% of 12,000 = 120 × 177 = ₹21,240.

### Q341 · Different Rate Slabs (SI)

A person borrows a sum at simple interest of 6% p.a. for the first 2 years, 8% p.a. for the next 3 years, and 12% p.a. for the period beyond 5 years. If he pays ₹14,400 as total interest for 8 years, how much did he borrow?

A. ₹20,000
B. ₹24,000
C. ₹18,000
D. ₹15,000

**Answer:** A. ₹20,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹20,000 (Correct):** Period beyond 5 years = 8 - 5 = 3 years. Total SI% = (2 × 6)% + (3 × 8)% + (3 × 12)% = 12% + 24% + 36% = 72%. Given 72% of P = ₹14,400 ⇒ P = (14,400 × 100) / 72 = ₹20,000.
- **B — ₹24,000:** Result of taking the last slab as 2 years instead of 3 years (60% total SI).
- **C — ₹18,000:** Value obtained by taking 80% as total SI instead of 72%.
- **D — ₹15,000:** Result of taking the last slab as 5 years instead of 3 years (96% total SI).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 12% + 24% + 36% = 72%. Since 14,400 = 200 × 72, Principal = 200 × 100 = ₹20,000.

### Q342 · Different Rate Slabs (SI)

A sum of ₹10,000 is lent at R% p.a. simple interest for the first 2 years, (R + 3)% p.a. for the next 3 years, and (R + 5)% p.a. for the next 2 years. If the total simple interest at the end of 7 years is ₹6,800, find the value of R.

A. 6
B. 7
C. 8
D. 5

**Answer:** B. 7
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 7 (Correct):** Total SI% = (6,800 / 10,000) × 100 = 68%. Also, total SI% = 2R + 3(R + 3) + 2(R + 5) = 7R + 19. Equating: 7R + 19 = 68 ⇒ 7R = 49 ⇒ R = 7.
- **A — 6:** Value obtained if the constant term 19 is miscalculated as 26.
- **C — 8:** Result of dividing 68 by 8.5 instead of forming the linear equation.
- **D — 5:** Value obtained if the constant term is added as 33 instead of 19.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Total % = 68%. Sum of time = 7 yrs, extra % = 3×3 + 2×5 = 19%. So R = (68 - 19) / 7 = 7.

### Q343 · Different Rate Slabs (SI)

A sum of ₹16,000 earns a total simple interest of ₹12,800 in 9 years. If the rate of interest was 7.5% p.a. for the first 4 years, what was the rate of interest for the remaining 5 years?

A. 9%
B. 12%
C. 10%
D. 8.5%

**Answer:** C. 10%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 10% (Correct):** Total SI% = (12,800 / 16,000) × 100 = 80%. SI% for first 4 years = 4 × 7.5% = 30%. Remaining SI% for 5 years = 80% - 30% = 50%. Thus, Rate for remaining 5 years = 50% / 5 = 10% p.a.
- **A — 9%:** Result of taking total SI% as 75% instead of 80%.
- **B — 12%:** Value obtained by dividing 50% by 4 years instead of 5 years (12.5% rounded).
- **D — 8.5%:** Result of subtracting 37.5% from 80% and dividing by 5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Total % = 128/160 = 80%. First 4 yrs = 30%, so remaining 5 yrs = 50% → R = 50/5 = 10%.

### Q344 · CI - SI Difference (2 & 3 Years)

Find the difference between the compound interest and simple interest on ₹15,000 at 8% per annum for 2 years.

A. ₹96
B. ₹120
C. ₹84
D. ₹108

**Answer:** A. ₹96
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹96 (Correct):** For 2 years, CI - SI = P(R/100)² = 15,000 × (8/100)² = 15,000 × (64/10,000) = 1.5 × 64 = ₹96.
- **B — ₹120:** Result of computing 8% of ₹1,500 instead of 8% of ₹1,200 (1st year SI).
- **C — ₹84:** Value obtained by using 7% instead of 8% or arithmetic slip.
- **D — ₹108:** Result of multiplying 1.5 × 72 instead of 1.5 × 64.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 2-yr CI - SI = R% of 1st year SI = 8% of (8% of 15,000) = 8% of 1,200 = ₹96.

### Q345 · CI - SI Difference (2 & 3 Years)

If the difference between compound interest and simple interest on a certain sum at 9% per annum for 2 years is ₹162, find the sum.

A. ₹18,000
B. ₹20,000
C. ₹22,500
D. ₹16,200

**Answer:** B. ₹20,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹20,000 (Correct):** Using CI - SI = P(R/100)²: 162 = P × (9/100)² = P × (81/10,000). Therefore, P = (162 × 10,000) / 81 = 2 × 10,000 = ₹20,000.
- **A — ₹18,000:** Result of dividing 162 by 0.009 instead of 0.0081 (or miscalculating 162/81).
- **C — ₹22,500:** Value obtained by using 7.2% instead of 8.1%.
- **D — ₹16,200:** Result of multiplying 162 by 100 instead of 10,000/81.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 2-yr Diff% = R²/100 = 81/100 = 0.81%. Since 0.81% = ₹162, 100% = (162/81) × 10,000 = ₹20,000.

### Q346 · CI - SI Difference (2 & 3 Years)

The difference between the compound interest and simple interest on ₹12,800 for 2 years is ₹288. Find the rate of interest per annum.

A. 12%
B. 18%
C. 15%
D. 12.5%

**Answer:** C. 15%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 15% (Correct):** CI - SI = P(R/100)² ⇒ 288 = 12,800 × (R/100)² ⇒ (R/100)² = 288 / 12,800 = 9 / 400. Taking square root: R/100 = 3/20 ⇒ R = 15%.
- **A — 12%:** Result of approximating without simplifying 288/12800 to 9/400.
- **B — 18%:** Value obtained if you take (R/100) instead of (R/100)² or miscalculate √(9/400).
- **D — 12.5%:** Result of confusing 3/20 (15%) with 1/8 (12.5%).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Simplify Diff/P = 288/12800 = 9/400. Take square root: 3/20 = 15%!

### Q347 · CI - SI Difference (2 & 3 Years)

The difference between compound interest and simple interest on a certain sum at 15% per annum for 3 years is ₹1,134. Find the principal.

A. ₹12,000
B. ₹15,000
C. ₹18,000
D. ₹16,000

**Answer:** D. ₹16,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹16,000 (Correct):** For 3 years, CI - SI = P(R/100)² × (300 + R)/100. Substituting R = 15: 1,134 = P × (15/100)² × (315/100) = P × (9/400) × (63/20) = P × (567/8,000). Thus, P = (1,134 × 8,000) / 567 = 2 × 8,000 = ₹16,000.
- **A — ₹12,000:** Result of using only the 2-year difference formula P(R/100)² with a factor of 4.
- **B — ₹15,000:** Value obtained by miscalculating (300 + R)/100 as 300/100.
- **C — ₹18,000:** Result of an arithmetic error in simplifying 9 × 63 = 567.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For R = 15% = 3/20, take P = 20³ = 8,000 units. 3-yr CI - SI = 3(20×3²) + 3³ = 540 + 27 = 567 units = ₹1,134 → 1 unit = ₹2 → P = ₹16,000.

### Q348 · CI - SI Difference (2 & 3 Years)

Find the difference between the compound interest and simple interest on ₹25,000 at 10% per annum for 3 years.

A. ₹775
B. ₹750
C. ₹825
D. ₹675

**Answer:** A. ₹775
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹775 (Correct):** For 3 years, CI - SI = P(R/100)² × (300 + R)/100 = 25,000 × (1/10)² × (310/100) = 25,000 × (31/1,000) = 25 × 31 = ₹775.
- **B — ₹750:** Result of multiplying 2-year difference (₹250) by 3 instead of 3.1.
- **C — ₹825:** Value obtained by using 3.3% instead of 3.1% of P.
- **D — ₹675:** Result of miscalculating 25 × 31 as 675.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** At 10% for 3 yrs, CI = 33.1% and SI = 30%. Difference = 3.1% of 25,000 = 31 × 25 = ₹775.

### Q349 · CI - SI Difference (2 & 3 Years)

The ratio of the difference between compound interest and simple interest for 3 years to the difference between compound interest and simple interest for 2 years on the same sum at the same rate is 16 : 5. Find the rate of interest per annum.

A. 15%
B. 20%
C. 12%
D. 25%

**Answer:** B. 20%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 20% (Correct):** Ratio = [P(R/100)² × (300 + R)/100] / [P(R/100)²] = (300 + R) / 100. Given (300 + R) / 100 = 16 / 5 = 320 / 100 ⇒ 300 + R = 320 ⇒ R = 20%.
- **A — 15%:** Result of using ratio 63:20 instead of 16:5.
- **C — 12%:** Value obtained by subtracting 3 from 16/5 incorrectly.
- **D — 25%:** Result of using ratio 13:4 instead of 16:5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Ratio of 3-yr diff to 2-yr diff is always (3 + R/100) : 1. Here 16/5 = 3.2, so R/100 = 0.2 → R = 20%.

### Q350 · CI - SI Difference (2 & 3 Years)

On a certain sum of money, the simple interest for 2 years is ₹1,000 and the compound interest for 2 years is ₹1,040 at the same rate of interest. Find the principal.

A. ₹5,000
B. ₹6,000
C. ₹6,250
D. ₹8,000

**Answer:** C. ₹6,250
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹6,250 (Correct):** 1-year SI = ₹1,000 / 2 = ₹500. Difference between 2-year CI and SI = ₹1,040 - ₹1,000 = ₹40 (which is the interest on ₹500 for 1 year). Thus, Rate R = (40 / 500) × 100 = 8%. Since 8% of P = ₹500, P = (500 × 100) / 8 = ₹6,250.
- **A — ₹5,000:** Result of taking Rate = 10% instead of 8%.
- **B — ₹6,000:** Value obtained by miscalculating 50,000 / 8 as 6,000.
- **D — ₹8,000:** Result of taking 1-year SI as ₹1,000 instead of ₹500.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** R = (CI₂ - SI₂) / (SI₂ / 2) × 100 = 40/500 = 8%. Then P = 500 / 8% = ₹6,250.

### Q351 · CI - SI Difference (2 & 3 Years)

Find the difference between the compound interest compounded half-yearly and the simple interest on ₹12,000 at 10% per annum for 1 year.

A. ₹60
B. ₹120
C. ₹15
D. ₹30

**Answer:** D. ₹30
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹30 (Correct):** For half-yearly compounding, rate per half-year = 10% / 2 = 5%, and n = 2 half-years. Effective CI% = 5 + 5 + (5×5)/100 = 10.25%. SI% for 1 year = 10%. Difference = 0.25% of ₹12,000 = (1/400) × 12,000 = ₹30.
- **A — ₹60:** Result of taking the difference as 0.5% instead of 0.25%.
- **B — ₹120:** Value obtained by using 10% instead of 5% in the (R/100)² formula.
- **C — ₹15:** Result of halving the actual difference ₹30.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 1 yr half-yearly at R% is equivalent to 2 yrs at (R/2)%. Diff = P × (R/200)² = 12,000 × (5/100)² = ₹30.

### Q352 · CI - SI Difference (2 & 3 Years)

The simple interest and compound interest on a certain sum for 2 years at the same rate are ₹800 and ₹840 respectively. What will be the compound interest on the same sum for 3 years?

A. ₹1,324
B. ₹1,284
C. ₹1,240
D. ₹1,364

**Answer:** A. ₹1,324
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹1,324 (Correct):** 1st year SI (= 1st year CI) = ₹800 / 2 = ₹400. 2nd year CI = ₹840 - ₹400 = ₹440. Increase in CI = ₹440 - ₹400 = ₹40, so Rate R = (40 / 400) × 100 = 10%. Thus, 3rd year CI = ₹440 × 1.10 = ₹484. Total 3-year CI = ₹840 + ₹484 = ₹1,324.
- **B — ₹1,284:** Result of adding ₹444 instead of ₹484 for the 3rd year.
- **C — ₹1,240:** Value obtained by adding only ₹400 (1st year SI) to ₹840 instead of ₹484.
- **D — ₹1,364:** Result of adding ₹524 (using 20% increase) to ₹840.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 2nd-yr CI = 840 - 400 = 440, so R = 40/400 = 10%. 3rd-yr CI = 440 × 1.1 = 484 → Total = 840 + 484 = ₹1,324.

### Q353 · Compounding Frequencies

Find the compound interest on ₹16,000 at 10% per annum for 1.5 years, compounded half-yearly.

A. ₹2,400
B. ₹2,522
C. ₹2,480
D. ₹2,642

**Answer:** B. ₹2,522
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹2,522 (Correct):** For half-yearly compounding, rate per period r = 10% / 2 = 5% = 1/20, and number of periods n = 1.5 × 2 = 3. Amount = 16,000 × (21/20)³ = 16,000 × (9,261 / 8,000) = 2 × 9,261 = ₹18,522. CI = ₹18,522 - ₹16,000 = ₹2,522.
- **A — ₹2,400:** This is the simple interest for 1.5 years at 10% p.a., ignoring compounding.
- **C — ₹2,480:** Result of forgetting the final term (5%)³ in the 3-period binomial expansion.
- **D — ₹2,642:** Value obtained by using n = 3 with an incorrect rate of 5.25%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 5% for 3 periods = 15.7625%. Or use 20³ = 8000 → 21³ = 9261, diff = 1261 units. Here 16000/8000 = 2 → CI = 2 × 1261 = ₹2,522.

### Q354 · Compounding Frequencies

Find the compound interest on ₹24,000 at 20% per annum for 9 months, compounded quarterly.

A. ₹3,600
B. ₹3,720
C. ₹3,783
D. ₹3,963

**Answer:** C. ₹3,783
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹3,783 (Correct):** For quarterly compounding, rate per quarter r = 20% / 4 = 5% = 1/20, and number of quarters n = 9 / 3 = 3. Amount = 24,000 × (21/20)³ = 24,000 × (9,261 / 8,000) = 3 × 9,261 = ₹27,783. CI = ₹27,783 - ₹24,000 = ₹3,783.
- **A — ₹3,600:** This is the simple interest on ₹24,000 for 9 months at 20% p.a.
- **B — ₹3,720:** Result of using n = 2 quarters instead of 3, or omitting the last compounding step.
- **D — ₹3,963:** Value obtained by miscalculating 3 × 1,321 instead of 3 × 1,261.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Quarterly 20% p.a. = 5% per quarter; 9 months = 3 quarters. Since 24,000 = 3 × 8,000, CI = 3 × (21³ - 20³) = 3 × 1,261 = ₹3,783.

### Q355 · Compounding Frequencies

Find the compound interest on ₹10,000 at 15% per annum for 2 years, compounded 8-monthly.

A. ₹3,000
B. ₹3,225
C. ₹3,100
D. ₹3,310

**Answer:** D. ₹3,310
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹3,310 (Correct):** Rate for 8 months = 15% × (8/12) = 10%. Number of 8-month periods in 2 years (24 months) = 24 / 8 = 3. Effective CI% = 33.1% (from 1.1³ - 1). Thus, CI = 33.1% of ₹10,000 = ₹3,310.
- **A — ₹3,000:** This is the simple interest for 2 years at 15% p.a.
- **B — ₹3,225:** This is the compound interest if compounded annually for 2 years (32.25%).
- **C — ₹3,100:** Result of forgetting the 1% (10³) term in the 3-period 10% expansion.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 8-monthly rate = 15×(8/12) = 10%; 24 months = 3 periods → 10% for 3 periods = 33.1% → ₹3,310.

### Q356 · Compounding Frequencies

What is the compound interest on ₹8,000 at 12% per annum for 1 year 3 months, if the interest is compounded 5-monthly?

A. ₹1,261
B. ₹1,200
C. ₹1,241
D. ₹1,321

**Answer:** A. ₹1,261
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹1,261 (Correct):** Rate for 5 months = 12% × (5/12) = 5% = 1/20. Total time = 15 months = 3 periods of 5 months. Amount = 8,000 × (21/20)³ = ₹9,261. CI = ₹9,261 - ₹8,000 = ₹1,261.
- **B — ₹1,200:** This is the simple interest for 15 months at 12% p.a.
- **C — ₹1,241:** Result of an arithmetic error in computing 21³ - 20³ as 1,241.
- **D — ₹1,321:** Value obtained by confusing 5% (1261) with 10% (1331 - 1000 = 331).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 5-monthly rate = 12 × (5/12) = 5%; 15 months = 3 cycles. On ₹8,000 (= 20³), CI = 21³ - 20³ = ₹1,261.

### Q357 · Compounding Frequencies

Find the compound interest on ₹12,500 at 24% per annum for 10 months, compounded 5-monthly.

A. ₹2,500
B. ₹2,625
C. ₹2,750
D. ₹2,425

**Answer:** B. ₹2,625
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹2,625 (Correct):** Rate for 5 months = 24% × (5/12) = 10%. Number of 5-month periods in 10 months = 10 / 5 = 2. Effective CI% = 10 + 10 + (10×10)/100 = 21%. CI = 21% of ₹12,500 = ₹2,625.
- **A — ₹2,500:** This is the simple interest for 10 months at 24% p.a. (20%).
- **C — ₹2,750:** Result of using 22% instead of 21% as the effective compound rate.
- **D — ₹2,425:** Value obtained by miscalculating 21% of 12,500 as ₹2,425.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 5-monthly rate = 10%, 10 months = 2 cycles → 21% of 12,500 = 2500 + 125 = ₹2,625.

### Q358 · Compounding Frequencies

Find the difference between the compound interest on ₹20,000 for 1.5 years at 20% per annum when compounded half-yearly and when compounded annually.

A. ₹200
B. ₹240
C. ₹220
D. ₹180

**Answer:** C. ₹220
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹220 (Correct):** Compounded annually for 1.5 years: 20% for 1st year and 10% for next 0.5 year ⇒ Effective rate = 20 + 10 + (20×10)/100 = 32%. Compounded half-yearly: 10% for 3 periods ⇒ Effective rate = 33.1%. Difference = (33.1% - 32%) of ₹20,000 = 1.1% of 20,000 = ₹220.
- **A — ₹200:** Result of taking the difference as 1% instead of 1.1%.
- **B — ₹240:** Value obtained by using 31.9% or 31.8% for annual compounding.
- **D — ₹180:** Result of miscalculating 1.1% of 20,000.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Annual 1.5 yr = 20% + 10% successive = 32%. Half-yearly = 10% × 3 = 33.1%. Diff = 1.1% of 20,000 = ₹220.

### Q359 · Compounding Frequencies

Find the compound interest on ₹15,000 for 16 months at 12% per annum, compounded 8-monthly.

A. ₹2,400
B. ₹2,544
C. ₹2,448
D. ₹2,496

**Answer:** D. ₹2,496
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹2,496 (Correct):** Rate for 8 months = 12% × (8/12) = 8%. Number of 8-month periods in 16 months = 16 / 8 = 2. Effective CI% = 8 + 8 + (8×8)/100 = 16.64%. CI = 16.64% of ₹15,000 = 150 × 16.64 = ₹2,496.
- **A — ₹2,400:** This is the simple interest for 16 months at 12% p.a. (16%).
- **B — ₹2,544:** Result of using 16.96% instead of 16.64%.
- **C — ₹2,448:** Value obtained by miscalculating 150 × 16.64 as ₹2,448.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 8-monthly rate = 8%, 2 cycles → 16.64% of 15,000 = 2,400 + 96 = ₹2,496.

### Q360 · Compounding Frequencies

What is the effective annual rate of interest corresponding to a nominal rate of 14% per annum payable half-yearly?

A. 14.49%
B. 14.25%
C. 14.98%
D. 15.00%

**Answer:** A. 14.49%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 14.49% (Correct):** Rate per half-year = 14% / 2 = 7%. In 1 year, there are 2 half-years. Effective annual rate = 7 + 7 + (7 × 7)/100 = 14 + 0.49 = 14.49%.
- **B — 14.25%:** Result of dividing 49 by 200 instead of 100.
- **C — 14.98%:** Value obtained by doubling 0.49% to 0.98%.
- **D — 15.00%:** Result of rounding up 14.49% or using an incorrect successive formula.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Successive formula: R/2 + R/2 + (R/2)²/100 = 14 + 49/100 = 14.49%.

### Q361 · Compounding Frequencies

Find the compound interest on ₹30,000 for 2 years 4 months at 10% per annum, compounded annually.

A. ₹7,000
B. ₹7,510
C. ₹7,260
D. ₹7,630

**Answer:** B. ₹7,510
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹7,510 (Correct):** For 2 full years, factor = (11/10)². For remaining 4 months (1/3 year), rate = 10% × (1/3) = 10/3% = 1/30, so factor = 31/30. Amount = 30,000 × (11/10) × (11/10) × (31/30) = 10 × 121 × 31 = ₹37,510. CI = ₹37,510 - ₹30,000 = ₹7,510.
- **A — ₹7,000:** This is the simple interest for 2 years 4 months at 10% p.a.
- **C — ₹7,260:** Result of taking 2 years CI (₹6,300) and adding 1/3 of 1st year SI (₹1,000) instead of 1/3 of 3rd year's base.
- **D — ₹7,630:** Value obtained by taking 4 months as 0.4 year instead of 1/3 year.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Multiply factors: 30,000 × (11/10) × (11/10) × (31/30) = 37,510 → CI = ₹7,510.

### Q362 · Compounding Frequencies

Find the compound interest on ₹12,500 for 1 year 4 months at 18% per annum, compounded 8-monthly.

A. ₹3,000
B. ₹3,120
C. ₹3,180
D. ₹3,240

**Answer:** C. ₹3,180
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹3,180 (Correct):** Rate for 8 months = 18% × (8/12) = 12%. Time = 1 year 4 months = 16 months = 2 periods of 8 months. Effective CI% = 12 + 12 + (12×12)/100 = 25.44%. CI = 25.44% of ₹12,500 = 125 × 25.44 = ₹3,180.
- **A — ₹3,000:** This is the simple interest for 16 months at 18% p.a. (24%).
- **B — ₹3,120:** Result of taking 24.96% instead of 25.44%.
- **D — ₹3,240:** Value obtained by miscalculating 125 × 25.44 as ₹3,240.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 8-monthly rate = 12%, 2 cycles → 25.44%. Multiply 25.44 × 125 (= 1000/8) = 25,440 / 8 = ₹3,180.

### Q363 · Ratio of Amounts in Consecutive Years

A sum of money at compound interest amounts to ₹6,050 in 2 years and ₹6,655 in 3 years. Find the rate of interest per annum.

A. 12%
B. 8%
C. 9%
D. 10%

**Answer:** D. 10%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 10% (Correct):** In CI, the amount at the end of year 2 acts as the principal for year 3. Interest for 3rd year = ₹6,655 - ₹6,050 = ₹605. Rate R = (605 / 6,050) × 100 = 10% p.a.
- **A — 12%:** Result of using an incorrect base or miscalculating 605/6050.
- **B — 8%:** Value obtained by dividing 605 by 7,562.5 instead of 6,050.
- **C — 9%:** Result of dividing 605 by 6,655 (9.09% rounded).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Consecutive years in CI: R = [(A₃ - A₂) / A₂] × 100 = (605 / 6,050) × 100 = 10%.

### Q364 · Ratio of Amounts in Consecutive Years

A certain sum at compound interest amounts to ₹8,640 in 3 years and ₹10,368 in 4 years. Find the principal.

A. ₹5,000
B. ₹4,800
C. ₹5,400
D. ₹6,000

**Answer:** A. ₹5,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹5,000 (Correct):** Rate R = [(10,368 - 8,640) / 8,640] × 100 = (1,728 / 8,640) × 100 = 20% = 1/5. Thus, P × (6/5)³ = 8,640 ⇒ P × (216/125) = 8,640 ⇒ P = 40 × 125 = ₹5,000.
- **B — ₹4,800:** Result of subtracting 3 × 20% SI from ₹8,640 instead of compounding.
- **C — ₹5,400:** Value obtained by dividing ₹8,640 by 1.6 instead of (1.2)³.
- **D — ₹6,000:** Result of discounting for only 2 years instead of 3 years.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** R = 1728/8640 = 20% (factor 6/5). P = 8,640 × (5/6)³ = 40 × 125 = ₹5,000.

### Q365 · Ratio of Amounts in Consecutive Years

A sum of money at compound interest amounts to ₹7,200 in 2 years and ₹10,368 in 4 years. Find the sum.

A. ₹4,800
B. ₹5,000
C. ₹5,200
D. ₹4,500

**Answer:** B. ₹5,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹5,000 (Correct):** Since the time intervals are equal (0 to 2 yrs, and 2 to 4 yrs), the ratio of amounts is constant: P / A₂ = A₂ / A₄. Thus, P = A₂² / A₄ = (7,200 × 7,200) / 10,368 = ₹5,000.
- **A — ₹4,800:** Result of taking simple interest gap (10,368 - 7,200 = 3,168) and subtracting from 7,200.
- **C — ₹5,200:** Value obtained by miscalculating 7,200² / 10,368.
- **D — ₹4,500:** Result of using a wrong multiplying factor of 1.6 instead of 1.44.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equal time gap trick: P = A₂² / A₄ = (7,200 × 7,200) / 10,368 = ₹5,000.

### Q366 · Ratio of Amounts in Consecutive Years

A sum of money invested at compound interest amounts to ₹6,000 in 3 years and ₹9,000 in 6 years. Find the principal.

A. ₹3,000
B. ₹4,500
C. ₹4,000
D. ₹3,600

**Answer:** C. ₹4,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹4,000 (Correct):** For equal time intervals of 3 years, the multiplying factor is A₆ / A₃ = 9,000 / 6,000 = 3/2. Therefore, P × (3/2) = 6,000 ⇒ P = 6,000 × (2/3) = ₹4,000.
- **A — ₹3,000:** Result of applying simple interest logic: 6,000 - (9,000 - 6,000) = ₹3,000.
- **B — ₹4,500:** Value obtained by taking 3/4 of 6,000 instead of 2/3.
- **D — ₹3,600:** Result of taking 3/5 of 6,000 instead of 2/3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equal 3-yr gap: factor = 9,000/6,000 = 3/2. So P = 6,000 / (3/2) = ₹4,000.

### Q367 · Ratio of Amounts in Consecutive Years

A sum of money at compound interest amounts to ₹4,800 in 4 years and ₹7,200 in 6 years. What will it amount to in 8 years at the same rate?

A. ₹9,600
B. ₹10,000
C. ₹11,400
D. ₹10,800

**Answer:** D. ₹10,800
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹10,800 (Correct):** In 2 years (from year 4 to year 6), the amount grows by a factor of 7,200 / 4,800 = 3/2. In the next 2 years (from year 6 to year 8), it will grow by the same factor: A₈ = 7,200 × (3/2) = ₹10,800.
- **A — ₹9,600:** Result of adding the 2-year increment ₹2,400 linearly (SI logic): 7,200 + 2,400 = ₹9,600.
- **B — ₹10,000:** Value obtained by miscalculating 7,200 × 3/2.
- **C — ₹11,400:** Result of adding ₹4,200 instead of ₹3,600.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Every 2 years, amount multiplies by 7,200/4,800 = 1.5. So A₈ = 7,200 × 1.5 = ₹10,800.

### Q368 · Ratio of Amounts in Consecutive Years

The compound interest on a certain sum for the 2nd year is ₹1,320 and for the 3rd year is ₹1,452. Find the rate of interest per annum.

A. 10%
B. 12%
C. 9%
D. 11%

**Answer:** A. 10%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 10% (Correct):** In CI, the interest of any year is (1 + R/100) times the interest of the previous year. Increase = ₹1,452 - ₹1,320 = ₹132. Rate R = (132 / 1,320) × 100 = 10% p.a.
- **B — 12%:** Result of using a wrong denominator or arithmetic slip.
- **C — 9%:** Value obtained by dividing 132 by 1,452 (9.09% rounded).
- **D — 11%:** Result of miscalculating 1,452 - 1,320.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** CI of consecutive years grows by R%: R = (1,452 - 1,320) / 1,320 × 100 = 10%.

### Q369 · Ratio of Amounts in Consecutive Years

The compound interest earned on a sum in the 2nd year is ₹1,080 and in the 3rd year is ₹1,296. Find the principal.

A. ₹5,400
B. ₹4,500
C. ₹4,000
D. ₹5,000

**Answer:** B. ₹4,500
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹4,500 (Correct):** Rate R = [(1,296 - 1,080) / 1,080] × 100 = (216 / 1,080) × 100 = 20%. Since 2nd-year CI is 1.2 times 1st-year CI, 1st-year CI = 1,080 / 1.2 = ₹900. Now, 20% of P = ₹900 ⇒ P = 900 × 5 = ₹4,500.
- **A — ₹5,400:** Result of taking ₹1,080 directly as 20% of P instead of discounting it back to year 1.
- **C — ₹4,000:** Value obtained by assuming 1st-year CI is ₹800 instead of ₹900.
- **D — ₹5,000:** Result of miscalculating 900 × 5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** R = 216/1080 = 20%. 1st-yr CI = 1080 / 1.2 = 900. Since 20% of P = 900, P = ₹4,500.

### Q370 · Ratio of Amounts in Consecutive Years

At compound interest, the ratio of the amounts at the end of 2 years and 4 years is 25 : 36. Find the rate of interest per annum.

A. 22%
B. 18%
C. 20%
D. 25%

**Answer:** C. 20%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 20% (Correct):** A₄ / A₂ = (1 + R/100)² = 36 / 25. Taking square root on both sides: 1 + R/100 = 6 / 5 = 1.2 ⇒ R/100 = 0.2 ⇒ R = 20%.
- **A — 22%:** Result of taking half of (36 - 25)/25 × 100 = 44% (SI approximation).
- **B — 18%:** Value obtained by an arithmetic slip in (6/5 - 1) × 100.
- **D — 25%:** Result of taking 1/4 instead of 1/5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Gap is 2 years, so take square root of ratio: √(36/25) = 6/5 → 1/5 = 20%.

### Q371 · Equal Annual Installments (SI & CI)

A sum of ₹8,200 is borrowed at 5% per annum compound interest and paid back in 2 equal annual installments. Find the value of each installment.

A. ₹4,305
B. ₹4,512
C. ₹4,200
D. ₹4,410

**Answer:** D. ₹4,410
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹4,410 (Correct):** 5% = 1/20. For 2 equal installments: Year 1 ratio (P₁ : I) = 20 : 21 = 420 : 441; Year 2 ratio (P₂ : I) = 20² : 21² = 400 : 441. Total Principal = 420 + 400 = 820 units = ₹8,200 ⇒ 1 unit = ₹10. Each installment = 441 × 10 = ₹4,410.
- **A — ₹4,305:** Result of computing 4,100 × 1.05 instead of using compound discounting.
- **B — ₹4,512:** Value obtained by miscalculating 21².
- **C — ₹4,200:** Result of taking 10% simple interest on ₹4,100 without compounding.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 5% = 1/20 → (20:21) and (400:441). Equalize right side: 420:441 & 400:441. Total P = 820 units = 8,200 → Inst = 441 × 10 = ₹4,410.

### Q372 · Equal Annual Installments (SI & CI)

A loan of ₹6,620 is to be repaid in 3 equal annual installments at 10% per annum compound interest. Find the amount of each installment.

A. ₹2,662
B. ₹2,420
C. ₹2,928
D. ₹2,541

**Answer:** A. ₹2,662
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹2,662 (Correct):** 10% = 1/10. Equalizing installments to 11³ = 1,331 units: Year 1 P₁ = 10 × 11² = 1,210; Year 2 P₂ = 10² × 11 = 1,100; Year 3 P₃ = 10³ = 1,000. Total Principal = 1,210 + 1,100 + 1,000 = 3,310 units = ₹6,620 ⇒ 1 unit = ₹2. Each installment = 1,331 × 2 = ₹2,662.
- **B — ₹2,420:** Result of using 2-year installment factor 121 instead of 1,331.
- **C — ₹2,928:** Value obtained by adding an extra 10% to ₹2,662.
- **D — ₹2,541:** Result of using simple interest or miscalculating 1,331 × 2.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** At 10% for 3 installments, Total P = 3,310 units and each installment = 1,331 units. Here 6,620/3,310 = 2 → ₹2,662.

### Q373 · Equal Annual Installments (SI & CI)

A sum of money was borrowed and paid back in 2 equal annual installments of ₹5,760 each, allowing 20% compound interest per annum. What was the sum borrowed?

A. ₹9,600
B. ₹8,800
C. ₹8,640
D. ₹9,000

**Answer:** B. ₹8,800
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹8,800 (Correct):** 20% = 1/5. Equalizing installments to 6² = 36 units: Year 1 P₁ = 5 × 6 = 30 units; Year 2 P₂ = 5² = 25 units. Total Principal = 30 + 25 = 55 units. Given 36 units = ₹5,760 ⇒ 1 unit = ₹160. Sum borrowed = 55 × 160 = ₹8,800.
- **A — ₹9,600:** Result of subtracting 20% simple interest from total paid ₹11,520.
- **C — ₹8,640:** Value obtained by using 54 units instead of 55 units.
- **D — ₹9,000:** Result of miscalculating 55 × 160.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 20% = 1/5 → P = (5×6 + 5²) = 55 units, Inst = 36 units. Since 5,760/36 = 160, P = 55 × 160 = ₹8,800.

### Q374 · Equal Annual Installments (SI & CI)

A man borrows a sum at 16⅔% per annum compound interest and repays it in 3 equal annual installments of ₹6,860 each. Find the total interest paid by him.

A. ₹5,140
B. ₹5,520
C. ₹5,340
D. ₹4,980

**Answer:** C. ₹5,340
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹5,340 (Correct):** 16⅔% = 1/6. Equalizing installments to 7³ = 343 units: P₁ = 6 × 7² = 294, P₂ = 6² × 7 = 252, P₃ = 6³ = 216. Total P = 294 + 252 + 216 = 762 units. Total paid = 3 × 343 = 1,029 units. Interest = 1,029 - 762 = 267 units. Since 343 units = ₹6,860 ⇒ 1 unit = ₹20, Total interest = 267 × 20 = ₹5,340.
- **A — ₹5,140:** Result of taking total P as 772 units instead of 762 units.
- **B — ₹5,520:** Value obtained by miscalculating 294 + 252 + 216 as 753.
- **D — ₹4,980:** Result of taking 1 unit = ₹18.6 or arithmetic slip in 267 × 20.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 16⅔% = 1/6 → Inst = 7³ = 343 units (= ₹6,860 → 1u = ₹20). P = 294+252+216 = 762u. Interest = (1029-762)×20 = ₹5,340.

### Q375 · Equal Annual Installments (SI & CI)

What annual payment will discharge a debt of ₹6,450 due in 4 years at 5% per annum simple interest?

A. ₹1,612.50
B. ₹1,450
C. ₹1,350
D. ₹1,500

**Answer:** D. ₹1,500
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹1,500 (Correct):** Let each annual installment be ₹100. The last installment earns 0% interest; the 3rd earns 5%, 2nd earns 10%, and 1st earns 15%. Total value = 100 + 105 + 110 + 115 = 430 units. Given 430 units = ₹6,450 ⇒ 1 unit = ₹15. Annual payment = 100 × 15 = ₹1,500.
- **A — ₹1,612.50:** Result of simply dividing ₹6,450 by 4 without accounting for interest on installments.
- **B — ₹1,450:** Value obtained by using 445 units instead of 430 units.
- **C — ₹1,350:** Result of taking 100+105+110+115+120 by mistake.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** SI installment value = 100 + (100+R) + (100+2R) + (100+3R) = 430%. Here 6,450/430 = 15 → ₹1,500.

### Q376 · Equal Annual Installments (SI & CI)

What equal annual installment will discharge a debt of ₹9,600 due in 5 years at 10% per annum simple interest?

A. ₹1,600
B. ₹1,920
C. ₹1,500
D. ₹1,800

**Answer:** A. ₹1,600
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹1,600 (Correct):** With each installment = 100 units, their values at the end of 5 years are 100, 110, 120, 130, and 140. Total = 600 units = ₹9,600 ⇒ 1 unit = ₹16. Each installment = 100 × 16 = ₹1,600.
- **B — ₹1,920:** Result of dividing ₹9,600 directly by 5 years without interest adjustment.
- **C — ₹1,500:** Value obtained by summing up to 150 (total 650 units) instead of 140.
- **D — ₹1,800:** Result of taking 500 + 10% = 550 units instead of 600.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 5 installments at 10% SI = 100 + 110 + 120 + 130 + 140 = 600%. So Inst = 9,600 / 6 = ₹1,600.

### Q377 · Equal Annual Installments (SI & CI)

A debt of ₹6,720 due in 3 years at 12% per annum simple interest is to be paid off in 3 equal annual installments. Find each installment.

A. ₹2,240
B. ₹2,000
C. ₹1,800
D. ₹2,100

**Answer:** B. ₹2,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹2,000 (Correct):** With each installment = 100 units, their values at the end of 3 years are 100, 112, and 124. Total = 336 units = ₹6,720 ⇒ 1 unit = ₹20. Each installment = 100 × 20 = ₹2,000.
- **A — ₹2,240:** Result of dividing ₹6,720 directly by 3 installments.
- **C — ₹1,800:** Value obtained by using 373 units instead of 336 units.
- **D — ₹2,100:** Result of using 320 units instead of 336 units.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 3 installments at 12% SI = 100 + 112 + 124 = 336%. Since 6,720 = 20 × 336, Inst = ₹2,000.

### Q378 · Equal Annual Installments (SI & CI)

A scooter is available for ₹52,000 cash or for ₹10,000 cash down payment followed by 2 equal annual installments. If the rate of interest is 10% per annum compounded annually, find the value of each installment.

A. ₹23,100
B. ₹22,000
C. ₹24,200
D. ₹25,200

**Answer:** C. ₹24,200
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹24,200 (Correct):** Loan amount (Principal) = ₹52,000 - ₹10,000 = ₹42,000. At 10% = 1/10 for 2 installments: P₁ = 10 × 11 = 110 units, P₂ = 10² = 100 units, Installment = 11² = 121 units. Total P = 210 units = ₹42,000 ⇒ 1 unit = ₹200. Each installment = 121 × 200 = ₹24,200.
- **A — ₹23,100:** Result of taking 110% of ₹21,000 instead of compound discounting.
- **B — ₹22,000:** Value obtained by using 21 units instead of 210 units or forgetting compounding.
- **D — ₹25,200:** Result of miscalculating 121 × 200 or using ₹44,000 as the balance.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Balance = ₹42,000. At 10% for 2 yrs, P = 210u, Inst = 121u. Since 42,000/210 = 200, Inst = 121 × 200 = ₹24,200.

### Q379 · Equal Annual Installments (SI & CI)

A loan is repaid in 2 equal annual installments of ₹3,630 each at 10% per annum compound interest. Find the total interest paid.

A. ₹840
B. ₹900
C. ₹1,020
D. ₹960

**Answer:** D. ₹960
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹960 (Correct):** 10% = 1/10. Equalizing installments to 11² = 121 units: P = 110 + 100 = 210 units. Total paid = 2 × 121 = 242 units. Interest = 242 - 210 = 32 units. Given 121 units = ₹3,630 ⇒ 1 unit = ₹30. Total interest = 32 × 30 = ₹960.
- **A — ₹840:** Result of taking interest as 28 units instead of 32 units.
- **B — ₹900:** Value obtained by taking interest as 30 units (1 unit = ₹30).
- **C — ₹1,020:** Result of taking interest as 34 units instead of 32 units.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** At 10% for 2 installments: P = 210u, Paid = 242u → Interest = 32u. Here 3,630/121 = 30 → Interest = 32×30 = ₹960.

### Q380 · Dividing Sums & Mixed Applications

₹30,000 is divided into two parts and lent at 10% p.a. and 12% p.a. simple interest respectively. If the total annual interest received is ₹3,340, find the amount lent at 10% p.a.

A. ₹13,000
B. ₹17,000
C. ₹15,000
D. ₹12,000

**Answer:** A. ₹13,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹13,000 (Correct):** If all ₹30,000 were lent at 10%, interest would be ₹3,000. Extra interest = ₹3,340 - ₹3,000 = ₹340, which comes from the extra 2% on the second part. Thus, 2% of 2nd part = ₹340 ⇒ 2nd part = ₹17,000. 1st part = ₹30,000 - ₹17,000 = ₹13,000.
- **B — ₹17,000:** This is the amount lent at 12% p.a., not 10% p.a.
- **C — ₹15,000:** Result of assuming an equal split of ₹30,000.
- **D — ₹12,000:** Value obtained by miscalculating 340 / 2% as ₹18,000.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Assume all ₹30,000 at 10% → ₹3,000. Extra ₹340 / 2% = ₹17,000 (at 12%). So 10% part = ₹13,000.

### Q381 · Dividing Sums & Mixed Applications

A sum of ₹26,000 is divided into two parts such that the simple interest on the first part for 5 years at 10% p.a. is equal to the simple interest on the second part for 6 years at 9% p.a. Find the difference between the two parts.

A. ₹1,500
B. ₹1,000
C. ₹2,000
D. ₹500

**Answer:** B. ₹1,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹1,000 (Correct):** SI₁ = SI₂ ⇒ 50% of P₁ = 54% of P₂ ⇒ P₁ / P₂ = 54 / 50 = 27 / 25. Total = 27 + 25 = 52 units = ₹26,000 ⇒ 1 unit = ₹500. Difference = 27 - 25 = 2 units = 2 × 500 = ₹1,000.
- **A — ₹1,500:** Result of using a wrong ratio or 3 units instead of 2.
- **C — ₹2,000:** Value obtained by miscalculating 52 units or doubling 1,000.
- **D — ₹500:** This is the value of 1 unit, not the difference of 2 units.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** P₁ : P₂ = (9×6) : (10×5) = 27 : 25. Sum = 52u = 26,000 → 1u = 500. Diff = 2u = ₹1,000.

### Q382 · Dividing Sums & Mixed Applications

₹16,820 is divided between two brothers aged 12 years and 14 years so that both receive the same amount when they turn 18, at 5% per annum compound interest. Find the share of the younger brother.

A. ₹8,820
B. ₹8,410
C. ₹8,000
D. ₹7,600

**Answer:** C. ₹8,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹8,000 (Correct):** Younger brother's money grows for 18 - 12 = 6 years; elder's for 18 - 14 = 4 years. Since final amounts are equal: P_younger × (1.05)⁶ = P_elder × (1.05)⁴ ⇒ P_younger / P_elder = 1 / (21/20)² = 400 / 441. Total = 841 units = ₹16,820 ⇒ 1 unit = ₹20. Younger's share = 400 × 20 = ₹8,000.
- **A — ₹8,820:** This is the share of the elder brother (441 × 20), not the younger brother.
- **B — ₹8,410:** Result of splitting the amount equally (50-50).
- **D — ₹7,600:** Value obtained by using simple interest ratio instead of compound interest.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Age gap = 2 yrs at 5% (20:21) → Share ratio = 20² : 21² = 400 : 441. Sum = 841u = 16,820 → Younger = 400×20 = ₹8,000.

### Q383 · Dividing Sums & Mixed Applications

Equal sums are invested in two schemes at 12% per annum for 2 years: one at simple interest and the other at compound interest. If the compound interest exceeds the simple interest by ₹216, find the total interest earned from both schemes together.

A. ₹7,200
B. ₹7,632
C. ₹6,984
D. ₹7,416

**Answer:** D. ₹7,416
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹7,416 (Correct):** CI - SI = P(12/100)² = 216 ⇒ P × (144/10,000) = 216 ⇒ P = (216 × 10,000)/144 = ₹15,000. 2-year SI = 24% of ₹15,000 = ₹3,600. 2-year CI = ₹3,600 + ₹216 = ₹3,816. Total interest = ₹3,600 + ₹3,816 = ₹7,416.
- **A — ₹7,200:** Result of adding 2 × SI (₹3,600 + ₹3,600) and forgetting the ₹216 difference.
- **B — ₹7,632:** Value obtained by adding 2 × ₹216 instead of 1 × ₹216.
- **C — ₹6,984:** Result of subtracting ₹216 instead of adding it to 2 × SI.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 1.44% of P = 216 → P = ₹15,000. Total interest = 2×(24% of 15,000) + 216 = 7,200 + 216 = ₹7,416.

### Q384 · Dividing Sums & Mixed Applications

A man borrows ₹20,000 at 8% per annum simple interest and immediately lends it at 10% per annum compound interest for 2 years. Find his net gain at the end of 2 years.

A. ₹1,000
B. ₹800
C. ₹1,200
D. ₹960

**Answer:** A. ₹1,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹1,000 (Correct):** SI paid for 2 years = 2 × 8% = 16% of ₹20,000 = ₹3,200. CI received for 2 years = 10 + 10 + (10×10)/100 = 21% of ₹20,000 = ₹4,200. Net gain = ₹4,200 - ₹3,200 = ₹1,000.
- **B — ₹800:** Result of taking 2-year CI as 20% (SI) instead of 21%, giving 20% - 16% = 4% (₹800).
- **C — ₹1,200:** Value obtained by taking 6% net gain instead of 5%.
- **D — ₹960:** Result of miscalculating 5% of ₹20,000.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Net gain% = 21% (CI) - 16% (SI) = 5%. 5% of 20,000 = ₹1,000.

### Q385 · Dividing Sums & Mixed Applications

₹8,000 amounts to ₹9,440 in 3 years at simple interest. If the rate of interest had been 2.5% per annum higher, what would it have amounted to in the same time?

A. ₹9,840
B. ₹10,040
C. ₹10,240
D. ₹9,640

**Answer:** B. ₹10,040
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹10,040 (Correct):** Extra interest for 3 years at 2.5% p.a. = 3 × 2.5% = 7.5% of ₹8,000 = 0.075 × 8,000 = ₹600. New Amount = ₹9,440 + ₹600 = ₹10,040.
- **A — ₹9,840:** Result of adding only 5% (2 years' extra interest = ₹400) instead of 7.5%.
- **C — ₹10,240:** Value obtained by adding 10% (₹800) instead of 7.5%.
- **D — ₹9,640:** Result of adding only 1 year's extra interest (2.5% = ₹200) instead of 3 years'.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** New Amount = Old Amount + P × ΔR × T / 100 = 9,440 + 80 × 7.5 = ₹10,040.
