# Section III: Work, Time & Motion

## Chapter 7: Time, Work & Pipes-Cisterns

### Q386 · LCM Efficiency Method

Rohan can finish a project in 18 days, Sohan in 24 days, and Mohan in 36 days. If all three work together, in how many days will the project be completed?

A. 8 days
B. 9 days
C. 7.2 days
D. 6 days

**Answer:** A. 8 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 8 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(18, 24, 36) = 72 units. Daily efficiencies: Rohan = 72/18 = 4, Sohan = 72/24 = 3, Mohan = 72/36 = 2 units/day. Combined efficiency = 4 + 3 + 2 = 9 units/day. Time = 72 / 9 = 8 days.
- **B — 9 days:** Obtained by miscalculating Mohan's efficiency as 1 unit/day instead of 2 units/day (72 / 8).
- **C — 7.2 days:** Obtained by mistakenly adding 1 to combined efficiency (72 / 10).
- **D — 6 days:** Result of adding 3 extra units to combined efficiency (72 / 12).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Take LCM of days as total work; divide LCM by the sum of individual daily efficiencies.

### Q387 · LCM Efficiency Method

A and B together can complete a task in 12 days, B and C together in 15 days, and C and A together in 20 days. In how many days can A alone complete the task?

A. 24 days
B. 30 days
C. 45 days
D. 60 days

**Answer:** B. 30 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 30 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(12, 15, 20) = 60 units. Efficiencies: (A+B) = 5, (B+C) = 4, (C+A) = 3. Summing: 2(A+B+C) = 12, so (A+B+C) = 6 units/day. A's efficiency = 6 - (B+C) = 6 - 4 = 2 units/day. Time for A alone = 60 / 2 = 30 days.
- **A — 24 days:** Result of forgetting to halve the sum of pair efficiencies when finding A+B+C.
- **C — 45 days:** Obtained by using an incorrect difference while subtracting B+C from A+B+C.
- **D — 60 days:** Obtained by finding C's solo time (60 / (6 - 5) = 60 days) instead of A's.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** A's efficiency = [(A+B) + (C+A) - (B+C)] / 2 = (5 + 3 - 4) / 2 = 2 units/day.

### Q388 · LCM Efficiency Method

P can complete 2/5 of a work in 8 days, while Q can complete 3/4 of the same work in 18 days. Working together, in how many days can they complete 11/15 of the work?

A. 8 days
B. 6 days
C. 7 days
D. 9 days

**Answer:** A. 8 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 8 days (Correct):** P completes full work in 8 * (5/2) = 20 days. Q completes full work in 18 * (4/3) = 24 days. Total work = LCM(20, 24) = 120 units. P = 6, Q = 5 units/day, so P+Q = 11 units/day. Required work = (11/15) * 120 = 88 units. Time = 88 / 11 = 8 days.
- **B — 6 days:** Obtained by taking required work as 66 units instead of 88 units.
- **C — 7 days:** Result of miscomputing P's full-work time as 16 days instead of 20 days.
- **D — 9 days:** Obtained by computing time for 3/4 of the work and rounding up.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Convert fractional work to full-work days first by multiplying days by the reciprocal of the fraction.

### Q389 · LCM Efficiency Method

X, Y, and Z together can finish a job in 10 days. X alone can do it in 30 days and Y alone in 45 days. In how many days can Z alone finish the job?

A. 18 days
B. 20 days
C. 25 days
D. 22.5 days

**Answer:** D. 22.5 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 22.5 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(10, 30, 45) = 90 units. (X+Y+Z) = 9 units/day, X = 3 units/day, Y = 2 units/day. Z's efficiency = 9 - 3 - 2 = 4 units/day. Time for Z alone = 90 / 4 = 22.5 days.
- **A — 18 days:** Obtained by subtracting only X's efficiency from (X+Y+Z) and misdividing.
- **B — 20 days:** Result of taking Z's efficiency as 4.5 units/day instead of 4 units/day.
- **C — 25 days:** Obtained by using 100 as total work and dividing by 4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Z's efficiency = (X+Y+Z) - X - Y; divide the LCM by this difference.

### Q390 · LCM Efficiency Method

A can do a piece of work in 12 days and B can do it in 18 days. They work together for 4 days, after which C joins them and the remaining work is finished in 2 more days. In how many days can C alone do the whole work?

A. 10 days
B. 14 days
C. 16 days
D. 12 days

**Answer:** D. 12 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 12 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(12, 18) = 36 units. A = 3, B = 2 units/day. In 4 days, A+B do 4 * 5 = 20 units. Remaining = 36 - 20 = 16 units, done by (A+B+C) in 2 days, so A+B+C = 8 units/day. C = 8 - 5 = 3 units/day. Time for C alone = 36 / 3 = 12 days.
- **A — 10 days:** Obtained by dividing 36 by 3.6 due to an error in computing remaining work.
- **B — 14 days:** Result of miscalculating (A+B)'s 4-day work as 18 units instead of 20 units.
- **C — 16 days:** Obtained by subtracting 3 from 8 incorrectly when finding C's efficiency.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Find remaining work after 4 days, divide by 2 to get (A+B+C)'s rate, then subtract (A+B).

### Q391 · LCM Efficiency Method

A, B, and C can do a piece of work in 20, 30, and 60 days respectively. In how many days can A complete the work if he is assisted by B and C on every third day?

A. 15 days
B. 12 days
C. 18 days
D. 16 days

**Answer:** A. 15 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 15 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(20, 30, 60) = 60 units. A = 3, B = 2, C = 1 unit/day. Work in 3-day cycle: Day 1 (A) = 3, Day 2 (A) = 3, Day 3 (A+B+C) = 6. Total in 3 days = 12 units. Number of 3-day cycles = 60 / 12 = 5. Total days = 5 * 3 = 15 days.
- **B — 12 days:** Obtained by assuming B and C assist A on every second day instead of every third day.
- **C — 18 days:** Result of counting only 10 units per 3-day cycle instead of 12 units.
- **D — 16 days:** Obtained by adding 1 extra day to the 5 cycles.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Compute work done in one 3-day block (3A + B + C = 12 units) and scale up to total work.

### Q392 · LCM Efficiency Method

A and B can do a job in 10 days, B and C in 12 days, and C and A in 15 days. In how many days will all three working together finish the job?

A. 6 days
B. 8 days
C. 7.5 days
D. 9 days

**Answer:** B. 8 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 8 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(10, 12, 15) = 60 units. (A+B) = 6, (B+C) = 5, (C+A) = 4. Summing: 2(A+B+C) = 15, so A+B+C = 7.5 units/day. Time = 60 / 7.5 = 8 days.
- **A — 6 days:** Obtained by taking A+B+C = 10 units/day instead of 7.5 units/day.
- **C — 7.5 days:** Result of confusing combined efficiency (7.5) with the number of days.
- **D — 9 days:** Obtained by rounding 60/7.5 upward after an arithmetic error.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Time for all three together = (2 * LCM) / sum of pair efficiencies = 120 / 15 = 8 days.

### Q393 · LCM Efficiency Method

A completes 1/3 of a work in 5 days and B completes 2/5 of the same work in 10 days. In how many days can both A and B together complete 4/5 of the work?

A. 6 days
B. 8 days
C. 7.5 days
D. 9 days

**Answer:** C. 7.5 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 7.5 days (Correct):** A's full-work time = 5 * 3 = 15 days. B's full-work time = 10 * (5/2) = 25 days. Total work = LCM(15, 25) = 75 units. A = 5, B = 3 units/day. Combined = 8 units/day. 4/5 of work = 60 units. Time = 60 / 8 = 7.5 days.
- **A — 6 days:** Obtained by miscalculating A+B efficiency as 10 units/day.
- **B — 8 days:** Result of finding the time for 4/5 of the work using 64 units instead of 60.
- **D — 9 days:** Obtained by finding time for the whole work (75/8) instead of 4/5 of it.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Full-work days: A = 15, B = 25. Together for 4/5 work = (4/5) * (15*25)/(15+25) = 7.5 days.

### Q394 · LCM Efficiency Method

A alone can do a work in 16 days and B alone in 24 days. With the help of C, they complete the work in 8 days. In how many days can C alone complete the work?

A. 36 days
B. 40 days
C. 42 days
D. 48 days

**Answer:** D. 48 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 48 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(16, 24, 8) = 48 units. A = 3, B = 2, (A+B+C) = 6 units/day. C = 6 - (3 + 2) = 1 unit/day. Time for C alone = 48 / 1 = 48 days.
- **A — 36 days:** Obtained by taking total work as 36 units instead of 48 units.
- **B — 40 days:** Result of miscalculating (A+B+C) efficiency as 6.2 units/day.
- **C — 42 days:** Obtained by subtracting A's efficiency twice.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** C's 1-day work = 1/8 - 1/16 - 1/24 = (6 - 3 - 2)/48 = 1/48, so 48 days.

### Q395 · LCM Efficiency Method

A and B together can complete a work in 18 days, while A alone can complete it in 27 days. In how many days can B alone complete 2/3 of the same work?

A. 36 days
B. 54 days
C. 27 days
D. 45 days

**Answer:** A. 36 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 36 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(18, 27) = 54 units. (A+B) = 3, A = 2 units/day. B = 3 - 2 = 1 unit/day. 2/3 of work = (2/3) * 54 = 36 units. Time for B = 36 / 1 = 36 days.
- **B — 54 days:** Obtained by finding the time for the entire work (54 days) instead of 2/3 of it.
- **C — 27 days:** Result of finding A's full-work time instead of B's 2/3-work time.
- **D — 45 days:** Obtained by multiplying 54 by 5/6 instead of 2/3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** B's full time = (18 * 27)/(27 - 18) = 54 days; for 2/3 work, multiply 54 by 2/3 = 36 days.

### Q396 · Efficiency Ratio & Percentage

A is twice as efficient as B. Working together, they can complete a piece of work in 14 days. In how many days can A alone complete the work?

A. 28 days
B. 21 days
C. 42 days
D. 18 days

**Answer:** B. 21 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 21 days (Correct):** Efficiency ratio A : B = 2 : 1. Combined efficiency = 2 + 1 = 3 units/day. Total work = 3 * 14 = 42 units. Time for A alone = 42 / 2 = 21 days.
- **A — 28 days:** Obtained by multiplying 14 by 2 without accounting for combined efficiency.
- **C — 42 days:** Result of finding B's solo time (42 / 1 = 42 days) instead of A's.
- **D — 18 days:** Obtained by dividing 36 by 2 instead of 42 by 2.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Total work = (sum of efficiency ratio) * combined days; divide by A's ratio.

### Q397 · Efficiency Ratio & Percentage

A is 60% more efficient than B. If A alone can complete a task in 13 days, in how many days will A and B together complete the task?

A. 8 days
B. 7 days
C. 9 days
D. 10 days

**Answer:** A. 8 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 8 days (Correct):** Let B's efficiency = 5 units/day, then A's efficiency = 5 * 1.6 = 8 units/day. Total work = 8 * 13 = 104 units. Combined efficiency = 8 + 5 = 13 units/day. Time together = 104 / 13 = 8 days.
- **B — 7 days:** Obtained by taking A's efficiency as 7 units/day instead of 8 units/day.
- **C — 9 days:** Result of miscalculating combined efficiency as 11.5 units/day.
- **D — 10 days:** Obtained by taking B's efficiency as 60% of A's rather than A being 60% more than B.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 60% more = 8:5 ratio; together time = (8 * 13) / (8 + 5) = 8 days.

### Q398 · Efficiency Ratio & Percentage

P is 25% less efficient than Q. Together they can complete a job in 12 days. In how many days can Q alone complete the job?

A. 18 days
B. 24 days
C. 21 days
D. 28 days

**Answer:** C. 21 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 21 days (Correct):** Let Q's efficiency = 4 units/day, then P's efficiency = 3 units/day. Combined efficiency = 7 units/day. Total work = 7 * 12 = 84 units. Time for Q alone = 84 / 4 = 21 days.
- **A — 18 days:** Obtained by taking P:Q = 3:5 instead of 3:4.
- **B — 24 days:** Result of dividing 84 by 3.5 instead of 4.
- **D — 28 days:** Obtained by finding P's solo time (84 / 3 = 28 days) instead of Q's.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 25% less means P:Q = 3:4; Q's time = (7 * 12) / 4 = 21 days.

### Q399 · Efficiency Ratio & Percentage

The efficiencies of A, B, and C are in the ratio 2 : 3 : 5. Working together, they can finish a work in 6 days. In how many days can B alone finish 40% of the work?

A. 10 days
B. 12 days
C. 6 days
D. 8 days

**Answer:** D. 8 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 8 days (Correct):** Combined efficiency = 2 + 3 + 5 = 10 units/day. Total work = 10 * 6 = 60 units. 40% of work = 0.4 * 60 = 24 units. Time for B alone = 24 / 3 = 8 days.
- **A — 10 days:** Obtained by taking 50% of the work (30 units) instead of 40%.
- **B — 12 days:** Result of dividing 24 by A's efficiency (2) instead of B's efficiency (3).
- **C — 6 days:** Obtained by taking 30% of the work (18 units) instead of 40%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Total work = 60 units; 40% = 24 units; divide by B's efficiency 3 -> 8 days.

### Q400 · Efficiency Ratio & Percentage

A is 50% more efficient than B, and therefore A takes 4 days less than B to finish a work. In how many days will they finish the work together?

A. 4.8 days
B. 5.2 days
C. 4.5 days
D. 6 days

**Answer:** A. 4.8 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 4.8 days (Correct):** Efficiency ratio A : B = 3 : 2, so time ratio A : B = 2 : 3. Difference = 1 part = 4 days, hence A takes 8 days and B takes 12 days. Total work = 24 units. Combined efficiency = 3 + 2 = 5 units/day. Time together = 24 / 5 = 4.8 days.
- **B — 5.2 days:** Obtained by taking total work as 26 units instead of 24 units.
- **C — 4.5 days:** Result of averaging 8 and 12 and halving improperly.
- **D — 6 days:** Obtained by dividing 24 by 4 instead of 5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Time ratio is inverse of efficiency ratio (2:3); 1 part = 4 days gives A=8, B=12, together = 96/20 = 4.8 days.

### Q401 · Efficiency Ratio & Percentage

A is three times as efficient as B, and B is twice as efficient as C. If all three together finish a work in 8 days, in how many days can C alone finish it?

A. 64 days
B. 72 days
C. 48 days
D. 36 days

**Answer:** B. 72 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 72 days (Correct):** Let C = 1 unit/day, then B = 2 and A = 6 units/day. Combined efficiency = 6 + 2 + 1 = 9 units/day. Total work = 9 * 8 = 72 units. Time for C alone = 72 / 1 = 72 days.
- **A — 64 days:** Obtained by taking combined efficiency as 8 units/day instead of 9.
- **C — 48 days:** Result of taking A = 3 instead of 6 (forgetting A is 3 times B, not C).
- **D — 36 days:** Obtained by finding B's solo time (72 / 2 = 36 days) instead of C's.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Set C = 1, B = 2, A = 6; sum = 9, so C's days = 9 * 8 = 72 days.

### Q402 · Efficiency Ratio & Percentage

A is 40% more efficient than B. If B alone can complete a work in 24 days, in how many days can A and B together complete it?

A. 12 days
B. 8 days
C. 10 days
D. 14 days

**Answer:** C. 10 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 10 days (Correct):** Efficiency ratio A : B = 7 : 5. Total work = 5 * 24 = 120 units. Combined efficiency = 7 + 5 = 12 units/day. Time together = 120 / 12 = 10 days.
- **A — 12 days:** Obtained by dividing 120 by 10 instead of 12.
- **B — 8 days:** Result of miscalculating total work as 7 * 24 = 168 and dividing by 21.
- **D — 14 days:** Obtained by taking A's solo time (120 / 7 ≈ 17.1) or miscomputing 140/10.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** A:B = 7:5; together time = (5 * 24) / (7 + 5) = 10 days.

### Q403 · Efficiency Ratio & Percentage

A can complete a job in 18 days. B is 20% more efficient than A. In how many days can B alone complete the same job?

A. 14 days
B. 16 days
C. 12 days
D. 15 days

**Answer:** D. 15 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 15 days (Correct):** Efficiency ratio A : B = 5 : 6. Total work = 5 * 18 = 90 units. Time for B alone = 90 / 6 = 15 days.
- **A — 14 days:** Obtained by subtracting 20% of 18 (3.6) and rounding down.
- **B — 16 days:** Result of taking 18 / 1.125 instead of 18 / 1.2.
- **C — 12 days:** Obtained by using A:B = 2:3 instead of 5:6.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 20% more efficient means time becomes 18 / 1.2 = 15 days.

### Q404 · Efficiency Ratio & Percentage

A takes twice as much time as B and thrice as much time as C to finish a piece of work. Working together, they finish the work in 4 days. How many days will B alone take to finish it?

A. 8 days
B. 10 days
C. 16 days
D. 12 days

**Answer:** D. 12 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 12 days (Correct):** Time ratio A : B : C = 6 : 3 : 2, so efficiency ratio A : B : C = 1 : 2 : 3. Combined efficiency = 6 units/day. Total work = 6 * 4 = 24 units. Time for B alone = 24 / 2 = 12 days.
- **A — 8 days:** Obtained by finding C's solo time (24 / 3 = 8 days) instead of B's.
- **B — 10 days:** Result of taking combined efficiency as 5 instead of 6.
- **C — 16 days:** Obtained by misreading B's efficiency as 1.5 instead of 2.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Time 6:3:2 gives efficiency 1:2:3; total work = 6 * 4 = 24, so B = 24/2 = 12 days.

### Q405 · Efficiency Ratio & Percentage

The efficiency ratio of X and Y is 4 : 5. X works on a task for 5 days and leaves, and then Y finishes the remaining task in 8 days. In how many days can Y alone do the whole task?

A. 12 days
B. 15 days
C. 10 days
D. 16 days

**Answer:** A. 12 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 12 days (Correct):** Work done by X in 5 days = 4 * 5 = 20 units. Work done by Y in 8 days = 5 * 8 = 40 units. Total work = 20 + 40 = 60 units. Time for Y alone = 60 / 5 = 12 days.
- **B — 15 days:** Obtained by dividing total work (60) by X's efficiency (4) instead of Y's (5).
- **C — 10 days:** Result of adding 5 + 8 - 3 without computing total work units.
- **D — 16 days:** Obtained by taking X's work as 40 and Y's as 40.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Total work = 4(5) + 5(8) = 60 units; Y alone = 60 / 5 = 12 days.

### Q406 · Alternate-Day Working

A can do a work in 12 days and B in 15 days. If they work on alternate days starting with A, in how many days will the work be completed?

A. 13 days
B. 13.25 days
C. 13.5 days
D. 14 days

**Answer:** B. 13.25 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 13.25 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(12, 15) = 60 units. A = 5, B = 4 units/day. In 1 cycle of 2 days (A + B), work done = 9 units. In 6 cycles (12 days), work = 54 units. On Day 13, A does 5 units -> 59 units. Remaining 1 unit is done by B in 1/4 = 0.25 day. Total = 13.25 days.
- **A — 13 days:** Obtained by stopping after 6 cycles and adding 1 day without finishing the last 1 unit.
- **C — 13.5 days:** Result of dividing the last 1 unit by 2 instead of B's efficiency 4.
- **D — 14 days:** Obtained by rounding 13.25 days up to the next whole day.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Find full 2-day cycles (6 * 9 = 54), then track remaining 6 units day-by-day: A(5) + B(1/4).

### Q407 · Alternate-Day Working

A can complete a task in 10 days and B in 15 days. They work on alternate days starting with B. In how many days will the task be completed?

A. 10 days
B. 12 days
C. 11 days
D. 12.5 days

**Answer:** B. 12 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 12 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(10, 15) = 30 units. A = 3, B = 2 units/day. In 2 days (B + A), work = 2 + 3 = 5 units. Number of 2-day cycles = 30 / 5 = 6. Total days = 6 * 2 = 12 days.
- **A — 10 days:** Obtained by dividing 30 by A's efficiency (3) instead of the 2-day cycle sum.
- **C — 11 days:** Result of miscounting 6 cycles as 11 days.
- **D — 12.5 days:** Obtained by adding an extra half day unnecessarily.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When total work is an exact multiple of the 2-day cycle work (30 = 6 * 5), total days = 6 * 2 = 12.

### Q408 · Alternate-Day Working

A can finish a job in 16 days and B in 12 days. Starting with A, they work on alternate days. In how many days will the job be finished?

A. 13.25 days
B. 13.5 days
C. 13.75 days
D. 14 days

**Answer:** C. 13.75 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 13.75 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(16, 12) = 48 units. A = 3, B = 4 units/day. 2-day cycle (A+B) = 7 units. In 6 cycles (12 days), work = 42 units. Day 13 (A) does 3 units -> 45 units. Remaining 3 units done by B in 3/4 = 0.75 day. Total = 13.75 days.
- **A — 13.25 days:** Obtained by dividing remaining 3 units by 12 instead of B's efficiency 4.
- **B — 13.5 days:** Result of assuming B does 6 units/day instead of 4.
- **D — 14 days:** Obtained by rounding 13.75 days up to 14 days.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 6 cycles = 42 units (12 days); Day 13 A adds 3 -> 45; Day 14 B needs 3/4 day -> 13.75 days.

### Q409 · Alternate-Day Working

A, B, and C can do a piece of work in 10, 20, and 30 days respectively. A works every day, while B and C assist him on alternate days (B on Day 1, C on Day 2, and so on). In how many days will the work be completed?

A. 6 days
B. 7.5 days
C. 8 days
D. 7 days

**Answer:** D. 7 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 7 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(10, 20, 30) = 60 units. A = 6, B = 3, C = 2 units/day. Day 1 (A+B) = 9 units; Day 2 (A+C) = 8 units. 2-day cycle = 17 units. In 3 cycles (6 days), work = 51 units. On Day 7, (A+B) does 9 units -> 51 + 9 = 60 units. Total = 7 days.
- **A — 6 days:** Obtained by stopping after 3 cycles (6 days) and ignoring the remaining 9 units.
- **B — 7.5 days:** Result of dividing remaining 9 units by 6 instead of (A+B) = 9.
- **C — 8 days:** Obtained by taking 2-day cycle work as 15 units instead of 17 units.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 2-day block = (A+B) + (A+C) = 9 + 8 = 17; 3 blocks (6 days) = 51; Day 7 (A+B=9) finishes 60.

### Q410 · Alternate-Day Working

A can complete a work in 9 days and B in 12 days. If they work on alternate days starting with A, how many days will it take to complete the work?

A. 10.25 days
B. 10.5 days
C. 10.75 days
D. 11 days

**Answer:** A. 10.25 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 10.25 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(9, 12) = 36 units. A = 4, B = 3 units/day. 2-day cycle = 7 units. In 5 cycles (10 days), work = 35 units. Remaining 1 unit is done by A on Day 11 in 1/4 = 0.25 day. Total = 10.25 days.
- **B — 10.5 days:** Obtained by dividing remaining 1 unit by 2 instead of A's efficiency 4.
- **C — 10.75 days:** Result of assuming B works on Day 11 (1/3 day) instead of A.
- **D — 11 days:** Obtained by rounding 10.25 days up to 11 days.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 5 cycles = 35 units in 10 days; Day 11 is A's turn (4/day), so 1/4 day -> 10.25 days.

### Q411 · Alternate-Day Working

A can do a work in 14 days and B can do it in 21 days. They work on alternate days starting with B. In how many days will the work be finished?

A. 16 days
B. 17 days
C. 16.5 days
D. 18 days

**Answer:** B. 17 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 17 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(14, 21) = 42 units. A = 3, B = 2 units/day. 2-day cycle (B+A) = 5 units. In 8 cycles (16 days), work = 40 units. On Day 17, B does the remaining 2 units. Total = 17 days.
- **A — 16 days:** Obtained by stopping after 8 cycles (16 days) without accounting for the last 2 units.
- **C — 16.5 days:** Result of assuming A works on Day 17 (2/3 day) instead of B.
- **D — 18 days:** Obtained by miscalculating 8 cycles as 17 days and adding 1.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 8 cycles (16 days) = 40 units; Day 17 belongs to B (2 units/day), finishing the last 2 units.

### Q412 · Alternate-Day Working

A can complete a job in 18 days and B in 24 days. If they work on alternate days starting with A, in how many days will the job be completed?

A. 20 days
B. 21 days
C. 20.5 days
D. 20.75 days

**Answer:** C. 20.5 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 20.5 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(18, 24) = 72 units. A = 4, B = 3 units/day. 2-day cycle = 7 units. In 10 cycles (20 days), work = 70 units. Remaining 2 units are done by A on Day 21 in 2/4 = 0.5 day. Total = 20.5 days.
- **A — 20 days:** Obtained by ignoring the remaining 2 units after 10 cycles.
- **B — 21 days:** Result of rounding 20.5 days up to 21 days.
- **D — 20.75 days:** Obtained by dividing remaining 2 units by B's efficiency 3 (0.67) and misrounding.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 10 cycles (20 days) = 70 units; Day 21 is A's turn (4/day), so 2/4 = 0.5 day -> 20.5 days.

### Q413 · Alternate-Day Working

A, B, and C can finish a work in 12, 18, and 36 days respectively. If they work one day each in the order A, B, C, A, B, C..., in how many days will the work be completed?

A. 15 days
B. 16 days
C. 18 days
D. 20 days

**Answer:** C. 18 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 18 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(12, 18, 36) = 36 units. A = 3, B = 2, C = 1 unit/day. In 1 cycle of 3 days, work = 3 + 2 + 1 = 6 units. Number of cycles = 36 / 6 = 6. Total days = 6 * 3 = 18 days.
- **A — 15 days:** Obtained by taking 3-day cycle work as 7.2 units.
- **B — 16 days:** Result of miscalculating total work as 32 units.
- **D — 20 days:** Obtained by adding 2 extra days to the 6 cycles.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 3-day cycle = 3+2+1 = 6 units; 36 / 6 = 6 cycles -> 6 * 3 = 18 days.

### Q414 · Alternate-Day Working

A can finish a task in 8 days and B in 10 days. They work on alternate days starting with B. In how many days will the task be completed?

A. 8.8 days
B. 9.2 days
C. 8.5 days
D. 9 days

**Answer:** D. 9 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 9 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(8, 10) = 40 units. A = 5, B = 4 units/day. 2-day cycle = 9 units. In 4 cycles (8 days), work = 36 units. On Day 9, B does the remaining 4 units. Total = 9 days.
- **A — 8.8 days:** Obtained by dividing 40 by 4.5 (average daily rate) without checking turn order.
- **B — 9.2 days:** Result of assuming A works on Day 9 instead of B (4/5 = 0.8 -> 8.8).
- **C — 8.5 days:** Obtained by taking 4.5 cycles = 9 days and subtracting 0.5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 4 cycles (8 days) = 36 units; Day 9 is B's turn (4/day), finishing the remaining 4 units exactly.

### Q415 · Leaving Before Completion

A can do a work in 12 days and B in 18 days. They start working together, but A leaves 3 days before the completion of the work. In how many total days was the work completed?

A. 9 days
B. 8 days
C. 10 days
D. 11 days

**Answer:** A. 9 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 9 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(12, 18) = 36 units. A = 3, B = 2 units/day. If A had not left, in 3 extra days A would do 3 * 3 = 9 units. Adjusted work = 36 + 9 = 45 units. Total days = 45 / (3 + 2) = 9 days.
- **B — 8 days:** Obtained by subtracting A's 3 days of work instead of adding it.
- **C — 10 days:** Result of adding B's 3-day work (6) instead of A's (9) and rounding.
- **D — 11 days:** Obtained by adding 2 days to 9 due to double-counting the last 3 days.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When A leaves X days before completion, add A's X-day work to total work and divide by (A+B).

### Q416 · Leaving Before Completion

A, B, and C can complete a job in 24, 30, and 40 days respectively. They started working together, but C left 4 days before completion. In how many days was the job completed?

A. 10 days
B. 11 days
C. 12 days
D. 13 days

**Answer:** B. 11 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 11 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(24, 30, 40) = 120 units. A = 5, B = 4, C = 3 units/day. Add C's 4-day work = 4 * 3 = 12 units. Adjusted work = 120 + 12 = 132 units. Total days = 132 / (5 + 4 + 3) = 132 / 12 = 11 days.
- **A — 10 days:** Obtained by dividing 120 by 12 directly, ignoring that C left early.
- **C — 12 days:** Result of adding A's 4-day work (20) instead of C's (12) and misdividing.
- **D — 13 days:** Obtained by subtracting C's 4-day work from 120.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Add C's 4-day work (12) to 120 -> 132 / 12 = 11 days.

### Q417 · Leaving Before Completion

A can do a job in 10 days and B in 15 days. They work together for 3 days, after which A leaves. In how many days will B alone finish the remaining job?

A. 6 days
B. 8 days
C. 7.5 days
D. 9 days

**Answer:** C. 7.5 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 7.5 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(10, 15) = 30 units. A = 3, B = 2 units/day. In 3 days, A+B do 3 * 5 = 15 units. Remaining = 30 - 15 = 15 units. Time for B alone = 15 / 2 = 7.5 days.
- **A — 6 days:** Obtained by dividing remaining 15 units by A's efficiency (3) instead of B's (2).
- **B — 8 days:** Result of miscalculating 3-day work as 14 units.
- **D — 9 days:** Obtained by finding total days (3 + 6) instead of remaining days.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 3 days of (A+B) = 3 * (1/10 + 1/15) = 1/2 work; remaining 1/2 work by B = 15/2 = 7.5 days.

### Q418 · Leaving Before Completion

A can finish a work in 16 days and B in 24 days. They start together, but B leaves 4 days before completion. Find the total number of days taken to finish the work.

A. 10.4 days
B. 12 days
C. 11.2 days
D. 10.8 days

**Answer:** C. 11.2 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 11.2 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(16, 24) = 48 units. A = 3, B = 2 units/day. Add B's 4-day work = 4 * 2 = 8 units. Adjusted work = 48 + 8 = 56 units. Total days = 56 / (3 + 2) = 56 / 5 = 11.2 days.
- **A — 10.4 days:** Obtained by adding A's 4-day work (12) instead of B's (8) and dividing by 5.8.
- **B — 12 days:** Result of adding A's 4-day work (12) -> 60 / 5 = 12 days.
- **D — 10.8 days:** Obtained by miscalculating 56 / 5 as 10.8.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Add B's 4-day work (8) to 48 -> 56 / 5 = 11.2 days.

### Q419 · Leaving Before Completion

A can complete a task in 20 days and B in 25 days. A starts the task alone, and after 2 days B joins him. In how many total days will the task be completed?

A. 10 days
B. 11 days
C. 13 days
D. 12 days

**Answer:** D. 12 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 12 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(20, 25) = 100 units. A = 5, B = 4 units/day. In first 2 days, A does 2 * 5 = 10 units. Remaining = 90 units, done by (A+B) at 9 units/day in 90 / 9 = 10 days. Total = 2 + 10 = 12 days.
- **A — 10 days:** Obtained by reporting only the days A and B worked together (10 days), forgetting the first 2 days.
- **B — 11 days:** Result of dividing 100 by 9 directly.
- **C — 13 days:** Obtained by adding 3 initial days instead of 2.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** First 2 days (A alone) = 10 units; remaining 90 / (5+4) = 10 days; total = 2 + 10 = 12 days.

### Q420 · Leaving Before Completion

A, B, and C can do a work in 12, 15, and 20 days respectively. All three start together, A leaves after 1 day, and B leaves 2 days before completion. Find the total days taken.

A. 9 days
B. 8 days
C. 10 days
D. 7 days

**Answer:** A. 9 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 9 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(12, 15, 20) = 60 units. A = 5, B = 4, C = 3 units/day. Remove A's 1-day work (5) -> 55 units left for B and C. Add B's 2-day work (8) -> 63 units. Total days = 63 / (B + C) = 63 / 7 = 9 days.
- **B — 8 days:** Obtained by subtracting B's 2-day work instead of adding it.
- **C — 10 days:** Result of forgetting to subtract A's 1-day work (68/7).
- **D — 7 days:** Obtained by dividing 63 by (A+B) = 9 instead of (B+C) = 7.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Subtract work of early-leaver (A: 5) and add work of near-end leaver (B: 8), then divide by (B+C).

### Q421 · Leaving Before Completion

A can complete a job in 18 days and B in 27 days. They start together, and A leaves 3 days before completion. In how many total days is the job completed?

A. 11.6 days
B. 12.6 days
C. 12 days
D. 13.2 days

**Answer:** B. 12.6 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 12.6 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(18, 27) = 54 units. A = 3, B = 2 units/day. Add A's 3-day work = 9 units. Adjusted work = 54 + 9 = 63 units. Total days = 63 / (3 + 2) = 12.6 days.
- **A — 11.6 days:** Obtained by adding B's 3-day work (6) -> 60/5 = 12, or miscalculating 58/5.
- **C — 12 days:** Result of adding B's 3-day work (6) instead of A's (9) -> 60 / 5 = 12 days.
- **D — 13.2 days:** Obtained by miscalculating 63 / 5 as 13.2.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Add A's 3-day work (9) to 54 -> 63 / 5 = 12.6 days.

### Q422 · Leaving Before Completion

A can do a work in 14 days and B in 21 days. They start working together, but after some days A leaves and B finishes the remaining work in 6 days. After how many days did A leave?

A. 5 days
B. 7 days
C. 6 days
D. 8 days

**Answer:** C. 6 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 6 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(14, 21) = 42 units. A = 3, B = 2 units/day. In the last 6 days, B alone does 6 * 2 = 12 units. Remaining 42 - 12 = 30 units were done by A+B together at 5 units/day in 30 / 5 = 6 days. Thus, A left after 6 days.
- **A — 5 days:** Obtained by taking B's last 6 days work as 17 units.
- **B — 7 days:** Result of dividing 42 by 6 instead of subtracting B's solo work first.
- **D — 8 days:** Obtained by dividing 30 by A's efficiency (3) -> 10, or miscalculating 30/4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Subtract B's last 6 days (12 units) from 42; divide remaining 30 by (A+B) = 5 -> 6 days.

### Q423 · Leaving Before Completion

A, B, and C can finish a job in 20, 30, and 40 days respectively. All three start together; A leaves after 4 days and B leaves 4 days before completion. Find the total days taken.

A. 14 days
B. 15 days
C. 18 days
D. 16 days

**Answer:** D. 16 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 16 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(20, 30, 40) = 120 units. A = 6, B = 4, C = 3 units/day. Remove A's 4-day work (24) -> 96 units left. Add B's 4-day work (16) -> 112 units. Total days = 112 / (B + C) = 112 / 7 = 16 days.
- **A — 14 days:** Obtained by forgetting to add B's 4-day work (96 / 7 ≈ 13.7).
- **B — 15 days:** Result of subtracting B's 4-day work instead of adding it.
- **C — 18 days:** Obtained by dividing 126 by 7.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** (120 - 4*6 + 4*4) / (4 + 3) = 112 / 7 = 16 days.

### Q424 · Leaving Before Completion

A, B, and C can complete a task in 15, 20, and 30 days respectively. They start together, A leaves after 3 days, and B leaves 4 days before completion. In how many total days was the task completed?

A. 10 days
B. 11 days
C. 14 days
D. 12 days

**Answer:** D. 12 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 12 days (Correct):** Total work = LCM(15, 20, 30) = 60 units. A = 4, B = 3, C = 2 units/day. Remove A's 3-day work (12) -> 48 units. Add B's 4-day work (12) -> 60 units. Total days = 60 / (B + C) = 60 / 5 = 12 days.
- **A — 10 days:** Obtained by forgetting to add B's 4-day work (48 / 5 ≈ 9.6).
- **B — 11 days:** Result of miscalculating B+C as 5.5 units/day.
- **C — 14 days:** Obtained by adding A's 3-day work instead of subtracting it.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** (60 - 3*4 + 4*3) / (3 + 2) = 60 / 5 = 12 days.

### Q425 · Chain Rule (M1*D1*H1/W1)

18 men working 8 hours a day can complete a work in 15 days. In how many days will 20 men working 9 hours a day complete the same work?

A. 12 days
B. 10 days
C. 14 days
D. 15 days

**Answer:** A. 12 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 12 days (Correct):** Using M1 * D1 * H1 = M2 * D2 * H2: 18 * 15 * 8 = 20 * D2 * 9. D2 = (18 * 15 * 8) / (20 * 9) = 2160 / 180 = 12 days.
- **B — 10 days:** Obtained by misplacing one factor in the numerator/denominator.
- **C — 14 days:** Result of inverting the hours ratio (9/8 instead of 8/9).
- **D — 15 days:** Obtained by assuming days remain unchanged at 15.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** D2 = 15 * (18/20) * (8/9) = 12 days.

### Q426 · Chain Rule (M1*D1*H1/W1)

12 workers can build a 120-metre wall in 8 days. How many workers are required to build a 180-metre wall in 6 days?

A. 18 workers
B. 24 workers
C. 20 workers
D. 28 workers

**Answer:** B. 24 workers
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 24 workers (Correct):** Using (M1 * D1) / W1 = (M2 * D2) / W2: (12 * 8) / 120 = (M2 * 6) / 180. Simplifying: 96 / 120 = M2 / 30, so M2 = (4/5) * 30 = 24 workers.
- **A — 18 workers:** Obtained by multiplying 12 by 180/120 without adjusting for the change in days (8 to 6).
- **C — 20 workers:** Result of an arithmetic slip while simplifying 96 * 180 / (120 * 6).
- **D — 28 workers:** Obtained by inverting the days ratio (6/8 instead of 8/6) and miscalculating.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** M2 = 12 * (8/6) * (180/120) = 12 * (4/3) * (3/2) = 24 workers.

### Q427 · Chain Rule (M1*D1*H1/W1)

A garrison of 400 soldiers has food provisions for 30 days. After 10 days, 100 more soldiers join the garrison. For how many more days will the remaining food last?

A. 15 days
B. 18 days
C. 16 days
D. 20 days

**Answer:** C. 16 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 16 days (Correct):** After 10 days, remaining food is sufficient for 400 soldiers for (30 - 10) = 20 days. Now total soldiers = 400 + 100 = 500. Days = (400 * 20) / 500 = 16 days.
- **A — 15 days:** Obtained by dividing 400 * 20 by 533 instead of 500.
- **B — 18 days:** Result of using 100 soldiers leaving instead of joining.
- **D — 20 days:** Obtained by using 30 days instead of remaining 20 days.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Remaining man-days = 400 * (30 - 10) = 8000; divide by new strength 500 -> 16 days.

### Q428 · Chain Rule (M1*D1*H1/W1)

A contractor undertakes to complete a road in 40 days and employs 50 men. After 20 days, he finds that only 1/3 of the road is completed. How many extra men must he employ to finish the work on time?

A. 40 men
B. 60 men
C. 50 men
D. 75 men

**Answer:** C. 50 men
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 50 men (Correct):** Remaining work = 1 - 1/3 = 2/3; remaining days = 40 - 20 = 20 days. Using (M1*D1)/W1 = (M2*D2)/W2: (50 * 20) / (1/3) = ((50 + x) * 20) / (2/3). This gives 50 * 2 = 50 + x, so x = 50 extra men.
- **A — 40 men:** Obtained by miscalculating remaining work as 3/5 instead of 2/3.
- **B — 60 men:** Result of miscalculating total men needed as 110 instead of 100.
- **D — 75 men:** Obtained by reporting total men needed (100) minus 25.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** To double the work (1/3 to 2/3) in the same 20 days, double the workforce: 50 extra men.

### Q429 · Chain Rule (M1*D1*H1/W1)

15 machines produce 300 units in 4 days working 6 hours a day. How many units will 20 machines produce in 6 days working 8 hours a day?

A. 600 units
B. 720 units
C. 900 units
D. 800 units

**Answer:** D. 800 units
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 800 units (Correct):** Using W2 = W1 * (M2/M1) * (D2/D1) * (H2/H1): W2 = 300 * (20/15) * (6/4) * (8/6) = 300 * (4/3) * (3/2) * (4/3) = 800 units.
- **A — 600 units:** Obtained by omitting the hours factor (8/6).
- **B — 720 units:** Result of omitting one of the scaling ratios.
- **C — 900 units:** Obtained by miscalculating (4/3)*(3/2)*(4/3) as 3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Scale output directly: 300 * (20/15) * (6/4) * (8/6) = 800 units.

### Q430 · Chain Rule (M1*D1*H1/W1)

24 men can complete a job in 16 days. 8 days after they started, 8 men left the job. In how many days will the remaining work be completed by the remaining men?

A. 12 days
B. 10 days
C. 14 days
D. 16 days

**Answer:** A. 12 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 12 days (Correct):** After 8 days, remaining work = 24 * (16 - 8) = 24 * 8 = 192 man-days. Remaining men = 24 - 8 = 16. Days needed = 192 / 16 = 12 days.
- **B — 10 days:** Obtained by dividing 160 by 16 instead of 192 by 16.
- **C — 14 days:** Result of adding 8 men instead of subtracting 8 men.
- **D — 16 days:** Obtained by reporting the original 16 days.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Remaining man-days = 24 * 8 = 192; divide by 16 remaining men -> 12 days.

### Q431 · Chain Rule (M1*D1*H1/W1)

A fort had food provisions for 150 men for 45 days. After 10 days, 25 men left the fort. How many days will the remaining food last for the remaining men?

A. 40 days
B. 42 days
C. 38 days
D. 45 days

**Answer:** B. 42 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 42 days (Correct):** Remaining food = 150 * (45 - 10) = 150 * 35 man-days. Remaining men = 150 - 25 = 125. Days = (150 * 35) / 125 = 6 * 7 = 42 days.
- **A — 40 days:** Obtained by dividing 150 * 35 by 131.25.
- **C — 38 days:** Result of subtracting 10 days from 45 and adding only 3 days.
- **D — 45 days:** Obtained by assuming the food still lasts 45 days.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Days = 35 * (150 / 125) = 35 * (6/5) = 42 days.

### Q432 · Chain Rule (M1*D1*H1/W1)

If 16 persons working 7 hours a day can dig a 40-metre trench in 10 days, in how many days can 14 persons working 8 hours a day dig a 60-metre trench?

A. 12 days
B. 14 days
C. 15 days
D. 18 days

**Answer:** C. 15 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 15 days (Correct):** Using (M1*D1*H1)/W1 = (M2*D2*H2)/W2: (16 * 10 * 7) / 40 = (14 * D2 * 8) / 60. LHS = 28. RHS = (112/60) * D2. D2 = 28 * 60 / 112 = 60 / 4 = 15 days.
- **A — 12 days:** Obtained by forgetting to multiply by the trench length ratio (60/40).
- **B — 14 days:** Result of an arithmetic error in 28 * 60 / 112.
- **D — 18 days:** Obtained by inverting the hours ratio (8/7 instead of 7/8).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** D2 = 10 * (16/14) * (7/8) * (60/40) = 10 * (8/7) * (7/8) * (3/2) = 15 days.

### Q433 · Chain Rule (M1*D1*H1/W1)

A contractor employs 30 men to finish a project in 30 days. After 20 days, he finds that 3/4 of the project is already completed. How many men can he dismiss so that the project finishes exactly on time?

A. 5 men
B. 12 men
C. 15 men
D. 10 men

**Answer:** D. 10 men
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 10 men (Correct):** Remaining work = 1 - 3/4 = 1/4; remaining days = 30 - 20 = 10 days. Using (M1*D1)/W1 = (M2*D2)/W2: (30 * 20) / (3/4) = (M2 * 10) / (1/4). This gives 200 = 10 * M2, so M2 = 20 men needed. Men to dismiss = 30 - 20 = 10 men.
- **A — 5 men:** Obtained by miscalculating M2 as 25 men.
- **B — 12 men:** Result of miscalculating M2 as 18 men.
- **C — 15 men:** Obtained by halving 30 without using the 3:1 work ratio.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Needed men = 20; dismissed = 30 - 20 = 10 men.

### Q434 · Men-Women-Boys Equivalence

4 men or 6 women can complete a piece of work in 20 days. In how many days can 2 men and 7 women complete the same work?

A. 12 days
B. 10 days
C. 15 days
D. 14 days

**Answer:** A. 12 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 12 days (Correct):** 4 men = 6 women, so 2 men = 3 women. Therefore, 2 men + 7 women = 3 + 7 = 10 women. Since 6 women take 20 days, 10 women take (6 * 20) / 10 = 12 days.
- **B — 10 days:** Obtained by converting 2 men to 4 women instead of 3 women.
- **C — 15 days:** Result of dividing 6 * 20 by 8 instead of 10.
- **D — 14 days:** Obtained by averaging 20 and 8.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Convert 'and' into one unit using 'or': 2M + 7W = 10W; days = (6 * 20) / 10 = 12.

### Q435 · Men-Women-Boys Equivalence

3 men or 4 women or 6 boys can finish a job in 24 days. In how many days will 1 man, 1 woman, and 1 boy working together finish the same job?

A. 28 days
B. 32 days
C. 36 days
D. 24 days

**Answer:** B. 32 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 32 days (Correct):** 1 man's time = 3 * 24 = 72 days; 1 woman's time = 4 * 24 = 96 days; 1 boy's time = 6 * 24 = 144 days. LCM(72, 96, 144) = 288 units. Efficiencies: 1M = 4, 1W = 3, 1B = 2. Combined = 9 units/day. Time = 288 / 9 = 32 days.
- **A — 28 days:** Obtained by miscalculating combined efficiency as 10.3.
- **C — 36 days:** Result of using LCM = 288 and dividing by 8 instead of 9.
- **D — 24 days:** Obtained by assuming 1M + 1W + 1B equals 3M.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Days = 24 / (1/3 + 1/4 + 1/6) = 24 / (9/12) = 32 days.

### Q436 · Men-Women-Boys Equivalence

2 men and 3 women can finish a work in 10 days, while 3 men and 2 women can finish it in 8 days. In how many days can 2 men and 1 woman finish the work?

A. 10 days
B. 12 days
C. 12.5 days
D. 15 days

**Answer:** C. 12.5 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 12.5 days (Correct):** 10(2M + 3W) = 8(3M + 2W) => 20M + 30W = 24M + 16W => 4M = 14W => 2M = 7W. Total work = (7W + 3W) * 10 = 100W-days. Now 2M + 1W = 7W + 1W = 8W. Time = 100 / 8 = 12.5 days.
- **A — 10 days:** Obtained by taking 2M + 1W = 10W instead of 8W.
- **B — 12 days:** Result of rounding 12.5 down to 12 days.
- **D — 15 days:** Obtained by taking 2M = 6W instead of 7W.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equate total man-woman days: 4M = 14W -> 2M = 7W; substitute into total work 100W / 8W = 12.5.

### Q437 · Men-Women-Boys Equivalence

5 men and 4 boys can do a work in 6 days, while 3 men and 6 boys can do it in 8 days. In how many days can 1 man and 2 boys complete the same work?

A. 20 days
B. 24 days
C. 18 days
D. 22 days

**Answer:** B. 24 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 24 days (Correct):** 6(5M + 4B) = 8(3M + 6B) => 30M + 24B = 24M + 48B => 6M = 24B => 1M = 4B. Total work = (5*4B + 4B) * 6 = 144B-days. 1M + 2B = 4B + 2B = 6B. Time = 144 / 6 = 24 days.
- **A — 20 days:** Obtained by taking 1M = 3B instead of 4B.
- **C — 18 days:** Result of dividing 144 by 8 instead of 6.
- **D — 22 days:** Obtained by miscalculating total work as 132B-days.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equate work: 6M = 24B -> 1M = 4B; 1M+2B = 6B -> 144 / 6 = 24 days.

### Q438 · Men-Women-Boys Equivalence

6 men or 9 women can reap a field in 15 days. In how many days can 4 men and 6 women reap the same field?

A. 12 days
B. 10.5 days
C. 11.25 days
D. 13.5 days

**Answer:** C. 11.25 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 11.25 days (Correct):** 6 men = 9 women, so 2 men = 3 women, hence 4 men = 6 women. Therefore, 4 men + 6 women = 6 + 6 = 12 women. Since 9 women take 15 days, 12 women take (9 * 15) / 12 = 135 / 12 = 11.25 days.
- **A — 12 days:** Obtained by rounding 11.25 up to 12 days.
- **B — 10.5 days:** Result of dividing 135 by 12.8 instead of 12.
- **D — 13.5 days:** Obtained by taking 4 men = 4 women instead of 6 women.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 4M + 6W = 12W; days = (9 * 15) / 12 = 11.25 days.

### Q439 · Men-Women-Boys Equivalence

8 men and 12 women can complete a task in 10 days, while 6 men and 8 women can complete it in 14 days. In how many days can 1 woman alone complete the task?

A. 240 days
B. 260 days
C. 300 days
D. 280 days

**Answer:** D. 280 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 280 days (Correct):** 10(8M + 12W) = 14(6M + 8W) => 80M + 120W = 84M + 112W => 4M = 8W => 1M = 2W. Total work = (8*2W + 12W) * 10 = 280W-days. Thus, 1 woman alone takes 280 days.
- **A — 240 days:** Obtained by taking 1M = 1.5W instead of 2W.
- **B — 260 days:** Result of miscalculating 16W + 12W as 26W.
- **C — 300 days:** Obtained by taking 1M = 2.25W.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 4M = 8W gives 1M = 2W; total work = (16W + 12W) * 10 = 280 woman-days.

### Q440 · Men-Women-Boys Equivalence

4 men and 6 boys can do a work in 8 days, whereas 3 men and 7 boys can do it in 10 days. In how many days can 1 man and 9 boys do the same work?

A. 20 days
B. 16 days
C. 18 days
D. 24 days

**Answer:** A. 20 days
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 20 days (Correct):** 8(4M + 6B) = 10(3M + 7B) => 32M + 48B = 30M + 70B => 2M = 22B => 1M = 11B. Total work = (4*11B + 6B) * 8 = 50B * 8 = 400B-days. 1M + 9B = 11B + 9B = 20B. Time = 400 / 20 = 20 days.
- **B — 16 days:** Obtained by dividing 400 by 25 instead of 20.
- **C — 18 days:** Result of miscalculating 1M = 10B instead of 11B.
- **D — 24 days:** Obtained by miscalculating total work as 480B-days.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 2M = 22B -> 1M = 11B; total work = 400B; 1M+9B = 20B -> 400/20 = 20 days.

### Q441 · Wages Distribution

A can do a work in 12 days and B in 18 days. They work together to complete the work and earn ₹3,000. What is A's share?

A. ₹1,200
B. ₹1,800
C. ₹1,500
D. ₹2,000

**Answer:** B. ₹1,800
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹1,800 (Correct):** When working together for the same duration, wages are shared in the ratio of efficiencies. Ratio of days A : B = 12 : 18 = 2 : 3, so efficiency ratio A : B = 3 : 2. A's share = (3 / 5) * 3000 = ₹1,800.
- **A — ₹1,200:** Obtained by finding B's share (2/5 of ₹3,000) instead of A's share.
- **C — ₹1,500:** Result of splitting the wages equally.
- **D — ₹2,000:** Obtained by using an incorrect ratio 2 : 1.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Wages split in inverse ratio of days (3:2); A's share = 3/5 * 3000 = ₹1,800.

### Q442 · Wages Distribution

A, B, and C can do a work in 10, 12, and 15 days respectively. They finish the work together and receive ₹4,500. Find the difference between A's and C's shares.

A. ₹300
B. ₹450
C. ₹600
D. ₹900

**Answer:** C. ₹600
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹600 (Correct):** Total work = LCM(10, 12, 15) = 60 units. Efficiencies: A = 6, B = 5, C = 4 (sum = 15). Difference between A and C = (6 - 4) / 15 * 4500 = (2 / 15) * 4500 = ₹600.
- **A — ₹300:** Obtained by finding the difference between A and B (1/15 * 4500).
- **B — ₹450:** Result of taking 1/10 of ₹4,500.
- **D — ₹900:** Obtained by taking 3/15 of ₹4,500 instead of 2/15.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Efficiency ratio = 6:5:4; difference (6-4)/15 = 2/15 of ₹4,500 = ₹600.

### Q443 · Wages Distribution

A and B undertake a job for ₹2,400. A alone can do it in 8 days and B alone in 12 days. With the help of C, they finish it in 4 days. What is C's share?

A. ₹300
B. ₹500
C. ₹600
D. ₹400

**Answer:** D. ₹400
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹400 (Correct):** Total work = LCM(8, 12, 4) = 24 units. A = 3, B = 2, (A+B+C) = 6 units/day. Thus C = 6 - 3 - 2 = 1 unit/day. C's share = (1 / 6) * 2400 = ₹400.
- **A — ₹300:** Obtained by taking C's share as 1/8 of ₹2,400.
- **B — ₹500:** Result of an arithmetic slip while dividing 2400 by 6.
- **C — ₹600:** Obtained by dividing ₹2,400 by 4 days instead of 6 efficiency units.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** C's share fraction = 1 - 4/8 - 4/12 = 1/6; 1/6 * 2400 = ₹400.

### Q444 · Wages Distribution

A, B, and C work for 4, 5, and 6 days respectively to complete a project. If their daily wages are in the ratio 3 : 4 : 5 and their total earning is ₹6,200, find B's share.

A. ₹2,000
B. ₹1,200
C. ₹3,000
D. ₹2,400

**Answer:** A. ₹2,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ₹2,000 (Correct):** Total wage ratio = (4 * 3) : (5 * 4) : (6 * 5) = 12 : 20 : 30 = 6 : 10 : 15 (sum = 31). B's share = (10 / 31) * 6200 = ₹2,000.
- **B — ₹1,200:** Obtained by finding A's share (6/31 * 6200).
- **C — ₹3,000:** Result of finding C's share (15/31 * 6200).
- **D — ₹2,400:** Obtained by dividing ₹6,200 using only daily wage ratio 3:4:5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Multiply days by daily wage ratio: 12:20:30 = 6:10:15; B = 10/31 * 6200 = ₹2,000.

### Q445 · Wages Distribution

A can do a job in 20 days and B in 30 days. They work together for 6 days, and then C finishes the remaining job in 6 days. If the total wage is ₹4,800, find C's share.

A. ₹1,600
B. ₹2,400
C. ₹2,000
D. ₹1,800

**Answer:** B. ₹2,400
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹2,400 (Correct):** Total work = LCM(20, 30) = 60 units. A = 3, B = 2 units/day. In 6 days, A+B do 6 * 5 = 30 units (half the work). C does the remaining 30 units (half the work). C's share = (30 / 60) * 4800 = ₹2,400.
- **A — ₹1,600:** Obtained by dividing ₹4,800 equally among A, B, and C.
- **C — ₹2,000:** Result of miscalculating A+B's 6-day work as 35 units.
- **D — ₹1,800:** Obtained by using 6/16 of ₹4,800.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Wages are proportional to work done: A+B did 1/2, so C did 1/2 -> ₹2,400.

### Q446 · Wages Distribution

X and Y earn ₹3,600 for a job. X alone can do it in 15 days and Y alone in 20 days. They work on alternate days starting with X. Find X's share.

A. ₹1,800
B. ₹2,000
C. ₹2,160
D. ₹1,440

**Answer:** C. ₹2,160
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹2,160 (Correct):** Total work = LCM(15, 20) = 60 units. X = 4, Y = 3 units/day. 2-day cycle = 7 units. In 8 cycles (16 days), work = 56 units. On Day 17, X finishes the remaining 4 units. So X worked 9 days (36 units) and Y worked 8 days (24 units). Ratio = 36 : 24 = 3 : 2. X's share = (3/5) * 3600 = ₹2,160.
- **A — ₹1,800:** Obtained by splitting the wage equally.
- **B — ₹2,000:** Result of dividing ₹3,600 in the ratio of efficiencies (4:3) without counting actual days worked.
- **D — ₹1,440:** Obtained by finding Y's share (2/5 * 3600) instead of X's.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** In alternate-day work, count actual work done by each: X does 36/60 = 3/5 of work -> ₹2,160.

### Q447 · Pipes & Cisterns

Pipe A can fill a tank in 12 hours, Pipe B can fill it in 15 hours, and Pipe C can empty it in 20 hours. If all three pipes are opened together, in how many hours will the tank be full?

A. 8 hours
B. 9 hours
C. 12 hours
D. 10 hours

**Answer:** D. 10 hours
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 10 hours (Correct):** Total capacity = LCM(12, 15, 20) = 60 units. Rates: A = +5, B = +4, C = -3 units/hour. Net rate = 5 + 4 - 3 = 6 units/hour. Time = 60 / 6 = 10 hours.
- **A — 8 hours:** Obtained by adding C's rate instead of subtracting (60 / 12 = 5, or miscalculation).
- **B — 9 hours:** Result of taking net rate as 6.67 units/hour.
- **C — 12 hours:** Obtained by miscalculating A+B-C as 5 units/hour (60 / 5 = 12).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Net rate = (+5) + (+4) + (-3) = +6 units/hr; time = 60 / 6 = 10 hours.

### Q448 · Pipes & Cisterns

A pipe can fill a cistern in 8 hours, but due to a leak at the bottom, it takes 12 hours to fill. If the cistern is full, in how many hours will the leak alone empty it?

A. 24 hours
B. 16 hours
C. 20 hours
D. 32 hours

**Answer:** A. 24 hours
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 24 hours (Correct):** Total capacity = LCM(8, 12) = 24 units. Inlet rate = +3 units/hour; (Inlet - Leak) = +2 units/hour. Leak rate = 3 - 2 = 1 unit/hour. Time to empty = 24 / 1 = 24 hours.
- **B — 16 hours:** Obtained by taking leak rate as 1.5 units/hour.
- **C — 20 hours:** Result of adding 8 and 12 directly.
- **D — 32 hours:** Obtained by multiplying 8 by 4 instead of using LCM.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Leak time = (8 * 12) / (12 - 8) = 96 / 4 = 24 hours.

### Q449 · Pipes & Cisterns

Two pipes A and B can fill a tank in 20 minutes and 30 minutes respectively. Both are opened together, after how many minutes should pipe A be closed so that the tank is full in 18 minutes?

A. 6 minutes
B. 8 minutes
C. 10 minutes
D. 12 minutes

**Answer:** B. 8 minutes
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 8 minutes (Correct):** Capacity = LCM(20, 30) = 60 units. A = 3, B = 2 units/min. Since B runs for all 18 minutes, B fills 18 * 2 = 36 units. Remaining 60 - 36 = 24 units must be filled by A in 24 / 3 = 8 minutes.
- **A — 6 minutes:** Obtained by assuming A runs for 18 minutes and finding B's time.
- **C — 10 minutes:** Result of miscalculating remaining units as 30 instead of 24.
- **D — 12 minutes:** Obtained by taking 36 / 3 = 12 minutes.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** B works full 18 min (36 units); A fills remaining 24 units in 24/3 = 8 minutes.

### Q450 · Pipes & Cisterns

Pipe A can fill a tank in 4 hours and Pipe B can empty it in 6 hours. If they are opened on alternate hours starting with A, in how many hours will the tank be full?

A. 18 hours
B. 24 hours
C. 19 hours
D. 20 hours

**Answer:** C. 19 hours
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 19 hours (Correct):** Capacity = LCM(4, 6) = 12 units. A = +3, B = -2 units/hour. In 2 hours (A then B), net fill = +1 unit. Before the final fill of 3 units by A, 12 - 3 = 9 units must be filled. 9 cycles = 18 hours gives 9 units. In the 19th hour, A fills 3 units to reach 12. Total = 19 hours.
- **A — 18 hours:** Obtained by stopping at 9 cycles (18 hours) without adding the final filling hour.
- **B — 24 hours:** Result of multiplying 12 units by 2 hours/unit (24 hours), ignoring that the tank stays full once A finishes.
- **D — 20 hours:** Obtained by adding 1 extra hour after the 19th hour.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Always subtract inlet's 1-hour fill (3) first: 9 cycles (18h) = 9 units; +1h by A = 19 hours.
