## Chapter 8: Time, Speed & Distance (Trains & Boats)

### Q451 · Average Speed & Harmonic Mean

A car travels from town A to town B at a speed of 45 km/h and returns from B to A at 55 km/h. What is the average speed of the car for the entire round trip?

A. 49.5 km/h
B. 50 km/h
C. 48.5 km/h
D. 51.2 km/h

**Answer:** A. 49.5 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 49.5 km/h (Correct):** For equal distances at speeds S1 = 45 km/h and S2 = 55 km/h, Average Speed = (2 * S1 * S2) / (S1 + S2) = (2 * 45 * 55) / (45 + 55) = 4950 / 100 = 49.5 km/h.
- **B — 50 km/h:** Result of taking the arithmetic mean (45 + 55)/2 instead of the harmonic mean.
- **C — 48.5 km/h:** Value obtained from a rounding or subtraction error after computing the product.
- **D — 51.2 km/h:** Result of miscalculating the denominator or adding an extra 1.7 km/h.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For two equal distances at speeds x and y, always use 2xy/(x+y); note that the harmonic mean is always slightly less than the arithmetic mean (50 km/h).

### Q452 · Average Speed & Harmonic Mean

A cyclist covers three equal stretches of a journey at speeds of 12 km/h, 15 km/h, and 20 km/h respectively. Find his average speed for the whole journey.

A. 15.67 km/h
B. 15 km/h
C. 14.4 km/h
D. 16 km/h

**Answer:** B. 15 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 15 km/h (Correct):** Let each equal stretch be LCM(12, 15, 20) = 60 km. Total distance = 3 * 60 = 180 km. Times taken = 60/12 = 5 h, 60/15 = 4 h, 60/20 = 3 h. Total time = 5 + 4 + 3 = 12 hours. Average speed = 180 / 12 = 15 km/h.
- **A — 15.67 km/h:** Result of taking the simple arithmetic mean (12 + 15 + 20)/3 = 15.67 km/h.
- **C — 14.4 km/h:** Value obtained by using only two speeds or miscalculating total time as 12.5 hours.
- **D — 16 km/h:** Result of taking the average of the extreme speeds (12 + 20)/2 = 16 km/h.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Assume each equal distance to be the LCM of the speeds (60 km here) to turn fractions into whole-number hours in seconds.

### Q453 · Average Speed & Harmonic Mean

A man drives 180 km at 45 km/h, the next 240 km at 60 km/h, and the final 180 km at 90 km/h. What is his average speed for the entire journey?

A. 65 km/h
B. 62.5 km/h
C. 60 km/h
D. 58.5 km/h

**Answer:** C. 60 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 60 km/h (Correct):** Total distance = 180 + 240 + 180 = 600 km. Time taken = 180/45 + 240/60 + 180/90 = 4 + 4 + 2 = 10 hours. Average speed = 600 / 10 = 60 km/h.
- **A — 65 km/h:** Result of averaging the three speeds directly: (45 + 60 + 90)/3 = 65 km/h.
- **B — 62.5 km/h:** Value obtained if total time is miscalculated as 9.6 hours.
- **D — 58.5 km/h:** Result of taking total distance as 585 km or miscomputing one time segment.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When distances are unequal, never use the harmonic mean; always compute Total Distance / Total Time.

### Q454 · Average Speed & Harmonic Mean

A person covers one-third of a journey at 20 km/h, one-fourth at 30 km/h, and the remaining distance at 50 km/h. What is his average speed for the whole journey?

A. 33.33 km/h
B. 32 km/h
C. 28.5 km/h
D. 30 km/h

**Answer:** D. 30 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 30 km/h (Correct):** Remaining fraction = 1 - 1/3 - 1/4 = 5/12. Let total distance = 120 km (multiple of 12 and speeds). Distances: 40 km at 20 km/h (2 h), 30 km at 30 km/h (1 h), 50 km at 50 km/h (1 h). Total time = 2 + 1 + 1 = 4 hours. Average speed = 120 / 4 = 30 km/h.
- **A — 33.33 km/h:** Result of taking the arithmetic mean (20 + 30 + 50)/3 = 33.33 km/h.
- **B — 32 km/h:** Value obtained by using 3/12 instead of 5/12 for the remaining distance.
- **C — 28.5 km/h:** Result of miscalculating total time as 4.21 hours.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Pick a convenient total distance (like 120 km or 600 km) divisible by all denominators and speeds so every time term becomes an integer.

### Q455 · Average Speed & Harmonic Mean

A bus travels at 48 km/h for 2.5 hours and then at 60 km/h for 1.5 hours. Find the average speed of the bus for the 4-hour journey.

A. 52.5 km/h
B. 54 km/h
C. 53.5 km/h
D. 55 km/h

**Answer:** A. 52.5 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 52.5 km/h (Correct):** Distance 1 = 48 * 2.5 = 120 km. Distance 2 = 60 * 1.5 = 90 km. Total distance = 120 + 90 = 210 km. Total time = 2.5 + 1.5 = 4 hours. Average speed = 210 / 4 = 52.5 km/h.
- **B — 54 km/h:** Result of taking the unweighted average (48 + 60)/2 = 54 km/h.
- **C — 53.5 km/h:** Value obtained by swapping the two time durations (48*1.5 + 60*2.5)/4 = 55.5 or a rounding slip.
- **D — 55 km/h:** Result of adding 1 km/h after miscalculating 48 * 2.5 as 130 km.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When time intervals t1 and t2 are given, average speed is the time-weighted mean: (S1*t1 + S2*t2)/(t1 + t2).

### Q456 · Average Speed & Harmonic Mean

A motorist travels from P to Q at 36 km/h and returns at 54 km/h. If the total time taken for the round trip is 5 hours, what is the distance between P and Q?

A. 216 km
B. 108 km
C. 112.5 km
D. 90 km

**Answer:** B. 108 km
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 108 km (Correct):** Average speed = (2 * 36 * 54) / (36 + 54) = 3888 / 90 = 43.2 km/h. Total distance (round trip) = 43.2 * 5 = 216 km. One-way distance PQ = 216 / 2 = 108 km.
- **A — 216 km:** Result of reporting the total round-trip distance (216 km) instead of the one-way distance.
- **C — 112.5 km:** Value obtained by using arithmetic mean 45 km/h: 45 * 5 / 2 = 112.5 km.
- **D — 90 km:** Result of multiplying the harmonic mean by 2.5 incorrectly or using 5 hours for one way.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** One-way distance = [S1*S2 / (S1 + S2)] * Total Time = (36 * 54 / 90) * 5 = 108 km (no factor of 2 needed!).

### Q457 · Average Speed & Harmonic Mean

A train covers the first 40% of a journey at 40 km/h and the remaining 60% at 60 km/h. What is its average speed for the entire journey?

A. 48 km/h
B. 52 km/h
C. 50 km/h
D. 49.5 km/h

**Answer:** C. 50 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 50 km/h (Correct):** Let total distance = 100 km. First 40 km at 40 km/h takes 1 hour. Remaining 60 km at 60 km/h takes 1 hour. Total time = 2 hours. Average speed = 100 / 2 = 50 km/h.
- **A — 48 km/h:** Result of blindly applying 2*40*60/(40+60) = 48 km/h, which only works for equal (50%-50%) distances.
- **B — 52 km/h:** Value obtained from weighted arithmetic mean (0.4*40 + 0.6*60) = 52 km/h.
- **D — 49.5 km/h:** Result of averaging 48 km/h and 51 km/h or miscalculating total time.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Assume total distance = 100 km for percentage splits; here 40/40 + 60/60 = 2 h, so 100/2 = 50 km/h mentally!

### Q458 · Average Speed & Harmonic Mean

A hiker walks uphill at 4 km/h and returns downhill along the same path at 6 km/h. If he takes 3 hours 45 minutes in total, how far did he walk in total (both ways)?

A. 9 km
B. 18.75 km
C. 15 km
D. 18 km

**Answer:** D. 18 km
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 18 km (Correct):** Average speed = (2 * 4 * 6) / (4 + 6) = 48 / 10 = 4.8 km/h. Total time = 3 h 45 min = 3.75 hours = 15/4 hours. Total distance (both ways) = 4.8 * (15/4) = 1.2 * 15 = 18 km.
- **A — 9 km:** Result of finding only the one-way distance (9 km) instead of total distance walked.
- **B — 18.75 km:** Value obtained by using arithmetic mean 5 km/h: 5 * 3.75 = 18.75 km.
- **C — 15 km:** Result of converting 3 h 45 min as 3.45 hours or miscalculating 4.8 * 3.75.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Average speed = 4.8 km/h; multiply by 15/4 hours directly: (4.8/4)*15 = 1.2*15 = 18 km.

### Q459 · Average Speed & Harmonic Mean

A car travels at speeds of 30 km/h, 40 km/h, 60 km/h, and 120 km/h over four equal consecutive distances. What is its average speed for the whole journey?

A. 48 km/h
B. 62.5 km/h
C. 50 km/h
D. 45 km/h

**Answer:** A. 48 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 48 km/h (Correct):** Let each equal distance = LCM(30, 40, 60, 120) = 120 km. Total distance = 4 * 120 = 480 km. Times = 120/30 + 120/40 + 120/60 + 120/120 = 4 + 3 + 2 + 1 = 10 hours. Average speed = 480 / 10 = 48 km/h.
- **B — 62.5 km/h:** Result of taking the arithmetic mean (30 + 40 + 60 + 120)/4 = 62.5 km/h.
- **C — 50 km/h:** Value obtained by miscounting the sum of times as 9.6 hours.
- **D — 45 km/h:** Result of omitting one segment or miscalculating 480/10.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Set each equal segment to LCM = 120 km; times become 4, 3, 2, 1 (sum = 10 h), giving 480/10 = 48 km/h instantly.

### Q460 · Early/Late & Speed-Time Ratios

Walking at 4 km/h, a student reaches school 15 minutes late. Walking at 6 km/h, he reaches 10 minutes early. What is the distance from his home to school?

A. 4 km
B. 5 km
C. 6 km
D. 4.5 km

**Answer:** B. 5 km
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 5 km (Correct):** Using D = [S1 * S2 / (S2 - S1)] * dT: Here S1 = 4 km/h, S2 = 6 km/h, and time difference dT = 15 + 10 = 25 minutes = 25/60 hours = 5/12 hours. D = (4 * 6 / (6 - 4)) * (5/12) = (24 / 2) * (5/12) = 12 * (5/12) = 5 km.
- **A — 4 km:** Result of subtracting the time differences (15 - 10 = 5 min) instead of adding late + early.
- **C — 6 km:** Value obtained by using 30 minutes instead of 25 minutes for dT.
- **D — 4.5 km:** Result of forgetting to convert minutes properly or miscalculating 12 * 5/12.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Late + Early means ADD the times (15+10 = 25 min). Formula: [4*6/(6-4)] * (25/60) = 12 * 5/12 = 5 km.

### Q461 · Early/Late & Speed-Time Ratios

A man covers a certain distance by scooter at 30 km/h and reaches his office 12 minutes late. If he travels at 40 km/h, he still reaches 3 minutes late. Find the distance to his office.

A. 24 km
B. 30 km
C. 18 km
D. 15 km

**Answer:** C. 18 km
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 18 km (Correct):** Both cases are late, so dT = 12 - 3 = 9 minutes = 9/60 = 3/20 hours. D = [30 * 40 / (40 - 30)] * (3/20) = (1200 / 10) * (3/20) = 120 * (3/20) = 18 km.
- **A — 24 km:** Result of using dT = 12 minutes instead of 12 - 3 = 9 minutes.
- **B — 30 km:** Value obtained by adding 12 + 3 = 15 minutes instead of subtracting (late-late).
- **D — 15 km:** Result of arithmetic error in 120 * 3/20.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Same-side deviations (Late-Late or Early-Early) -> SUBTRACT: dT = 12 - 3 = 9 min. D = 120 * (9/60) = 18 km.

### Q462 · Early/Late & Speed-Time Ratios

Walking at 5/7 of his usual speed, a man reaches his destination 16 minutes late. What is his usual time to cover the distance?

A. 56 minutes
B. 32 minutes
C. 48 minutes
D. 40 minutes

**Answer:** D. 40 minutes
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 40 minutes (Correct):** Since speed becomes 5/7 of usual, time becomes 7/5 of usual time T. Extra time = (7/5 - 1)T = (2/5)T = 16 minutes. Therefore, T = 16 * 5 / 2 = 40 minutes.
- **A — 56 minutes:** Result of reporting the new delayed time (40 + 16 = 56 minutes) instead of the usual time.
- **B — 32 minutes:** Value obtained by multiplying 16 by 2 instead of 5/2.
- **C — 48 minutes:** Result of setting (2/6)T = 16 instead of (2/5)T = 16.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If speed is a/b of usual (a < b), Usual Time = [a / (b - a)] * delay = [5 / (7 - 5)] * 16 = 40 minutes!

### Q463 · Early/Late & Speed-Time Ratios

By increasing his speed by 25%, a cyclist reaches his workplace 12 minutes early. Find his usual travel time.

A. 60 minutes
B. 48 minutes
C. 45 minutes
D. 72 minutes

**Answer:** A. 60 minutes
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 60 minutes (Correct):** 25% = 1/4 increase, so new speed = 5/4 of usual speed. Thus new time = 4/5 of usual time T. Time saved = (1 - 4/5)T = (1/5)T = 12 minutes. Hence T = 12 * 5 = 60 minutes.
- **B — 48 minutes:** Result of finding the new reduced time (60 - 12 = 48 minutes) instead of usual time.
- **C — 45 minutes:** Value obtained by taking 25% of 180 or miscalculating 12 * 3.75.
- **D — 72 minutes:** Result of using (1/6)T = 12 instead of (1/5)T = 12.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Speed +25% (4 -> 5) means Time (5 -> 4), saving 1 unit = 12 min. Usual time = 5 units = 60 min.

### Q464 · Early/Late & Speed-Time Ratios

If a train runs at 50 km/h, it arrives at its destination late by 11 minutes, but if it runs at 60 km/h, it arrives late by 5 minutes. What is the correct (scheduled) time for the train to complete its journey?

A. 30 minutes
B. 25 minutes
C. 35 minutes
D. 20 minutes

**Answer:** B. 25 minutes
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 25 minutes (Correct):** dT = 11 - 5 = 6 minutes = 1/10 hour. Distance = [50 * 60 / (60 - 50)] * (1/10) = 300 * (1/10) = 30 km. Time taken at 60 km/h = 30/60 = 0.5 hour = 30 minutes. Since it is still 5 minutes late at 60 km/h, scheduled time = 30 - 5 = 25 minutes.
- **A — 30 minutes:** Result of stopping after finding the time at 60 km/h (30 minutes) without subtracting the 5-minute delay.
- **C — 35 minutes:** Value obtained by adding 5 minutes instead of subtracting (30 + 5 = 35 minutes).
- **D — 20 minutes:** Result of subtracting 11 minutes from 30 minutes instead of 5 minutes.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use ratio: Speed ratio 5:6 -> Time ratio 6:5. Difference 1 unit = 11 - 5 = 6 min. Time at 60 km/h = 5*6 = 30 min, so right time = 30 - 5 = 25 min.

### Q465 · Early/Late & Speed-Time Ratios

A car travels a distance at a certain speed. Had it moved 5 km/h faster, it would have taken 40 minutes less. Had it moved 4 km/h slower, it would have taken 40 minutes more. Find the distance.

A. 160 km
B. 120 km
C. 240 km
D. 180 km

**Answer:** C. 240 km
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 240 km (Correct):** When time saved equals time lost (both 40 min = 2/3 h), usual speed S = (2 * d1 * d2) / (d1 - d2) = (2 * 5 * 4) / (5 - 4) = 40 km/h. Then D = [S(S+5)/5] * (2/3) = (40 * 45 / 5) * (2/3) = 360 * (2/3) = 240 km.
- **A — 160 km:** Result of using time = 40/90 or miscalculating S = 30 km/h.
- **B — 120 km:** Value obtained by forgetting the factor of 2 in S = 2*d1*d2/(d1-d2) or halving the distance.
- **D — 180 km:** Result of using S = 35 km/h in the distance formula.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When dT is identical for +a and -b, Usual Speed S = 2ab/(a-b) = 2*5*4/1 = 40 km/h. Then plug into S(S+a)/a * dT.

### Q466 · Early/Late & Speed-Time Ratios

The speeds of three cars are in the ratio 3 : 4 : 5. What is the ratio of the times taken by them to cover the same distance?

A. 5 : 4 : 3
B. 12 : 15 : 20
C. 15 : 12 : 10
D. 20 : 15 : 12

**Answer:** D. 20 : 15 : 12
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 20 : 15 : 12 (Correct):** For a fixed distance, Time is inversely proportional to Speed. Ratio of times = 1/3 : 1/4 : 1/5. Multiplying by LCM(3, 4, 5) = 60 gives 20 : 15 : 12.
- **A — 5 : 4 : 3:** Result of simply reversing the speed ratio (5 : 4 : 3), which only works for two speeds.
- **B — 12 : 15 : 20:** Value obtained by reversing the correct time ratio.
- **C — 15 : 12 : 10:** Result of multiplying by 45 or using wrong LCM.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Hide one number and multiply the other two: (4*5) : (3*5) : (3*4) = 20 : 15 : 12.

### Q467 · Early/Late & Speed-Time Ratios

A person can cover a distance in 10 hours if he travels at a certain speed. If he increases his speed by 20%, how much time will he save?

A. 1 hour 40 minutes
B. 2 hours
C. 1 hour 20 minutes
D. 1 hour 30 minutes

**Answer:** A. 1 hour 40 minutes
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 1 hour 40 minutes (Correct):** Speed increases by 20% (5 -> 6), so time decreases in ratio 6 : 5. Since 6 units = 10 hours, 1 unit saved = 10/6 hours = 5/3 hours = 1 hour 40 minutes.
- **B — 2 hours:** Result of taking 20% of 10 hours = 2 hours directly.
- **C — 1 hour 20 minutes:** Value obtained by converting 5/3 hours as 1 hour 20 minutes (50 min/h error).
- **D — 1 hour 30 minutes:** Result of taking 15% of 10 hours = 1.5 hours.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Speed +20% (1/5) -> Time -16.67% (1/6). 1/6 of 10 hours = 100 minutes = 1 hr 40 min.

### Q468 · Early/Late & Speed-Time Ratios

In a 1000 m race, A beats B by 100 m and B beats C by 100 m. By how many metres does A beat C in the same race?

A. 200 m
B. 190 m
C. 180 m
D. 210 m

**Answer:** B. 190 m
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 190 m (Correct):** When A runs 1000 m, B runs 900 m (ratio A:B = 10:9). When B runs 1000 m, C runs 900 m (ratio B:C = 10:9). Combining ratios: A:B:C = 100 : 90 : 81. Thus when A runs 1000 m, C runs 810 m, so A beats C by 1000 - 810 = 190 m.
- **A — 200 m:** Result of simply adding the two margins: 100 + 100 = 200 m.
- **C — 180 m:** Value obtained by subtracting 20 instead of 10 from 200 m.
- **D — 210 m:** Result of adding 10% of 100 m instead of subtracting it.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Successive percentage drop: 10% and 10% -> 10 + 10 - (10*10)/100 = 19% of 1000 m = 190 m.

### Q469 · Early/Late & Speed-Time Ratios

A delivery van leaves a warehouse at 8:00 AM at 45 km/h. After covering one-third of the distance, it faces traffic and travels the rest at 30 km/h, reaching 20 minutes later than scheduled. If its scheduled speed was 45 km/h for the whole trip, what is the total distance?

A. 90 km
B. 60 km
C. 45 km
D. 75 km

**Answer:** C. 45 km
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 45 km (Correct):** The first 1/3 distance has no delay (speed = 45 km/h). The delay of 20 min = 1/3 hour occurs only on the remaining 2/3 distance (say d). On distance d, speed drops from 45 to 30 km/h: d = [45*30/(45-30)] * (1/3) = 90 * (1/3) = 30 km. Since 2/3 of total distance = 30 km, total distance = 30 * 3/2 = 45 km.
- **A — 90 km:** Result of treating 30 km as 1/3 of the distance instead of 2/3, giving 90 km.
- **B — 60 km:** Value obtained by doubling 30 km to 60 km.
- **D — 75 km:** Result of using dT = 20 min on the full distance without scaling by 3/2.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Apply the dT formula only to the affected 2/3 segment (gets 30 km), then scale 2/3 -> 1 to get 45 km.

### Q470 · Stoppage Time per Hour

Excluding stoppages, the speed of a bus is 60 km/h and including stoppages, it is 45 km/h. For how many minutes does the bus stop per hour?

A. 12 minutes
B. 20 minutes
C. 10 minutes
D. 15 minutes

**Answer:** D. 15 minutes
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 15 minutes (Correct):** Stoppage time per hour = [(Speed without stoppages - Speed with stoppages) / Speed without stoppages] * 60 = [(60 - 45) / 60] * 60 = 15 minutes.
- **A — 12 minutes:** Result of dividing by 75 or miscalculating 15/60 * 60.
- **B — 20 minutes:** Value obtained by dividing the difference 15 by the lower speed 45: (15/45)*60 = 20 minutes.
- **C — 10 minutes:** Result of taking 1/6 of an hour instead of 1/4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Stoppage min/hr = (Difference in speeds / Faster speed) * 60. Here (15/60)*60 = 15 minutes.

### Q471 · Stoppage Time per Hour

A train without stoppages travels at an average speed of 72 km/h, and with stoppages it covers the same distance at an average speed of 60 km/h. How many minutes per hour does the train stop?

A. 10 minutes
B. 12 minutes
C. 8 minutes
D. 15 minutes

**Answer:** A. 10 minutes
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 10 minutes (Correct):** Stoppage time per hour = [(72 - 60) / 72] * 60 = (12 / 72) * 60 = (1/6) * 60 = 10 minutes.
- **B — 12 minutes:** Result of dividing by the slower speed 60 instead of 72: (12/60)*60 = 12 minutes.
- **C — 8 minutes:** Value obtained by miscomputing 12/72 as 1/7.5.
- **D — 15 minutes:** Result of using 1/4 of an hour instead of 1/6.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Always put the NON-STOP speed in the denominator: (72-60)/72 * 60 = 10 min.

### Q472 · Stoppage Time per Hour

A passenger train runs at 80 km/h without stoppages. Because of frequent halts, it stops for 12 minutes every hour. What is its effective average speed including stoppages?

A. 68 km/h
B. 64 km/h
C. 60 km/h
D. 66 km/h

**Answer:** B. 64 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 64 km/h (Correct):** In 1 hour (60 minutes), the train stops for 12 minutes and runs for 60 - 12 = 48 minutes = 48/60 = 4/5 hour. Effective distance covered in 1 hour = 80 * (4/5) = 64 km/h.
- **A — 68 km/h:** Result of subtracting 12 directly from 80: 80 - 12 = 68 km/h.
- **C — 60 km/h:** Value obtained by using 45 minutes running time instead of 48 minutes.
- **D — 66 km/h:** Result of miscalculating 80 * 0.8 as 66 km/h.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 12 min/hr = 20% stoppage time, so speed drops by 20%: 80 - 20% of 80 = 64 km/h.

### Q473 · Stoppage Time per Hour

Excluding stoppages, a truck moves at 54 km/h and including stoppages, at 45 km/h. In a journey of 9 hours (including stoppages), for how much total time did the truck stop?

A. 1 hour 15 minutes
B. 1 hour 48 minutes
C. 1 hour 30 minutes
D. 1 hour 20 minutes

**Answer:** C. 1 hour 30 minutes
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 1 hour 30 minutes (Correct):** Stoppage per hour = (54 - 45) / 54 = 9 / 54 = 1/6 hour = 10 minutes per hour. Over 9 hours, total stoppage time = 9 * (1/6) = 1.5 hours = 1 hour 30 minutes.
- **A — 1 hour 15 minutes:** Result of miscalculating 9/54 as 1/7 or taking 7.5 hours.
- **B — 1 hour 48 minutes:** Value obtained by dividing by 45 instead of 54: (9/45)*9 = 1.8 hours = 1 h 48 min.
- **D — 1 hour 20 minutes:** Result of rounding 1.33 hours instead of 1.5 hours.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Fraction of time stopped = (54-45)/54 = 1/6. Total stoppage = 1/6 of 9 hours = 1.5 hours.

### Q474 · Stoppage Time per Hour

A metro train has a running speed of 90 km/h and stops for 1.5 minutes at each station. If there are 8 stations on a 45 km route (excluding the origin and destination), what is its average speed for the route?

A. 75 km/h
B. 70 km/h
C. 60 km/h
D. 64.29 km/h

**Answer:** D. 64.29 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 64.29 km/h (Correct):** Pure running time for 45 km at 90 km/h = 45/90 = 0.5 hour = 30 minutes. Total stoppage time at 8 intermediate stations = 8 * 1.5 = 12 minutes. Total journey time = 30 + 12 = 42 minutes = 42/60 = 7/10 hour. Average speed = 45 / (7/10) = 450 / 7 = 64.29 km/h.
- **A — 75 km/h:** Result of counting only 4 stations or 6 minutes of stoppage (45 / 0.6 = 75 km/h).
- **B — 70 km/h:** Value obtained by using 38.5 minutes total time.
- **C — 60 km/h:** Result of taking 10 stations (including origin/destination): 30 + 15 = 45 min, giving 60 km/h.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Compute Running Time + Stoppage Time = 30 + 12 = 42 min (0.7 h), then 45 / 0.7 = 450/7 = 64.29 km/h.

### Q475 · Relative Speed & Chase Problems

A thief steals a car at 2:00 PM and drives it at 45 km/h. The theft is discovered at 2:30 PM and the owner chases him in another car at 55 km/h. At what time will the owner catch the thief?

A. 4:45 PM
B. 4:30 PM
C. 5:00 PM
D. 4:15 PM

**Answer:** A. 4:45 PM
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 4:45 PM (Correct):** In 30 minutes (0.5 h), the thief gets a head start of 45 * 0.5 = 22.5 km. Relative speed = 55 - 45 = 10 km/h. Time to catch up = 22.5 / 10 = 2.25 hours = 2 hours 15 minutes after 2:30 PM, which is 4:45 PM.
- **B — 4:30 PM:** Result of adding 2 hours instead of 2 hours 15 minutes to 2:30 PM.
- **C — 5:00 PM:** Value obtained by adding 2 hours 15 minutes to 2:45 PM or miscalculating head start.
- **D — 4:15 PM:** Result of adding 2 hours 15 minutes to 2:00 PM instead of 2:30 PM.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Always add catch-up time to the CHASER'S start time (2:30 PM + 22.5/10 h = 4:45 PM).

### Q476 · Relative Speed & Chase Problems

A policeman spots a thief from a distance of 300 m. Both start running simultaneously in the same direction, the thief at 10 km/h and the policeman at 12 km/h. How far has the thief run before he is caught?

A. 1.2 km
B. 1.5 km
C. 1.8 km
D. 1.0 km

**Answer:** B. 1.5 km
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 1.5 km (Correct):** Relative speed = 12 - 10 = 2 km/h. Distance to close = 300 m = 0.3 km. Time to catch = 0.3 / 2 = 0.15 hours. Distance run by thief = 10 * 0.15 = 1.5 km.
- **A — 1.2 km:** Result of subtracting the 300 m gap from 1.5 km.
- **C — 1.8 km:** Value obtained by finding the distance run by the policeman (12 * 0.15 = 1.8 km) instead of the thief.
- **D — 1.0 km:** Result of dividing 300 m by 3 instead of 2.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Distance ratio = Speed ratio = 12 : 10 = 6 : 5. Difference 1 unit = 300 m, so Thief's distance (5 units) = 5 * 300 = 1500 m = 1.5 km!

### Q477 · Relative Speed & Chase Problems

Two cities A and B are 330 km apart. A train starts from A towards B at 8:00 AM at 60 km/h. Another train starts from B towards A at 9:00 AM at 75 km/h. At what time will they meet?

A. 11:30 AM
B. 12:00 PM
C. 11:00 AM
D. 10:30 AM

**Answer:** C. 11:00 AM
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 11:00 AM (Correct):** From 8:00 AM to 9:00 AM, the first train covers 60 km. Remaining distance at 9:00 AM = 330 - 60 = 270 km. Relative speed (opposite directions) = 60 + 75 = 135 km/h. Time to meet = 270 / 135 = 2 hours after 9:00 AM = 11:00 AM.
- **A — 11:30 AM:** Result of miscalculating 270 / 135 as 2.5 hours.
- **B — 12:00 PM:** Value obtained by dividing 330 by 135 without subtracting the first hour's 60 km, or adding 3 hours to 9:00 AM.
- **D — 10:30 AM:** Result of adding 2 hours to 8:30 AM instead of 9:00 AM.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Bring both trains to the same start time (9:00 AM): remaining 270 km / (60+75) = 2 hours -> 11:00 AM.

### Q478 · Relative Speed & Chase Problems

A constable chases a pickpocket who is 150 m ahead. The constable runs 1 km in 8 minutes, while the pickpocket runs 1 km in 10 minutes. In how many minutes will the constable catch the pickpocket?

A. 8 minutes
B. 5 minutes
C. 4.5 minutes
D. 6 minutes

**Answer:** D. 6 minutes
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 6 minutes (Correct):** Constable's speed = 1000 / 8 = 125 m/min. Pickpocket's speed = 1000 / 10 = 100 m/min. Relative speed = 125 - 100 = 25 m/min. Time to catch = 150 / 25 = 6 minutes.
- **A — 8 minutes:** Result of dividing 150 by 18.75 or confusing the constable's 8 min/km.
- **B — 5 minutes:** Value obtained by adding speeds or miscalculating 1000/8 as 130 m/min.
- **C — 4.5 minutes:** Result of dividing 150 by 33.3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Convert both to m/min immediately: 1000/8 = 125 and 1000/10 = 100; gap 150 / diff 25 = 6 min.

### Q479 · Relative Speed & Chase Problems

Two friends X and Y start walking towards each other from two points 42 km apart at 4 km/h and 3 km/h respectively. At the same time, a dog starts running from X towards Y at 10 km/h, touches Y, turns back to X, and continues back and forth until X and Y meet. What total distance does the dog run?

A. 60 km
B. 54 km
C. 48 km
D. 70 km

**Answer:** A. 60 km
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 60 km (Correct):** Time until X and Y meet = Distance / Relative Speed = 42 / (4 + 3) = 42 / 7 = 6 hours. The dog runs continuously for those 6 hours at 10 km/h, so total distance = 10 * 6 = 60 km.
- **B — 54 km:** Result of miscalculating meeting time as 5.4 hours.
- **C — 48 km:** Value obtained by using only the first leg of the dog's run or subtracting 12 km.
- **D — 70 km:** Result of using relative speed 4 + 3 = 7 as 6, giving 7 hours * 10.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Never sum the infinite series of trips! Simply find meeting time (42/7 = 6 h) and multiply by dog's speed (6 * 10 = 60 km).

### Q480 · Relative Speed & Chase Problems

Two persons P and Q start simultaneously from A and B towards each other. After crossing each other, P takes 16 hours to reach B and Q takes 9 hours to reach A. If P's speed is 27 km/h, what is Q's speed?

A. 20.25 km/h
B. 36 km/h
C. 48 km/h
D. 32 km/h

**Answer:** B. 36 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 36 km/h (Correct):** Using the standard meeting theorem: Speed_P / Speed_Q = sqrt(Time_Q / Time_P) = sqrt(9 / 16) = 3 / 4. Given Speed_P = 27 km/h, Speed_Q = 27 * 4 / 3 = 36 km/h.
- **A — 20.25 km/h:** Result of inverting the ratio: 27 * 3 / 4 = 20.25 km/h.
- **C — 48 km/h:** Value obtained by forgetting the square root: 27 * 16 / 9 = 48 km/h.
- **D — 32 km/h:** Result of taking the ratio 16:9 wrongly or arithmetic slip.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Ratio of speeds after crossing = sqrt(t2 / t1) = sqrt(9/16) = 3/4. Since P = 27 (9*3), Q = 9*4 = 36 km/h.

### Q481 · Relative Speed & Chase Problems

Buses start from a terminal at intervals of 10 minutes and travel at 20 km/h. A man coming from the opposite direction towards the terminal meets the buses at intervals of 8 minutes. What is the speed of the man?

A. 4 km/h
B. 6 km/h
C. 5 km/h
D. 2.5 km/h

**Answer:** C. 5 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 5 km/h (Correct):** Distance covered by bus in (10 - 8) = 2 minutes equals distance covered by man in 8 minutes. Thus Speed_man * 8 = 20 * 2, giving Speed_man = 40 / 8 = 5 km/h.
- **A — 4 km/h:** Result of using Speed_bus * 2 / 10 = 4 km/h instead of dividing by 8.
- **B — 6 km/h:** Value obtained by miscalculating 20 * 2.4 / 8.
- **D — 2.5 km/h:** Result of dividing 20 by 8 directly: 20/8 = 2.5 km/h.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Man's speed = Bus speed * (T_bus - T_meet) / T_meet = 20 * (10 - 8) / 8 = 5 km/h.

### Q482 · Relative Speed & Chase Problems

A rifle is fired twice from the same place at an interval of 11 minutes 45 seconds, but a person in a train approaching the place hears the second shot 11 minutes after the first. If the speed of sound is 330 m/s, what is the speed of the train?

A. 20 m/s
B. 25 m/s
C. 15 m/s
D. 22.5 m/s

**Answer:** D. 22.5 m/s
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 22.5 m/s (Correct):** Time difference = 11 min 45 s - 11 min = 45 seconds. Distance covered by sound in 45 s equals distance covered by train in 11 min (660 s). Thus V_train * 660 = 330 * 45 -> V_train = (330 * 45) / 660 = 45 / 2 = 22.5 m/s.
- **A — 20 m/s:** Result of miscalculating 45/2 or rounding down to 20 m/s.
- **B — 25 m/s:** Value obtained by dividing 330 * 50 / 660 = 25 m/s.
- **C — 15 m/s:** Result of dividing 330 * 30 / 660 = 15 m/s.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Train speed = Sound speed * (dT / T_heard) = 330 * (45 / 660) = 45/2 = 22.5 m/s.

### Q483 · Relative Speed & Chase Problems

A hare sees a hound 100 m away and starts running at 12 km/h. After 1 minute, the hound spots the hare and chases it at 16 km/h. How long after the hound starts will it catch the hare?

A. 4.5 minutes
B. 3.5 minutes
C. 5 minutes
D. 6 minutes

**Answer:** A. 4.5 minutes
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 4.5 minutes (Correct):** In 1 minute, the hare runs 12 * (1000/60) = 200 m. Total gap when hound starts = 100 + 200 = 300 m. Relative speed = 16 - 12 = 4 km/h = 4000/60 = 200/3 m/min. Time = 300 / (200/3) = 9/2 = 4.5 minutes.
- **B — 3.5 minutes:** Result of forgetting the 100 m initial gap (200 / 66.67 = 3 min) or miscalculating.
- **C — 5 minutes:** Value obtained by adding 1 minute to 4 minutes.
- **D — 6 minutes:** Result of using gap = 400 m instead of 300 m.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Convert 4 km/h to 200/3 m/min; gap = 100 + 200 = 300 m, so 300 / (200/3) = 4.5 min.

### Q484 · Trains: Pole, Platform & Bridge

A train 180 m long is running at a speed of 72 km/h. How many seconds will it take to cross a telegraph pole?

A. 10 seconds
B. 9 seconds
C. 8 seconds
D. 12 seconds

**Answer:** B. 9 seconds
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 9 seconds (Correct):** Speed in m/s = 72 * (5/18) = 20 m/s. Time to cross a pole = Length / Speed = 180 / 20 = 9 seconds.
- **A — 10 seconds:** Result of using 18 m/s instead of 20 m/s.
- **C — 8 seconds:** Value obtained by miscalculating 180 / 22.5.
- **D — 12 seconds:** Result of dividing 180 by 15 instead of 20.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 72 km/h = 4 * 18 km/h = 4 * 5 = 20 m/s. Time = 180 / 20 = 9 seconds.

### Q485 · Trains: Pole, Platform & Bridge

A train 240 m long crosses a 360 m long platform in 25 seconds. What is the speed of the train in km/h?

A. 72 km/h
B. 90 km/h
C. 86.4 km/h
D. 79.2 km/h

**Answer:** C. 86.4 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 86.4 km/h (Correct):** Total distance = 240 + 360 = 600 m. Speed = 600 / 25 = 24 m/s. In km/h: 24 * (18/5) = 432 / 5 = 86.4 km/h.
- **A — 72 km/h:** Result of using only the platform or miscalculating 24 * 3 = 72 km/h.
- **B — 90 km/h:** Value obtained by using 25 m/s instead of 24 m/s.
- **D — 79.2 km/h:** Result of using 22 m/s (subtracting lengths wrongly).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Total length = 600 m; 600/25 = 24 m/s. Multiply 24 by 3.6 -> 86.4 km/h.

### Q486 · Trains: Pole, Platform & Bridge

A train passes a standing man in 8 seconds and crosses a 264 m long bridge in 20 seconds. Find the length of the train.

A. 180 m
B. 160 m
C. 192 m
D. 176 m

**Answer:** D. 176 m
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 176 m (Correct):** Extra time of 20 - 8 = 12 seconds is used purely to cover the 264 m bridge. Speed of train = 264 / 12 = 22 m/s. Length of train = 22 * 8 = 176 m.
- **A — 180 m:** Result of rounding speed to 22.5 m/s.
- **B — 160 m:** Value obtained by taking speed = 20 m/s.
- **C — 192 m:** Result of multiplying 24 * 8 = 192 m.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Length = Bridge * t_man / (t_bridge - t_man) = 264 * 8 / 12 = 22 * 8 = 176 m.

### Q487 · Trains: Pole, Platform & Bridge

A 150 m long train running at 54 km/h crosses a tunnel in 30 seconds. What is the length of the tunnel?

A. 300 m
B. 450 m
C. 250 m
D. 350 m

**Answer:** A. 300 m
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 300 m (Correct):** Speed = 54 * (5/18) = 15 m/s. Total distance covered in 30 s = 15 * 30 = 450 m. Length of tunnel = 450 - 150 = 300 m.
- **B — 450 m:** Result of reporting the total distance (450 m) without subtracting the train's length.
- **C — 250 m:** Value obtained by subtracting 200 m instead of 150 m.
- **D — 350 m:** Result of miscalculating 54 * 5/18 as 16.67 m/s.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 54 km/h = 15 m/s -> 15 * 30 = 450 m total, minus 150 m train = 300 m tunnel.

### Q488 · Trains: Pole, Platform & Bridge

A train crosses a 160 m platform in 18 seconds and a 280 m platform in 24 seconds. What is the speed of the train in km/h?

A. 60 km/h
B. 72 km/h
C. 64 km/h
D. 80 km/h

**Answer:** B. 72 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 72 km/h (Correct):** Difference in platform lengths = 280 - 160 = 120 m, covered in 24 - 18 = 6 seconds. Speed = 120 / 6 = 20 m/s = 20 * (18/5) = 72 km/h.
- **A — 60 km/h:** Result of dividing 280 - 160 by 7.2 instead of 6.
- **C — 64 km/h:** Value obtained by arithmetic error in converting m/s to km/h.
- **D — 80 km/h:** Result of using (280+160)/(24+18) or wrong difference.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Speed = (P2 - P1) / (t2 - t1) = 120 / 6 = 20 m/s = 72 km/h (train length cancels out!).

### Q489 · Trains: Pole, Platform & Bridge

A train 210 m long takes 14 seconds to pass a milestone. How long will it take to cross a bridge that is 390 m long?

A. 36 seconds
B. 38 seconds
C. 40 seconds
D. 42 seconds

**Answer:** C. 40 seconds
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 40 seconds (Correct):** Speed = 210 / 14 = 15 m/s. Total distance to cross the bridge = 210 + 390 = 600 m. Time = 600 / 15 = 40 seconds.
- **A — 36 seconds:** Result of miscalculating total distance as 540 m.
- **B — 38 seconds:** Value obtained by using speed = 15.8 m/s.
- **D — 42 seconds:** Result of using 630 m as total distance.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Distance ratio: (210+390)/210 = 600/210 = 20/7. Time = 14 * (20/7) = 40 seconds.

### Q490 · Trains: Pole, Platform & Bridge

A train travelling at 90 km/h crosses a bridge of length equal to twice its own length in 36 seconds. Find the length of the train.

A. 450 m
B. 600 m
C. 250 m
D. 300 m

**Answer:** D. 300 m
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 300 m (Correct):** Speed = 90 * (5/18) = 25 m/s. Total distance covered = 25 * 36 = 900 m. If train length is L, bridge is 2L, so L + 2L = 3L = 900 m -> L = 300 m.
- **A — 450 m:** Result of dividing 900 m by 2 instead of 3.
- **B — 600 m:** Value obtained by reporting the bridge length (600 m) instead of train length.
- **C — 250 m:** Result of miscalculating 25 * 36 as 750 m.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 90 km/h = 25 m/s; 25 * 36 = 900 m = 3L, so L = 300 m.

### Q491 · Trains: Pole, Platform & Bridge

A train running at 45 km/h crosses a 225 m long bridge in 32 seconds. What is the length of the train?

A. 175 m
B. 185 m
C. 165 m
D. 200 m

**Answer:** A. 175 m
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 175 m (Correct):** Speed = 45 * (5/18) = 12.5 m/s. Total distance = 12.5 * 32 = 400 m. Train length = 400 - 225 = 175 m.
- **B — 185 m:** Result of miscalculating 12.5 * 32 as 410 m.
- **C — 165 m:** Value obtained by subtracting 235 instead of 225.
- **D — 200 m:** Result of using speed = 13.28 m/s.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 12.5 m/s = 100/8 m/s. So 32 * (100/8) = 400 m; 400 - 225 = 175 m.

### Q492 · Trains: Pole, Platform & Bridge

Two trains of lengths 140 m and 160 m are running at 60 km/h and 48 km/h respectively. How long does the faster train take to cross a 160 m long platform?

A. 15 seconds
B. 18 seconds
C. 20 seconds
D. 12 seconds

**Answer:** B. 18 seconds
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 18 seconds (Correct):** Faster train: length = 140 m, speed = 60 km/h = 60 * (5/18) = 50/3 m/s. Total distance = 140 + 160 = 300 m. Time = 300 / (50/3) = 18 seconds.
- **A — 15 seconds:** Result of using 20 m/s (72 km/h) instead of 50/3 m/s.
- **C — 20 seconds:** Value obtained by using the slower train's speed (48 km/h) or wrong total distance.
- **D — 12 seconds:** Result of using only the platform length 160 m.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Ignore the second train (distractor)! Total distance = 140 + 160 = 300 m; 300 / (50/3) = 18 s.

### Q493 · Trains: Pole, Platform & Bridge

A train crosses a pole in 12 seconds and a 150 m long platform in 22 seconds. How long will it take to cross a 225 m long bridge?

A. 25 seconds
B. 30 seconds
C. 27 seconds
D. 24 seconds

**Answer:** C. 27 seconds
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 27 seconds (Correct):** Time for 150 m platform = 22 - 12 = 10 seconds -> Speed = 150 / 10 = 15 m/s. Time to cross 225 m bridge = 12 + (225 / 15) = 12 + 15 = 27 seconds.
- **A — 25 seconds:** Result of adding 13 seconds instead of 15 seconds to 12 seconds.
- **B — 30 seconds:** Value obtained by doubling the bridge time or miscalculating 225/15 as 18.
- **D — 24 seconds:** Result of using 150/12 instead of 150/10 for speed.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Speed = 150 / (22-12) = 15 m/s. Bridge takes 225/15 = 15 s extra on top of 12 s pole time = 27 s.

### Q494 · Trains: Moving Objects & Crossing

A train 125 m long passes a man running at 5 km/h in the same direction in which the train is going, in 10 seconds. Find the speed of the train.

A. 45 km/h
B. 40 km/h
C. 54 km/h
D. 50 km/h

**Answer:** D. 50 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 50 km/h (Correct):** Relative speed = 125 / 10 = 12.5 m/s = 12.5 * (18/5) = 45 km/h. Since both move in the same direction, Train speed - Man speed = 45 -> Train speed = 45 + 5 = 50 km/h.
- **A — 45 km/h:** Result of reporting the relative speed (45 km/h) without adding the man's speed.
- **B — 40 km/h:** Value obtained by subtracting 5 km/h instead of adding (45 - 5 = 40 km/h).
- **C — 54 km/h:** Result of miscalculating 12.5 * 18/5 as 49 km/h.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Relative speed = 125/10 = 12.5 m/s = 45 km/h; same direction means Train = 45 + 5 = 50 km/h.

### Q495 · Trains: Moving Objects & Crossing

A train 220 m long is running at 63 km/h. In how many seconds will it pass a jogger running at 9 km/h in the opposite direction?

A. 11 seconds
B. 12 seconds
C. 10 seconds
D. 14 seconds

**Answer:** A. 11 seconds
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 11 seconds (Correct):** Opposite directions -> Relative speed = 63 + 9 = 72 km/h = 72 * (5/18) = 20 m/s. Time = 220 / 20 = 11 seconds.
- **B — 12 seconds:** Result of subtracting speeds (63 - 9 = 54 km/h = 15 m/s) or rounding 220/18.
- **C — 10 seconds:** Value obtained by rounding 220 / 22 = 10 seconds.
- **D — 14 seconds:** Result of using 54 km/h = 15 m/s: 220/15 = 14.67 s.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Opposite -> ADD: 63 + 9 = 72 km/h = 20 m/s. Time = 220 / 20 = 11 s.

### Q496 · Trains: Moving Objects & Crossing

Two trains 140 m and 160 m long run at speeds of 60 km/h and 48 km/h respectively in opposite directions on parallel tracks. How long do they take to cross each other completely?

A. 12 seconds
B. 10 seconds
C. 15 seconds
D. 9 seconds

**Answer:** B. 10 seconds
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 10 seconds (Correct):** Total distance = 140 + 160 = 300 m. Relative speed = 60 + 48 = 108 km/h = 108 * (5/18) = 30 m/s. Time = 300 / 30 = 10 seconds.
- **A — 12 seconds:** Result of miscalculating 108 * 5/18 as 25 m/s (300/25 = 12 s).
- **C — 15 seconds:** Value obtained by using 20 m/s instead of 30 m/s.
- **D — 9 seconds:** Result of subtracting train lengths or using 33.3 m/s.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 108 km/h = 6 * 18 -> 6 * 5 = 30 m/s. Total length 300 m / 30 = 10 seconds.

### Q497 · Trains: Moving Objects & Crossing

Two trains of lengths 180 m and 220 m are running in the same direction on parallel tracks at 72 km/h and 54 km/h respectively. How long will the faster train take to completely overtake the slower train?

A. 60 seconds
B. 72 seconds
C. 80 seconds
D. 40 seconds

**Answer:** C. 80 seconds
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 80 seconds (Correct):** Total distance = 180 + 220 = 400 m. Relative speed (same direction) = 72 - 54 = 18 km/h = 18 * (5/18) = 5 m/s. Time = 400 / 5 = 80 seconds.
- **A — 60 seconds:** Result of using only 300 m total length or 6.67 m/s.
- **B — 72 seconds:** Value obtained by subtracting 180 from 220 or miscalculating 400/5.5.
- **D — 40 seconds:** Result of using only 200 m distance (400/2) instead of 400 m.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Same direction -> SUBTRACT speeds: 72 - 54 = 18 km/h = 5 m/s. 400 / 5 = 80 s.

### Q498 · Trains: Moving Objects & Crossing

A train 150 m long passes a man sitting in another train moving in the opposite direction at 54 km/h in 5 seconds. What is the speed of the first train?

A. 60 km/h
B. 48 km/h
C. 72 km/h
D. 54 km/h

**Answer:** D. 54 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 54 km/h (Correct):** Relative speed = 150 / 5 = 30 m/s = 30 * (18/5) = 108 km/h. Since they move in opposite directions, V1 + 54 = 108 -> V1 = 54 km/h.
- **A — 60 km/h:** Result of miscalculating 30 * 18/5 as 114 km/h.
- **B — 48 km/h:** Value obtained by subtracting 60 instead of 54.
- **C — 72 km/h:** Result of using 35 m/s = 126 km/h.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Crossing a person in another train uses ONLY the first train's length (150 m)! 150/5 = 30 m/s = 108 km/h; 108 - 54 = 54 km/h.

### Q499 · Trains: Moving Objects & Crossing

Two trains of equal length take 10 seconds and 15 seconds respectively to cross a telegraph pole. If the length of each train is 120 m, in what time will they cross each other when travelling in opposite directions?

A. 12 seconds
B. 12.5 seconds
C. 10 seconds
D. 15 seconds

**Answer:** A. 12 seconds
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 12 seconds (Correct):** Speed of train 1 = 120 / 10 = 12 m/s. Speed of train 2 = 120 / 15 = 8 m/s. Relative speed = 12 + 8 = 20 m/s. Total distance = 120 + 120 = 240 m. Time = 240 / 20 = 12 seconds.
- **B — 12.5 seconds:** Result of taking the arithmetic mean of 10 and 15: (10 + 15)/2 = 12.5 seconds.
- **C — 10 seconds:** Value obtained by using only 120 m instead of 240 m.
- **D — 15 seconds:** Result of taking the harmonic mean without dividing by 2 or wrong speed.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For equal-length trains in opposite directions, crossing time = harmonic mean of their pole times = 2*10*15/(10+15) = 12 s!

### Q500 · Trains: Moving Objects & Crossing

Two trains of equal length take 12 seconds and 18 seconds respectively to cross a pole. In how much time will they cross each other when running in the same direction?

A. 36 seconds
B. 72 seconds
C. 60 seconds
D. 48 seconds

**Answer:** B. 72 seconds
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 72 seconds (Correct):** Let length of each train = LCM(12, 18) = 36 m. Speeds = 36/12 = 3 m/s and 36/18 = 2 m/s. Relative speed (same direction) = 3 - 2 = 1 m/s. Total distance = 36 + 36 = 72 m. Time = 72 / 1 = 72 seconds.
- **A — 36 seconds:** Result of using only 1 train's length (36 m) instead of 2L (72 m).
- **C — 60 seconds:** Value obtained by miscalculating 2*12*18/(18-12) without the factor of 2 or using wrong difference.
- **D — 48 seconds:** Result of taking 12 * 4 = 48 seconds.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Same-direction crossing time for equal trains = 2*t1*t2 / |t1 - t2| = 2*12*18 / 6 = 72 s!

### Q501 · Trains: Moving Objects & Crossing

A train 240 m long overtakes a cyclist riding at 10 km/h in the same direction in 16 seconds. How long will it take to cross another cyclist riding at 8 km/h in the opposite direction?

A. 10 seconds
B. 15 seconds
C. 12 seconds
D. 16 seconds

**Answer:** C. 12 seconds
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 12 seconds (Correct):** In the first case, relative speed = 240 / 16 = 15 m/s = 15 * (18/5) = 54 km/h. Since the train overtakes in the same direction, train speed = 54 + 10 = 64 km/h. Against the second cyclist in the opposite direction, relative speed = 64 + 8 = 72 km/h = 20 m/s. Time = 240 / 20 = 12 seconds.
- **A — 10 seconds:** Result of miscalculating 240 / 24 = 10 seconds.
- **B — 15 seconds:** Value obtained by subtracting 8 km/h instead of adding (64 - 8 = 56 km/h).
- **D — 16 seconds:** Result of assuming the crossing time is the same as the overtaking time (16 seconds).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** First relative speed = 240/16 = 15 m/s = 54 km/h -> Train = 64 km/h; opposite cyclist (+8) = 72 km/h = 20 m/s -> 240/20 = 12 s.

### Q502 · Trains: Moving Objects & Crossing

A 200 m long train running at 66 km/h crosses a man walking at 6 km/h in the opposite direction. How many seconds does it take?

A. 12 seconds
B. 8 seconds
C. 9 seconds
D. 10 seconds

**Answer:** D. 10 seconds
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 10 seconds (Correct):** Opposite direction -> Relative speed = 66 + 6 = 72 km/h = 20 m/s. Time = 200 / 20 = 10 seconds.
- **A — 12 seconds:** Result of subtracting speeds (66 - 6 = 60 km/h = 16.67 m/s).
- **B — 8 seconds:** Value obtained by using 25 m/s instead of 20 m/s.
- **C — 9 seconds:** Result of dividing 180 by 20 instead of 200 by 20.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 66 + 6 = 72 km/h = 20 m/s; 200 / 20 = 10 seconds.

### Q503 · Trains: Moving Objects & Crossing

Two trains A and B of lengths 240 m and 300 m run in opposite directions. A runs at 50 km/h and B at 58 km/h. How long will it take for them to clear each other from the moment they meet?

A. 18 seconds
B. 16 seconds
C. 20 seconds
D. 15 seconds

**Answer:** A. 18 seconds
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 18 seconds (Correct):** Total distance = 240 + 300 = 540 m. Relative speed = 50 + 58 = 108 km/h = 108 * (5/18) = 30 m/s. Time = 540 / 30 = 18 seconds.
- **B — 16 seconds:** Result of using 480 m instead of 540 m.
- **C — 20 seconds:** Value obtained by miscalculating 540 / 27 = 20 seconds.
- **D — 15 seconds:** Result of dividing 450 by 30.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 50 + 58 = 108 km/h = 30 m/s; (240 + 300)/30 = 540/30 = 18 s.

### Q504 · Boats & Streams

A boat travels 36 km downstream in 3 hours and 24 km upstream in 4 hours. Find the speed of the boat in still water.

A. 10 km/h
B. 9 km/h
C. 8 km/h
D. 6 km/h

**Answer:** B. 9 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 9 km/h (Correct):** Downstream speed D = 36 / 3 = 12 km/h. Upstream speed U = 24 / 4 = 6 km/h. Speed of boat in still water = (D + U) / 2 = (12 + 6) / 2 = 9 km/h.
- **A — 10 km/h:** Result of miscalculating D or U and averaging to 10 km/h.
- **C — 8 km/h:** Value obtained by subtracting 1 instead of taking (12+6)/2.
- **D — 6 km/h:** Result of reporting the upstream speed (6 km/h) directly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Still-water speed = (D + U)/2 = (12 + 6)/2 = 9 km/h; stream speed = (D - U)/2 = 3 km/h.

### Q505 · Boats & Streams

A swimmer's speed in still water is 11 km/h and the speed of the river current is 3 km/h. How long will he take to swim 28 km downstream and 24 km upstream?

A. 4 hours
B. 6 hours
C. 5 hours
D. 4.5 hours

**Answer:** C. 5 hours
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 5 hours (Correct):** Downstream speed = 11 + 3 = 14 km/h; Upstream speed = 11 - 3 = 8 km/h. Total time = 28/14 + 24/8 = 2 + 3 = 5 hours.
- **A — 4 hours:** Result of using still-water speed 11 km/h or miscalculating 24/8 as 2.
- **B — 6 hours:** Value obtained by swapping downstream and upstream speeds.
- **D — 4.5 hours:** Result of averaging the two speeds to 11 km/h.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** D = 14 km/h (28/14 = 2 h), U = 8 km/h (24/8 = 3 h); total = 5 hours.

### Q506 · Boats & Streams

A boat can go 15 km/h in still water and the stream flows at 5 km/h. If it takes 6 hours to go to a place and return, what is the distance to that place?

A. 45 km
B. 36 km
C. 80 km
D. 40 km

**Answer:** D. 40 km
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 40 km (Correct):** D = 15 + 5 = 20 km/h; U = 15 - 5 = 10 km/h. Let one-way distance be d. Then d/20 + d/10 = 6 -> 3d/20 = 6 -> d = 40 km.
- **A — 45 km:** Result of using still-water speed directly: 15 * 6 / 2 = 45 km.
- **B — 36 km:** Value obtained by miscalculating 3d/20 = 6.
- **C — 80 km:** Result of reporting the round-trip distance (80 km) instead of one-way distance.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** One-way distance = Total Time * (B^2 - S^2) / (2B) = 6 * (225 - 25) / 30 = 6 * 200 / 30 = 40 km.

### Q507 · Boats & Streams

A man rows at 12 km/h in still water. If the river flows at 4 km/h, it takes him 3 hours more to row upstream than to row the same distance downstream. Find the distance.

A. 48 km
B. 36 km
C. 24 km
D. 32 km

**Answer:** A. 48 km
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 48 km (Correct):** D = 12 + 4 = 16 km/h; U = 12 - 4 = 8 km/h. Let distance = d. Then d/8 - d/16 = 3 -> d/16 = 3 -> d = 48 km.
- **B — 36 km:** Result of using B = 12 directly: 12 * 3 = 36 km.
- **C — 24 km:** Value obtained by adding d/8 + d/16 = 3 -> 3d/16 = 3 -> 16 km or halving 48.
- **D — 32 km:** Result of miscalculating d/12 = 3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Distance = dT * (B^2 - S^2) / (2S) = 3 * (144 - 16) / 8 = 3 * 128 / 8 = 48 km.

### Q508 · Boats & Streams

A boat takes twice as long to row a certain distance against the stream as to row the same distance with the stream. If the speed of the stream is 4 km/h, what is the speed of the boat in still water?

A. 8 km/h
B. 12 km/h
C. 16 km/h
D. 10 km/h

**Answer:** B. 12 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 12 km/h (Correct):** Time ratio U:D = 2:1 -> Speed ratio D:U = 2:1. Thus (B + S)/(B - S) = 2/1 -> B + 4 = 2(B - 4) -> B = 12 km/h.
- **A — 8 km/h:** Result of doubling the stream speed: 2 * 4 = 8 km/h.
- **C — 16 km/h:** Value obtained by multiplying 4 * 4 = 16 km/h.
- **D — 10 km/h:** Result of using (B+4)/B = 2 or arithmetic slip.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If Time_up = n * Time_down, then Boat Speed = Stream * (n + 1)/(n - 1) = 4 * (3/1) = 12 km/h!

### Q509 · Boats & Streams

A boat covers 24 km upstream and 36 km downstream in 6 hours, while it covers 36 km upstream and 24 km downstream in 6.5 hours. Find the speed of the current.

A. 4 km/h
B. 8 km/h
C. 2 km/h
D. 12 km/h

**Answer:** C. 2 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 2 km/h (Correct):** Let 1/U = x and 1/D = y. 24x + 36y = 6 and 36x + 24y = 6.5. Adding: 60(x+y) = 12.5 -> x+y = 12.5/60 = 5/24. Subtracting: 12(x-y) = 0.5 -> x-y = 1/24. Thus x = 3/24 = 1/8 (U = 8 km/h) and y = 2/24 = 1/12 (D = 12 km/h). Speed of current = (12 - 8)/2 = 2 km/h.
- **A — 4 km/h:** Result of forgetting to divide (D - U) by 2: 12 - 8 = 4 km/h.
- **B — 8 km/h:** Value obtained by reporting the upstream speed (8 km/h) instead of current speed.
- **D — 12 km/h:** Result of reporting the downstream speed (12 km/h).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Add and subtract the two equations to find 1/U + 1/D and 1/U - 1/D immediately, avoiding messy elimination.

### Q510 · Boats & Streams

A river flows at 3 km/h and a boat's speed in still water is 9 km/h. What is the average speed of the boat for a round trip between two docks?

A. 9 km/h
B. 8.5 km/h
C. 7.5 km/h
D. 8 km/h

**Answer:** D. 8 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 8 km/h (Correct):** Downstream speed = 9 + 3 = 12 km/h; Upstream speed = 9 - 3 = 6 km/h. Average speed for equal distances = (2 * 12 * 6) / (12 + 6) = 144 / 18 = 8 km/h.
- **A — 9 km/h:** Result of assuming the current cancels out on a round trip, leaving 9 km/h.
- **B — 8.5 km/h:** Value obtained by averaging 9 and 8.
- **C — 7.5 km/h:** Result of miscalculating 144 / 18 as 7.5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Round-trip average speed in a river = (B^2 - S^2) / B = (81 - 9) / 9 = 72 / 9 = 8 km/h (always less than B!).

### Q511 · Boats & Streams

A man can row 40 km downstream in 2.5 hours and returns the same distance against the current in 5 hours. Find the speed of the current.

A. 4 km/h
B. 8 km/h
C. 12 km/h
D. 6 km/h

**Answer:** A. 4 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 4 km/h (Correct):** Downstream speed D = 40 / 2.5 = 16 km/h. Upstream speed U = 40 / 5 = 8 km/h. Speed of current = (16 - 8) / 2 = 4 km/h.
- **B — 8 km/h:** Result of forgetting to divide (16 - 8) by 2, giving 8 km/h.
- **C — 12 km/h:** Value obtained by computing the still-water speed (16 + 8)/2 = 12 km/h instead of current.
- **D — 6 km/h:** Result of miscalculating 40/2.5 as 20 km/h.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** D = 40/2.5 = 16, U = 40/5 = 8; Current = (16 - 8)/2 = 4 km/h.

### Q512 · Boats & Streams

A motorboat in still water travels at 18 km/h. It goes 42 km upstream and returns to the starting point in 5 hours 15 minutes. What is the speed of the stream?

A. 4 km/h
B. 6 km/h
C. 3 km/h
D. 5 km/h

**Answer:** B. 6 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 6 km/h (Correct):** Total time = 5.25 hours = 21/4 hours. Let stream speed be S. Then 42/(18 - S) + 42/(18 + S) = 21/4 -> 2/(18 - S) + 2/(18 + S) = 1/4 -> 72 / (324 - S^2) = 1/4 -> 324 - S^2 = 288 -> S^2 = 36 -> S = 6 km/h.
- **A — 4 km/h:** Result of testing S = 4 without checking total time (gives 4.91 h).
- **C — 3 km/h:** Value obtained by using 5 hours instead of 5.25 hours.
- **D — 5 km/h:** Result of arithmetic error in 324 - 288 = 25.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Plug in options: with S = 6, U = 12 (42/12 = 3.5 h) and D = 24 (42/24 = 1.75 h); 3.5 + 1.75 = 5.25 h = 5 h 15 min!

### Q513 · Boats & Streams

A person rows 750 m against the stream in 11 minutes 15 seconds and returns the same distance downstream in 7 minutes 30 seconds. What is his rowing speed in still water?

A. 4 km/h
B. 6 km/h
C. 5 km/h
D. 4.5 km/h

**Answer:** C. 5 km/h
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 5 km/h (Correct):** 11 min 15 s = 675 s -> U = 750 / 675 = 10/9 m/s = (10/9)*(18/5) = 4 km/h. 7 min 30 s = 450 s -> D = 750 / 450 = 5/3 m/s = (5/3)*(18/5) = 6 km/h. Still-water speed = (4 + 6) / 2 = 5 km/h.
- **A — 4 km/h:** Result of reporting the upstream speed (4 km/h) instead of still-water speed.
- **B — 6 km/h:** Value obtained by reporting the downstream speed (6 km/h).
- **D — 4.5 km/h:** Result of misconverting 11 min 15 s as 11.15 minutes.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Convert to km/h via *18/5: U = (750/675)*(18/5) = 4 km/h, D = (750/450)*(18/5) = 6 km/h -> avg = 5 km/h.

### Q514 · Circular Track Meetings

Two runners A and B start from the same point on a 1200 m circular track at 6 m/s and 4 m/s respectively in opposite directions. After how many seconds will they meet for the first time?

A. 600 seconds
B. 300 seconds
C. 200 seconds
D. 120 seconds

**Answer:** D. 120 seconds
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 120 seconds (Correct):** Moving in opposite directions, relative speed = 6 + 4 = 10 m/s. Time for first meeting anywhere on the track = 1200 / 10 = 120 seconds.
- **A — 600 seconds:** Result of subtracting speeds (6 - 4 = 2 m/s) as if running in the same direction: 1200/2 = 600 s.
- **B — 300 seconds:** Value obtained by finding the time for B alone (1200/4 = 300 s).
- **C — 200 seconds:** Result of finding the time for A alone (1200/6 = 200 s).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** First meeting anywhere on a circular track: Opposite -> L/(v1+v2) = 1200/10 = 120 s.

### Q515 · Circular Track Meetings

Two cyclists start from the same point on a 900 m circular track in the same direction at speeds of 15 m/s and 10 m/s. After how many seconds will they meet again at the starting point?

A. 180 seconds
B. 90 seconds
C. 60 seconds
D. 270 seconds

**Answer:** A. 180 seconds
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 180 seconds (Correct):** Time for one lap by cyclist 1 = 900 / 15 = 60 s. Time for one lap by cyclist 2 = 900 / 10 = 90 s. They meet at the starting point at LCM(60, 90) = 180 seconds.
- **B — 90 seconds:** Result of reporting only the slower cyclist's lap time (90 s).
- **C — 60 seconds:** Value obtained by reporting only the faster cyclist's lap time (60 s).
- **D — 270 seconds:** Result of adding 90 + 180 or taking wrong LCM.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Meeting AT THE STARTING POINT always = LCM(L/v1, L/v2) = LCM(60, 90) = 180 s (independent of direction!).
