## Chapter 10: Mensuration (2D & 3D)

### Q581 · Triangle (Scalene / Heron's Formula)

The sides of a triangle are 13 cm, 14 cm, and 15 cm. What is the length of the altitude drawn to the side of length 14 cm?

A. 6 cm
B. 12 cm
C. 11.2 cm
D. 14 cm

**Answer:** B. 12 cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 12 cm (Correct):** Semi-perimeter s = (13 + 14 + 15) / 2 = 21 cm. By Heron's formula, Area = √[21 × (21 - 13) × (21 - 14) × (21 - 15)] = √(21 × 8 × 7 × 6) = √7056 = 84 cm². Since Area = (1/2) × base × height, 84 = (1/2) × 14 × h ⇒ h = 168 / 14 = 12 cm.
- **A — 6 cm:** Obtained by forgetting to multiply the area by 2 when solving (1/2) × base × h = Area.
- **C — 11.2 cm:** Obtained by dividing 2 × 84 = 168 by the longest side 15 cm instead of 14 cm.
- **D — 14 cm:** Confusing the base length itself with the altitude of the triangle.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Memorize the classic SSC triplet-combination triangle (13, 14, 15): its area is always 84 cm² and the altitude to base 14 is 12 cm (splitting it into 5-12-13 and 9-12-15 right triangles).

### Q582 · Triangle (Scalene / Heron's Formula)

Find the area of a triangular field whose sides are 17 m, 25 m, and 28 m.

A. 420 m²
B. 175 m²
C. 210 m²
D. 245 m²

**Answer:** C. 210 m²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 210 m² (Correct):** Semi-perimeter s = (17 + 25 + 28) / 2 = 70 / 2 = 35 m. Area = √[35 × (35 - 17) × (35 - 25) × (35 - 28)] = √(35 × 18 × 10 × 7) = √(5 × 7 × 2 × 9 × 2 × 5 × 7) = 5 × 7 × 2 × 3 = 210 m².
- **A — 420 m²:** Obtained by forgetting to take the square root or doubling the area using perimeter instead of semi-perimeter.
- **B — 175 m²:** Result of mistakenly using (1/2) × 25 × 14 assuming a right angle without checking Pythagoras.
- **D — 245 m²:** Obtained by miscalculating (35 - 17) as 16 or factoring 35 × 7 incorrectly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Never multiply out large numbers inside Heron's radical! Factor each term into prime/square pairs immediately: 35×18×10×7 = (5×7)×(2×3²)×(2×5)×7 = 5×7×2×3 = 210.

### Q583 · Triangle (Scalene / Heron's Formula)

What is the inradius of a triangle whose sides are 9 cm, 40 cm, and 41 cm?

A. 4 cm
B. 8 cm
C. 20.5 cm
D. 4.5 cm

**Answer:** A. 4 cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 4 cm (Correct):** Notice that 9² + 40² = 81 + 1600 = 1681 = 41², so it is a right-angled triangle. Area = (1/2) × 9 × 40 = 180 cm². Semi-perimeter s = (9 + 40 + 41) / 2 = 45 cm. Inradius r = Area / s = 180 / 45 = 4 cm.
- **B — 8 cm:** Obtained by omitting the 1/2 factor in the triangle area (using 360 / 45 = 8 cm).
- **C — 20.5 cm:** Result of computing the circumradius (hypotenuse / 2 = 41 / 2) instead of the inradius.
- **D — 4.5 cm:** Obtained by taking half of the shortest side (9 / 2) instead of computing r = Area / s.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For any right-angled triangle with legs a, b and hypotenuse c, skip Heron's formula and use r = (a + b - c) / 2 = (9 + 40 - 41) / 2 = 4 cm in 3 seconds!

### Q584 · Triangle (Scalene / Heron's Formula)

A triangular park has sides 25 m, 39 m, and 56 m. Find the cost of levelling the park at the rate of ₹15 per m².

A. ₹12,600
B. ₹5,850
C. ₹3,150
D. ₹6,300

**Answer:** D. ₹6,300
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹6,300 (Correct):** Semi-perimeter s = (25 + 39 + 56) / 2 = 120 / 2 = 60 m. Area = √[60 × (60 - 25) × (60 - 39) × (60 - 56)] = √(60 × 35 × 21 × 4) = √176400 = 420 m². Cost of levelling = 420 × 15 = ₹6,300.
- **A — ₹12,600:** Obtained by doubling the area or using perimeter s = 120 instead of semi-perimeter 60.
- **B — ₹5,850:** Result of estimating the area as (1/2) × 25 × 39 × 15 / ... or miscomputing 390 × 15.
- **C — ₹3,150:** Obtained by dividing the correct cost by 2 mistakenly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Factor inside the root: 60×35×21×4 = (4×15)×(5×7)×(3×7)×4 = 4 × 7 × 15 = 420 m². Multiply 420 × 15 as 4200 + 2100 = ₹6,300.

### Q585 · Triangle (Equilateral & Isosceles)

If the height of an equilateral triangle is 9√3 cm, what is its area?

A. 81√3 cm²
B. 162√3 cm²
C. 27√3 cm²
D. 54√3 cm²

**Answer:** A. 81√3 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 81√3 cm² (Correct):** For an equilateral triangle of side a, height h = (√3 / 2)a. Given (√3 / 2)a = 9√3 ⇒ a = 18 cm. Area = (√3 / 4)a² = (√3 / 4) × 18 × 18 = 81√3 cm².
- **B — 162√3 cm²:** Obtained by forgetting to divide by 2 in (1/2) × base × height (18 × 9√3 = 162√3).
- **C — 27√3 cm²:** Result of using a = 9 instead of a = 18 in the area formula.
- **D — 54√3 cm²:** Obtained by finding the perimeter (3 × 18 = 54) and attaching √3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use the direct height-to-area formula for an equilateral triangle: Area = h² / √3 = (9√3)² / √3 = 243 / √3 = 81√3 cm².

### Q586 · Triangle (Equilateral & Isosceles)

The difference between the circumradius and the inradius of an equilateral triangle is 6 cm. Find the area of the triangle.

A. 36√3 cm²
B. 54√3 cm²
C. 108√3 cm²
D. 144√3 cm²

**Answer:** C. 108√3 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 108√3 cm² (Correct):** In an equilateral triangle of side a, circumradius R = a / √3 and inradius r = a / (2√3). Given R - r = 6 ⇒ a / (2√3) = 6 ⇒ a = 12√3 cm. Area = (√3 / 4)a² = (√3 / 4) × (12√3)² = (√3 / 4) × 432 = 108√3 cm².
- **A — 36√3 cm²:** Obtained by taking R - r = a / √3 instead of a / (2√3), giving side a = 6√3.
- **B — 54√3 cm²:** Result of halving the correct area or using (√3 / 8)a².
- **D — 144√3 cm²:** Obtained by squaring 12 to 144 and dropping the factor of 3/4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** In an equilateral triangle, R : r = 2 : 1, so R - r = r = 6 cm, and height h = 3r = 18 cm. Then Area = h² / √3 = 324 / √3 = 108√3 cm².

### Q587 · Triangle (Equilateral & Isosceles)

What is the area of the circumcircle of an equilateral triangle of side 12 cm?

A. 12π cm²
B. 48π cm²
C. 36π cm²
D. 144π cm²

**Answer:** B. 48π cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 48π cm² (Correct):** Circumradius of an equilateral triangle R = a / √3 = 12 / √3 = 4√3 cm. Area of the circumcircle = πR² = π × (4√3)² = 48π cm².
- **A — 12π cm²:** Obtained by finding the area of the incircle (r = 2√3, area = 12π) instead of the circumcircle.
- **C — 36π cm²:** Result of taking R = a / 2 = 6 cm instead of a / √3.
- **D — 144π cm²:** Obtained by using R = a = 12 cm directly in πR².
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Remember: for an equilateral triangle of side a, Circumcircle Area = πa² / 3 and Incircle Area = πa² / 12. Here π(144)/3 = 48π cm².

### Q588 · Triangle (Equilateral & Isosceles)

The perimeter of an isosceles triangle is 36 cm and its base is 16 cm. Find its area.

A. 96 cm²
B. 80 cm²
C. 64 cm²
D. 48 cm²

**Answer:** D. 48 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 48 cm² (Correct):** Let each equal side be a. Perimeter = 2a + 16 = 36 ⇒ 2a = 20 ⇒ a = 10 cm. The altitude to the base bisects it into two segments of 8 cm each. Height h = √(10² - 8²) = √(100 - 64) = 6 cm. Area = (1/2) × 16 × 6 = 48 cm².
- **A — 96 cm²:** Obtained by forgetting the 1/2 factor in (1/2) × base × height.
- **B — 80 cm²:** Result of using the equal side 10 cm as the height: (1/2) × 16 × 10 = 80 cm².
- **C — 64 cm²:** Obtained by taking height = 8 cm (the half-base) instead of 6 cm.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Spot the 3-4-5 right triangle! Half-base = 8, equal side = 10 ⇒ (6, 8, 10) triplet gives height = 6 cm instantly, so Area = 8 × 6 = 48 cm².

### Q589 · Triangle (Equilateral & Isosceles)

The area of an isosceles right-angled triangle is 72 cm². What is its perimeter?

A. (24 + 12√2) cm
B. (12 + 12√2) cm
C. (24 + 6√2) cm
D. (18 + 12√2) cm

**Answer:** A. (24 + 12√2) cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — (24 + 12√2) cm (Correct):** Let each equal perpendicular side be a. Area = (1/2)a² = 72 ⇒ a² = 144 ⇒ a = 12 cm. Hypotenuse = a√2 = 12√2 cm. Therefore, perimeter = a + a + a√2 = (24 + 12√2) cm.
- **B — (12 + 12√2) cm:** Obtained by forgetting to double the equal side (12 instead of 24) when summing the perimeter.
- **C — (24 + 6√2) cm:** Result of halving the hypotenuse mistakenly.
- **D — (18 + 12√2) cm:** Obtained by taking a = 9 instead of 12.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For an isosceles right triangle of area A, each leg is √(2A) = √144 = 12 cm, and Perimeter = 2a + a√2 = (24 + 12√2) cm.

### Q590 · Rhombus & Parallelogram

The diagonals of a rhombus are 16 cm and 30 cm. Find the perimeter of the rhombus.

A. 34 cm
B. 68 cm
C. 136 cm
D. 92 cm

**Answer:** B. 68 cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 68 cm (Correct):** The diagonals of a rhombus bisect each other at 90°. Half-diagonals are 16 / 2 = 8 cm and 30 / 2 = 15 cm. Side a = √(8² + 15²) = √(64 + 225) = √289 = 17 cm. Perimeter = 4 × 17 = 68 cm.
- **A — 34 cm:** Obtained by finding the semi-perimeter (2 × 17) instead of the full perimeter.
- **C — 136 cm:** Result of using full diagonals (16 and 30) instead of half-diagonals, giving side = 34 cm and perimeter = 136 cm.
- **D — 92 cm:** Obtained by simply doubling the sum of the two diagonals: 2 × (16 + 30) = 92 cm.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use the (8, 15, 17) Pythagorean triplet on half-diagonals (8, 15) to get side = 17 cm, then multiply by 4 for perimeter = 68 cm.

### Q591 · Rhombus & Parallelogram

The perimeter of a rhombus is 52 cm and the length of one of its diagonals is 24 cm. What is the area of the rhombus?

A. 240 cm²
B. 60 cm²
C. 120 cm²
D. 156 cm²

**Answer:** C. 120 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 120 cm² (Correct):** Side of the rhombus = 52 / 4 = 13 cm. Half of the given diagonal = 24 / 2 = 12 cm. Half of the other diagonal = √(13² - 12²) = √(169 - 144) = 5 cm, so the other diagonal is 10 cm. Area = (1/2) × 24 × 10 = 120 cm².
- **A — 240 cm²:** Obtained by forgetting the (1/2) factor in Area = (1/2) × d₁ × d₂.
- **B — 60 cm²:** Result of multiplying half-diagonals (12 × 5) instead of (1/2) × d₁ × d₂.
- **D — 156 cm²:** Obtained by multiplying side × half-diagonal (13 × 12) instead of using the second diagonal.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Side = 52/4 = 13, half-diagonal = 12 ⇒ (5, 12, 13) triplet gives second half-diagonal = 5, so d₂ = 10 and Area = (24 × 10)/2 = 120 cm².

### Q592 · Rhombus & Parallelogram

Each side of a rhombus is 20 cm and one of its diagonals is 24 cm. Find the perpendicular distance (height) between its opposite sides.

A. 38.4 cm
B. 16 cm
C. 12 cm
D. 19.2 cm

**Answer:** D. 19.2 cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 19.2 cm (Correct):** Half of the given diagonal = 24 / 2 = 12 cm. Half of the other diagonal = √(20² - 12²) = √(400 - 144) = √256 = 16 cm, so d₂ = 32 cm. Area = (1/2) × 24 × 32 = 384 cm². Since a rhombus is also a parallelogram, Area = base × height ⇒ 384 = 20 × h ⇒ h = 19.2 cm.
- **A — 38.4 cm:** Obtained by forgetting the (1/2) in the rhombus area formula before dividing by side 20.
- **B — 16 cm:** Result of confusing the half-diagonal 16 cm with the perpendicular height.
- **C — 12 cm:** Obtained by dividing the given diagonal 24 by 2 instead of computing Area / side.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use (12, 16, 20) [3-4-5 × 4] to get d₂ = 32 cm. Then Height = (d₁ × d₂) / (2 × side) = (24 × 32) / 40 = 19.2 cm.

### Q593 · Rhombus & Parallelogram

The adjacent sides of a parallelogram are 15 cm and 12 cm. If the distance between the longer sides is 8 cm, find the distance between the shorter sides.

A. 10 cm
B. 6.4 cm
C. 12 cm
D. 9.6 cm

**Answer:** A. 10 cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 10 cm (Correct):** Area of the parallelogram = base × corresponding height = 15 × 8 = 120 cm². Using the shorter side (12 cm) as base, Area = 12 × h ⇒ 120 = 12 × h ⇒ h = 10 cm.
- **B — 6.4 cm:** Obtained by pairing the height 8 cm with the shorter side 12 cm and dividing by 15 (12 × 8 / 15 = 6.4 cm).
- **C — 12 cm:** Result of confusing the shorter side length 12 cm with the distance between them.
- **D — 9.6 cm:** Obtained by computing (12 × 8) / 10 mistakenly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Area is constant: b₁h₁ = b₂h₂ ⇒ 15 × 8 = 12 × h₂ ⇒ h₂ = 120 / 12 = 10 cm.

### Q594 · Trapezium

The parallel sides of a trapezium are 24 cm and 18 cm, and its area is 294 cm². What is the perpendicular distance between the parallel sides?

A. 7 cm
B. 14 cm
C. 28 cm
D. 12.25 cm

**Answer:** B. 14 cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 14 cm (Correct):** Area of a trapezium = (1/2) × (sum of parallel sides) × height. Thus, 294 = (1/2) × (24 + 18) × h ⇒ 294 = 21 × h ⇒ h = 294 / 21 = 14 cm.
- **A — 7 cm:** Obtained by forgetting the (1/2) factor in the trapezium area formula, solving 42 × h = 294.
- **C — 28 cm:** Result of multiplying 294 by 2 without dividing by 42 properly (getting 28 cm).
- **D — 12.25 cm:** Obtained by dividing the area 294 by the longer parallel side 24 cm.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Average of parallel sides = (24 + 18) / 2 = 21 cm. Simply divide Area / Average = 294 / 21 = 14 cm.

### Q595 · Trapezium

The area of a trapezium is 384 cm² and the perpendicular distance between its parallel sides is 12 cm. If the parallel sides are in the ratio 3 : 5, find the length of the longer parallel side.

A. 24 cm
B. 20 cm
C. 40 cm
D. 32 cm

**Answer:** C. 40 cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 40 cm (Correct):** Let the parallel sides be 3x and 5x. Area = (1/2) × (3x + 5x) × 12 = 48x. Given 48x = 384 ⇒ x = 8 cm. Length of the longer parallel side = 5x = 5 × 8 = 40 cm.
- **A — 24 cm:** Obtained by finding the shorter parallel side (3x = 24 cm) instead of the longer one.
- **B — 20 cm:** Result of forgetting the (1/2) factor in the area formula, giving 96x = 384 ⇒ x = 4 and 5x = 20 cm.
- **D — 32 cm:** Obtained by finding the average of the parallel sides (384 / 12 = 32 cm) instead of the longer side.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Sum of parallel sides = (2 × Area) / h = 768 / 12 = 64 cm. Split 64 in ratio 3 : 5 ⇒ longer side = (5/8) × 64 = 40 cm.

### Q596 · Trapezium

The parallel sides of an isosceles trapezium are 20 cm and 36 cm, and each of its non-parallel sides is 17 cm. Find the area of the trapezium.

A. 476 cm²
B. 840 cm²
C. 210 cm²
D. 420 cm²

**Answer:** D. 420 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 420 cm² (Correct):** Dropping perpendiculars from the shorter parallel side to the longer one cuts off two congruent right triangles at the ends, each with base = (36 - 20) / 2 = 8 cm and hypotenuse = 17 cm. Height h = √(17² - 8²) = √(289 - 64) = 15 cm. Area = (1/2) × (20 + 36) × 15 = 28 × 15 = 420 cm².
- **A — 476 cm²:** Obtained by using the slant side 17 cm directly as the height: 28 × 17 = 476 cm².
- **B — 840 cm²:** Result of forgetting the (1/2) factor in the trapezium area formula.
- **C — 210 cm²:** Obtained by halving the area mistakenly or taking height = 7.5 cm.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Horizontal projection = (36 - 20)/2 = 8 cm. The (8, 15, 17) triplet gives height = 15 cm immediately; Area = 28 × 15 = 420 cm².

### Q597 · Trapezium

The parallel sides of a trapezium are 40 cm and 25 cm, and the non-parallel sides are 13 cm and 14 cm. What is the area of the trapezium?

A. 364 cm²
B. 438.75 cm²
C. 728 cm²
D. 312 cm²

**Answer:** A. 364 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 364 cm² (Correct):** Draw a line parallel to the 13 cm side through a vertex of the 25 cm side to form a parallelogram (with opposite sides 25 cm and 13 cm) and a triangle with sides 13 cm, 14 cm, and (40 - 25) = 15 cm. For the (13, 14, 15) triangle, s = 21 cm and Area = √(21 × 8 × 7 × 6) = 84 cm². Thus height h = (2 × 84) / 15 = 11.2 cm. Trapezium Area = (1/2) × (40 + 25) × 11.2 = 65 × 5.6 = 364 cm².
- **B — 438.75 cm²:** Obtained by using the average of non-parallel sides (13.5 cm) as the height: 32.5 × 13.5 = 438.75 cm².
- **C — 728 cm²:** Result of forgetting the (1/2) factor in the trapezium area formula.
- **D — 312 cm²:** Obtained by miscalculating the height of the (13, 14, 15) triangle on base 15 as 9.6 cm.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Form the (13, 14, 15) triangle on base diff 40 - 25 = 15: its area is 84, so h = 168/15 = 11.2 cm. Then (65/2) × 11.2 = 65 × 5.6 = 364 cm².

### Q598 · Rectangle & Square (Paths & Percentage Change)

A rectangular park is 45 m long and 30 m wide. A path of uniform width 2.5 m is constructed outside all around it. Find the area of the path.

A. 350 m²
B. 400 m²
C. 193.75 m²
D. 375 m²

**Answer:** B. 400 m²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 400 m² (Correct):** Outer length = 45 + 2(2.5) = 50 m; outer breadth = 30 + 2(2.5) = 35 m. Area of outer rectangle = 50 × 35 = 1750 m². Area of park = 45 × 30 = 1350 m². Area of path = 1750 - 1350 = 400 m².
- **A — 350 m²:** Obtained by subtracting 2w instead of adding 2w (computing an inside path instead of an outside path).
- **C — 193.75 m²:** Result of adding width w = 2.5 only once (47.5 × 32.5 - 1350) instead of 2w on both sides.
- **D — 375 m²:** Obtained by using perimeter × width: 2(45 + 30) × 2.5 = 375 m², missing the four corner squares.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For an outside path of width w around an l × b rectangle: Path Area = 2w(l + b + 2w) = 5 × (45 + 30 + 5) = 5 × 80 = 400 m².

### Q599 · Rectangle & Square (Paths & Percentage Change)

A rectangular lawn 60 m × 40 m has two roads each 5 m wide running through its middle, one parallel to its length and the other parallel to its breadth. Find the cost of gravelling the roads at ₹60 per m².

A. ₹30,000
B. ₹31,500
C. ₹28,500
D. ₹27,000

**Answer:** C. ₹28,500
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹28,500 (Correct):** Area of road parallel to length = 60 × 5 = 300 m². Area of road parallel to breadth = 40 × 5 = 200 m². Area of the overlapping central square = 5 × 5 = 25 m². Total road area = 300 + 200 - 25 = 475 m². Cost = 475 × 60 = ₹28,500.
- **A — ₹30,000:** Obtained by forgetting to subtract the overlapping 5 × 5 m² central square (500 × 60 = ₹30,000).
- **B — ₹31,500:** Result of adding the central square instead of subtracting it (525 × 60 = ₹31,500).
- **D — ₹27,000:** Obtained by subtracting 2 × 25 = 50 m² instead of 25 m².
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Crossroads area = w(l + b - w) = 5 × (60 + 40 - 5) = 475 m². Multiply by ₹60: 475 × 60 = ₹28,500.

### Q600 · Rectangle & Square (Paths & Percentage Change)

A path of uniform width 3 m runs inside along the boundary of a square field of side 34 m. What is the area of the path?

A. 444 m²
B. 408 m²
C. 297 m²
D. 372 m²

**Answer:** D. 372 m²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 372 m² (Correct):** Side of the inner square = 34 - 2(3) = 28 m. Area of the square field = 34² = 1156 m². Area of the inner square = 28² = 784 m². Area of the path = 1156 - 784 = 372 m².
- **A — 444 m²:** Obtained by adding 2w instead of subtracting 2w (computing an outside path: 40² - 34² = 444 m²).
- **B — 408 m²:** Result of multiplying perimeter × width (4 × 34 × 3 = 408 m²) without subtracting the 4 corner overlaps.
- **C — 297 m²:** Obtained by subtracting w = 3 only once or miscalculating 34 - 2w.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For an inside path of width w in a square of side a: Path Area = 4w(a - w) = 4 × 3 × (34 - 3) = 12 × 31 = 372 m².

### Q601 · Rectangle & Square (Paths & Percentage Change)

If the length of a rectangle is increased by 30% and its breadth is decreased by 20%, what is the percentage change in its area?

A. 4% increase
B. 10% increase
C. 4% decrease
D. 6% increase

**Answer:** A. 4% increase
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 4% increase (Correct):** Using the successive percentage change formula x + y + (xy)/100 with x = +30 and y = -20: Net change = 30 - 20 + (30 × (-20)) / 100 = 10 - 6 = +4%, which is a 4% increase.
- **B — 10% increase:** Obtained by simply taking 30% - 20% = 10% and ignoring the cross-product term.
- **C — 4% decrease:** Result of a sign error on 10 - 6 or thinking the decrease dominates.
- **D — 6% increase:** Obtained by computing only the cross-product (30 × 20) / 100 = 6%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use x + y + xy/100: 30 - 20 - (600/100) = +4%. Or multiply factors: 1.3 × 0.8 = 1.04 ⇒ 4% increase.

### Q602 · Rectangle & Square (Paths & Percentage Change)

The diagonal of a rectangle is 25 cm and its perimeter is 62 cm. Find the area of the rectangle.

A. 336 cm²
B. 168 cm²
C. 192 cm²
D. 312 cm²

**Answer:** B. 168 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 168 cm² (Correct):** Given 2(l + b) = 62 ⇒ l + b = 31 cm, and √(l² + b²) = 25 ⇒ l² + b² = 625. Using (l + b)² = l² + b² + 2lb: 31² = 625 + 2lb ⇒ 961 - 625 = 2lb ⇒ 2lb = 336 ⇒ Area (lb) = 168 cm².
- **A — 336 cm²:** Obtained by forgetting to divide 2lb = 336 by 2.
- **C — 192 cm²:** Result of guessing sides 24 and 8 without checking l + b = 31.
- **D — 312 cm²:** Obtained by halving 625 mistakenly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use Area = [(P/2)² - d²] / 2 = (31² - 25²) / 2 = [(31 + 25)(31 - 25)] / 2 = (56 × 6) / 2 = 168 cm².

### Q603 · Rectangle & Square (Paths & Percentage Change)

A wire bent in the form of a square encloses an area of 484 cm². If the same wire is bent into a circle, what is the area enclosed by the circle? (Take π = 22/7)

A. 308 cm²
B. 1,232 cm²
C. 616 cm²
D. 154 cm²

**Answer:** C. 616 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 616 cm² (Correct):** Side of the square = √484 = 22 cm. Length of wire (perimeter) = 4 × 22 = 88 cm. For the circle, 2πr = 88 ⇒ 2 × (22/7) × r = 88 ⇒ r = 14 cm. Area of the circle = πr² = (22/7) × 14 × 14 = 616 cm².
- **A — 308 cm²:** Obtained by finding the area of a semicircle instead of a full circle.
- **B — 1,232 cm²:** Result of doubling the area mistakenly.
- **D — 154 cm²:** Obtained by taking r = 7 cm instead of 14 cm.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Memorize the standard circle table for π = 22/7: when C = 44, r = 7, A = 154; when C = 88 (doubled), r = 14 and A = 4 × 154 = 616 cm².

### Q604 · Circle, Circular Ring & Sector

Find the area of a circular ring whose outer and inner radii are 14 cm and 7 cm respectively. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 154 cm²
B. 616 cm²
C. 308 cm²
D. 462 cm²

**Answer:** D. 462 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 462 cm² (Correct):** Area of a circular ring = π(R² - r²) = (22/7) × (14² - 7²) = (22/7) × (196 - 49) = (22/7) × 147 = 22 × 21 = 462 cm².
- **A — 154 cm²:** Obtained by computing π(R - r)² = (22/7) × 7² = 154 cm² instead of π(R² - r²).
- **B — 616 cm²:** Result of finding only the outer circle area πR² = 616 cm².
- **C — 308 cm²:** Obtained by halving the outer circle area mistakenly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Since R = 2r, Outer Area = 4 × Inner Area = 4 × 154 = 616, so Ring Area = 3 × 154 = 462 cm²!

### Q605 · Circle, Circular Ring & Sector

A circular park of radius 21 m is surrounded by a path of uniform width 7 m outside it. Find the area of the path. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 1,078 m²
B. 154 m²
C. 1,386 m²
D. 2,464 m²

**Answer:** A. 1,078 m²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 1,078 m² (Correct):** Inner radius r = 21 m, outer radius R = 21 + 7 = 28 m. Area of path = π(R² - r²) = π(R + r)(R - r) = (22/7) × (28 + 21) × (28 - 21) = (22/7) × 49 × 7 = 22 × 49 = 1,078 m².
- **B — 154 m²:** Obtained by computing πw² = (22/7) × 7² = 154 m² instead of π(R² - r²).
- **C — 1,386 m²:** Result of finding only the area of the inner circular park (π × 21² = 1,386 m²).
- **D — 2,464 m²:** Obtained by finding the total outer circle area (π × 28² = 2,464 m²) without subtracting the park.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use π(R + r)(R - r): here R - r = 7 cancels the 7 in 22/7, leaving 22 × (28 + 21) = 22 × 49 = 1,078 m².

### Q606 · Circle, Circular Ring & Sector

The circumference of a circle exceeds its diameter by 30 cm. What is the radius of the circle? (Take π = 22/7)

A. 14 cm
B. 7 cm
C. 3.5 cm
D. 10.5 cm

**Answer:** B. 7 cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 7 cm (Correct):** Given 2πr - 2r = 30 ⇒ 2r(π - 1) = 30 ⇒ 2r(22/7 - 1) = 30 ⇒ 2r × (15/7) = 30 ⇒ (30/7)r = 30 ⇒ r = 7 cm.
- **A — 14 cm:** Obtained by finding the diameter (2r = 14 cm) instead of the radius.
- **C — 3.5 cm:** Result of halving the radius mistakenly.
- **D — 10.5 cm:** Obtained by using 2πr - r = 30 instead of 2πr - 2r = 30.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** With π = 22/7, (C - d) = 2r(22/7 - 1) = (30/7)r. Whenever C - d = 30, r is immediately 7 cm!

### Q607 · Circle, Circular Ring & Sector

Find the area of a sector of a circle of radius 21 cm whose central angle is 120°. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 44 cm²
B. 1,386 cm²
C. 462 cm²
D. 231 cm²

**Answer:** C. 462 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 462 cm² (Correct):** Area of sector = (θ / 360°) × πr² = (120° / 360°) × (22/7) × 21 × 21 = (1/3) × 22 × 3 × 21 = 22 × 21 = 462 cm².
- **A — 44 cm²:** Obtained by computing the arc length (θ/360) × 2πr = 44 cm instead of the sector area.
- **B — 1,386 cm²:** Result of finding the area of the entire circle without multiplying by 120°/360°.
- **D — 231 cm²:** Obtained by dividing by 6 instead of 3 (using 60°/360°).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 120° is 1/3 of a circle. Since r = 21 = 3×7, full circle area = 9 × 154 = 1,386; one-third of it is 3 × 154 = 462 cm².

### Q608 · Circle, Circular Ring & Sector

An arc of a circle of radius 14 cm subtends an angle of 72° at the centre. What is the perimeter of the sector? (Take π = 22/7)

A. 17.6 cm
B. 31.6 cm
C. 123.2 cm
D. 45.6 cm

**Answer:** D. 45.6 cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 45.6 cm (Correct):** Arc length l = (θ / 360°) × 2πr = (72° / 360°) × 2 × (22/7) × 14 = (1/5) × 88 = 17.6 cm. Perimeter of sector = l + 2r = 17.6 + 2(14) = 17.6 + 28 = 45.6 cm.
- **A — 17.6 cm:** Obtained by calculating only the arc length (17.6 cm) and forgetting to add the two radii.
- **B — 31.6 cm:** Result of adding the radius only once (17.6 + 14 = 31.6 cm) instead of 2r.
- **C — 123.2 cm:** Obtained by computing the area of the sector (123.2 cm²) instead of its perimeter.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Perimeter of a sector is ALWAYS (arc length + 2r). Here 72°/360° = 1/5 of circumference 88 = 17.6, plus 28 = 45.6 cm.

### Q609 · Circle, Circular Ring & Sector

The area of a sector of a circle of radius 14 cm is 154 cm². What is the central angle of the sector? (Take π = 22/7)

A. 90°
B. 45°
C. 60°
D. 120°

**Answer:** A. 90°
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 90° (Correct):** Full circle area = (22/7) × 14 × 14 = 616 cm². Sector area / Circle area = θ / 360° ⇒ 154 / 616 = θ / 360° ⇒ 1 / 4 = θ / 360° ⇒ θ = 90°.
- **B — 45°:** Obtained by taking 1/8 of 360° instead of 1/4.
- **C — 60°:** Result of dividing 360° by 6 instead of 4.
- **D — 120°:** Obtained by dividing 360° by 3 instead of 4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For r = 14, full circle area is 616 cm² (4 × 154). Since sector area is 154 cm², it is 1/4 of the circle ⇒ 360° / 4 = 90°.

### Q610 · Circle, Circular Ring & Sector

How many revolutions will a wheel of radius 35 cm make to cover a distance of 1.1 km? (Take π = 22/7)

A. 250
B. 500
C. 1,000
D. 50

**Answer:** B. 500
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 500 (Correct):** Circumference of the wheel = 2πr = 2 × (22/7) × 35 = 220 cm = 2.2 m. Total distance = 1.1 km = 1100 m. Number of revolutions = 1100 / 2.2 = 500.
- **A — 250:** Obtained by using diameter = 35 cm or halving 500 mistakenly.
- **C — 1,000:** Result of using πr instead of 2πr (giving circumference 1.1 m and 1000 revolutions).
- **D — 50:** Obtained by converting 1.1 km to 110 m instead of 1100 m.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Radius 35 cm = 5 × 7 cm ⇒ Circumference = 5 × 44 = 220 cm = 2.2 m. Revolutions = 1100 / 2.2 = 500.

### Q611 · Cube & Cuboid

If the length of the space diagonal of a cube is 8√3 cm, find its total surface area.

A. 256 cm²
B. 512 cm²
C. 384 cm²
D. 192 cm²

**Answer:** C. 384 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 384 cm² (Correct):** Space diagonal of a cube = a√3 = 8√3 ⇒ side a = 8 cm. Total surface area = 6a² = 6 × 8² = 6 × 64 = 384 cm².
- **A — 256 cm²:** Obtained by computing the lateral surface area (4a² = 256 cm²) instead of the total surface area.
- **B — 512 cm²:** Result of computing the volume (a³ = 512) instead of the total surface area.
- **D — 192 cm²:** Obtained by dividing 384 by 2 or using 3a².
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For a cube, TSA = 2 × (diagonal)² = 2 × (8√3)² = 2 × 192 = 384 cm² — no need to even find the side!

### Q612 · Cube & Cuboid

Three solid metal cubes of edges 6 cm, 8 cm, and 10 cm are melted and recast into a single cube. What is the total surface area of the new cube?

A. 576 cm²
B. 1,728 cm²
C. 1,200 cm²
D. 864 cm²

**Answer:** D. 864 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 864 cm² (Correct):** Volume of new cube = 6³ + 8³ + 10³ = 216 + 512 + 1000 = 1728 cm³. Edge of new cube a = ∛1728 = 12 cm. Total surface area = 6a² = 6 × 12² = 6 × 144 = 864 cm².
- **A — 576 cm²:** Obtained by computing the lateral surface area (4a² = 576 cm²) instead of the total surface area.
- **B — 1,728 cm²:** Result of reporting the volume (1,728 cm³) instead of the surface area.
- **C — 1,200 cm²:** Obtained by summing the TSAs of the three small cubes (6×(36+64+100) = 1,200 cm²) instead of conserving volume.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Memorize the (6, 8, 10 → 12) cubic-sum identity: 6³ + 8³ + 10³ = 12³! Then TSA = 6 × 12² = 864 cm².

### Q613 · Cube & Cuboid

What is the length of the longest rod that can be placed in a room of dimensions 12 m × 9 m × 8 m?

A. 17 m
B. 15 m
C. 29 m
D. 21 m

**Answer:** A. 17 m
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 17 m (Correct):** The longest rod fits along the space diagonal of the cuboid: d = √(l² + b² + h²) = √(12² + 9² + 8²) = √(144 + 81 + 64) = √289 = 17 m.
- **B — 15 m:** Obtained by computing only the floor diagonal √(12² + 9²) = 15 m, ignoring the height.
- **C — 29 m:** Result of simply adding the three dimensions: 12 + 9 + 8 = 29 m.
- **D — 21 m:** Obtained by miscalculating √(144 + 81 + 64) as 21 m.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Pair two dimensions using a triplet first: (9, 12, 15), then pair 15 with 8: (8, 15, 17) ⇒ 17 m in 3 seconds!

### Q614 · Cube & Cuboid

The sum of the length, breadth, and height of a cuboid is 24 cm and the length of its diagonal is 15 cm. Find the total surface area of the cuboid.

A. 175.5 cm²
B. 351 cm²
C. 801 cm²
D. 360 cm²

**Answer:** B. 351 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 351 cm² (Correct):** We know (l + b + h)² = (l² + b² + h²) + 2(lb + bh + hl). Here l + b + h = 24 and √(l² + b² + h²) = 15 ⇒ l² + b² + h² = 225. Thus, 24² = 225 + TSA ⇒ TSA = 576 - 225 = 351 cm².
- **A — 175.5 cm²:** Obtained by dividing 351 by 2 mistakenly (computing lb + bh + hl instead of 2(lb + bh + hl)).
- **C — 801 cm²:** Result of adding 576 + 225 = 801 instead of subtracting.
- **D — 360 cm²:** Obtained by computing 24 × 15 = 360.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Direct identity: TSA = (l + b + h)² - d² = 24² - 15² = (24 + 15)(24 - 15) = 39 × 9 = 351 cm².

### Q615 · Cube & Cuboid

The areas of three adjacent faces of a cuboid are 12 cm², 15 cm², and 20 cm². What is the volume of the cuboid?

A. 3,600 cm³
B. 94 cm³
C. 60 cm³
D. 120 cm³

**Answer:** C. 60 cm³
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 60 cm³ (Correct):** Let the face areas be lb = 12, bh = 15, hl = 20. Multiplying all three gives (lbh)² = 12 × 15 × 20 = 3600. Therefore, Volume V = lbh = √3600 = 60 cm³.
- **A — 3,600 cm³:** Obtained by forgetting to take the square root of 12 × 15 × 20.
- **B — 94 cm³:** Result of computing the total surface area 2(12 + 15 + 20) = 94 instead of volume.
- **D — 120 cm³:** Obtained by doubling the volume mistakenly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Volume = √(A₁ × A₂ × A₃) = √(12 × 15 × 20) = √(3×4 × 3×5 × 4×5) = 3 × 4 × 5 = 60 cm³.

### Q616 · Cube & Cuboid

An open rectangular water tank (open at the top) is 10 m long, 8 m wide, and 5 m deep. Find the cost of plastering its inner walls and floor at ₹12 per m².

A. ₹4,080
B. ₹2,160
C. ₹4,800
D. ₹3,120

**Answer:** D. ₹3,120
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹3,120 (Correct):** Area of 4 inner walls = 2(l + b)h = 2(10 + 8) × 5 = 180 m². Area of floor = l × b = 10 × 8 = 80 m². Total area = 180 + 80 = 260 m². Cost = 260 × 12 = ₹3,120.
- **A — ₹4,080:** Obtained by including the open top (using full TSA = 340 m² × 12 = ₹4,080).
- **B — ₹2,160:** Result of plastering only the 4 walls (180 × 12 = ₹2,160) and forgetting the floor.
- **C — ₹4,800:** Obtained by multiplying volume (10 × 8 × 5 = 400) by ₹12.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Open-top area = 2(l + b)h + lb = 2(18)(5) + 80 = 260 m². Multiply 260 × 12 = 2600 + 520 = ₹3,120.

### Q617 · Right Circular Cylinder

Find the total surface area of a solid right circular cylinder of radius 7 cm and height 15 cm. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 968 cm²
B. 660 cm²
C. 814 cm²
D. 2,310 cm²

**Answer:** A. 968 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 968 cm² (Correct):** Total surface area of a cylinder = 2πr(r + h) = 2 × (22/7) × 7 × (7 + 15) = 44 × 22 = 968 cm².
- **B — 660 cm²:** Obtained by computing the curved surface area (2πrh = 660 cm²) instead of the total surface area.
- **C — 814 cm²:** Result of adding only one circular base (πr² = 154) to the CSA: 660 + 154 = 814 cm².
- **D — 2,310 cm²:** Obtained by computing the volume (πr²h = 2,310) instead of TSA.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** With r = 7, 2πr = 44. Multiply 44 × (r + h) = 44 × 22 = 88 × 11 = 968 cm².

### Q618 · Right Circular Cylinder

The curved surface area of a right circular cylinder is 880 cm² and its height is 20 cm. Find its volume. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 6,160 cm³
B. 3,080 cm³
C. 1,540 cm³
D. 12,320 cm³

**Answer:** B. 3,080 cm³
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 3,080 cm³ (Correct):** CSA = 2πrh = 880 ⇒ 2 × (22/7) × r × 20 = 880 ⇒ r = (880 × 7) / (44 × 20) = 7 cm. Volume = πr²h = (22/7) × 7 × 7 × 20 = 154 × 20 = 3,080 cm³.
- **A — 6,160 cm³:** Obtained by using V = 2πr²h (forgetting that CSA has 2πrh while V has πr²h).
- **C — 1,540 cm³:** Result of dividing by 2 mistakenly.
- **D — 12,320 cm³:** Obtained by taking r = 14 cm (forgetting the 2 in 2πrh).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Shortcut: Volume = (CSA × r) / 2! First get r = 880 / (2π×20) = 7 cm, then V = (880 × 7) / 2 = 3,080 cm³.

### Q619 · Right Circular Cylinder

The radii of two right circular cylinders are in the ratio 2 : 3 and their heights are in the ratio 5 : 3. What is the ratio of their volumes?

A. 10 : 9
B. 4 : 9
C. 20 : 27
D. 27 : 20

**Answer:** C. 20 : 27
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 20 : 27 (Correct):** Volume of a cylinder is proportional to r²h. Therefore, V₁ : V₂ = (r₁² × h₁) : (r₂² × h₂) = (2² × 5) : (3² × 3) = (4 × 5) : (9 × 3) = 20 : 27.
- **A — 10 : 9:** Obtained by forgetting to square the radius ratio (computing 2 × 5 : 3 × 3 = 10 : 9, which is the ratio of CSAs).
- **B — 4 : 9:** Result of ignoring the height ratio and reporting r₁² : r₂².
- **D — 27 : 20:** Obtained by reversing the ratio V₂ : V₁.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For cylinder volume ratios, square the radius ratio and multiply by height ratio: (4 × 5) : (9 × 3) = 20 : 27.

### Q620 · Right Circular Cylinder

A rectangular sheet of paper 44 cm × 18 cm is rolled along its length to form a right circular cylinder. Find the volume of the cylinder. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 1,134 cm³
B. 5,544 cm³
C. 792 cm³
D. 2,772 cm³

**Answer:** D. 2,772 cm³
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 2,772 cm³ (Correct):** Rolling along the length means the length 44 cm becomes the circumference of the base: 2πr = 44 ⇒ 2 × (22/7) × r = 44 ⇒ r = 7 cm, and height h = 18 cm. Volume = πr²h = (22/7) × 7 × 7 × 18 = 154 × 18 = 2,772 cm³.
- **A — 1,134 cm³:** Obtained by rolling along the breadth (2πr = 18, h = 44) instead of the length.
- **B — 5,544 cm³:** Result of taking r = 14 cm (using πr = 44 instead of 2πr = 44) or doubling the volume.
- **C — 792 cm³:** Obtained by computing the curved surface area (44 × 18 = 792 cm²) instead of volume.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Circumference 44 cm immediately gives r = 7 cm and base area = 154 cm². Volume = 154 × 18 = 3080 - 308 = 2,772 cm³.

### Q621 · Right Circular Cylinder

The ratio of the curved surface area to the total surface area of a right circular cylinder is 1 : 3. If the total surface area is 1,848 cm², find the radius of the cylinder. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 14 cm
B. 7 cm
C. 21 cm
D. 28 cm

**Answer:** A. 14 cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 14 cm (Correct):** Since TSA - CSA = 2πr² (area of the two circular bases) and CSA : TSA = 1 : 3, the two bases account for 1 - 1/3 = 2/3 of the TSA. Thus, 2πr² = (2/3) × 1848 = 1232 ⇒ πr² = 616 ⇒ (22/7)r² = 616 ⇒ r² = 196 ⇒ r = 14 cm.
- **B — 7 cm:** Obtained by finding the height h = r / 2 = 7 cm instead of the radius.
- **C — 21 cm:** Result of solving πr² = 1232 or miscalculating √196.
- **D — 28 cm:** Obtained by reporting the diameter (2r = 28 cm) instead of the radius.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** TSA - CSA = 2πr². Since CSA = (1/3)TSA, 2πr² = (2/3)TSA = 1232 ⇒ πr² = 616 ⇒ r = 14 cm (standard circle value!).

### Q622 · Right Circular Cone

A solid right circular cone has a base radius of 7 cm and a vertical height of 24 cm. Find its total surface area. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 550 cm²
B. 704 cm²
C. 1,232 cm²
D. 682 cm²

**Answer:** B. 704 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 704 cm² (Correct):** Slant height l = √(r² + h²) = √(7² + 24²) = √625 = 25 cm. Total surface area = πr(r + l) = (22/7) × 7 × (7 + 25) = 22 × 32 = 704 cm².
- **A — 550 cm²:** Obtained by computing only the curved surface area (πrl = 550 cm²) and omitting the circular base.
- **C — 1,232 cm²:** Result of computing the volume ((1/3)πr²h = 1,232 cm³) instead of TSA.
- **D — 682 cm²:** Obtained by using vertical height h = 24 cm in place of slant height l = 25 cm: 22 × (7 + 24) = 682 cm².
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use the (7, 24, 25) triplet for l = 25 cm. With r = 7, πr = 22, so TSA = 22 × (7 + 25) = 22 × 32 = 704 cm².

### Q623 · Right Circular Cone

A conical tent has a base radius of 14 m and a vertical height of 48 m. What is the area of the canvas required to make the tent? (Take π = 22/7)

A. 2,112 m²
B. 2,816 m²
C. 2,200 m²
D. 9,856 m²

**Answer:** C. 2,200 m²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 2,200 m² (Correct):** Slant height l = √(14² + 48²) = √(196 + 2304) = √2500 = 50 m. Canvas required = CSA = πrl = (22/7) × 14 × 50 = 44 × 50 = 2,200 m².
- **A — 2,112 m²:** Obtained by using vertical height 48 m instead of slant height 50 m in πrl.
- **B — 2,816 m²:** Result of computing TSA = πr(r + l) = 2,816 m² (a tent does not have canvas on the ground).
- **D — 9,856 m²:** Obtained by computing the volume (1/3)πr²h = 9,856 m³ instead of canvas area.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** (14, 48, 50) is just 2 × (7, 24, 25), so l = 50 m. Canvas = πrl = 44 × 50 = 2,200 m².

### Q624 · Right Circular Cone

The volume of a right circular cone is 1,232 cm³ and its height is 24 cm. Find its curved surface area. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 528 cm²
B. 704 cm²
C. 1,100 cm²
D. 550 cm²

**Answer:** D. 550 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 550 cm² (Correct):** Volume = (1/3)πr²h = 1232 ⇒ (1/3) × (22/7) × r² × 24 = 1232 ⇒ (176/7)r² = 1232 ⇒ r² = 49 ⇒ r = 7 cm. Slant height l = √(7² + 24²) = 25 cm. CSA = πrl = (22/7) × 7 × 25 = 550 cm².
- **A — 528 cm²:** Obtained by using vertical height 24 cm instead of slant height 25 cm in πrl.
- **B — 704 cm²:** Result of computing the total surface area (550 + 154 = 704 cm²) instead of CSA.
- **C — 1,100 cm²:** Obtained by multiplying by 2 (using 2πrl) instead of πrl.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** From V = (1/3)πr²(24) = 8πr² = 1232 ⇒ πr² = 154 ⇒ r = 7 cm. Then (7, 24, 25) gives l = 25 and CSA = 22 × 25 = 550 cm².

### Q625 · Right Circular Cone

The radius and slant height of a cone are in the ratio 4 : 7. If its curved surface area is 792 cm², find its radius. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 12 cm
B. 21 cm
C. 9 cm
D. 24 cm

**Answer:** A. 12 cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 12 cm (Correct):** Let r = 4x and l = 7x. CSA = πrl = (22/7) × 4x × 7x = 88x². Given 88x² = 792 ⇒ x² = 9 ⇒ x = 3. Therefore, radius r = 4x = 4 × 3 = 12 cm.
- **B — 21 cm:** Obtained by finding the slant height (7x = 21 cm) instead of the radius.
- **C — 9 cm:** Result of reporting x² = 9 instead of r = 4x.
- **D — 24 cm:** Obtained by reporting the diameter (2r = 24 cm) instead of the radius.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Notice the 7 in 7x cancels the 7 in 22/7: 88x² = 792 ⇒ x² = 9 ⇒ x = 3, so r = 4 × 3 = 12 cm.

### Q626 · Right Circular Cone

A sector of a circle of radius 15 cm with a central angle of 216° is folded to form a right circular cone by joining its two bounding radii. What is the base radius of the cone?

A. 12 cm
B. 9 cm
C. 15 cm
D. 18 cm

**Answer:** B. 9 cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 9 cm (Correct):** When a sector is folded into a cone, the arc length of the sector becomes the circumference of the cone's base. Arc length = (216° / 360°) × 2π(15) = (3/5) × 30π = 18π cm. Setting 2πr = 18π gives r = 9 cm.
- **A — 12 cm:** Obtained by finding the vertical height of the cone √(15² - 9²) = 12 cm instead of its base radius.
- **C — 15 cm:** Result of confusing the slant height (sector radius 15 cm) with the base radius.
- **D — 18 cm:** Obtained by setting πr = 18π (forgetting the 2 in circumference 2πr).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Direct shortcut: Cone radius r = R × (θ / 360°) = 15 × (216 / 360) = 15 × (3/5) = 9 cm!

### Q627 · Sphere & Hemisphere

The surface area of a solid sphere is 5,544 cm². Find its volume. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 19,404 cm³
B. 12,936 cm³
C. 38,808 cm³
D. 51,744 cm³

**Answer:** C. 38,808 cm³
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 38,808 cm³ (Correct):** Surface area = 4πr² = 5544 ⇒ 4 × (22/7) × r² = 5544 ⇒ r² = (5544 × 7) / 88 = 441 ⇒ r = 21 cm. Volume = (4/3)πr³ = (4/3) × (22/7) × 21 × 21 × 21 = 4 × 22 × 441 = 38,808 cm³.
- **A — 19,404 cm³:** Obtained by computing the volume of a hemisphere ((2/3)πr³ = 19,404 cm³) instead of a full sphere.
- **B — 12,936 cm³:** Result of forgetting the factor of 4 in (4/3)πr³ (computing (1/3)πr³ = 12,936 cm³).
- **D — 51,744 cm³:** Obtained by forgetting to divide by 3 in (4/3)πr³.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Shortcut: Volume of sphere = (Surface Area × r) / 3! Once you get r = 21, V = (5544 × 21) / 3 = 5544 × 7 = 38,808 cm³.

### Q628 · Sphere & Hemisphere

Find the total surface area of a solid hemisphere of radius 14 cm. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 1,232 cm²
B. 2,464 cm²
C. 616 cm²
D. 1,848 cm²

**Answer:** D. 1,848 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 1,848 cm² (Correct):** Total surface area of a solid hemisphere = CSA + base area = 2πr² + πr² = 3πr² = 3 × (22/7) × 14 × 14 = 3 × 616 = 1,848 cm².
- **A — 1,232 cm²:** Obtained by computing only the curved surface area (2πr² = 1,232 cm²) and omitting the flat circular base.
- **B — 2,464 cm²:** Result of computing the surface area of a full sphere (4πr² = 2,464 cm²).
- **C — 616 cm²:** Obtained by computing only the area of the circular base (πr² = 616 cm²).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** With r = 14, πr² = 616 cm². Solid hemisphere TSA = 3πr² = 3 × 616 = 1,848 cm².

### Q629 · Sphere & Hemisphere

A solid sphere is cut into two equal hemispheres. What is the percentage increase in the total surface area?

A. 50%
B. 100%
C. 25%
D. 33.33%

**Answer:** A. 50%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 50% (Correct):** Original surface area of the sphere = 4πr². Cutting it into two equal hemispheres exposes two new circular faces, each of area πr². Total new surface area = 4πr² + 2πr² = 6πr². Percentage increase = (2πr² / 4πr²) × 100 = 50%.
- **B — 100%:** Obtained by assuming cutting into two halves doubles the surface area (100% increase).
- **C — 25%:** Result of counting only one new circular face instead of two: (πr² / 4πr²) × 100 = 25%.
- **D — 33.33%:** Obtained by dividing the increase 2πr² by the new area 6πr² instead of the original area 4πr².
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Every straight cut through a sphere's center adds 2 circular faces (2πr²), which is half of 4πr² ⇒ 50% increase!

### Q630 · Sphere & Hemisphere

If the volumes of two spheres are in the ratio 27 : 64, what is the ratio of their surface areas?

A. 3 : 4
B. 9 : 16
C. 81 : 256
D. 16 : 9

**Answer:** B. 9 : 16
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 9 : 16 (Correct):** Volume ratio V₁ : V₂ = (r₁ : r₂)³ = 27 : 64 ⇒ r₁ : r₂ = ∛27 : ∛64 = 3 : 4. Surface area ratio SA₁ : SA₂ = (r₁ : r₂)² = 3² : 4² = 9 : 16.
- **A — 3 : 4:** Obtained by reporting the ratio of radii (3 : 4) instead of squaring it for surface areas.
- **C — 81 : 256:** Result of squaring 9 : 16 or forgetting to take the cube root first.
- **D — 16 : 9:** Obtained by reversing the ratio.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Volumes are in ratio k³, so take cube roots (3 : 4) and then square them (9 : 16) for surface areas.

### Q631 · Sphere & Hemisphere

The volume of a hemisphere is 19,404 cm³. Find its curved surface area. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 4,158 cm²
B. 5,544 cm²
C. 2,772 cm²
D. 1,386 cm²

**Answer:** C. 2,772 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 2,772 cm² (Correct):** Volume = (2/3)πr³ = 19404 ⇒ (2/3) × (22/7) × r³ = 19404 ⇒ r³ = (19404 × 21) / 44 = 441 × 21 = 21³ ⇒ r = 21 cm. Curved surface area = 2πr² = 2 × (22/7) × 21 × 21 = 44 × 63 = 2,772 cm².
- **A — 4,158 cm²:** Obtained by computing the total surface area (3πr² = 4,158 cm²) instead of the curved surface area.
- **B — 5,544 cm²:** Result of computing the surface area of a full sphere (4πr² = 5,544 cm²).
- **D — 1,386 cm²:** Obtained by computing only the base area (πr² = 1,386 cm²).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Since V = (2/3)πr³ and CSA = 2πr², notice CSA = 3V / r. Once r = 21, CSA = (3 × 19404) / 21 = 19404 / 7 = 2,772 cm².

### Q632 · Hollow Cylinder & Frustum

A hollow cylindrical iron pipe is 21 cm long and its outer and inner diameters are 10 cm and 8 cm respectively. Find the volume of the iron used in making the pipe. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 2,376 cm³
B. 66 cm³
C. 1,650 cm³
D. 594 cm³

**Answer:** D. 594 cm³
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 594 cm³ (Correct):** Outer radius R = 10 / 2 = 5 cm, inner radius r = 8 / 2 = 4 cm, height h = 21 cm. Volume of iron = π(R² - r²)h = (22/7) × (5² - 4²) × 21 = 22 × 9 × 3 = 594 cm³.
- **A — 2,376 cm³:** Obtained by using diameters (10 and 8) instead of radii (5 and 4) in the formula.
- **B — 66 cm³:** Result of using (R - r)² = (5 - 4)² = 1 instead of (R² - r²) = 9.
- **C — 1,650 cm³:** Obtained by computing the full outer cylinder volume πR²h = 1,650 cm³ without subtracting the hollow core.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use π(R + r)(R - r)h: here h = 21 cancels 7 to give 3, so 22 × 9 × 1 × 3 = 594 cm³.

### Q633 · Hollow Cylinder & Frustum

A hollow cylinder open at both ends has an outer radius of 14 cm, an inner radius of 7 cm, and a height of 10 cm. Find its total surface area. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 2,244 cm²
B. 1,320 cm²
C. 440 cm²
D. 1,782 cm²

**Answer:** A. 2,244 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 2,244 cm² (Correct):** Total surface area of an open hollow cylinder = outer CSA + inner CSA + 2 × ring area = 2π(R + r)h + 2π(R² - r²) = 2π(R + r)(h + R - r) = 2 × (22/7) × (14 + 7) × (10 + 14 - 7) = 2 × 22 × 3 × 17 = 132 × 17 = 2,244 cm².
- **B — 1,320 cm²:** Obtained by computing only the outer and inner CSAs (2π(R + r)h = 1,320 cm²) and forgetting the two end rings.
- **C — 440 cm²:** Result of using (R - r) instead of (R + r) in the curved surface area.
- **D — 1,782 cm²:** Obtained by adding only one end ring (1,320 + 462 = 1,782 cm²) instead of two.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Factor the hollow cylinder TSA formula cleanly: 2π(R + r)(h + R - r) = 2 × (22/7) × 21 × 17 = 132 × 17 = 2,244 cm².

### Q634 · Hollow Cylinder & Frustum

A bucket in the form of a frustum of a cone has top and bottom radii of 14 cm and 7 cm respectively, and a vertical height of 24 cm. Find its curved surface area. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 1,584 cm²
B. 1,650 cm²
C. 550 cm²
D. 1,804 cm²

**Answer:** B. 1,650 cm²
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 1,650 cm² (Correct):** Slant height l = √[h² + (R - r)²] = √[24² + (14 - 7)²] = √(576 + 49) = √625 = 25 cm. CSA of frustum = π(R + r)l = (22/7) × (14 + 7) × 25 = 22 × 3 × 25 = 1,650 cm².
- **A — 1,584 cm²:** Obtained by using the vertical height 24 cm instead of the slant height 25 cm.
- **C — 550 cm²:** Result of using (R - r) = 7 instead of (R + r) = 21 in the CSA formula.
- **D — 1,804 cm²:** Obtained by adding the bottom circular base πr² = 154 cm² to the CSA.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** R - r = 7 and h = 24 ⇒ (7, 24, 25) triplet gives l = 25 cm! Then π(R + r)l = (22/7) × 21 × 25 = 66 × 25 = 1,650 cm².

### Q635 · Hollow Cylinder & Frustum

The radii of the circular ends of a frustum of a cone are 21 cm and 7 cm, and its height is 15 cm. Find the volume of the frustum. (Take π = 22/7)

A. 7,700 cm³
B. 30,030 cm³
C. 10,010 cm³
D. 8,470 cm³

**Answer:** C. 10,010 cm³
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 10,010 cm³ (Correct):** Volume of frustum = (1/3)πh(R² + r² + Rr) = (1/3) × (22/7) × 15 × (21² + 7² + 21 × 7) = (22/7) × 5 × (441 + 49 + 147) = (22/7) × 5 × 637 = 22 × 5 × 91 = 10,010 cm³.
- **A — 7,700 cm³:** Obtained by omitting the Rr term in the frustum volume formula: (22/7) × 5 × 490 = 7,700 cm³.
- **B — 30,030 cm³:** Result of forgetting the (1/3) factor in the frustum volume formula.
- **D — 8,470 cm³:** Obtained by using (R² + r² + Rr/2) or miscalculating 21 × 7.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Factor 7² out of (21² + 7² + 21×7) = 49(9 + 1 + 3) = 49 × 13 = 637! Then (22/7) × 5 × 49 × 13 = 22 × 35 × 13 = 10,010 cm³.

### Q636 · Melting & Recasting Solids

A solid metallic sphere of radius 6 cm is melted and drawn into a wire of uniform circular cross-section of radius 0.2 cm. What is the length of the wire (in metres)?

A. 720 m
B. 18 m
C. 28.8 m
D. 72 m

**Answer:** D. 72 m
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 72 m (Correct):** Equating volumes: (4/3)πR³ = πr²L ⇒ (4/3) × 6³ = (0.2)² × L ⇒ 288 = 0.04 × L ⇒ L = 288 / 0.04 = 7,200 cm = 72 m.
- **A — 720 m:** Obtained by dividing 7,200 cm by 10 instead of 100 when converting centimetres to metres.
- **B — 18 m:** Result of using diameter 0.4 cm as radius or dividing 72 by 4 mistakenly.
- **C — 28.8 m:** Obtained by forgetting to square 0.2 or misplacing the decimal point.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Cancel π on both sides immediately! L = (4 × 216) / (3 × 0.04) = 288 × 25 = 7,200 cm = 72 m.

### Q637 · Melting & Recasting Solids

How many solid spherical balls of radius 3 cm can be made by melting a solid right circular cylinder of radius 6 cm and height 18 cm?

A. 18
B. 24
C. 6
D. 36

**Answer:** A. 18
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 18 (Correct):** Number of balls n = Volume of cylinder / Volume of one sphere = (π × 6² × 18) / [(4/3)π × 3³] = (36 × 18) / 36 = 18.
- **B — 24:** Obtained by forgetting the (4/3) factor in sphere volume (using πr³ instead).
- **C — 6:** Result of using diameter 6 cm as sphere radius instead of 3 cm.
- **D — 36:** Obtained by doubling 18 mistakenly (using 2/3 instead of 4/3).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Since (4/3) × 3³ = 36, the denominator equals 6² = 36 in the numerator — they cancel out cleanly leaving n = 18!

### Q638 · Melting & Recasting Solids

A cylindrical well of diameter 14 m is dug 12 m deep. The earth taken out is spread evenly all around it to form a circular embankment of width 7 m. Find the height of the embankment.

A. 3 m
B. 4 m
C. 1 m
D. 12 m

**Answer:** B. 4 m
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 4 m (Correct):** Radius of well r = 14 / 2 = 7 m. Volume of earth dug out = πr²h = π × 7² × 12 = 588π m³. Outer radius of embankment R = 7 + 7 = 14 m. Ring area = π(R² - r²) = π(14² - 7²) = 147π m². Height of embankment = 588π / 147π = 4 m.
- **A — 3 m:** Obtained by dividing the volume by the outer circle area πR² = 196π instead of the ring area 147π.
- **C — 1 m:** Result of using diameter 14 m as the radius of the well.
- **D — 12 m:** Obtained by forgetting to divide by (R² - r²) / r² = 3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Since R = 2r, the ring area π(4r² - r²) = 3πr² is exactly 3 times the well's base area! So embankment height = depth / 3 = 12 / 3 = 4 m.

### Q639 · Melting & Recasting Solids

A solid cylinder of radius 8 cm and height 12 cm is melted and recast into a solid cone of base radius 16 cm. What is the height of the cone?

A. 3 cm
B. 18 cm
C. 9 cm
D. 36 cm

**Answer:** C. 9 cm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 9 cm (Correct):** Equating volumes: πr₁²h₁ = (1/3)πr₂²h₂ ⇒ 8² × 12 = (1/3) × 16² × H ⇒ 64 × 12 = (1/3) × 256 × H ⇒ 12 = (4/3)H ⇒ H = 9 cm.
- **A — 3 cm:** Obtained by forgetting the (1/3) factor in the cone's volume (solving 64 × 12 = 256 × H ⇒ H = 3 cm).
- **B — 18 cm:** Result of halving the radius ratio instead of squaring it.
- **D — 36 cm:** Obtained by multiplying 12 by 3 without dividing by (16/8)² = 4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Radius doubled (×2) ⇒ base area quadrupled (×4). To keep volume equal, cone height H = 3 × (12 / 4) = 9 cm.

### Q640 · Regular Prism & Pyramid

The base of a right prism is an equilateral triangle of side 8 cm, and the height of the prism is 15 cm. Find the volume of the prism.

A. 80√3 cm³
B. 480√3 cm³
C. 360 cm³
D. 240√3 cm³

**Answer:** D. 240√3 cm³
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 240√3 cm³ (Correct):** Area of equilateral triangular base = (√3 / 4) × 8² = 16√3 cm². Volume of right prism = Base Area × Height = 16√3 × 15 = 240√3 cm³.
- **A — 80√3 cm³:** Obtained by multiplying by 1/3 (confusing a prism with a pyramid).
- **B — 480√3 cm³:** Result of using (√3 / 2)a² instead of (√3 / 4)a² for the equilateral triangle's area.
- **C — 360 cm³:** Obtained by computing the lateral surface area (3 × 8 × 15 = 360 cm²) instead of the volume.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Prism Volume = Base Area × Height (never 1/3!). Base area = 16√3, × 15 = 240√3 cm³.
