## Chapter 11: Trigonometry & Heights-Distances

### Q641 · Pythagorean Identities

If sin θ + cos θ = √3, what is the value of (tan θ + cot θ)?

A. 2/3
B. 1
C. 2
D. √3

**Answer:** B. 1
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 1 (Correct):** Squaring both sides of sin θ + cos θ = √3 gives sin²θ + cos²θ + 2 sin θ cos θ = 3. Since sin²θ + cos²θ = 1, we get 1 + 2 sin θ cos θ = 3, so 2 sin θ cos θ = 2 and sin θ cos θ = 1. Now tan θ + cot θ = (sin θ / cos θ) + (cos θ / sin θ) = (sin²θ + cos²θ) / (sin θ cos θ) = 1 / 1 = 1.
- **A — 2/3:** Result of forgetting to subtract 1 from k² when computing 2 sin θ cos θ, leading to 2 / 3.
- **C — 2:** Value obtained by confusing sin θ cos θ = 1 with tan θ + cot θ = 2 sin θ cos θ = 2.
- **D — √3:** Mistake of taking tan θ + cot θ = √3 directly without squaring the given equation.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If sin θ + cos θ = k, then tan θ + cot θ = 2 / (k² - 1) directly. For k = √3, 2 / (3 - 1) = 1.

### Q642 · Pythagorean Identities

Simplify: (1 + tan²θ)(1 - sin θ)(1 + sin θ), where 0° ≤ θ < 90°.

A. 1
B. 2
C. 0
D. -1

**Answer:** A. 1
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 1 (Correct):** Using the identities 1 + tan²θ = sec²θ and (1 - sin θ)(1 + sin θ) = 1 - sin²θ = cos²θ, the expression becomes sec²θ · cos²θ = (1 / cos²θ) · cos²θ = 1.
- **B — 2:** Result of mistakenly writing (1 - sin θ)(1 + sin θ) = 1 + sin²θ instead of 1 - sin²θ.
- **C — 0:** Value obtained by replacing 1 + tan²θ with cosec²θ instead of sec²θ.
- **D — -1:** Result of dropping the sec²θ factor and leaving cos²θ or sin²θ instead of cancelling to 1.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Put θ = 0° (or 45°) whenever the options are constants: (1 + 0)(1 - 0)(1 + 0) = 1 in 3 seconds.

### Q643 · Pythagorean Identities

If sin⁴θ - cos⁴θ = 2/3, then find the value of (2 sin²θ - 1).

A. 1/3
B. -2/3
C. 2/3
D. 4/3

**Answer:** C. 2/3
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 2/3 (Correct):** Factor sin⁴θ - cos⁴θ as (sin²θ - cos²θ)(sin²θ + cos²θ). Since sin²θ + cos²θ = 1, we have sin²θ - cos²θ = 2/3. Substituting cos²θ = 1 - sin²θ gives sin²θ - (1 - sin²θ) = 2 sin²θ - 1 = 2/3.
- **A — 1/3:** Result of dividing 2/3 by 2 after mistakenly thinking 2 sin²θ - 1 is half of sin²θ - cos²θ.
- **B — -2/3:** Value obtained by computing 1 - 2/3 instead of recognizing the expressions are identical.
- **D — 4/3:** Mistake of doubling 2/3 to 4/3 due to the coefficient 2 in 2 sin²θ - 1.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Remember the identity sin⁴θ - cos⁴θ = sin²θ - cos²θ = 2 sin²θ - 1 = 1 - 2 cos²θ; no calculation needed!

### Q644 · Pythagorean Identities

If sec²θ + tan²θ = 7/12, what is the value of (sec⁴θ - tan⁴θ)?

A. 49/144
B. 12/7
C. 5/12
D. 7/12

**Answer:** D. 7/12
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 7/12 (Correct):** Factor sec⁴θ - tan⁴θ = (sec²θ - tan²θ)(sec²θ + tan²θ). Using the Pythagorean identity sec²θ - tan²θ = 1, the product equals 1 × (7/12) = 7/12.
- **A — 49/144:** Result of squaring 7/12 to 49/144 instead of using difference of squares.
- **B — 12/7:** Value obtained by taking the reciprocal 12/7 under the wrong assumption that sec²θ - tan²θ is inverted.
- **C — 5/12:** Mistake of subtracting 1 from 7/12 instead of multiplying by 1.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Since sec²θ - tan²θ = 1, sec⁴θ - tan⁴θ is always equal to sec²θ + tan²θ.

### Q645 · Pythagorean Identities

Find the value of (sin⁶θ + cos⁶θ - 1) / (sin⁴θ + cos⁴θ - 1), where θ is an acute angle.

A. 2/3
B. 3/2
C. -3/2
D. 1

**Answer:** B. 3/2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 3/2 (Correct):** Using sin⁶θ + cos⁶θ = 1 - 3 sin²θ cos²θ and sin⁴θ + cos⁴θ = 1 - 2 sin²θ cos²θ, the numerator is (1 - 3 sin²θ cos²θ) - 1 = -3 sin²θ cos²θ and the denominator is (1 - 2 sin²θ cos²θ) - 1 = -2 sin²θ cos²θ. Their ratio is (-3) / (-2) = 3/2.
- **A — 2/3:** Result of inverting the numerator and denominator to get 2/3.
- **C — -3/2:** Value obtained by dropping the negative sign in only one of the terms.
- **D — 1:** Mistake of thinking sin⁶θ + cos⁶θ = 1 - 2 sin²θ cos²θ, which would make the ratio 1.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Memorize: sin⁴θ + cos⁴θ = 1 - 2s²c² and sin⁶θ + cos⁶θ = 1 - 3s²c², or simply plug θ = 45°: (1/8 + 1/8 - 1)/(1/4 + 1/4 - 1) = (-3/4)/(-1/2) = 3/2.

### Q646 · Pythagorean Identities

If cosec²θ + cot²θ = 5/3, then the value of (cosec⁴θ - cot⁴θ) is:

A. 5/3
B. 2/3
C. 25/9
D. 3/5

**Answer:** A. 5/3
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 5/3 (Correct):** Factor cosec⁴θ - cot⁴θ = (cosec²θ - cot²θ)(cosec²θ + cot²θ). Since cosec²θ - cot²θ = 1, the value is 1 × (5/3) = 5/3.
- **B — 2/3:** Result of subtracting 1 from 5/3 to get 2/3 instead of multiplying by 1.
- **C — 25/9:** Value obtained by squaring 5/3 to 25/9.
- **D — 3/5:** Mistake of taking the reciprocal 3/5 instead of keeping 5/3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Just like sec⁴ - tan⁴, cosec⁴θ - cot⁴θ always equals cosec²θ + cot²θ because cosec²θ - cot²θ = 1.

### Q647 · Pythagorean Identities

What is the value of 3(sin⁴θ + cos⁴θ) - 2(sin⁶θ + cos⁶θ)?

A. 0
B. 2
C. 1
D. -1

**Answer:** C. 1
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 1 (Correct):** Using sin⁴θ + cos⁴θ = 1 - 2 sin²θ cos²θ and sin⁶θ + cos⁶θ = 1 - 3 sin²θ cos²θ, we get 3(1 - 2 sin²θ cos²θ) - 2(1 - 3 sin²θ cos²θ) = 3 - 6 sin²θ cos²θ - 2 + 6 sin²θ cos²θ = 1.
- **A — 0:** Result of adding the 6 sin²θ cos²θ terms instead of cancelling them, or misreading the coefficients.
- **B — 2:** Value obtained by evaluating 3 - 2 + 1 = 2 due to a sign error.
- **D — -1:** Mistake of forgetting the constant 3 - 2 = 1 and assuming all terms cancel to 0.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Put θ = 0°: 3(0 + 1) - 2(0 + 1) = 3 - 2 = 1 instantly.

### Q648 · Pythagorean Identities

If cos θ + cos²θ = 1, find the value of sin¹²θ + 3 sin¹⁰θ + 3 sin⁸θ + sin⁶θ - 1.

A. 1
B. 2
C. -1
D. 0

**Answer:** D. 0
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 0 (Correct):** From cos θ + cos²θ = 1, we get cos θ = 1 - cos²θ = sin²θ. Squaring both sides gives cos²θ = sin⁴θ. Adding these gives sin²θ + sin⁴θ = cos θ + cos²θ = 1. Notice that sin¹²θ + 3 sin¹⁰θ + 3 sin⁸θ + sin⁶θ = (sin⁴θ + sin²θ)³ = 1³ = 1. Subtracting 1 yields 1 - 1 = 0.
- **A — 1:** Result of forgetting to subtract the final 1 at the end of the expression.
- **B — 2:** Value obtained by evaluating (sin⁴θ + sin²θ)³ + 1 instead of subtracting 1.
- **C — -1:** Mistake of taking the cube root or mis-grouping the middle coefficients to get -1.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Spot the binomial cube (a + b)³ = a³ + 3a²b + 3ab² + b³ with a = sin⁴θ and b = sin²θ. Since a + b = 1, the cube is 1, minus 1 gives 0.

### Q649 · Standard Angle Table

Evaluate: (4/3)cot²30° + 3 sin²60° - 2 cosec²60° - (3/4)tan²30°.

A. 10/3
B. 23/6
C. 8/3
D. 4/3

**Answer:** A. 10/3
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 10/3 (Correct):** Substitute standard values: cot 30° = √3 (so cot²30° = 3), sin 60° = √3/2 (sin²60° = 3/4), cosec 60° = 2/√3 (cosec²60° = 4/3), tan 30° = 1/√3 (tan²30° = 1/3). Thus (4/3)(3) + 3(3/4) - 2(4/3) - (3/4)(1/3) = 4 + 9/4 - 8/3 - 1/4 = 4 + (9/4 - 1/4) - 8/3 = 4 + 2 - 8/3 = 6 - 8/3 = 10/3.
- **B — 23/6:** Result of adding 1/4 instead of subtracting it in the last term, giving 4 + 10/4 - 8/3 = 23/6.
- **C — 8/3:** Value obtained by taking cosec 60° = √3/2 instead of 2/√3.
- **D — 4/3:** Mistake of taking cot 30° = 1/√3 instead of √3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Group fractions with the same denominator first: (9/4 - 1/4) = 2, turning four fractions into a trivial integer 6 - 8/3 = 10/3.

### Q650 · Standard Angle Table

If tan(A + B) = √3 and tan(A - B) = 1/√3, where 0° < A + B ≤ 90° and A > B, find the values of A and B respectively.

A. 30°, 15°
B. 50°, 10°
C. 45°, 15°
D. 60°, 30°

**Answer:** C. 45°, 15°
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 45°, 15° (Correct):** Since tan 60° = √3 and tan 30° = 1/√3, we have A + B = 60° and A - B = 30°. Adding the two equations gives 2A = 90°, so A = 45°. Substituting back gives B = 60° - 45° = 15°.
- **A — 30°, 15°:** Result of swapping A and B or misreading tan 30° as √3.
- **B — 50°, 10°:** Value obtained by forgetting to divide by 2 when solving 2A = 90°.
- **D — 60°, 30°:** Mistake of taking tan 45° = √3 or tan 15° = 1/√3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When A + B = α and A - B = β, always use A = (α + β)/2 and B = (α - β)/2 mentally.

### Q651 · Standard Angle Table

Find the value of (sin²30° + cos²45° - tan²60°) / (sec²30° - cot²45°).

A. -3/4
B. -27/4
C. 27/4
D. -9/4

**Answer:** B. -27/4
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — -27/4 (Correct):** Substitute values: sin 30° = 1/2 (sin²30° = 1/4), cos 45° = 1/√2 (cos²45° = 1/2), tan 60° = √3 (tan²60° = 3), sec 30° = 2/√3 (sec²30° = 4/3), cot 45° = 1 (cot²45° = 1). Numerator = 1/4 + 1/2 - 3 = 3/4 - 3 = -9/4. Denominator = 4/3 - 1 = 1/3. Dividing gives (-9/4) ÷ (1/3) = -27/4.
- **A — -3/4:** Result of multiplying (-9/4) by (1/3) instead of dividing by (1/3).
- **C — 27/4:** Value obtained by dropping the negative sign in 3/4 - 3.
- **D — -9/4:** Mistake of using sec 30° = √2 instead of 2/√3, making the denominator 1.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Compute numerator (-9/4) and denominator (1/3) separately, then multiply numerator by 3 to get -27/4.

### Q652 · Standard Angle Table

If x sin 45° cos²60° = (tan²60° · cosec 30°) / (sec 45° · cot²30°), then the value of x is:

A. 4
B. 4√2
C. 2
D. 8

**Answer:** D. 8
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 8 (Correct):** Substitute standard values: sin 45° = 1/√2, cos 60° = 1/2 (cos²60° = 1/4), tan 60° = √3 (tan²60° = 3), cosec 30° = 2, sec 45° = √2, cot 30° = √3 (cot²30° = 3). LHS = x · (1/√2) · (1/4) = x / (4√2). RHS = (3 · 2) / (√2 · 3) = 2 / √2 = √2. Equating LHS and RHS: x / (4√2) = √2, so x = 4√2 · √2 = 8.
- **A — 4:** Result of forgetting to square cos 60° on the LHS, leaving x / (2√2) = √2.
- **B — 4√2:** Value obtained by cancelling √2 incorrectly as x/4 = 2/√2 instead of multiplying by √2.
- **C — 2:** Mistake of setting cot 30° = 1/√3 instead of √3 on the RHS.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Cancel √2 from both sides' denominators immediately: x/4 = (3 × 2)/3 = 2, so x = 8.

### Q653 · Standard Angle Table

If 2 sin²θ - 3 sin θ + 1 = 0 and 0° < θ < 90°, what is the value of θ?

A. 30°
B. 60°
C. 45°
D. 90°

**Answer:** A. 30°
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 30° (Correct):** Factor the quadratic in sin θ: 2 sin²θ - 3 sin θ + 1 = (2 sin θ - 1)(sin θ - 1) = 0. This gives sin θ = 1/2 or sin θ = 1. Since 0° < θ < 90°, sin θ = 1 (which corresponds to θ = 90°) is excluded. Hence sin θ = 1/2, which gives θ = 30°.
- **B — 60°:** Value obtained by accepting sin θ = 1 without checking the strict inequality 0° < θ < 90°.
- **C — 45°:** Result of confusing sin θ = 1/2 (30°) with cos θ = 1/2 (60°).
- **D — 90°:** Mistake of assuming sin θ = 1/√2 solves the quadratic.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Test θ = 30°, 45°, 60° directly: at θ = 30°, 2(1/4) - 3(1/2) + 1 = 1/2 - 3/2 + 1 = 0!

### Q654 · Standard Angle Table

Evaluate: (cos 0° + sin 45° + sin 30°)(sin 90° - cos 45° + cos 60°).

A. 2
B. 7/4
C. 11/4
D. 5/4

**Answer:** B. 7/4
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 7/4 (Correct):** Substitute standard values: cos 0° = 1, sin 45° = 1/√2, sin 30° = 1/2, sin 90° = 1, cos 45° = 1/√2, cos 60° = 1/2. The product becomes (1 + 1/√2 + 1/2)(1 - 1/√2 + 1/2) = (3/2 + 1/√2)(3/2 - 1/√2) = (3/2)² - (1/√2)² = 9/4 - 1/2 = 7/4.
- **A — 2:** Result of forgetting to square 1/√2 or subtracting 1/4 instead of 1/2.
- **C — 11/4:** Value obtained by adding 9/4 + 1/2 instead of subtracting.
- **D — 5/4:** Mistake of taking cos 0° = 0 instead of 1, giving (1/2)² - 1/2 = -1/4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Recognize the (a + b)(a - b) pattern with a = 1 + 1/2 = 3/2 and b = 1/√2, giving 9/4 - 1/2 = 7/4 in 10 seconds.

### Q655 · Standard Angle Table

If √3 tan θ = 3 sin θ (0° < θ < 90°), find the value of (sin²θ - cos²θ).

A. -1/3
B. 2/3
C. 1/3
D. 1/2

**Answer:** C. 1/3
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 1/3 (Correct):** Rewrite √3 tan θ = 3 sin θ as √3 (sin θ / cos θ) = 3 sin θ. Since 0° < θ < 90°, sin θ ≠ 0, so we can divide both sides by sin θ: √3 / cos θ = 3, giving cos θ = √3 / 3 = 1/√3. Then sin²θ = 1 - cos²θ = 1 - 1/3 = 2/3. Therefore, sin²θ - cos²θ = 2/3 - 1/3 = 1/3.
- **A — -1/3:** Result of computing cos²θ - sin²θ instead of sin²θ - cos²θ.
- **B — 2/3:** Value obtained by reporting sin²θ = 2/3 without subtracting cos²θ.
- **D — 1/2:** Mistake of inverting cos θ = √3/3 to get cos²θ = 3 or miscomputing 1 - 1/3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Cancel sin θ immediately to get cos θ = 1/√3, then use sin²θ - cos²θ = 1 - 2 cos²θ = 1 - 2/3 = 1/3.

### Q656 · Standard Angle Table

If cos²θ / (cot²θ - cos²θ) = 3, where 0° < θ < 90°, then the value of θ is:

A. 30°
B. 45°
C. 15°
D. 60°

**Answer:** D. 60°
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 60° (Correct):** Simplify the LHS: cos²θ / (cot²θ - cos²θ) = cos²θ / (cos²θ/sin²θ - cos²θ) = 1 / (1/sin²θ - 1) = 1 / ((1 - sin²θ)/sin²θ) = sin²θ / cos²θ = tan²θ. Given tan²θ = 3 and 0° < θ < 90°, we get tan θ = √3, so θ = 60°.
- **A — 30°:** Result of confusing tan θ = √3 (60°) with tan θ = 1/√3 (30°).
- **B — 45°:** Value obtained by misidentifying tan 45° = √3.
- **C — 15°:** Mistake of taking θ = 90° where cot 90° = 0 and the expression is undefined.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Always remember cos²θ / (cot²θ - cos²θ) simplifies to tan²θ; or test θ = 60° directly: (1/4)/(1/3 - 1/4) = (1/4)/(1/12) = 3.

### Q657 · sec + tan = k Identities

If sec θ + tan θ = 5 (0° < θ < 90°), find the value of sin θ.

A. 5/13
B. 13/12
C. 12/13
D. 5/12

**Answer:** C. 12/13
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 12/13 (Correct):** Given sec θ + tan θ = 5. Using sec²θ - tan²θ = 1, we have sec θ - tan θ = 1/5. Adding the two equations gives 2 sec θ = 5 + 1/5 = 26/5, so sec θ = 13/5 and cos θ = 5/13. Subtracting gives 2 tan θ = 5 - 1/5 = 24/5, so tan θ = 12/5. Thus sin θ = tan θ · cos θ = (12/5)(5/13) = 12/13.
- **A — 5/13:** Value of cos θ instead of sin θ.
- **B — 13/12:** Result of computing (k² + 1)/(k² - 1) = 13/12, which is cosec θ instead of sin θ.
- **D — 5/12:** Mistake of taking 2 tan θ = 24/5 as tan θ = 12/5 and reporting tan θ or 5/12 instead of sin θ.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If sec θ + tan θ = k, then sin θ = (k² - 1) / (k² + 1) directly. For k = 5: (25 - 1)/(25 + 1) = 24/26 = 12/13.

### Q658 · sec + tan = k Identities

If sec θ - tan θ = 1/3 (0° < θ < 90°), then what is the value of (sec θ + cot θ)?

A. 29/12
B. 3
C. 11/12
D. 25/12

**Answer:** A. 29/12
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 29/12 (Correct):** Given sec θ - tan θ = 1/3, its reciprocal gives sec θ + tan θ = 3. Adding both gives 2 sec θ = 3 + 1/3 = 10/3, so sec θ = 5/3. Subtracting gives 2 tan θ = 3 - 1/3 = 8/3, so tan θ = 4/3, which means cot θ = 3/4. Therefore, sec θ + cot θ = 5/3 + 3/4 = (20 + 9)/12 = 29/12.
- **B — 3:** Result of adding sec θ + tan θ = 5/3 + 4/3 = 3 instead of sec θ + cot θ.
- **C — 11/12:** Value obtained by computing sec θ - cot θ = 5/3 - 3/4 = 11/12.
- **D — 25/12:** Mistake of forgetting to invert tan θ = 4/3 to cot θ = 3/4, or mixing numerators to get 25/12.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Spot the 3-4-5 right triangle immediately from sec θ + tan θ = 3: sec = 5/3, tan = 4/3, cot = 3/4.

### Q659 · cosec + cot = k Identities

If cosec θ + cot θ = 2 + √5 (0° < θ < 90°), find the value of (sin θ + cos θ).

A. √5/5
B. 2√5/5
C. 3√5
D. 3√5/5

**Answer:** D. 3√5/5
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 3√5/5 (Correct):** Given cosec θ + cot θ = √5 + 2. Its reciprocal is cosec θ - cot θ = 1 / (√5 + 2) = √5 - 2. Adding the two equations gives 2 cosec θ = 2√5, so cosec θ = √5 and sin θ = 1/√5. Subtracting gives 2 cot θ = 4, so cot θ = 2. Since cos θ = cot θ · sin θ, we get cos θ = 2/√5. Hence sin θ + cos θ = 1/√5 + 2/√5 = 3/√5 = (3√5)/5.
- **A — √5/5:** Value of cos θ - sin θ = 1/√5 = √5/5 instead of sin θ + cos θ.
- **B — 2√5/5:** Result of reporting 2/√5 (cos θ alone) without adding sin θ.
- **C — 3√5:** Mistake of multiplying by √5 incorrectly to get 3√5 instead of 3√5/5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Rationalize 1/(√5 + 2) = √5 - 2 mentally; half the sum is cosec θ = √5, half the difference is cot θ = 2, forming a 1-2-√5 triangle.

### Q660 · sec + tan = k Identities

If sec θ + tan θ = p (where p > 1), then (p² - 1) / (p² + 1) is equal to:

A. cos θ
B. sin θ
C. tan θ
D. sec θ

**Answer:** B. sin θ
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — sin θ (Correct):** Given sec θ + tan θ = p, we have sec θ - tan θ = 1/p. Adding gives 2 sec θ = p + 1/p = (p² + 1)/p, so sec θ = (p² + 1)/(2p) and cos θ = 2p/(p² + 1). Subtracting gives 2 tan θ = p - 1/p = (p² - 1)/p, so tan θ = (p² - 1)/(2p). Thus sin θ = tan θ · cos θ = [(p² - 1)/(2p)] · [2p/(p² + 1)] = (p² - 1)/(p² + 1).
- **A — cos θ:** Result of taking cos θ = 2p/(p² + 1) instead of sin θ.
- **C — tan θ:** Value obtained by inverting tan θ to get cot θ.
- **D — sec θ:** Mistake of taking sec θ = (p² + 1)/(2p) as the final answer.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Put p = 2 (a quick valid number > 1): (4 - 1)/(4 + 1) = 3/5. Here sec + tan = 2 gives sec = 5/4, tan = 3/4, so sin = 3/5!

### Q661 · cosec + cot = k Identities

If cosec θ - cot θ = 1/4 (0° < θ < 90°), what is the value of cos θ?

A. 15/17
B. 8/17
C. 8/15
D. 15/8

**Answer:** A. 15/17
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 15/17 (Correct):** Given cosec θ - cot θ = 1/4, we get cosec θ + cot θ = 4. Adding both equations gives 2 cosec θ = 4 + 1/4 = 17/4, so cosec θ = 17/8 and sin θ = 8/17. Using the 8-15-17 Pythagorean triple (or cos θ = √(1 - 64/289) = 15/17), we obtain cos θ = 15/17.
- **B — 8/17:** Value of sin θ = 8/17 instead of cos θ.
- **C — 8/15:** Result of reporting tan θ = 8/15 instead of cos θ.
- **D — 15/8:** Mistake of taking cosec θ = 17/8 and inverting to 15/8 (which is cot θ).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For cosec θ ± cot θ = k, cos θ = ±(k² - 1)/(k² + 1). With k = 4: (16 - 1)/(16 + 1) = 15/17.

### Q662 · sec + tan = k Identities

If sec θ + tan θ = 7/2 (0° < θ < 90°), find the value of cosec θ.

A. 45/53
B. 53/28
C. 53/45
D. 28/45

**Answer:** C. 53/45
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 53/45 (Correct):** Given sec θ + tan θ = 7/2, its reciprocal is sec θ - tan θ = 2/7. Adding gives 2 sec θ = 7/2 + 2/7 = 53/14, so sec θ = 53/28. Subtracting gives 2 tan θ = 7/2 - 2/7 = 45/14, so tan θ = 45/28. Since sin θ = tan θ / sec θ = 45/53, its reciprocal cosec θ = 53/45.
- **A — 45/53:** Value of sin θ = 45/53 instead of cosec θ.
- **B — 53/28:** Result of reporting sec θ = 53/28 instead of cosec θ.
- **D — 28/45:** Mistake of taking cos θ = 28/53 and inverting or mixing up 28 and 45.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use sin θ = (k² - 1)/(k² + 1) with k = 7/2: cosec θ = (49/4 + 1)/(49/4 - 1) = 53/45 in 15 seconds.

### Q663 · cosec + cot = k Identities

If cosec θ + cot θ = 3 (0° < θ < 90°), find the value of (tan θ + sec θ).

A. 1/2
B. 2
C. 3
D. 31/12

**Answer:** B. 2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 2 (Correct):** Given cosec θ + cot θ = 3, we have cosec θ - cot θ = 1/3. Adding gives 2 cosec θ = 10/3, so cosec θ = 5/3 and sin θ = 3/5. This corresponds to a 3-4-5 right triangle with cos θ = 4/5, tan θ = 3/4, and sec θ = 5/4. Thus tan θ + sec θ = 3/4 + 5/4 = 8/4 = 2.
- **A — 1/2:** Result of subtracting tan θ from sec θ: 5/4 - 3/4 = 1/2.
- **C — 3:** Value obtained by reporting sec θ + cot θ = 5/4 + 4/3 = 31/12.
- **D — 31/12:** Mistake of confusing cosec θ + cot θ = 3 with sec θ + tan θ = 3, which gives 3 directly.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Whenever cosec θ + cot θ = 3, the triangle is 3-4-5 (since 5/3 + 4/3 = 3), so sec θ + tan θ = 5/4 + 3/4 = 2.

### Q664 · sin + cosec = 2 Type

If sin θ + cosec θ = 2, what is the value of sin¹⁰⁰θ + cosec¹⁰⁰θ?

A. 200
B. 1
C. 0
D. 2

**Answer:** D. 2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 2 (Correct):** Given sin θ + cosec θ = 2, i.e. sin θ + 1/sin θ = 2. Multiplying by sin θ gives sin²θ - 2 sin θ + 1 = 0, or (sin θ - 1)² = 0, so sin θ = 1 and cosec θ = 1. Therefore, sin¹⁰⁰θ + cosec¹⁰⁰θ = 1¹⁰⁰ + 1¹⁰⁰ = 1 + 1 = 2.
- **A — 200:** Result of multiplying 2 by 100 instead of raising 1 to the 100th power.
- **B — 1:** Value obtained by assuming sin¹⁰⁰θ + cosec¹⁰⁰θ = 1.
- **C — 0:** Mistake of thinking the sum alternates or becomes 0 for even powers.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If x + 1/x = 2, then x = 1 always, so xⁿ + 1/xⁿ = 2 for every positive integer n.

### Q665 · sin + cosec = 2 Type

If cos θ + sec θ = 2, find the value of cos²⁰²⁵θ + sec²⁰²⁵θ.

A. 1
B. 2
C. 0
D. -2

**Answer:** B. 2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 2 (Correct):** Given cos θ + sec θ = 2, i.e. cos θ + 1/cos θ = 2, which implies (cos θ - 1)² = 0, so cos θ = 1 and sec θ = 1. Hence cos²⁰²⁵θ + sec²⁰²⁵θ = 1²⁰²⁵ + 1²⁰²⁵ = 1 + 1 = 2.
- **A — 1:** Result of thinking odd powers give 0 or -2 when cos θ = 1.
- **C — 0:** Value obtained by reporting cos²⁰²⁵θ = 1 without adding sec²⁰²⁵θ = 1.
- **D — -2:** Mistake of multiplying 2 by 2025.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** cos θ + sec θ = 2 forces cos θ = 1 (θ = 0°), so any positive power sum cosⁿθ + secⁿθ equals 2.

### Q666 · sin + cosec = 2 Type

If tan θ + cot θ = 2 (0° < θ < 90°), find the value of (tan⁷θ + cot⁷θ + sin²θ).

A. 2
B. 9/4
C. 3/2
D. 5/2

**Answer:** D. 5/2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 5/2 (Correct):** From tan θ + cot θ = 2, we get tan θ + 1/tan θ = 2, which forces tan θ = 1 and cot θ = 1. Since 0° < θ < 90°, θ = 45°, so sin θ = 1/√2 and sin²θ = 1/2. Thus tan⁷θ + cot⁷θ + sin²θ = 1⁷ + 1⁷ + 1/2 = 2 + 1/2 = 5/2.
- **A — 2:** Result of forgetting to add sin²45° = 1/2, leaving just 2.
- **B — 9/4:** Value obtained by using sin 45° = 1/√2 instead of sin²45° = 1/2, giving 2 + 1/√2.
- **C — 3/2:** Mistake of taking sin²45° = 1/4 instead of 1/2, giving 9/4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** tan θ + cot θ = 2 immediately means θ = 45°; plug θ = 45° to get 1 + 1 + 1/2 = 5/2.

### Q667 · sin + cosec = 2 Type

If sin θ + cosec θ = 2 (0° < θ ≤ 90°), then what is the value of (sin⁴θ + cosec⁴θ) / (cos²θ + 1)?

A. 2
B. 1
C. 4
D. 1/2

**Answer:** A. 2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 2 (Correct):** From sin θ + cosec θ = 2, we get sin θ = 1, so θ = 90° and cos θ = 0. Thus sin⁴θ + cosec⁴θ = 1⁴ + 1⁴ = 2, and cos²θ + 1 = 0 + 1 = 1. The ratio is 2 / 1 = 2.
- **B — 1:** Result of assuming cos 90° = 1 instead of 0, giving 2 / 2 = 1.
- **C — 4:** Value obtained by forgetting cosec⁴θ = 1 in the numerator, giving 1 / 1 = 1.
- **D — 1/2:** Mistake of substituting θ = 0° instead of 90°, making cosec 0° undefined or miscomputed as 0.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Substitute θ = 90° immediately whenever sin θ + cosec θ = 2: (1 + 1) / (0 + 1) = 2.

### Q668 · sin + cosec = 2 Type

If cos θ + sec θ = -2, find the value of cos⁵θ + sec⁵θ.

A. 2
B. 0
C. -2
D. -1

**Answer:** C. -2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — -2 (Correct):** Given cos θ + sec θ = -2, i.e. cos θ + 1/cos θ = -2, we get cos²θ + 2 cos θ + 1 = 0, so (cos θ + 1)² = 0 and cos θ = -1. Therefore sec θ = -1. For odd power 5, cos⁵θ + sec⁵θ = (-1)⁵ + (-1)⁵ = -1 + (-1) = -2.
- **A — 2:** Result of treating (-1)⁵ as +1 (which holds only for even powers).
- **B — 0:** Value obtained by cancelling (-1)⁵ and (-1)⁵ to 0 instead of adding them.
- **D — -1:** Mistake of reporting (-1)⁵ = -1 without adding the second term.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If x + 1/x = -2, then x = -1; thus xⁿ + 1/xⁿ = -2 for odd n and +2 for even n.

### Q669 · Complementary Angles

If sin(3x - 20°) = cos(3y + 20°), where both angles are acute, what is the value of (x + y)?

A. 45°
B. 30°
C. 60°
D. 90°

**Answer:** B. 30°
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 30° (Correct):** Since sin A = cos B for acute angles implies A + B = 90°, we have (3x - 20°) + (3y + 20°) = 90°. The -20° and +20° cancel, leaving 3(x + y) = 90°, so x + y = 30°.
- **A — 45°:** Result of equating the two angles directly (3x - 20 = 3y + 20) instead of summing them to 90°.
- **C — 60°:** Value obtained by forgetting to divide 90° by 3.
- **D — 90°:** Mistake of summing the angles to 180° instead of 90°, giving x + y = 60°.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Equate the sum of angles to 90°: 3(x + y) = 90° → x + y = 30° in 5 seconds.

### Q670 · Complementary Angles

Find the value of tan 1° · tan 2° · tan 3° · ... · tan 89°.

A. 1
B. 0
C. 45
D. -1

**Answer:** A. 1
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 1 (Correct):** Pair complementary angles using tan(90° - θ) = cot θ and tan θ · cot θ = 1: (tan 1° · tan 89°)(tan 2° · tan 88°)...(tan 44° · tan 46°) · tan 45°. Each of the 44 pairs equals 1, and tan 45° = 1, so the entire product is 1.
- **B — 0:** Result of confusing the product with cos 1°·cos 2°·...·cos 179° which equals 0.
- **C — 45:** Value obtained by counting 45 pairs and adding 1s instead of multiplying them.
- **D — -1:** Mistake of thinking tan 45° = 0 or tan 89° cancels to -1.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** In any product tan 1°·tan 2°·...·tan 89°, complementary pairs multiply to 1 and the middle term tan 45° = 1, so the answer is always 1.

### Q671 · Complementary Angles

What is the value of cos 1° · cos 2° · cos 3° · ... · cos 179°?

A. 1
B. -1
C. 1/2
D. 0

**Answer:** D. 0
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 0 (Correct):** The product cos 1° · cos 2° · cos 3° · ... · cos 179° includes the factor cos 90°. Since cos 90° = 0, multiplying all factors together yields 0.
- **A — 1:** Result of pairing cos θ with cos(180° - θ) = -cos θ and forgetting the unpaired cos 90° = 0 term.
- **B — -1:** Value obtained by confusing this cosine product with tan 1°·tan 2°·...·tan 89° = 1.
- **C — 1/2:** Mistake of assuming cos 90° = 1.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Always scan long trigonometric products for a 90° term: cos 90° = 0 makes the entire product 0 immediately.

### Q672 · Complementary Angles

Evaluate: (sin 35° / cos 55°)² + (cos 55° / sin 35°)² - 2 cos 60°.

A. 2 - √3
B. 3/2
C. 1
D. 3

**Answer:** C. 1
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 1 (Correct):** Since 35° + 55° = 90°, we have sin 35° = cos 55°. Therefore sin 35° / cos 55° = 1 and cos 55° / sin 35° = 1. Substituting these and cos 60° = 1/2 gives 1² + 1² - 2(1/2) = 1 + 1 - 1 = 1.
- **A — 2 - √3:** Result of using cos 60° = √3/2 instead of 1/2.
- **B — 3/2:** Value obtained by dropping the factor of 2 in 2 cos 60°, giving 2 - 1/2 = 3/2.
- **D — 3:** Mistake of adding 2 cos 60° = 1 instead of subtracting it, giving 3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Complementary ratios equal 1, so the expression is simply 1 + 1 - 2(1/2) = 1.

### Q673 · Complementary Angles

If sec 4θ = cosec(θ - 20°), where 4θ is an acute angle, find the value of θ.

A. 22°
B. 14°
C. 40°
D. 27.5°

**Answer:** A. 22°
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 22° (Correct):** Since sec A = cosec B for acute angles implies A + B = 90°, we have 4θ + (θ - 20°) = 90°. Thus 5θ - 20° = 90°, so 5θ = 110° and θ = 22°.
- **B — 14°:** Result of subtracting 20° from 90° instead of adding: 5θ = 70° → θ = 14°.
- **C — 40°:** Value obtained by setting 4θ = θ - 20° or 4θ + θ - 20° = 180°, giving θ = 40°.
- **D — 27.5°:** Mistake of dividing 110° by 4 instead of 5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Add the angles to 90°: 5θ = 90° + 20° = 110° → θ = 22°.

### Q674 · Complementary Angles

Find the value of sin²5° + sin²10° + sin²15° + ... + sin²85° + sin²90°.

A. 17/2
B. 19/2
C. 9
D. 10

**Answer:** B. 19/2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 19/2 (Correct):** Using sin²(90° - θ) = cos²θ, each complementary pair sums to sin²θ + cos²θ = 1. From 5° to 85° in steps of 5°, the pairs are (5°,85°), (10°,80°), ..., (40°,50°), which is 8 pairs summing to 8 × 1 = 8. The remaining terms are sin²45° = (1/√2)² = 1/2 and sin²90° = 1² = 1. Total = 8 + 1/2 + 1 = 19/2.
- **A — 17/2:** Result of forgetting the final sin²90° = 1 term, giving 8 + 1/2 = 17/2.
- **C — 9:** Value obtained by forgetting sin²45° = 1/2, giving 8 + 1 = 9.
- **D — 10:** Mistake of counting 9 pairs instead of 8 pairs, giving 10.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Count total terms from 5° to 85° (step 5°): 17 terms → 8 pairs (= 8) + middle term sin²45° (= 1/2), plus sin²90° (= 1) = 9.5 = 19/2.

### Q675 · Complementary Angles

Evaluate: cot 12° · cot 38° · cot 52° · cot 60° · cot 78°.

A. √3
B. 1/2
C. 1
D. 1/√3

**Answer:** D. 1/√3
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 1/√3 (Correct):** Group complementary angles using cot(90° - θ) = tan θ and tan θ · cot θ = 1: (cot 12° · cot 78°)(cot 38° · cot 52°) · cot 60°. Since 12° + 78° = 90° and 38° + 52° = 90°, both pairs equal 1. Multiplying by cot 60° = 1/√3 gives 1 · 1 · (1/√3) = 1/√3.
- **A — √3:** Result of substituting cot 60° = √3 instead of 1/√3.
- **B — 1/2:** Value obtained by assuming cot 60° = 1/2.
- **C — 1:** Mistake of ignoring cot 60° and reporting the product as 1.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Pair 12°+78°=90° and 38°+52°=90° to cancel to 1; only cot 60° = 1/√3 remains.

### Q676 · Quadrant Signs & Allied Angles

If sin θ = -3/5 and θ lies in the third quadrant, find the value of (tan θ + sec θ).

A. 2
B. -2
C. -1/2
D. 1/2

**Answer:** C. -1/2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — -1/2 (Correct):** In Quadrant III, only tan θ and cot θ are positive; sin θ, cos θ, sec θ, and cosec θ are negative. Given sin θ = -3/5, we have cos θ = -√(1 - 9/25) = -4/5. Therefore tan θ = (-3/5) / (-4/5) = 3/4 and sec θ = 1 / (-4/5) = -5/4. Thus tan θ + sec θ = 3/4 - 5/4 = -2/4 = -1/2.
- **A — 2:** Result of taking both tan θ and sec θ as positive (ignoring Quadrant III sign), giving 3/4 + 5/4 = 2.
- **B — -2:** Value obtained by making tan θ negative as well (-3/4 - 5/4 = -2).
- **D — 1/2:** Mistake of swapping numerator and denominator in cos θ to get 1/2.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use the 3-4-5 triple and ASTC rule (Quadrant III: tan positive, sec negative): 3/4 - 5/4 = -1/2.

### Q677 · Quadrant Signs & Allied Angles

If tan θ = -12/5 and θ lies in the second quadrant, what is the value of (sin θ - cos θ)?

A. 17/13
B. 7/13
C. -17/13
D. -7/13

**Answer:** A. 17/13
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 17/13 (Correct):** In Quadrant II, sin θ is positive and cos θ is negative. Using the 5-12-13 Pythagorean triple for |tan θ| = 12/5, we get sin θ = 12/13 and cos θ = -5/13. Therefore sin θ - cos θ = 12/13 - (-5/13) = 17/13.
- **B — 7/13:** Result of subtracting 5/13 instead of -5/13 (forgetting cos θ is negative in Q2).
- **C — -17/13:** Value obtained by making sin θ negative and cos θ positive, giving -17/13.
- **D — -7/13:** Mistake of computing cos θ - sin θ = -17/13 or forgetting the sign of tan θ.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** In Q2, sin > 0 and cos < 0, so sin θ - cos θ = |sin θ| + |cos θ| = (12 + 5)/13 = 17/13.

### Q678 · Quadrant Signs & Allied Angles

Evaluate: sin 150° + cos 120° - tan 135°.

A. -1
B. 1
C. 2
D. 0

**Answer:** B. 1
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 1 (Correct):** Convert each angle using allied-angle identities: sin 150° = sin(180° - 30°) = sin 30° = 1/2; cos 120° = cos(180° - 60°) = -cos 60° = -1/2; tan 135° = tan(180° - 45°) = -tan 45° = -1. Thus sin 150° + cos 120° - tan 135° = 1/2 + (-1/2) - (-1) = 0 + 1 = 1.
- **A — -1:** Result of forgetting the negative sign of tan 135° = -1, giving 0 - 1 = -1.
- **C — 2:** Value obtained by taking cos 120° = +1/2 instead of -1/2, giving 1/2 + 1/2 + 1 = 2.
- **D — 0:** Mistake of taking both cos 120° and tan 135° as positive, giving 1/2 + 1/2 - 1 = 0.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Remember in Q2 only sin is positive: sin 150° (+1/2) and cos 120° (-1/2) cancel to 0, leaving -(-1) = 1.

### Q679 · Quadrant Signs & Allied Angles

If cos θ = 5/13 and θ lies in the fourth quadrant, find the value of (cosec θ + cot θ).

A. 3/2
B. -2/3
C. -3/2
D. 2/3

**Answer:** C. -3/2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — -3/2 (Correct):** In Quadrant IV, cos θ is positive while sin θ, cosec θ, and cot θ are negative. From cos θ = 5/13 and the 5-12-13 triple, sin θ = -12/13. Thus cosec θ = -13/12 and cot θ = cos θ / sin θ = -5/12. Adding gives cosec θ + cot θ = -13/12 - 5/12 = -18/12 = -3/2.
- **A — 3/2:** Result of ignoring Quadrant IV signs and computing +(13 + 5)/12 = 3/2.
- **B — -2/3:** Value obtained by making only cosec θ negative and cot θ positive, giving -13/12 + 5/12 = -2/3.
- **D — 2/3:** Mistake of subtracting 5/12 from 13/12 to get 2/3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** In Q4, both cosec θ and cot θ are negative; sum is -(13 + 5)/12 = -18/12 = -3/2.

### Q680 · Quadrant Signs & Allied Angles

Evaluate: cos 210° · sin 300° + tan 225° · sec 240°.

A. 11/4
B. -11/4
C. 5/4
D. -5/4

**Answer:** D. -5/4
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — -5/4 (Correct):** Evaluate each term by quadrant: cos 210° = cos(180° + 30°) = -cos 30° = -√3/2 (Q3); sin 300° = sin(360° - 60°) = -sin 60° = -√3/2 (Q4); tan 225° = tan(180° + 45°) = tan 45° = 1 (Q3); sec 240° = sec(180° + 60°) = -sec 60° = -2 (Q3). Thus the expression equals (-√3/2)(-√3/2) + (1)(-2) = 3/4 - 2 = -5/4.
- **A — 11/4:** Result of taking sec 240° = +2 instead of -2, giving 3/4 + 2 = 11/4.
- **B — -11/4:** Value obtained by making (-√3/2)(-√3/2) = -3/4, giving -3/4 - 2 = -11/4.
- **C — 5/4:** Mistake of taking sin 300° = +√3/2, giving -3/4 + 2 = 5/4 (if sec 240° is also +2).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Compute the two products separately: (-√3/2)(-√3/2) = +3/4 and 1 × (-2) = -2, so 3/4 - 2 = -5/4.

### Q681 · Algebraic Trig Simplification

If 5 sin θ = 4 (0° < θ < 90°), find the value of (sec θ + 4 cot θ) / (4 tan θ - 5 cos θ).

A. 14/25
B. 2
C. 1/2
D. 7/3

**Answer:** B. 2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 2 (Correct):** From 5 sin θ = 4, we have sin θ = 4/5. Using the 3-4-5 right triangle (with 0° < θ < 90°), cos θ = 3/5, sec θ = 5/3, tan θ = 4/3, and cot θ = 3/4. Numerator = 5/3 + 4(3/4) = 5/3 + 3 = 14/3. Denominator = 4(4/3) - 5(3/5) = 16/3 - 3 = 7/3. Ratio = (14/3) / (7/3) = 2.
- **A — 14/25:** Result of adding 3 instead of subtracting 3 in the denominator, giving (14/3) / (25/3) = 14/25.
- **C — 1/2:** Value obtained by swapping sin θ and cos θ (taking cos θ = 4/5), giving (5/4 + 16/3) / (3 - 4) = -79/12.
- **D — 7/3:** Mistake of inverting the final ratio to 1/2.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use the 3-4-5 triangle (P=4, B=3, H=5): (5/3 + 3) / (16/3 - 3) = (14/3) / (7/3) = 2.

### Q682 · Algebraic Trig Simplification

If 3 sin θ + 4 cos θ = 5, find the value of (4 sin θ - 3 cos θ).

A. 0
B. 5
C. 1
D. 3

**Answer:** A. 0
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 0 (Correct):** Square both given and target expressions using the identity (a sin θ + b cos θ)² + (b sin θ - a cos θ)² = a² + b². Here (3 sin θ + 4 cos θ)² + (4 sin θ - 3 cos θ)² = 3² + 4² = 25. Substituting 3 sin θ + 4 cos θ = 5 gives 5² + (4 sin θ - 3 cos θ)² = 25, so (4 sin θ - 3 cos θ)² = 0 and 4 sin θ - 3 cos θ = 0.
- **B — 5:** Result of computing √(3² + 4²) = 5 instead of √(3² + 4² - 5²) = 0.
- **C — 1:** Value obtained by subtracting 3 from 4 directly to get 1.
- **D — 3:** Mistake of taking 5² - 3² - 4² incorrectly or reporting 3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If a sin θ + b cos θ = c, then b sin θ - a cos θ = ±√(a² + b² - c²). Since 3² + 4² = 5², the answer is immediately 0.

### Q683 · Algebraic Trig Simplification

If 5 tan θ = 4, find the value of (5 sin θ - 3 cos θ) / (5 sin θ + 2 cos θ).

A. 13/33
B. 17/22
C. 1/6
D. 1/5

**Answer:** C. 1/6
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 1/6 (Correct):** Divide both numerator and denominator of (5 sin θ - 3 cos θ) / (5 sin θ + 2 cos θ) by cos θ: (5 tan θ - 3) / (5 tan θ + 2). Since 5 tan θ = 4, substitute 4 directly: (4 - 3) / (4 + 2) = 1/6.
- **A — 13/33:** Result of plugging sin θ = 5 and cos θ = 4 (swapped), giving (25 - 12)/(25 + 8) = 13/33.
- **B — 17/22:** Value obtained by adding in the numerator and subtracting in the denominator, giving 7/2.
- **D — 1/5:** Mistake of forgetting to multiply 3 and 2 by cos θ = 5 when substituting ratios, giving (4 - 3)/(4 + 2) only if 5 tan θ = 4 was used, or (20 - 3)/(20 + 2) = 17/22.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Since tan θ = 4/5 = sin θ / cos θ, plug sin θ = 4 and cos θ = 5 directly into homogeneous fractions: (20 - 15)/(20 + 10) = 5/30 = 1/6.

### Q684 · Algebraic Trig Simplification

If 7 sin θ + 24 cos θ = 25 (0° < θ < 90°), what is the value of tan θ?

A. 24/7
B. 7/25
C. 24/25
D. 7/24

**Answer:** D. 7/24
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 7/24 (Correct):** Notice that 7² + 24² = 49 + 576 = 625 = 25². Dividing 7 sin θ + 24 cos θ = 25 by 25 gives (7/25) sin θ + (24/25) cos θ = 1. Comparing with sin²θ + cos²θ = 1, we get sin θ = 7/25 and cos θ = 24/25. Therefore tan θ = sin θ / cos θ = 7/24.
- **A — 24/7:** Value of cot θ = 24/7 instead of tan θ.
- **B — 7/25:** Result of reporting sin θ = 7/25 instead of tan θ.
- **C — 24/25:** Mistake of reporting cos θ = 24/25 instead of tan θ.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Whenever a sin θ + b cos θ = c forms a Pythagorean triple (a² + b² = c²), sin θ = a/c, cos θ = b/c, so tan θ = a/b directly!

### Q685 · Algebraic Trig Simplification

If (sin θ + cos θ) / (sin θ - cos θ) = 5/3, find the value of (tan²θ + 1) / (tan²θ - 1).

A. 17/15
B. 5/3
C. 15/17
D. 17/8

**Answer:** A. 17/15
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 17/15 (Correct):** Apply componendo and dividendo to (sin θ + cos θ) / (sin θ - cos θ) = 5/3: (sin θ / cos θ) = (5 + 3) / (5 - 3) = 8/2 = 4, so tan θ = 4. Substituting tan θ = 4 into (tan²θ + 1) / (tan²θ - 1) gives (16 + 1) / (16 - 1) = 17/15.
- **B — 5/3:** Result of forgetting to square tan θ = 4, giving (4 + 1)/(4 - 1) = 5/3.
- **C — 15/17:** Value obtained by inverting the final fraction to 15/17.
- **D — 17/8:** Mistake of taking tan θ = 5/3 without applying componendo-dividendo, giving 34/16 = 17/8.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use componendo-dividendo in one line: tan θ = (5 + 3)/(5 - 3) = 4, so (4² + 1)/(4² - 1) = 17/15.

### Q686 · Algebraic Trig Simplification

If x = a(sin θ + cos θ) and y = b(sin θ - cos θ), then what is the value of (x²/a² + y²/b²)?

A. 1
B. 2
C. 0
D. 4

**Answer:** B. 2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 2 (Correct):** From x = a(sin θ + cos θ) and y = b(sin θ - cos θ), we have x/a = sin θ + cos θ and y/b = sin θ - cos θ. Squaring and adding gives x²/a² + y²/b² = (sin θ + cos θ)² + (sin θ - cos θ)² = 2(sin²θ + cos²θ) = 2(1) = 2.
- **A — 1:** Result of forgetting the factor of 2 when adding (s + c)² + (s - c)².
- **C — 0:** Value obtained by subtracting instead of adding, which gives 4 sin θ cos θ or 0 at θ = 0°.
- **D — 4:** Mistake of assuming (sin θ + cos θ)² + (sin θ - cos θ)² = 4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Set θ = 0° (or 90°): x = a, y = -b, so x²/a² + y²/b² = 1 + 1 = 2 in 5 seconds.

### Q687 · Algebraic Trig Simplification

If tan θ = 3/4 (0° < θ < 90°), find the value of [(1 - cos θ)(1 + cos θ)] / [(1 - sin θ)(1 + sin θ)].

A. 3/4
B. 16/9
C. 9/25
D. 9/16

**Answer:** D. 9/16
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 9/16 (Correct):** Simplify the expression using (1 - cos θ)(1 + cos θ) = 1 - cos²θ = sin²θ and (1 - sin θ)(1 + sin θ) = 1 - sin²θ = cos²θ. Thus the fraction equals sin²θ / cos²θ = tan²θ. Since tan θ = 3/4, tan²θ = (3/4)² = 9/16.
- **A — 3/4:** Result of forgetting to square 3/4, reporting 3/4 instead of 9/16.
- **B — 16/9:** Value obtained by inverting to cot²θ = 16/9.
- **C — 9/25:** Mistake of reporting sin²θ = 9/25 instead of tan²θ = 9/16.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Notice (1 - cos²θ)/(1 - sin²θ) = sin²θ/cos²θ = tan²θ directly, so just square 3/4 to get 9/16.

### Q688 · Maxima & Minima

Find the maximum value of (8 sin θ + 15 cos θ + 10).

A. 17
B. 33
C. 27
D. -7

**Answer:** C. 27
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 27 (Correct):** The maximum value of (a sin θ + b cos θ) is √(a² + b²). Here a = 8 and b = 15, so the maximum of (8 sin θ + 15 cos θ) is √(8² + 15²) = √(64 + 225) = √289 = 17. Adding 10 gives the overall maximum value 17 + 10 = 27.
- **A — 17:** Result of forgetting to add the constant 10, reporting 17.
- **B — 33:** Value obtained by adding 8 + 15 + 10 = 33 instead of using √(a² + b²).
- **D — -7:** Mistake of finding the minimum value (-17 + 10 = -7) instead of the maximum.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Recognize the 8-15-17 Pythagorean triple: max = 17 + 10 = 27 in 3 seconds.

### Q689 · Maxima & Minima

What is the minimum value of (9 sin²θ + 4 cosec²θ)?

A. 6
B. 12
C. 13
D. 26

**Answer:** B. 12
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 12 (Correct):** For a sin²θ + b cosec²θ, AM ≥ GM gives 9 sin²θ + 4 cosec²θ ≥ 2√(9 × 4) = 2 × 6 = 12. Equality holds when 9 sin²θ = 4 cosec²θ, i.e., sin⁴θ = 4/9, so sin²θ = 2/3 ≤ 1, which is valid for real θ. Thus the minimum value is 12.
- **A — 6:** Result of forgetting the factor of 2 in 2√(ab), reporting √36 = 6.
- **C — 13:** Value obtained by using a + b = 9 + 4 = 13 (which applies only when a < b).
- **D — 26:** Mistake of computing 2(9 + 4) = 26 instead of 2√(9 × 4).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For a sin²θ + b cosec²θ: if a ≥ b, minimum = 2√(ab) = 2√36 = 12.

### Q690 · Maxima & Minima

Find the minimum value of (4 sin²θ + 9 cosec²θ).

A. 13
B. 12
C. 6
D. 5

**Answer:** A. 13
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 13 (Correct):** By AM ≥ GM, 4 sin²θ + 9 cosec²θ ≥ 2√(4 × 9) = 12, with equality only when 4 sin²θ = 9 cosec²θ, i.e., sin⁴θ = 9/4 or sin²θ = 3/2 > 1, which is impossible since 0 ≤ sin²θ ≤ 1. Writing f(t) = 4t + 9/t for t = sin²θ ∈ (0, 1], f(t) is strictly decreasing on (0, 1] because its minimum on (0, ∞) is at t = 3/2 > 1. Hence the minimum on (0, 1] occurs at t = 1 (θ = 90°), giving 4(1) + 9(1) = 13.
- **B — 12:** Classic trap of blindly applying 2√(ab) = 12 without checking whether sin²θ = √(b/a) ≤ 1.
- **C — 6:** Result of forgetting the factor of 2 in √(ab) = 6.
- **D — 5:** Value obtained by taking 9 - 4 = 5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** SSC Trap Alert! For a sin²θ + b cosec²θ: if a ≥ b, min = 2√(ab); if a < b, min = a + b! Here 4 < 9, so min = 4 + 9 = 13.

### Q691 · Maxima & Minima

What are the minimum and maximum values of (7 cos²θ + 5 sin²θ) respectively?

A. 0 and 12
B. -7 and 7
C. 7 and 5
D. 5 and 7

**Answer:** D. 5 and 7
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 5 and 7 (Correct):** Rewrite 7 cos²θ + 5 sin²θ = 5(cos²θ + sin²θ) + 2 cos²θ = 5 + 2 cos²θ. Since 0 ≤ cos²θ ≤ 1, the minimum value is 5 + 2(0) = 5 and the maximum value is 5 + 2(1) = 7.
- **A — 0 and 12:** Result of confusing a sin²θ + b cos²θ with a sin θ + b cos θ and computing ±√(7² + 5²).
- **B — -7 and 7:** Value obtained by assuming the minimum can be negative (-7) because cos θ can be negative.
- **C — 7 and 5:** Mistake of swapping minimum and maximum values (reporting 7 and 5).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For a sin²θ + b cos²θ, the minimum is always min(a, b) and the maximum is always max(a, b). Here min = 5, max = 7.

### Q692 · Maxima & Minima

Find the minimum value of (16 tan²θ + 25 cot²θ).

A. 20
B. 41
C. 40
D. 82

**Answer:** C. 40
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 40 (Correct):** Since tan²θ can take any positive value from 0 to ∞, AM ≥ GM applies without restriction: 16 tan²θ + 25 cot²θ ≥ 2√(16 tan²θ · 25 cot²θ) = 2√(16 × 25) = 2 × 4 × 5 = 40. Equality holds when tan²θ = 5/4, which is valid.
- **A — 20:** Result of using a + b = 16 + 25 = 41 (which is only for sin²/cosec² when a < b).
- **B — 41:** Value obtained by forgetting the factor of 2 in 2√(ab), giving √400 = 20.
- **D — 82:** Mistake of computing 2(16 + 25) = 82.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For a tan²θ + b cot²θ, there is no range restriction on tan θ, so the minimum is ALWAYS 2√(ab) = 2 × 4 × 5 = 40.

### Q693 · Heights & Distances

A vertical tower casts a shadow of length 20√3 m on the ground when the Sun's altitude is 30°. Find the height of the tower.

A. 20 m
B. 60 m
C. 10√3 m
D. 20√3 m

**Answer:** A. 20 m
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 20 m (Correct):** Let the height of the tower be h. In the right triangle formed by the tower and its shadow, tan 30° = h / (20√3). Since tan 30° = 1/√3, we get h = 20√3 × (1/√3) = 20 m.
- **B — 60 m:** Result of multiplying by √3 instead of dividing by √3 (using tan 60° instead of tan 30°).
- **C — 10√3 m:** Value obtained by using sin 30° = 1/2 instead of tan 30° = 1/√3, giving 10√3 m.
- **D — 20√3 m:** Mistake of assuming the angle is 45° so height equals shadow length (20√3 m).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** In a 30°-60°-90° triangle, base (opposite 60°) = √3 × height (opposite 30°). Divide 20√3 by √3 → 20 m.

### Q694 · Heights & Distances

From the top of a 60 m high cliff, the angle of depression of a boat on the sea is 45°. Find the horizontal distance of the boat from the base of the cliff.

A. 20√3 m
B. 60 m
C. 60√3 m
D. 30 m

**Answer:** B. 60 m
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 60 m (Correct):** The angle of elevation from the boat to the top of the cliff equals the angle of depression (45°). In the right triangle, tan 45° = height / distance = 60 / d. Since tan 45° = 1, d = 60 m.
- **A — 20√3 m:** Result of dividing 60 by √3 (as if the angle were 60°).
- **C — 60√3 m:** Value obtained by multiplying 60 by √3 (as if the angle were 30°).
- **D — 30 m:** Mistake of dividing 60 by 2 (using sin 30° = 1/2).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** At 45° elevation/depression, height and horizontal distance are always equal (1 : 1 ratio).

### Q695 · Heights & Distances

Two ships are sailing on opposite sides of a lighthouse of height 100 m. The angles of depression of the two ships from the top of the lighthouse are 30° and 45°. What is the distance between the two ships?

A. 100(√3 - 1) m
B. 100(1/√3 + 1) m
C. 100(√3 + 1) m
D. 200 m

**Answer:** C. 100(√3 + 1) m
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 100(√3 + 1) m (Correct):** Let the distances of the two ships from the base of the 100 m lighthouse be d₁ and d₂. For the 30° ship, tan 30° = 100 / d₁, so d₁ = 100√3 m. For the 45° ship, tan 45° = 100 / d₂, so d₂ = 100 m. Since the ships are on opposite sides, total distance = d₁ + d₂ = 100√3 + 100 = 100(√3 + 1) m.
- **A — 100(√3 - 1) m:** Result of subtracting distances (as if the ships were on the same side of the lighthouse).
- **B — 100(1/√3 + 1) m:** Value obtained by using 100/√3 instead of 100√3 for the 30° ship.
- **D — 200 m:** Mistake of dividing 100 by (√3 + 1) instead of multiplying.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Opposite sides → ADD distances: d = h(cot α + cot β) = 100(√3 + 1) m. Same side → SUBTRACT.
