## Chapter 12: Algebra & Data Interpretation

### Q696 · Reciprocal Identities

If x + 1/x = 5, find the value of x^2 + 1/x^2.

A. 21
B. 25
C. 23
D. 27

**Answer:** C. 23
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 23 (Correct):** Using the standard identity (x + 1/x)^2 = x^2 + 1/x^2 + 2:
x^2 + 1/x^2 = 5^2 - 2 = 25 - 2 = 23.
- **A — 21:** Result of subtracting 4 instead of 2 from 5^2.
- **B — 25:** Result of squaring 5 and forgetting to subtract 2.
- **D — 27:** Result of adding 2 instead of subtracting 2 after squaring.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If x + 1/x = k, then x^2 + 1/x^2 = k^2 - 2. Compute 25 - 2 = 23 in under 5 seconds.

### Q697 · Reciprocal Identities

If x - 1/x = 4, find the value of x^2 + 1/x^2.

A. 18
B. 14
C. 16
D. 20

**Answer:** A. 18
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 18 (Correct):** Using the identity (x - 1/x)^2 = x^2 + 1/x^2 - 2:
x^2 + 1/x^2 = 4^2 + 2 = 16 + 2 = 18.
- **B — 14:** Result of subtracting 2 instead of adding 2 after squaring.
- **C — 16:** Result of squaring 4 and forgetting to add 2.
- **D — 20:** Result of adding 4 instead of 2 to 4^2.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If x - 1/x = k, then x^2 + 1/x^2 = k^2 + 2. Always add 2 when moving from minus to plus.

### Q698 · Reciprocal Identities

If x + 1/x = 4, find the value of x^3 + 1/x^3.

A. 64
B. 76
C. 48
D. 52

**Answer:** D. 52
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 52 (Correct):** Using the cubic identity (x + 1/x)^3 = x^3 + 1/x^3 + 3(x + 1/x):
x^3 + 1/x^3 = 4^3 - 3(4) = 64 - 12 = 52.
- **A — 64:** Result of cubing 4 and forgetting to subtract 3k.
- **B — 76:** Result of adding 3k instead of subtracting 3k from k^3.
- **C — 48:** Result of subtracting 4k (16) instead of 3k (12) from 64.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If x + 1/x = k, then x^3 + 1/x^3 = k^3 - 3k = k(k^2 - 3). Here 4 * (16 - 3) = 52.

### Q699 · Reciprocal Identities

If x - 1/x = 5, find the value of x^3 - 1/x^3.

A. 110
B. 140
C. 125
D. 135

**Answer:** B. 140
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 140 (Correct):** Using the cubic identity (x - 1/x)^3 = x^3 - 1/x^3 - 3(x - 1/x):
x^3 - 1/x^3 = 5^3 + 3(5) = 125 + 15 = 140.
- **A — 110:** Result of subtracting 3k instead of adding 3k to k^3.
- **C — 125:** Result of cubing 5 and forgetting to add 3k.
- **D — 135:** Result of adding 2k (10) instead of 3k (15) to 125.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** If x - 1/x = k, then x^3 - 1/x^3 = k^3 + 3k = k(k^2 + 3). Here 5 * 28 = 140.

### Q700 · Reciprocal Identities

If x + 1/x = 3, find the value of x^4 + 1/x^4.

A. 47
B. 49
C. 51
D. 79

**Answer:** A. 47
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 47 (Correct):** First find x^2 + 1/x^2 = 3^2 - 2 = 7.
Then square again: x^4 + 1/x^4 = 7^2 - 2 = 49 - 2 = 47.
- **B — 49:** Result of forgetting to subtract 2 during the second squaring step.
- **C — 51:** Result of adding 2 instead of subtracting 2 during the second squaring step.
- **D — 79:** Result of forgetting to subtract 2 in the first step (9^2 - 2 = 79).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Apply (k^2 - 2) twice: 3 -> 3^2 - 2 = 7 -> 7^2 - 2 = 47.

### Q701 · Reciprocal Identities

If x + 1/x = 3, find the value of x^5 + 1/x^5.

A. 126
B. 129
C. 123
D. 117

**Answer:** C. 123
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 123 (Correct):** Compute x^2 + 1/x^2 = 3^2 - 2 = 7 and x^3 + 1/x^3 = 3^3 - 3(3) = 18.
Now, x^5 + 1/x^5 = (x^2 + 1/x^2)(x^3 + 1/x^3) - (x + 1/x) = (7 * 18) - 3 = 126 - 3 = 123.
- **A — 126:** Result of multiplying (x^2 + 1/x^2)(x^3 + 1/x^3) without subtracting (x + 1/x).
- **B — 129:** Result of adding (x + 1/x) instead of subtracting it from the product.
- **D — 117:** Result of subtracting k^2 (9) instead of k (3) from 126.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use x^5 + 1/x^5 = (x^2 + 1/x^2)(x^3 + 1/x^3) - (x + 1/x). For k = 3, 7 * 18 - 3 = 123.

### Q702 · Reciprocal Identities

If x + 1/x = 5 and x > 1, find the value of x^2 - 1/x^2.

A. 5√29
B. 5√21
C. 21
D. 5√19

**Answer:** B. 5√21
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 5√21 (Correct):** Since x > 1, x - 1/x = √((x + 1/x)^2 - 4) = √(25 - 4) = √21.
Therefore, x^2 - 1/x^2 = (x + 1/x)(x - 1/x) = 5√21.
- **A — 5√29:** Result of adding 4 instead of subtracting 4 inside the square root.
- **C — 21:** Result of computing (x - 1/x)^2 = 21 instead of multiplying (x + 1/x)(x - 1/x).
- **D — 5√19:** Result of subtracting 6 instead of 4 inside the square root.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When x + 1/x = k and x > 1, x^2 - 1/x^2 = k√(k^2 - 4). Here 5√(25 - 4) = 5√21.

### Q703 · Reciprocal Identities

If x^2 - 7x + 1 = 0, find the value of x^2 + 1/x^2.

A. 51
B. 49
C. 45
D. 47

**Answer:** D. 47
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 47 (Correct):** Divide x^2 - 7x + 1 = 0 by x (since x ≠ 0):
x - 7 + 1/x = 0 => x + 1/x = 7.
Thus, x^2 + 1/x^2 = 7^2 - 2 = 49 - 2 = 47.
- **A — 51:** Result of adding 2 instead of subtracting 2 after squaring 7.
- **B — 49:** Result of squaring 7 and forgetting to subtract 2.
- **C — 45:** Result of subtracting 4 instead of 2 from 49.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For x^2 - kx + 1 = 0, directly read x + 1/x = k, so x^2 + 1/x^2 = k^2 - 2 = 49 - 2 = 47.

### Q704 · Reciprocal Identities

If 2x + 1/(3x) = 5, find the value of 9x^2 + 1/(4x^2).

A. 56.25
B. 53.25
C. 54.25
D. 59.25

**Answer:** B. 53.25
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 53.25 (Correct):** Multiply 2x + 1/(3x) = 5 by 3/2 to match the target coefficients:
3x + 1/(2x) = 15/2 = 7.5.
Squaring both sides: 9x^2 + 1/(4x^2) + 2(3x)(1/(2x)) = 225/4
=> 9x^2 + 1/(4x^2) = 56.25 - 3 = 53.25.
- **A — 56.25:** Result of squaring 7.5 and forgetting to subtract the cross term 2ab = 3.
- **C — 54.25:** Result of subtracting 2 instead of 3 as the cross term 2(3x)(1/2x).
- **D — 59.25:** Result of adding 3 instead of subtracting 3 from 56.25.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Scale the equation by 3/2 so (3x) and (1/2x) appear, then compute (7.5)^2 - 2*(3/2) = 56.25 - 3 = 53.25.

### Q705 · Reciprocal Identities

If x + 1/(x - 3) = 8, find the value of (x - 3)^3 + 1/(x - 3)^3.

A. 110
B. 140
C. 488
D. 125

**Answer:** A. 110
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 110 (Correct):** Subtract 3 from both sides of x + 1/(x - 3) = 8:
(x - 3) + 1/(x - 3) = 5.
Let t = x - 3, so t + 1/t = 5.
Then t^3 + 1/t^3 = 5^3 - 3(5) = 125 - 15 = 110.
- **B — 140:** Result of adding 3k instead of subtracting 3k from 5^3.
- **C — 488:** Result of applying k^3 - 3k directly with k = 8 without subtracting 3 first.
- **D — 125:** Result of cubing 5 and forgetting to subtract 3k.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Shift the variable by subtracting 3 from both sides to get t + 1/t = 5, then apply k^3 - 3k = 110.

### Q706 · Reciprocal Identities

If x + 1/x = √5, find the value of x^3 + 1/x^3.

A. 8√5
B. 5√5
C. 2√5
D. 3√5

**Answer:** C. 2√5
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 2√5 (Correct):** Using x^3 + 1/x^3 = k^3 - 3k with k = √5:
x^3 + 1/x^3 = (√5)^3 - 3√5 = 5√5 - 3√5 = 2√5.
- **A — 8√5:** Result of adding 3√5 instead of subtracting 3√5 from 5√5.
- **B — 5√5:** Result of computing (√5)^3 and forgetting to subtract 3√5.
- **D — 3√5:** Result of subtracting 2√5 instead of 3√5 from 5√5.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For x + 1/x = √n, x^3 + 1/x^3 = (n - 3)√n. Here (5 - 3)√5 = 2√5.

### Q707 · Reciprocal Identities

If x^4 + 1/x^4 = 119 and x > 1, find the value of x - 1/x.

A. √13
B. √11
C. 4
D. 3

**Answer:** D. 3
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 3 (Correct):** First find x^2 + 1/x^2 = √(119 + 2) = √121 = 11.
Since x > 1, x - 1/x = √(11 - 2) = √9 = 3.
- **A — √13:** Result of adding 2 instead of subtracting 2 in the second step.
- **B — √11:** Result of stopping after finding x^2 + 1/x^2 = 11 and taking its square root.
- **C — 4:** Result of miscomputing √(11 + 5) or assuming 11 + 2 rounds up to 4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Step down twice: √(119 + 2) = 11 (always +2 to reach x^2 + 1/x^2), then √(11 - 2) = 3 (-2 to reach x - 1/x).

### Q708 · Cubic & Symmetric Identities

If a + b + c = 0 (where a, b, c ≠ 0), find the value of a^2/(bc) + b^2/(ca) + c^2/(ab).

A. 0
B. 1
C. 3
D. -3

**Answer:** C. 3
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 3 (Correct):** Taking the common denominator abc:
a^2/(bc) + b^2/(ca) + c^2/(ab) = (a^3 + b^3 + c^3)/(abc).
Since a + b + c = 0, a^3 + b^3 + c^3 = 3abc.
Therefore, (3abc)/(abc) = 3.
- **A — 0:** Result of assuming the fraction sum equals 0 because a + b + c = 0.
- **B — 1:** Result of assuming a^3 + b^3 + c^3 = abc instead of 3abc.
- **D — -3:** Result of a sign error assuming a^3 + b^3 + c^3 = -3abc.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Whenever a + b + c = 0, a^3 + b^3 + c^3 = 3abc, so a^2/(bc) + b^2/(ca) + c^2/(ab) is always 3.

### Q709 · Cubic & Symmetric Identities

If a = 15, b = 12, and c = -27, find the value of a^3 + b^3 + c^3.

A. 14,580
B. -14,580
C. -4,860
D. -15,580

**Answer:** B. -14,580
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — -14,580 (Correct):** Check the sum: a + b + c = 15 + 12 + (-27) = 0.
Since a + b + c = 0, a^3 + b^3 + c^3 = 3abc = 3 * 15 * 12 * (-27) = 540 * (-27) = -14,580.
- **A — 14,580:** Result of dropping the negative sign of c when computing 3abc.
- **C — -4,860:** Result of computing abc without multiplying by 3.
- **D — -15,580:** Result of an arithmetic carry error when multiplying 540 by 27.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Always check if a + b + c = 0 first; if so, skip cubing and compute 3abc directly.

### Q710 · Cubic & Symmetric Identities

If a = 99, b = 100, and c = 101, find the value of a^3 + b^3 + c^3 - 3abc.

A. 900
B. 1,800
C. 300
D. 600

**Answer:** A. 900
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 900 (Correct):** Using the identity a^3 + b^3 + c^3 - 3abc = (1/2)(a + b + c)[(a - b)^2 + (b - c)^2 + (c - a)^2]:
= (1/2)(99 + 100 + 101)[(-1)^2 + (-1)^2 + 2^2]
= (1/2)(300)(1 + 1 + 4) = 150 * 6 = 900.
- **B — 1,800:** Result of forgetting the 1/2 factor in the identity.
- **C — 300:** Result of taking [(a - b)^2 + (b - c)^2 + (c - a)^2] as 2 instead of 6.
- **D — 600:** Result of miscomputing (c - a)^2 as 2 instead of 4 inside the bracket.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** When a, b, c are consecutive integers (difference d = 1), a^3 + b^3 + c^3 - 3abc = 9b. Here 9 * 100 = 900.

### Q711 · Cubic & Symmetric Identities

If a + b + c = 12 and ab + bc + ca = 47, find the value of a^3 + b^3 + c^3 - 3abc.

A. 600
B. 1,164
C. 72
D. 36

**Answer:** D. 36
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 36 (Correct):** Using a^3 + b^3 + c^3 - 3abc = (a + b + c)[(a + b + c)^2 - 3(ab + bc + ca)]:
= 12 * [12^2 - 3(47)] = 12 * (144 - 141) = 12 * 3 = 36.
- **A — 600:** Result of using -2(ab + bc + ca) instead of -3(ab + bc + ca).
- **B — 1,164:** Result of forgetting to multiply (ab + bc + ca) by 3 inside the bracket.
- **C — 72:** Result of miscomputing 144 - 141 as 6 instead of 3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Memorize (a + b + c)[(a + b + c)^2 - 3(ab + bc + ca)]; it solves this in 10 seconds.

### Q712 · Cubic & Symmetric Identities

If a^2 + b^2 + c^2 = 2(a - b - c) - 3, find the value of 2a - 3b + 4c.

A. 1
B. -5
C. 9
D. -1

**Answer:** A. 1
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 1 (Correct):** Rearranging the equation:
(a^2 - 2a + 1) + (b^2 + 2b + 1) + (c^2 + 2c + 1) = 0
=> (a - 1)^2 + (b + 1)^2 + (c + 1)^2 = 0.
Since each square is non-negative, a = 1, b = -1, c = -1.
Thus, 2a - 3b + 4c = 2(1) - 3(-1) + 4(-1) = 2 + 3 - 4 = 1.
- **B — -5:** Result of reversing the signs of a, b, and c (taking a = -1, b = 1, c = 1).
- **C — 9:** Result of taking c = +1 instead of c = -1.
- **D — -1:** Result of taking b = +1 instead of b = -1.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Each variable equals half the coefficient on the RHS: a = 1, b = -1, c = -1. Plug directly into 2a - 3b + 4c.

### Q713 · Cubic & Symmetric Identities

If (a - 4)^2 + (b - 5)^2 + (c + 3)^2 = 0, find the value of a + b - c.

A. 6
B. -6
C. 2
D. 12

**Answer:** D. 12
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 12 (Correct):** A sum of squares equals zero only when each term is zero:
a - 4 = 0 => a = 4
b - 5 = 0 => b = 5
c + 3 = 0 => c = -3.
Therefore, a + b - c = 4 + 5 - (-3) = 9 + 3 = 12.
- **A — 6:** Result of taking c = +3 instead of c = -3 in a + b - c.
- **B — -6:** Result of reversing all signs to (-4, -5, +3).
- **C — 2:** Result of subtracting b instead of adding b (4 - 5 + 3).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Set each bracket to zero, flip the constant's sign, and compute 4 + 5 - (-3) = 12.

### Q714 · Cubic & Symmetric Identities

If a^2 + b^2 + c^2 = ab + bc + ca (where a, b, c ≠ 0), find the value of (a + c)/b.

A. 1
B. 2
C. 3
D. 0

**Answer:** B. 2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 2 (Correct):** Rewrite a^2 + b^2 + c^2 - ab - bc - ca = 0 as:
(1/2)[(a - b)^2 + (b - c)^2 + (c - a)^2] = 0.
This implies a = b = c.
Substituting b = a and c = a: (a + c)/b = (a + a)/a = 2a/a = 2.
- **A — 1:** Result of forgetting to add a and c before dividing by b.
- **C — 3:** Result of evaluating (a + b + c)/b instead of (a + c)/b.
- **D — 0:** Result of confusing the condition with a + b + c = 0.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** a^2 + b^2 + c^2 = ab + bc + ca always means a = b = c. Set a = b = c = 1 to evaluate instantly.

### Q715 · Cubic & Symmetric Identities

Simplify: (0.73^3 + 0.27^3) / (0.73^2 - 0.73 * 0.27 + 0.27^2).

A. 0.46
B. 0.54
C. 1
D. 1.46

**Answer:** C. 1
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 1 (Correct):** Using the identity (a^3 + b^3)/(a^2 - ab + b^2) = a + b with a = 0.73 and b = 0.27:
Value = 0.73 + 0.27 = 1.
- **A — 0.46:** Result of computing a - b instead of a + b.
- **B — 0.54:** Result of doubling 0.27 instead of adding 0.73 and 0.27.
- **D — 1.46:** Result of doubling 0.73 instead of adding 0.73 and 0.27.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Recognize (a^3 + b^3)/(a^2 - ab + b^2) = a + b. Simply add 0.73 + 0.27 = 1.

### Q716 · Cubic & Symmetric Identities

If a + b = 8 and ab = 15, find the value of a^3 + b^3.

A. 512
B. 467
C. 872
D. 152

**Answer:** D. 152
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 152 (Correct):** Using a^3 + b^3 = (a + b)^3 - 3ab(a + b):
a^3 + b^3 = 8^3 - 3(15)(8) = 512 - 360 = 152.
- **A — 512:** Result of computing (a + b)^3 and forgetting to subtract 3ab(a + b).
- **B — 467:** Result of subtracting 3ab (45) without multiplying by (a + b).
- **C — 872:** Result of adding 3ab(a + b) instead of subtracting it from 512.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Alternatively, spot factors of 15 that sum to 8: 5 and 3. Then 5^3 + 3^3 = 125 + 27 = 152.

### Q717 · Cubic & Symmetric Identities

If x + y = 10, xy = 21, and x > y, find the value of x^2 - y^2.

A. 58
B. 40
C. 16
D. 20

**Answer:** B. 40
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 40 (Correct):** Since x > y, x - y = √((x + y)^2 - 4xy) = √(10^2 - 4 * 21) = √(100 - 84) = √16 = 4.
Then x^2 - y^2 = (x + y)(x - y) = 10 * 4 = 40.
- **A — 58:** Result of computing x^2 + y^2 = 100 - 42 = 58 instead of x^2 - y^2.
- **C — 16:** Result of computing (x - y)^2 instead of (x + y)(x - y).
- **D — 20:** Result of dividing 40 by 2 due to a factor-of-2 error.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Spot two numbers with sum 10 and product 21: 7 and 3. Then 7^2 - 3^2 = 49 - 9 = 40.

### Q718 · Quadratic Equations

For what positive value of k does the quadratic equation 2x^2 + kx + 18 = 0 have real and equal roots?

A. 12
B. 6
C. 36
D. 24

**Answer:** A. 12
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 12 (Correct):** For real and equal roots, discriminant D = b^2 - 4ac = 0:
k^2 - 4(2)(18) = 0 => k^2 = 144.
Since k > 0, k = 12.
- **B — 6:** Result of setting b^2 = ac instead of 4ac (giving √36 = 6).
- **C — 36:** Result of setting k = 2 * 18 without taking the square root of 4ac.
- **D — 24:** Result of multiplying 2 * 12 after taking the square root.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Set b^2 = 4ac, so k = 2√(ac) = 2√(2 * 18) = 2 * 6 = 12.

### Q719 · Quadratic Equations

If α and β are the roots of x^2 - 7x + 10 = 0, find the value of α^2 + β^2.

A. 49
B. 39
C. 29
D. 69

**Answer:** C. 29
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 29 (Correct):** For x^2 - 7x + 10 = 0, sum of roots α + β = 7 and product αβ = 10.
Thus, α^2 + β^2 = (α + β)^2 - 2αβ = 7^2 - 2(10) = 49 - 20 = 29.
- **A — 49:** Result of computing (α + β)^2 and forgetting to subtract 2αβ.
- **B — 39:** Result of subtracting αβ once instead of 2αβ.
- **D — 69:** Result of adding 2αβ instead of subtracting it from 49.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Alternatively, factorize: roots are 5 and 2, so 5^2 + 2^2 = 25 + 4 = 29.

### Q720 · Quadratic Equations

If α and β are the roots of 3x^2 - 12x + 5 = 0, find the value of 1/α + 1/β.

A. -12/5
B. 12/5
C. 5/12
D. -5/12

**Answer:** B. 12/5
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 12/5 (Correct):** For 3x^2 - 12x + 5 = 0, sum of roots α + β = 12/3 = 4 and product αβ = 5/3.
Then 1/α + 1/β = (α + β)/(αβ) = 4 / (5/3) = 12/5.
- **A — -12/5:** Result of using +b/c instead of -b/c (sign error in sum of roots).
- **C — 5/12:** Result of inverting the ratio to c/(-b) = 5/12.
- **D — -5/12:** Result of inverting the ratio and making a sign error (-5/12).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** In ax^2 + bx + c = 0, 1/α + 1/β = -b/c directly (the 'a' cancels out). Here -(-12)/5 = 12/5.

### Q721 · Quadratic Equations

Find the quadratic equation whose roots are 3 + √2 and 3 - √2.

A. x^2 + 6x + 7 = 0
B. x^2 - 6x + 11 = 0
C. x^2 - 6x - 7 = 0
D. x^2 - 6x + 7 = 0

**Answer:** D. x^2 - 6x + 7 = 0
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — x^2 - 6x + 7 = 0 (Correct):** Sum of roots = (3 + √2) + (3 - √2) = 6.
Product of roots = (3 + √2)(3 - √2) = 9 - 2 = 7.
The quadratic equation is x^2 - (sum)x + product = 0 => x^2 - 6x + 7 = 0.
- **A — x^2 + 6x + 7 = 0:** Result of using + (sum)x instead of - (sum)x in the standard form.
- **B — x^2 - 6x + 11 = 0:** Result of adding 9 + 2 instead of subtracting 9 - 2 for the product of conjugates.
- **C — x^2 - 6x - 7 = 0:** Result of subtracting the product instead of adding it in the standard form.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use x^2 - (sum)x + product = 0. Sum = 2 * 3 = 6, Product = 3^2 - 2 = 7.

### Q722 · Quadratic Equations

If one root of the equation x^2 - 11x + k = 0 is 4, find the value of k.

A. 44
B. 16
C. 28
D. 7

**Answer:** C. 28
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 28 (Correct):** For x^2 - 11x + k = 0, the sum of roots is 11.
Since one root is 4, the other root is 11 - 4 = 7.
Product of roots k = 4 * 7 = 28.
- **A — 44:** Result of multiplying 11 by 4 instead of finding the second root first.
- **B — 16:** Result of squaring the given root 4 instead of finding the product of both roots.
- **D — 7:** Result of finding the second root (7) and stopping before computing k.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Substitute x = 4 directly: 4^2 - 11(4) + k = 0 => 16 - 44 + k = 0 => k = 28.

### Q723 · Quadratic Equations

Find the discriminant of the quadratic equation 4x^2 - 5x + 3 = 0.

A. -23
B. 23
C. 73
D. 13

**Answer:** A. -23
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — -23 (Correct):** For ax^2 + bx + c = 0 with a = 4, b = -5, c = 3:
Discriminant D = b^2 - 4ac = (-5)^2 - 4(4)(3) = 25 - 48 = -23.
- **B — 23:** Result of subtracting in the wrong order (48 - 25 = 23).
- **C — 73:** Result of adding b^2 + 4ac instead of subtracting b^2 - 4ac.
- **D — 13:** Result of forgetting the factor of 4 in 4ac (computing 25 - 12 = 13).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Compute b^2 - 4ac = 25 - 48 = -23 directly; negative D indicates complex roots.

### Q724 · Quadratic Equations

If α and β are the roots of x^2 - 5x + 6 = 0, find the value of α^3 + β^3.

A. 125
B. 215
C. 35
D. 95

**Answer:** C. 35
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 35 (Correct):** For x^2 - 5x + 6 = 0, sum α + β = 5 and product αβ = 6.
Then α^3 + β^3 = (α + β)^3 - 3αβ(α + β) = 5^3 - 3(6)(5) = 125 - 90 = 35.
- **A — 125:** Result of cubing (α + β) and forgetting to subtract 3αβ(α + β).
- **B — 215:** Result of adding 3αβ(α + β) instead of subtracting it from 125.
- **D — 95:** Result of forgetting the factor of 3 in 3αβ(α + β) (computing 125 - 30 = 95).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Factorize quickly: roots are 2 and 3, so 2^3 + 3^3 = 8 + 27 = 35.

### Q725 · Quadratic Equations

For what maximum integer value of k does kx^2 + 8x + 2 = 0 have two distinct real roots?

A. 7
B. 8
C. 9
D. 6

**Answer:** A. 7
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 7 (Correct):** For two distinct real roots, discriminant D > 0 (and k ≠ 0):
8^2 - 4(k)(2) > 0 => 64 - 8k > 0 => 8k < 64 => k < 8.
The maximum integer value of k satisfying k < 8 is 7.
- **B — 8:** Result of using D ≥ 0 instead of strict D > 0 for distinct roots.
- **C — 9:** Result of reversing the inequality sign to k > 8.
- **D — 6:** Result of an off-by-one error after correctly finding k < 8.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Distinct real roots require strict inequality D > 0, so k < 8 gives max integer 7 (not 8).

### Q726 · Quadratic Equations

If α and β are the roots of 2x^2 - 6x + 3 = 0, find the value of (α/β) + (β/α).

A. 6
B. 4
C. 8
D. 2

**Answer:** B. 4
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 4 (Correct):** For 2x^2 - 6x + 3 = 0, sum α + β = 6/2 = 3 and product αβ = 3/2.
Now α/β + β/α = (α^2 + β^2)/(αβ) = [(α + β)^2 - 2αβ]/(αβ) = (9 - 3)/(3/2) = 6 / 1.5 = 4.
- **A — 6:** Result of computing α^2 + β^2 = 6 and forgetting to divide by αβ.
- **C — 8:** Result of adding 2αβ instead of subtracting 2αβ in the numerator (12 / 1.5 = 8).
- **D — 2:** Result of forgetting to divide c by a = 2 when finding αβ (computing 6 / 3 = 2).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Use α/β + β/α = (α + β)^2/(αβ) - 2 = 9 / 1.5 - 2 = 6 - 2 = 4.

### Q727 · Linear Equations & Surds

For what value of k does the system 3x + 4y = 12 and 6x + ky = 24 have infinitely many solutions?

A. 4
B. 6
C. 12
D. 8

**Answer:** D. 8
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 8 (Correct):** For infinitely many solutions, a1/a2 = b1/b2 = c1/c2:
3/6 = 4/k = 12/24 => 1/2 = 4/k => k = 8.
- **A — 4:** Result of setting k equal to b1 directly without scaling by 2.
- **B — 6:** Result of setting k equal to a2 = 6.
- **C — 12:** Result of setting k equal to c1 = 12.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Notice the second equation is 2× the first (6x = 2*3x, 24 = 2*12), so k = 2 * 4 = 8.

### Q728 · Linear Equations & Surds

For what value of k does the system 4x + 5y = 9 and 8x + ky = 15 have no solution?

A. 10
B. 5
C. 8
D. 12

**Answer:** A. 10
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 10 (Correct):** For no solution (parallel lines), a1/a2 = b1/b2 ≠ c1/c2:
4/8 = 5/k ≠ 9/15 => 1/2 = 5/k => k = 10 (and 1/2 ≠ 3/5 holds).
- **B — 5:** Result of setting k equal to b1 = 5 directly.
- **C — 8:** Result of setting k equal to a2 = 8.
- **D — 12:** Result of cross-multiplying (4 * 15)/5 = 12 using the constant term.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Match the x- and y-coefficient ratios: 8/4 = 2, so k = 2 * 5 = 10.

### Q729 · Linear Equations & Surds

Find the area of the triangle formed by the line 3x + 4y = 24 and the coordinate axes.

A. 48 sq. units
B. 12 sq. units
C. 24 sq. units
D. 36 sq. units

**Answer:** C. 24 sq. units
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 24 sq. units (Correct):** Set y = 0 to find the x-intercept: 3x = 24 => x = 8.
Set x = 0 to find the y-intercept: 4y = 24 => y = 6.
Area of the right triangle = (1/2) * 8 * 6 = 24 sq. units.
- **A — 48 sq. units:** Result of multiplying the intercepts (8 * 6) without dividing by 2.
- **B — 12 sq. units:** Result of multiplying the coefficients 3 * 4 instead of using the intercepts.
- **D — 36 sq. units:** Result of miscomputing the y-intercept as 9 instead of 6.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Area of triangle formed by ax + by = c with axes is c^2 / (2ab). Here 24^2 / (2*3*4) = 576 / 24 = 24.

### Q730 · Linear Equations & Surds

At what point do the lines 2x + 3y = 13 and 5x - 2y = 4 intersect?

A. (3, 2)
B. (2, 3)
C. (4, 1)
D. (1, 4)

**Answer:** B. (2, 3)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — (2, 3) (Correct):** Multiply 2x + 3y = 13 by 2: 4x + 6y = 26.
Multiply 5x - 2y = 4 by 3: 15x - 6y = 12.
Adding both: 19x = 38 => x = 2.
Substituting x = 2 into 2(2) + 3y = 13 => 3y = 9 => y = 3. Intersection: (2, 3).
- **A — (3, 2):** Result of swapping the x and y coordinates.
- **C — (4, 1):** Result of an elimination arithmetic error satisfying neither equation.
- **D — (1, 4):** Result of solving 2x + 3y = 14 instead of 13.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** In MCQs, plug the options into 5x - 2y = 4: only (2, 3) gives 10 - 6 = 4. Takes 5 seconds!

### Q731 · Linear Equations & Surds

If x = 7 + 4√3, find the value of √x + 1/√x.

A. 2√3
B. 14
C. 8
D. 4

**Answer:** D. 4
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 4 (Correct):** Write 7 + 4√3 as (2 + √3)^2, since 2^2 + (√3)^2 + 4√3 = 4 + 3 + 4√3 = 7 + 4√3.
Thus √x = 2 + √3, and 1/√x = 1/(2 + √3) = 2 - √3.
Adding them: √x + 1/√x = (2 + √3) + (2 - √3) = 4.
- **A — 2√3:** Result of subtracting 1/√x instead of adding it to √x.
- **B — 14:** Result of computing x + 1/x = 14 instead of √x + 1/√x.
- **C — 8:** Result of doubling 4 after finding √x + 1/√x = 4.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Alternatively, x + 1/x = (7 + 4√3) + (7 - 4√3) = 14, so √x + 1/√x = √(14 + 2) = √16 = 4.

### Q732 · Linear Equations & Surds

If x = (√5 + 1)/(√5 - 1) and y = (√5 - 1)/(√5 + 1), find the value of x^2 + y^2.

A. 9
B. 11
C. 7
D. 5

**Answer:** C. 7
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 7 (Correct):** Notice xy = 1. Also, x + y = 2(5 + 1)/(5 - 1) = 12/4 = 3.
Therefore, x^2 + y^2 = (x + y)^2 - 2xy = 3^2 - 2(1) = 9 - 2 = 7.
- **A — 9:** Result of computing (x + y)^2 and forgetting to subtract 2xy.
- **B — 11:** Result of adding 2xy instead of subtracting 2xy from 9.
- **D — 5:** Result of subtracting 4 instead of 2 from 9.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** For x = (√a + √b)/(√a - √b) and y = 1/x, x + y = 2(a + b)/(a - b). Here 2(6)/4 = 3, so 3^2 - 2 = 7.

### Q733 · Linear Equations & Surds

If 2^x = 3^y = 6^(-z), find the value of 1/x + 1/y + 1/z.

A. 0
B. 1
C. -1
D. 2

**Answer:** A. 0
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 0 (Correct):** Let 2^x = 3^y = 6^(-z) = k.
Then 2 = k^(1/x), 3 = k^(1/y), and 6 = k^(-1/z).
Since 2 * 3 = 6, k^(1/x) * k^(1/y) = k^(-1/z) => 1/x + 1/y = -1/z.
Hence, 1/x + 1/y + 1/z = 0.
- **B — 1:** Result of writing 1/x + 1/y = 1/z with a sign error on z.
- **C — -1:** Result of equating the sum of reciprocals to -1 instead of 0.
- **D — 2:** Result of adding the bases' exponents 1 + 1 = 2.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Whenever a^x = b^y = c^(-z) and ab = c, 1/x + 1/y + 1/z = 0 automatically.

### Q734 · Linear Equations & Surds

If 3^(x - y) = 27 and 3^(x + y) = 243, find the value of x^2 - y^2.

A. 8
B. 15
C. 16
D. 12

**Answer:** B. 15
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 15 (Correct):** Since 27 = 3^3 and 243 = 3^5:
x - y = 3 and x + y = 5.
Therefore, x^2 - y^2 = (x - y)(x + y) = 3 * 5 = 15.
- **A — 8:** Result of adding (x - y) + (x + y) = 3 + 5 = 8 instead of multiplying them.
- **C — 16:** Result of finding x = 4 and computing x^2 = 16 without subtracting y^2.
- **D — 12:** Result of taking 243 as 3^4 instead of 3^5 (giving 3 * 4 = 12).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** No need to solve for x and y separately! Multiply the exponents directly: (x - y)(x + y) = 3 * 5 = 15.

### Q735 · Data Interpretation

The table shows car production (in thousands) by four companies over three years:
Company | 2021 | 2022 | 2023
A       |  40  |  50  |  60
B       |  50  |  65  |  80
C       |  60  |  70  |  95
D       |  50  |  55  |  65
Find the ratio of total production of Company A to Company C over the 3 years.

A. 3 : 4
B. 3 : 2
C. 4 : 5
D. 2 : 3

**Answer:** D. 2 : 3
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 2 : 3 (Correct):** Total production of Company A = 40 + 50 + 60 = 150 thousand.
Total production of Company C = 60 + 70 + 95 = 225 thousand.
Required ratio = 150 : 225 = 2 : 3 (dividing both by 75).
- **A — 3 : 4:** Result of miscalculating Company C's total as 200 instead of 225.
- **B — 3 : 2:** Result of reversing the ratio to Company C : Company A.
- **C — 4 : 5:** Result of miscalculating Company A's total as 180 instead of 150.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Divide 150 and 225 by 25 first (6 : 9), then by 3 to get 2 : 3 in seconds.

### Q736 · Data Interpretation

The table shows car production (in thousands) by four companies over three years:
Company | 2021 | 2022 | 2023
A       |  40  |  50  |  60
B       |  50  |  65  |  80
C       |  60  |  70  |  95
D       |  50  |  55  |  65
What is the percentage growth in the production of Company B from 2021 to 2023?

A. 37.5%
B. 60%
C. 50%
D. 30%

**Answer:** B. 60%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 60% (Correct):** Company B production in 2021 = 50 thousand; in 2023 = 80 thousand.
Growth = 80 - 50 = 30 thousand.
Percentage growth = (30 / 50) * 100 = 60%.
- **A — 37.5%:** Result of dividing the growth (30) by 2023's value (80) instead of 2021's base (50).
- **C — 50%:** Result of using 60 instead of 50 as the base or miscomputing 30/60.
- **D — 30%:** Result of taking the raw difference (30) as the percentage without dividing by 50.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Base is 50, so simply double the difference: (80 - 50) * 2 = 60%.

### Q737 · Data Interpretation

The table shows car production (in thousands) by four companies over three years:
Company | 2021 | 2022 | 2023
A       |  40  |  50  |  60
B       |  50  |  65  |  80
C       |  60  |  70  |  95
D       |  50  |  55  |  65
Find the average car production of all four companies in 2022.

A. 60 thousand
B. 65 thousand
C. 55 thousand
D. 80 thousand

**Answer:** A. 60 thousand
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 60 thousand (Correct):** Total production in 2022 = 50 + 65 + 70 + 55 = 240 thousand.
Average production = 240 / 4 = 60 thousand.
- **B — 65 thousand:** Result of averaging only Companies B and C or miscomputing the sum as 260.
- **C — 55 thousand:** Result of miscomputing the sum as 220 instead of 240.
- **D — 80 thousand:** Result of dividing 240 by 3 (number of years) instead of 4 (number of companies).
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Assume 60 as the mean and sum deviations: (-10) + (+5) + (+10) + (-5) = 0, so average is 60!

### Q738 · Data Interpretation

The table shows car production (in thousands) by four companies over three years:
Company | 2021 | 2022 | 2023
A       |  40  |  50  |  60
B       |  50  |  65  |  80
C       |  60  |  70  |  95
D       |  50  |  55  |  65
Total production of all companies in 2023 is what percent more than that in 2021?

A. 33.33%
B. 40%
C. 50%
D. 100%

**Answer:** C. 50%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 50% (Correct):** Total production in 2021 = 40 + 50 + 60 + 50 = 200 thousand.
Total production in 2023 = 60 + 80 + 95 + 65 = 300 thousand.
Percentage more = ((300 - 200) / 200) * 100 = (100 / 200) * 100 = 50%.
- **A — 33.33%:** Result of dividing the increase (100) by 2023's total (300) instead of 2021's total (200).
- **B — 40%:** Result of miscalculating 2023's total as 280 instead of 300.
- **D — 100%:** Result of reporting the absolute difference (100) as a percentage.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 300 vs 200 is a 3 : 2 ratio, which means 1/2 = 50% more.

### Q739 · Data Interpretation

A pie chart represents a family's monthly income of ₹72,000 distributed as: Rent = 90°, Food = 108°, Education = 54°, Transport = 36°, Savings = 72°. Find the difference between the expenditure on Food and Savings.

A. ₹3,600
B. ₹10,800
C. ₹14,400
D. ₹7,200

**Answer:** D. ₹7,200
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ₹7,200 (Correct):** Angle difference between Food and Savings = 108° - 72° = 36°.
Difference in expenditure = (36 / 360) * ₹72,000 = (1/10) * ₹72,000 = ₹7,200.
- **A — ₹3,600:** Result of taking an 18° difference instead of 36°.
- **B — ₹10,800:** Result of computing Education expenditure (54°) instead of Food minus Savings.
- **C — ₹14,400:** Result of computing Savings alone (72°) instead of the difference.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 1° = 72,000 / 360 = ₹200. So 36° difference = 36 * 200 = ₹7,200!

### Q740 · Data Interpretation

A pie chart represents a family's monthly income of ₹72,000 distributed as: Rent = 90°, Food = 108°, Education = 54°, Transport = 36°, Savings = 72°. What is the percentage share of Education in the total monthly income?

A. 15%
B. 18%
C. 20%
D. 12%

**Answer:** A. 15%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 15% (Correct):** Central angle for Education = 54°.
Percentage share = (54 / 360) * 100 = (3 / 20) * 100 = 15%.
- **B — 18%:** Result of dividing 54 by 3 instead of 3.6.
- **C — 20%:** Result of computing Savings' percentage share (72 / 3.6 = 20%) instead of Education's.
- **D — 12%:** Result of dividing 54 by 4.5 instead of 3.6.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** In a pie chart, 3.6° = 1%. Divide 54 by 3.6: 540 / 36 = 15%.

### Q741 · Data Interpretation

A pie chart represents a family's monthly income of ₹72,000 distributed as: Rent = 90°, Food = 108°, Education = 54°, Transport = 36°, Savings = 72°. Find the ratio of expenditure on Rent to Transport.

A. 2 : 5
B. 3 : 1
C. 5 : 2
D. 5 : 3

**Answer:** C. 5 : 2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 5 : 2 (Correct):** Since both belong to the same pie chart, the ratio of amounts equals the ratio of their central angles:
Rent : Transport = 90° : 36° = 5 : 2 (dividing both by 18).
- **A — 2 : 5:** Result of reversing the ratio to Transport : Rent.
- **B — 3 : 1:** Result of dividing 90 by 30 instead of 36, or miscomputing 90 : 36.
- **D — 5 : 3:** Result of misreading Education (54°) as Transport (36°) to get 90 : 54 = 5 : 3.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Never convert degrees to rupees for a ratio—simplify the degrees directly: 90 : 36 = 5 : 2.

### Q742 · Data Interpretation

A pie chart represents a family's monthly income of ₹72,000 distributed as: Rent = 90°, Food = 108°, Education = 54°, Transport = 36°, Savings = 72°. If the Savings amount increases by 25%, what is the new Savings amount?

A. ₹16,200
B. ₹18,000
C. ₹17,280
D. ₹21,600

**Answer:** B. ₹18,000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — ₹18,000 (Correct):** Current Savings = (72 / 360) * ₹72,000 = (1/5) * ₹72,000 = ₹14,400.
After a 25% increase: ₹14,400 * 1.25 = ₹14,400 + ₹3,600 = ₹18,000.
- **A — ₹16,200:** Result of increasing Savings by 12.5% instead of 25%.
- **C — ₹17,280:** Result of increasing Savings by 20% instead of 25%.
- **D — ₹21,600:** Result of increasing Savings by 50% instead of 25%.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 1° = ₹200, so Savings = 72 * 200 = ₹14,400. Add 1/4 of 14,400 (3,600) = ₹18,000.

### Q743 · Data Interpretation

The table shows students appeared and passed (in hundreds) in four streams of a college:
Stream     | Appeared | Passed
Science    |    12    |    9
Commerce   |    15    |   12
Arts       |    20    |   14
Vocational |    13    |    7
What is the pass percentage of the Commerce stream?

A. 75%
B. 70%
C. 85%
D. 80%

**Answer:** D. 80%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 80% (Correct):** In Commerce, 15 hundred appeared and 12 hundred passed.
Pass percentage = (12 / 15) * 100 = (4 / 5) * 100 = 80%.
- **A — 75%:** Result of computing Science's pass percentage (9/12 = 75%) instead of Commerce's.
- **B — 70%:** Result of computing Arts' pass percentage (14/20 = 70%) instead of Commerce's.
- **C — 85%:** Result of computing 12 / 14 instead of 12 / 15.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 12/15 simplifies immediately to 4/5 = 80%.

### Q744 · Data Interpretation

The table shows students appeared and passed (in hundreds) in four streams of a college:
Stream     | Appeared | Passed
Science    |    12    |    9
Commerce   |    15    |   12
Arts       |    20    |   14
Vocational |    13    |    7
Find the ratio of total passed students in Science and Commerce together to failed students in Arts and Vocational together.

A. 7 : 4
B. 4 : 7
C. 7 : 5
D. 21 : 33

**Answer:** A. 7 : 4
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 7 : 4 (Correct):** Passed in Science & Commerce = 9 + 12 = 21 hundred.
Failed in Arts = 20 - 14 = 6 hundred; Failed in Vocational = 13 - 7 = 6 hundred.
Total failed in Arts & Vocational = 6 + 6 = 12 hundred.
Required ratio = 21 : 12 = 7 : 4.
- **B — 4 : 7:** Result of reversing the ratio to Failed : Passed.
- **C — 7 : 5:** Result of miscomputing Vocational failed students as 9 instead of 6.
- **D — 21 : 33:** Result of taking total appeared (33) instead of failed (12) for Arts & Vocational.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Skip multiplying by 100 since the unit cancels in ratios: 21 : 12 = 7 : 4.

### Q745 · Data Interpretation

The table shows students appeared and passed (in hundreds) in four streams of a college:
Stream     | Appeared | Passed
Science    |    12    |    9
Commerce   |    15    |   12
Arts       |    20    |   14
Vocational |    13    |    7
Find the average number of students who appeared across all four streams.

A. 1,400
B. 1,500
C. 1,600
D. 1,050

**Answer:** B. 1,500
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 1,500 (Correct):** Total appeared students = (12 + 15 + 20 + 13) * 100 = 60 * 100 = 6,000.
Average = 6,000 / 4 = 1,500.
- **A — 1,400:** Result of miscomputing the sum as 56 hundred instead of 60 hundred.
- **C — 1,600:** Result of miscomputing the sum as 64 hundred instead of 60 hundred.
- **D — 1,050:** Result of computing the average of passed students (42/4 = 10.5 hundred) instead of appeared.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Sum in hundreds = 60, divide by 4 = 15 hundred = 1,500.

### Q746 · Data Interpretation

The table shows students appeared and passed (in hundreds) in four streams of a college:
Stream     | Appeared | Passed
Science    |    12    |    9
Commerce   |    15    |   12
Arts       |    20    |   14
Vocational |    13    |    7
What is the overall pass percentage across all four streams combined?

A. 65%
B. 72.5%
C. 75%
D. 70%

**Answer:** D. 70%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 70% (Correct):** Total appeared = 12 + 15 + 20 + 13 = 60 hundred.
Total passed = 9 + 12 + 14 + 7 = 42 hundred.
Overall pass percentage = (42 / 60) * 100 = (7 / 10) * 100 = 70%.
- **A — 65%:** Result of miscomputing total passed as 39 instead of 42.
- **B — 72.5%:** Result of taking the unweighted average of the 4 stream pass percentages.
- **C — 75%:** Result of computing 45/60 = 75% due to an addition slip.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 42/60 simplifies by 6 to 7/10 = 70%. Never average the individual stream percentages!

### Q747 · Data Interpretation

The table shows Exports and Imports (in ₹ crore) of a firm from 2019 to 2022:
Year | Exports | Imports
2019 |   120   |   100
2020 |   150   |   125
2021 |   160   |   140
2022 |   210   |   155
Find the total trade surplus (Exports - Imports) over the four years.

A. ₹100 crore
B. ₹140 crore
C. ₹120 crore
D. ₹80 crore

**Answer:** C. ₹120 crore
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — ₹120 crore (Correct):** Total Exports = 120 + 150 + 160 + 210 = ₹640 crore.
Total Imports = 100 + 125 + 140 + 155 = ₹520 crore.
Total trade surplus = 640 - 520 = ₹120 crore.
- **A — ₹100 crore:** Result of an addition error summing year-by-year surpluses to 100 instead of 120.
- **B — ₹140 crore:** Result of miscomputing 2022 surplus as 75 instead of 55.
- **D — ₹80 crore:** Result of omitting one year's surplus (20 or 40) from the total.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** Add year-by-year surpluses instead of large totals: 20 + 25 + 20 + 55 = ₹120 crore!

### Q748 · Data Interpretation

The table shows Exports and Imports (in ₹ crore) of a firm from 2019 to 2022:
Year | Exports | Imports
2019 |   120   |   100
2020 |   150   |   125
2021 |   160   |   140
2022 |   210   |   155
What is the percentage increase in Exports from 2020 to 2022?

A. 28.57%
B. 40%
C. 35%
D. 60%

**Answer:** B. 40%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 40% (Correct):** Exports in 2020 = 150; Exports in 2022 = 210.
Increase = 210 - 150 = 60.
Percentage increase = (60 / 150) * 100 = (2 / 5) * 100 = 40%.
- **A — 28.57%:** Result of dividing the increase (60) by 2022's value (210) instead of 2020's base (150).
- **C — 35%:** Result of miscomputing 60 / 150 as 35%.
- **D — 60%:** Result of reporting the absolute difference (60) as a percentage.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 60/150 = 2/5 = 40% immediately.

### Q749 · Data Interpretation

The table shows Exports and Imports (in ₹ crore) of a firm from 2019 to 2022:
Year | Exports | Imports
2019 |   120   |   100
2020 |   150   |   125
2021 |   160   |   140
2022 |   210   |   155
Find the ratio of average Exports to average Imports over the four years.

A. 16 : 13
B. 13 : 16
C. 15 : 13
D. 8 : 7

**Answer:** A. 16 : 13
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 16 : 13 (Correct):** Average Exports = 640 / 4 = 160.
Average Imports = 520 / 4 = 130.
Ratio = 160 : 130 = 16 : 13.
- **B — 13 : 16:** Result of reversing the ratio to Imports : Exports.
- **C — 15 : 13:** Result of miscalculating Total Exports as 600 instead of 640.
- **D — 8 : 7:** Result of taking 2021's ratio (160 : 140 = 8 : 7) instead of the 4-year average.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** The ratio of averages equals the ratio of totals (640 : 520 = 16 : 13)—skip dividing by 4!

### Q750 · Data Interpretation

The table shows Exports and Imports (in ₹ crore) of a firm from 2019 to 2022:
Year | Exports | Imports
2019 |   120   |   100
2020 |   150   |   125
2021 |   160   |   140
2022 |   210   |   155
In 2021, Imports are what percent of Exports?

A. 85%
B. 114.28%
C. 87.5%
D. 80%

**Answer:** C. 87.5%
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 87.5% (Correct):** In 2021, Imports = 140 and Exports = 160.
Required percentage = (140 / 160) * 100 = (7 / 8) * 100 = 87.5%.
- **A — 85%:** Result of approximating 7/8 as 85% instead of 87.5%.
- **B — 114.28%:** Result of computing Exports as a percent of Imports (160/140 = 114.28%).
- **D — 80%:** Result of computing 120/150 = 80% or misreading the 2021 row.
- **Formula / Shortcut:** 140/160 = 7/8, and 1/8 = 12.5%, so 7/8 = 100% - 12.5% = 87.5%.
